@@ i | About Bhakti – Introduction |
About Bhakti – INTRODUCTION
In this world full of desire, restlessness and duties, all of us are on a search.
What is that search? — for peace, for bliss, for a love that never diminishes.
But there is one truth that the great souls have given us.
What we seek is not somewhere outside; it is within us.
What awakens it? — Bhakti, devotion.

In the Bhagavad Gita, Lord Sri Krishna speaks of four kinds of devotees:
The four kinds of devotees
Ārta Bhakta – the devotee who takes refuge in the Lord in times of suffering
Arthārthī Bhakta – one who approaches the Lord seeking worldly good
Jijñāsu Bhakta – the seeker who longs to know God
Jñānī Bhakta – the devotee who asks for nothing and desires only the Lord
The Lord accepts all of them with compassion.
But the devotee who loves the Lord alone, expecting nothing, is the most dear to Him.
That is Prema Bhakti, devotion of pure love.
It is not an emotion;
it is a state;
it is not something we do; it is something we become.

The teaching of Prahlada
The boy Prahlada lived and showed the supreme truth of devotion.
He said:
śravaṇaṁ kīrtanaṁ viṣṇōḥ smaraṇaṁ pādasēvanam ।
arcanaṁ vandanaṁ dāsyaṁ sakhyamātmanivēdanam ॥
Meaning:
Devotion is —
listening (śravaṇam) to the Lord's divine play
singing (kīrtanam) His name
always remembering (smaraṇam) Him
serving His feet (pādasēvanam)
worshipping (arcanam) Him
bowing (vandanam) to Him
living as His servant (dāsyam)
being with Him as a friend (sakhyam)
offering oneself completely to Him (ātmanivēdanam)

The path of the great souls
Who showed us this path of devotion?
Bhadrachala Ramadasa – who saw Rama even in a prison cell
Purandaradasa – who spread devotion into every home
Sadashiva Brahmendra – the yogi absorbed in silence
Tyagaraja Swamigal – who melted in the name of Rama
Annamacharya – who sang of the Lord of Tirupati
Narayana Tirtha – who turned Krishna's divine play into music
Vijaya Gopala – who spread the glory of the divine name
Meera Bai – who melted completely in devotion to Krishna
Tulasidasa – for whom the name of Rama was life itself
The Alvars – who poured out devotion in Tamil
The Marathi saints – who lifted villages into devotion with their abhangs
All of them are united in one thing —
Nama Sankirtanam and Bhakti.

The glory of Nama Sankirtanam
Nama Sankirtanam is not a music performance.
It is not an achievement.
It is a path that dissolves the ego.
A way that opens the heart.
The Lord once said:
“There are only two simple ways to liberation —
Nama Sankirtanam and Prapatti (surrender).”
No great sacrifice, no difficult practice — one name is enough.
Even if that name begins on the tongue,
it will reach the heart.

What is bliss (ānandam)?
Bliss is not ordinary pleasure.
Pleasure comes and goes.
But bliss is steady.
It does not change even when the world changes.
That is ānandam.
The feeling “I am not alone; I belong to the Lord”
is bliss.

The role of kirtans
The kirtans we sing are not mere songs.
They are lamps on the path of devotion.
Devotion is not something done one day a week.
It is the flow of our life.
When we sing together, we grow together.
When we remember the name, the ego disappears.

About Harinamadwar
A sacred platform created to bring this journey of devotion together —
“Harinamadwar”
It is a confluence of:
satsang
nama kirtanam
sharing of devotion
spiritual growth
It is not a group.
It is a movement.
The name should live not only in books or on the stage,
but in our homes, in our breath, in our lives.

Conclusion
Let us walk in the path of the great souls.
Let us chant the name.
Let us live in love.
Let us abide in bliss.

Hari Nāma Saṅkīrtanam Jayati!

@@ 1.3 | Dhyana Slokam |
DHYANA SLOKAM
1. śuklāmbaradharaṁ viṣṇum
śuklāmbaradharaṁ viṣṇuṁ śaśivarṇaṁ caturbhujam । prasannavadanaṁ dhyāyēt sarvavighnōpa śāntayē ॥
Word meanings
śuklāmbaradharam – who wears white garments
viṣṇum – who pervades everything
śaśivarṇam – who shines like the moon
caturbhujam – who has four arms
prasannavadanam – whose face is full of grace
dhyāyēt – one should meditate
sarvavighnōpa śāntayē – for the removal of all obstacles
Summary
We meditate on Lord Vinayaka, who removes all obstacles and blesses our work with success.
Significance
It shows that every sacred undertaking should begin with remembering Vighneshwara.

2. śrīkāntō mātulō yasya
śrīkāntō mātulō yasya jananī sarvamaṅgalā । janakaḥ śaṅkarō dēvaḥ taṁ vandē kuñjarānanam ॥
Word meanings
śrīkāntaḥ – Mahavishnu
mātulaḥ – maternal uncle
jananī – mother
sarvamaṅgalā – Goddess Parvati
janakaḥ – father
śaṅkaraḥ – Lord Shiva
kuñjarānanam – Ganapati, the elephant-faced one
Summary
I bow to Ganapati, whose uncle is Vishnu, whose mother is Parvati and whose father is Shankara.
Significance
A beautiful slokam expressing the harmony of Shaiva and Vaishnava traditions.

3. bhagavan nāmasāmrājya
bhagavan nāmasāmrājya lakṣmī sarvasva vigraham । śrīmat bōdhēndra yōgīndra dēśikēndram upāsmahē ॥
Word meanings
bhagavan nāma sāmrājyam – the kingdom of the Lord's name
lakṣmī sarvasva vigraham – the embodiment of all its wealth
bōdhēndra yōgīndra – Sri Bodhendra Swamigal
upāsmahē – we meditate on
Summary
We meditate on Bodhendra Swamigal, who proclaimed the glory of the Lord's name to the world.
Significance
It shows the supreme importance of Nama Sankirtanam in Kali yuga.

4. prahlāda nārada parāśara
prahlāda nārada parāśara puṇḍarīka vyāsāmbarīṣa śuka śaunaka bhīṣma dālbhyān । rukmāṅgadārjuna vasiṣṭha vibhīṣaṇādīn puṇyānimān parama bhāgavatān smarāmi ॥
Summary
I remember the supreme devotees, exalted in devotion to the Lord, from Prahlada to Vibhishana.
Significance
It shows that the blessing of the Lord's devotees is needed to receive the Lord's grace.

5. visṛjya lajjām
visṛjya lajjāṁ yō dhītē mannāmāni nirantaram । kulakōṭi samāyuktō labhatē māmakaṁ padam ॥
Meaning
One who, casting off shyness, chants My names without ceasing, reaches My feet together with a crore generations of his family.
Significance
There should be no hesitation in Nama Sankirtanam.

6. viṣṇōr gānaṁ ca nṛttaṁ ca
viṣṇōr gānaṁ ca nṛttaṁ ca naṭanaṁ ca viśēṣataḥ । brahman brāhmaṇa jātīnāṁ kartavyaṁ nityakarmavat ॥
Meaning
Singing of Vishnu, dancing and enacting His deeds should be done like one's daily duties.
Significance
Music, dance and singing the Lord's praise — all are worship of the Lord.

7. kālakṣēpō na kartavyaḥ
kālakṣēpō na kartavyaḥ kṣīṇamāyuḥ kṣaṇē kṣaṇē । yamasya karuṇā nāsti kartavyaṁ harikīrtanam ॥
Meaning
Life grows shorter every moment; therefore one should chant Hari's name.
Significance
It reminds us of the impermanence of life.

8. harēr nāmaiva
harēr nāmaiva nāmaiva nāmaiva mama jīvanam । kalau nāstyēva nāstyēva nāstyēva gatiranyathā ॥
Meaning
Hari's name alone is my life; in Kali yuga there is no other way.
Significance
The dharma of Kali yuga — Nama Sankirtanam.

9. nāhaṁ vasāmi vaikuṇṭhē
nāhaṁ vasāmi vaikuṇṭhē na yōgī hṛdayē ravau । madbhaktā yatra gāyanti tatra tiṣṭhāmi nārada ॥
Meaning
Wherever My devotees sing of Me, there I am.
Significance
It shows that the Lord is directly present in Nama Sankirtanam.

10. ālōḍya sarvaśāstrāṇi
ālōḍya sarvaśāstrāṇi vicārya ca punaḥ punaḥ । idamēkaṁ suniṣpannaṁ dhyēyō nārāyaṇaḥ sadā ॥
Meaning
The conclusion of all the scriptures: one should always meditate on Narayana.
Significance
The essence of the scriptures — remembering Narayana.

11. vaidēhī sahitam
vaidēhīsahitaṁ suradrumatalē haimē mahāmaṇḍapē madhyē puṣpakamāsanē maṇimayē vīrāsanē susthitam । agrē vācayati prabhañjanasutē tattvaṁ munibhyaḥ paraṁ vyākhyāntaṁ bharatādibhiḥ parivṛtaṁ rāmaṁ bhajē śyāmalam ॥
Summary
I meditate on Sri Rama, seated with Sita in the Pushpaka, teaching the supreme truth to the sages with Hanuman before him.
Significance
It shows that the Ramayana is not just history; it is the teaching of the supreme truth.

12. añjanā nandanaṁ vīram
añjanā nandanaṁ vīraṁ jānakī śōkanāśanam । kapīśam akṣahantāraṁ vandē laṅkā bhayaṅkaram ॥
Meaning
I bow to Hanuman, son of Anjana, who removed Sita's sorrow and struck terror into Lanka.
Significance
Hanuman — the summit of devotion, courage and service.

13. buddhirbalaṁ yaśō dhairyam
buddhirbalaṁ yaśō dhairyaṁ nirbhayatvam arōgatā । ajāḍyaṁ vākpaṭutvaṁ ca hanumat smaraṇād bhavēt ॥
Meaning
Intellect, strength, fame, courage, fearlessness, health and eloquence — all come from remembering Hanuman.
Significance
Hanuman's grace is the foundation of every quality an upanyasaka (speaker) needs.

Overall essence of all the opening slokas
These opening slokas form the spiritual preparation of an upanyasaka:
first, removal of obstacles
then remembrance of the Guru
remembrance of the Lord's devotees
the glory of the name
the impermanence of time
the dharma of Kali yuga
the Lord's presence in Nama Sankirtanam
the essence of scripture: remembering Narayana
the truth of Rama
the grace of Hanuman
In this way these dhyana slokas form a spiritual path that prepares the speaker inwardly and outwardly.

@@ 1.4 | Tōḍaya Maṅgaḷam |
This Todaya Mangalam is an auspicious hymn to the Lord.
It is the tradition to sing it at the beginning of an upanyasam, bhajan or nama sankirtanam.
In it, many divine forms of the Supreme — Rama, Krishna, Narayana and Lord Venkatesha — are blessed with auspicious praise.

1. jaya jānakī ramaṇa
jaya jānakī ramaṇa jaya vibhīṣaṇa śaraṇa jaya sarōruha caraṇa jaya dīna karuṇa jaya jaya ॥
Word meanings
jānakī ramaṇa – beloved of Sita
vibhīṣaṇa śaraṇa – who gave refuge to Vibhishana
sarōruha caraṇa – whose feet are like lotuses
dīna karuṇa – the Supreme, compassionate to the poor
Summary
We praise Sita-Rama, the protector of those who surrender, the embodiment of compassion.
Significance
It reminds us of “saranagati” (surrender), the central teaching of the Ramayana.

2. jaya lōka śaraṇya
jaya lōka śaraṇya jaya bhakta kāruṇya jaya divya lāvaṇya jaya jagat puṇya jaya jaya ॥
Meaning
Victory to the Supreme — the refuge of the world, compassionate to devotees, of divine beauty, who sanctifies the world.
Significance
It shows the Lord as protector of the world and loving to His devotees.

3. sakala lōkāvāsa
sakala lōkāvāsa sākētapura vāsa akaḷaṅka nijadāsa abjamukha hāsa jaya jaya ॥
Word meanings
sakala lōkāvāsa – who dwells in all worlds
sākētapura vāsa – who lives in Ayodhya
akaḷaṅka nijadāsa – Lord of pure, faultless devotees
abjamukha hāsa – who smiles with a lotus-like face
Summary
We praise Rama who, though the Lord of Ayodhya, pervades all the worlds.

4. pāpa saṅgha vidāra
pāpa saṅgha vidāra paṅktimukha saṁhāra śrīpatē sukumāra sītā vihāra jaya jaya ॥
Meaning
Victory to Lord Rama — destroyer of sins, slayer of the rakshasas, Lord of Lakshmi, who delights with Sita.
Significance
The Lord destroys the sins of devotees; at the same time He is the loving family deity together with Sita.

5. mandāra mūlē
mandāra mūlē madanābhirāmaṁ bimbādharāpūrita vēṇunādam । gō gōpa gōpījana madhya saṁsthaṁ gōpaṁ bhajē gōkula pūrṇa candram ॥
Word meanings
mandāra mūlē – at the foot of the Mandara tree
madanābhirāmam – whose beauty enchants even Manmatha
vēṇunādam – the music of the flute
gōkula pūrṇa candram – Krishna, the full moon of Gokula
Summary
We meditate on Krishna, standing among the gopis and playing the flute.
Significance
The peak of madhurya bhakti, devotion of sweet love.

6. śaraṇu śaraṇu surēndra
śaraṇu śaraṇu surēndra sannuta śaraṇu śrī sati vallabha śaraṇu rākṣasa garva saṁhāra śaraṇu veṅkaṭa nāyaka ॥
Meaning
O Venkatanatha, praised by the gods, Lord of Lakshmi, destroyer of the demons' pride — I take refuge in You.
Significance
The feeling that the Lord of Tiruvenkatam is the giver of boons in Kali yuga.

7. animiṣēndrulu…
animiṣēndrulu munulu dikpatulamara kinnara siddhulu ghanatatō rambhādi kāntalu kāsinā reccharikayā ॥
Summary
Gods, sages, kinnaras and apsaras all sing auspicious praise to Venkatesha.
Significance
The worship of the Lord in His divine world.

8. parama pada gōvinda
parama pada gōvinda mādhava padmanābha janārdanā dharaṇīdharavara garuḍavāhana daitya bali mada bhañjanā ॥
Meaning
Lord of the supreme abode, Govinda, Madhava, rider of Garuda, destroyer of the demons' pride.
Significance
An auspicious hymn of the many divine names of Vishnu.

9. yennagala prahlāda
yennagala prahlāda bhaktulu yelamitō mimu koluvagipuḍu vinnapamu vinavayya dēvara veṅkaṭācala nāyaka ॥
Meaning
Devotees like Prahlada worship You; hear our prayer too, O Venkatesha!
Significance
The humble prayer of the devotee.

10. murahara nagadhara
murahara nagadhara mukunda mādhava garuḍa gamana paṅkajanābha parama puruṣa bhava bhañjana kēśava naramṛga śarīra namō namō ॥
Summary
Slayer of Mura, Mukunda, rider of Garuda, who took the form of Narasimha — salutations to You.
Significance
Auspicious remembrance of the various incarnations.

11. nārāyaṇa tē namō namō
nārāyaṇa tē namō namō nārada sannuta namō namō ॥
Meaning
O Narayana, praised by Narada — salutations to You.
Significance
The simplicity of remembering the name, and complete devotion.

12. tārāpati hara
tārāpati hara tapana kulōdbhava tāpasa munigaṇa vandya pada ॥
Meaning
Rama, born in the solar dynasty, whose feet are worshipped by sages.

13. mārīcāntaka
mārīcāntaka māruti sēvita vāridhi bandhana namō namō nārāyaṇa ॥
Meaning
Rama, slayer of Maricha, served by Hanuman, who built the bridge across the ocean.

14. ādi dēva
ādi dēva sakalāgama pūjita yādavakula mōhana rūpa vēdōddhara śrī veṅkaṭa nāyaka nādapriya tē namō namō ॥
Meaning
O Venkatanatha — praised by the Vedas, born in the Yadava clan, lover of music — salutations to You.

15. dēvēśa gaṇārādhita
dēvēśa gaṇārādhita divyāmbuja pāda śrī veṅkaṭagiri nāyaka śrīśa heccharikā ॥
Meaning
O Venkatesha, whose feet are worshipped by the gods — may all be auspicious.

16. kalimānuṣa
kalimānuṣa kaluṣāpaha kamanīya sukīrtē alamēlumaṅgā mōhana mūrtē heccharikā ॥
Meaning
O Venkatesha, who removes the sins of Kali yuga and shines with Alamelumanga — may all be auspicious.

17. mādhava bhavatudē jaya maṅgaḷam
mādhava bhavatudē jaya maṅgaḷaṁ madhu murahara tē maṅgaḷam ॥
Meaning
O Madhava, auspiciousness to You! O slayer of Madhu and Mura, auspiciousness to You!

18. vraja sundari vilōla
vraja sundarī vilōla vibudhēndra paripāla vijaya gōpāla bāla maṅgaḷaṁ mādhava ॥
Meaning
O Gopala Krishna, who captivated the gopis of Vraja and protects the gods — auspiciousness to You.

19. rāma gōvinda rāma
rāma gōvinda rāma rāma kalyāṇa rāma rāma paṭṭābhi rāma rāghava ॥
Meaning
We praise Pattabhi Rama, full of auspicious qualities.

20. gōvinda mādhava
gōvinda mādhava gōpāla kēśava narasiṁhācyuta nārāyaṇa ॥
Meaning
Govinda, Madhava, Narasimha, Narayana — we bow to You.

21. daśaratha nandana
daśaratha nandana sītā manōhara dānava saṁhāra dayānidhē ॥
Meaning
O son of Dasharatha! O beloved of Sita! O compassionate one who destroyed the demons!

22. rāma rāghava
rāma rāghava rājīva lōcana kāmita phalada karivarada ॥
Meaning
O lotus-eyed Rama! O giver of the fruits we desire!

23. kṛṣṇa kēśava
kṛṣṇa kēśava ambuja lōcana vāñchita phalada yadu varada ॥
Meaning
O lotus-eyed Krishna! O giver of boons to the Yadavas!

24. gōvindaṁ bhajarē
gōvindaṁ bhajarē mānasa gōpālaṁ bhajarē gōvindā… gōpālā… ॥
Summary
O mind! Sing the praise of Govinda and Gopala.

Overall essence of Todaya Mangalam
This Todaya Mangalam is an auspicious hymn to the Lord that brings together:
devotion to Rama
the sweetness of Krishna
the truth of Narayana
devotion to Venkatesha
the joy of nama sankirtanam
surrender
auspicious blessing

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Todaya Mangalam is not merely an opening hymn.
It is an auspicious path that awakens the Lord's many divine forms in the mind
and leads the devotee into the bliss of the name.
From devotion to Rama to the sweetness of Krishna,
from the grace of the Lord of Tiruvenkatam to the joy of nama sankirtanam,
these hymns give a complete experience of the Lord.”

@@ 2.1 | Śrī Bōdhēndra Svāmigaḷ |
Dhyana Slokam
yasya smaraṇa mātrēṇa nāma bhaktiḥ prajāyatē । taṁ namāmi yati śrēṣṭhaṁ bōdhēndraṁ jagatāṁ gurum ॥
Word meanings
yasya smaraṇa mātrēṇa – by merely remembering whom
nāma bhaktiḥ prajāyatē – devotion to the Lord's name arises
taṁ namāmi – I bow to him
yati śrēṣṭham – the best of sannyasis
bōdhēndram – Sri Bodhendra Swamigal
jagatāṁ gurum – the Guru of the world
Summary
Merely remembering Sri Bodhendra Swamigal awakens devotion to the Lord's name; we bow to such a Jagadguru.
Significance
Sri Bodhendra is revered as “the emperor of the doctrine of the Name”.
This slokam says that remembering him itself gives a taste for the Name.

Kirtanam
“Bhajarē mānasa”
Raga – Behag
Tala – Adi
Pallavi
bhajarē mānasa bōdhēndra yōgīndraṁ bhajana parāśaya āhlādana candram ॥
Word meanings
bhajarē mānasa – O mind, sing his praise
bōdhēndra yōgīndram – Bodhendra, lord of yogis
bhajana parāśaya – wholly absorbed in bhajan
āhlādana candram – who gives coolness like a moon of joy
Summary
O mind! Meditate on Bodhendra Swamigal, the embodiment of bliss, immersed in singing the Name.
Significance
It shows that the Lord's name is not merely a practice; it is an experience of bliss.

Anupallavi
nijagati niścaya jita vāraṇēndraṁ yativaramānanda pīyūṣa sāndram ॥
Word meanings
nijagati niścaya – who knew the path to liberation with certainty
jita vāraṇēndram – who conquered the elephant-like ego
yativara – the best of sannyasis
ānanda pīyūṣa sāndram – the form of nectar-like bliss
Summary
The great ascetic, firmly established on the path to liberation, who conquered ego — the form of bliss.
Significance
A Guru is not only learned; he abides in experienced bliss.

Charanam 1
manmatha kōṭi manōhara rūpaṁ mañjuḷa sūtrakaraṁ jita tāpam । unmata vādi vacōti durāpam ūrdhva puṇḍrāṅkita phālamapāpam ॥
Word meanings
manmatha kōṭi manōhara rūpam – beauty surpassing a crore of Manmathas
mañjuḷa sūtrakaram – who holds a beautiful japa mala
jita tāpam – who conquered sorrows
unmata vādi vacaḥ durāpam – whose wisdom is beyond the reach of argumentative debaters
ūrdhva puṇḍrāṅkita phālam – forehead marked with the upright sacred mark
apāpam – sinless
Summary
The Gurunatha in whom penance, devotion, wisdom and detachment all shone together.
Significance
This song shows both the outward discipline and the inner wisdom of a Sadguru.

Charanam 2
tāraka brahma sukhābdhi gambhīraṁ tāmara sākṣa vidhīśāvatāram । sārasa lōcana dīrgha pracāraṁ saurabha vara tuḷasīmaṇi hāram ॥
Word meanings
tāraka brahma – the Rama-nama
sukhābdhi gambhīram – deep like an ocean of bliss
vidhīśāvatāram – an incarnation with a divine aspect, like Brahma
sārasa lōcana – lotus eyes
dīrgha pracāram – wide fame
tuḷasīmaṇi hāram – wearing a tulasi garland
Summary
The divine Gurunatha, immersed in the bliss of Rama-nama.
Significance
It shows Bodhendra as the living, walking form of Rama-nama.

Charanam 3
kāmādi ṣaḍripu khaṇḍana dhīraṁ kāruṇya sāgara pūra vihāram । rāma nāma siddhānta vicāraṁ rāmadāsa hṛt pañjara kīram ॥
Word meanings
kāmādi ṣaḍripu khaṇḍanam – who conquered the six enemies beginning with desire
kāruṇya sāgara – ocean of compassion
rāma nāma siddhānta vicāram – who studied the doctrine of Rama-nama
rāmadāsa hṛt pañjara kīram – like a parrot in the cage of devotees' hearts
Summary
The compassionate Bodhendra, who spread the doctrine of Rama-nama to the world.
Significance
This charanam briefly states the whole purpose of Bodhendra Swamigal's life — “spreading Rama-nama”.

Overall essence
This kirtanam auspiciously praises Sri Bodhendra Swamigal as:
the propagator of the doctrine of the Name
the Guru whose nature is bliss
the worshipper of Rama-nama
an ocean of compassion
the ascetic who conquered ego
the Sadguru who lives in devotees' hearts

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Sri Bodhendra Swamigal is not merely the head of a math.
He is the sun of the doctrine of the Name, who revived Rama-nama in Kali yuga.
The tradition experiences that remembering him itself gives a taste for the Name.
This ‘Bhajarē mānasa’ kirtanam,
more than praising a Guru,
awakens in us the bliss of the path of nama sankirtanam.”

@@ 2.2 | Śrīdhara Veṅkaṭēśa Ayyāvāḷ |
Sreedhara Venkatesa Ayyaval
Dhyana Slokam
śrīkaṇṭhamiva bhāsvantaṁ śivanāma parāyaṇam । śrīdharaṁ veṅkaṭēśāryaṁ śrēyasē gurumāśrayē ॥
Word meanings
śrīkaṇṭhamiva bhāsvantam – who shines like Lord Shiva
śivanāma parāyaṇam – wholly devoted to the name of Shiva
śrīdharaṁ veṅkaṭēśāryam – Sridhara Venkatesa Ayyaval
śrēyasē – for the supreme good
gurum āśrayē – I take refuge in him as my Guru
Summary
I take as my Guru Sridhara Ayyaval, who lived immersed in Shiva's name and shone like Shiva himself.
Significance
The centre of Ayyaval's life was “remembrance of the Name”.
He was the great soul who spread Shiva's name simply among the people.

Kirtanam
“Śrī guru śrīdhara”
Raga – Shivaranjani
Tala – Adi
Pallavi
śrī guru śrīdhara veṅkaṭēśa prabhō māku nī daya kāvala ॥
Word meanings
śrī guru – the auspicious Gurunatha
śrīdhara veṅkaṭēśa prabhō – O Sridhara Venkatesa Ayyaval
māku nī daya kāvala – we need your compassion
Summary
O Ayyaval! We pray that you protect us with your grace.
Significance
It shows that the Guru's compassion is the foundation of spiritual progress.

Anupallavi
śiva nāma parāyaṇa ॥
Meaning
O you who are ever immersed in Shiva's name!
Significance
This is the great saying that sums up Ayyaval's life in one line.

Charanam 1
bāgugānē ilalō paraśiva nāmamuna bhaktulaku osagi paragati jūpina ॥
Word meanings
paraśiva nāmamuna – the holy name of Lord Shiva
bhaktulaku osagi – giving it to the devotees
paragati jūpina – you showed the supreme goal
Summary
Ayyaval gave Shiva's name to the people and showed them the path to liberation.
Significance
The doctrine of the Name: the Name itself is the path to liberation.

Charanam 2
yati bōdhēndrula nāthuḍutō kalasi satataṁ nāma pracāramu jēsitivi ॥
Word meanings
yati bōdhēndrula – with Sri Bodhendra Swamigal
kalasi – together
nāma pracāramu jēsitivi – you spread the Name
Summary
Together with Sri Bodhendra Swamigal, you spread nama sankirtanam to the world.
Significance
Bodhendra, Ayyaval and Sadashiva Brahmendra are regarded as the trinity of nama sankirtanam.

Charanam 3
nadi gaṅgana iṇṭi bāvilōna pilici narulaku nī mahima teliya jēsitivi ॥
Word meanings
gaṅgana – the Ganga
iṇṭi bāvilōna – in the well of your house
pilici – calling her
narulaku nī mahima – your glory to the people
teliya jēsitivi – you made known
Summary
You called the Ganga into the well of your own house and made your glory known to the world.
Significance
This is the famous episode of the “descent of the Ganga” in Ayyaval's life.
When people found fault with him on grounds of caste for feeding the hungry, the Lord Himself answered them through the Ganga.

Charanam 4
pannaga vidhinuta pavitra madhyārjuna sannidhilō jyōti rūpamaitivi ॥
Word meanings
pannaga vidhinuta – praised by Adishesha, Brahma and others
pavitra madhyārjuna – the holy kshetra of Tiruvidaimarudur
jyōti rūpamaitivi – you merged as a form of light
Summary
At Tiruvidaimarudur you merged with the Lord as a form of light.
Significance
Ayyaval's final state — union with Shiva.

Charanam 5
śiṣya kōṭulaku cinmayamaitivi dakṣa rāja pūjita śiva aṁśamaina ॥
Word meanings
śiṣya kōṭulaku – for crores of disciples
cinmayamaitivi – you shone as the form of pure consciousness and bliss
śiva aṁśamaina – an incarnation of an aspect of Shiva
Summary
Born as an aspect of Shiva, you gave wisdom to countless disciples.
Significance
It shows that a Guru is not merely a man; he is an incarnation of a divine aspect.

Overall essence
This kirtanam praises Sridhara Ayyaval as:
wholly devoted to Shiva's name
the propagator of the Name
the spiritual brother of Bodhendra
the compassionate Gurunatha
the great soul who brought the Ganga
an incarnation of an aspect of Shiva

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Sridhara Ayyaval's life teaches us a great truth —
the Lord's name knows no caste, no boundary, no barrier.
Through feeding the hungry he taught compassion;
through nama sankirtanam he showed the path to liberation.
His whole life was the living form of the message ‘Shiva's name is the supreme medicine’.”

@@ 2.3 | Śrī Sadguru Svāmigaḷ |
Sri Sadguru Swamigal
Dhyana Slokam
yasyāṅgaṁ kanakābha kāma sadṛśaṁ phālaṁ tripuṇḍrāṅkitam । vāṇī śrī raghunātha nāma sudhayābhyārdrā sakṛt dhārayā । cētasyambuja lōcanō yadupatiḥ khēlatyalaṁ rādhayā । taṁvai veṅkaṭa rāma dēśika varaṁ sarvātmanā'haṁ bhajē ॥
Word meanings
yasya aṅgaṁ kanakābham – whose body shines like gold
tripuṇḍrāṅkitam – forehead marked with sacred ash
vāṇī raghunātha nāma sudhayā ārdrā – speech moistened with the nectar of Rama's name
ambuja lōcanō yadupatiḥ – lotus-eyed Krishna
rādhayā khēlati – plays with Radha (in his heart)
veṅkaṭa rāma dēśika varam – the noble Venkatarama Desika
sarvātmanā bhajē – I worship with my whole heart
Summary
I bow to Sri Sadguru Swamigal, whose speech was soaked in Rama's name and whose mind was immersed in meditation on Krishna.
Significance
Sadguru Swamigal's life was a blissful path in which Rama-nama and Krishna bhajan blended as one.

Kirtanam
“Bhajē sadgurum”
Raga – Sahana
Tala – Adi

Pallavi
bhajē sadguruṁ aniśaṁ bhajē sadguruṁ guru rājamaniśam ॥
Word meanings
bhajē – I worship
sadgurum – the Sadguru
aniśam – always
guru rājam – the king among Gurus
Summary
I always worship Sadguru Swamigal, my Gurunatha.
Significance
Devotion to the Guru is the foundation of the nama sankirtanam tradition.

Charanam 1
tēja sābjahita karatulyaṁ divya bhavya guṇa paripūrṇam । rājaśēkharamiva sthitam akhilōttaraṇa nimittaṁ bhuvamavatīrṇam ॥
Word meanings
tējas – divine radiance
divya bhavya guṇa paripūrṇam – full of divine qualities
akhilōttaraṇa nimittam – for uplifting all people
bhuvam avatīrṇam – who descended to the earth
Summary
The divine Gurunatha who came to the earth for the good of the world.
Significance
Sadguru Swamigal was not a solitary yogi; he was a great soul who came to uplift society.

Charanam 2
gandha rākṣa tuḷasī mālaṁ skandha lambitākṣaya pātram । karavīṇaṁ suviśāla vakṣasaṁ kanaka nibhaṁ taṁ mṛdutara gātram ॥
Word meanings
tuḷasī mālam – wearing a tulasi garland
akṣaya pātram – the bhajan alms-bowl
karavīṇam – a veena in his hand
kanaka nibham – radiant like gold
Summary
The bhajan yogi, with veena and tulasi garland, delighting in the Lord's name.
Significance
The living image of the nama sankirtanam tradition.

Charanam 3
pṛṣṭha lambi nāmāṁśukā vēṣṭita śirasaṁ bahu sarasam । puṇḍrālika vara nāsakapōlaṁ puṇḍarīka nayanaṁ mṛdu hāsam ॥
Word meanings
nāmāṁśukā – cloth printed with the Lord's name
puṇḍarīka nayanam – lotus-like eyes
mṛdu hāsam – a gentle smile
Summary
The Sadguru with a compassionate face and a serene, divine appearance.
Significance
Even the Sadguru's form becomes a teaching for devotees.

Charanam 4
indu bimba sama succhatraṁ nandanīya maddaḷa gānam । sundara cāmara kētana kāñcana daṇḍa dhāri sannuta dhāmānam ॥
Word meanings
maddaḷa gānam – bhajan music with the drum
cāmara kētanam – royal honours
kāñcana daṇḍa dhāri – disciples holding golden staffs
Summary
The Gurunatha honoured with royal reverence, together with the bhajan group.
Significance
Bhajan of the Lord's name was raised to the level of a royal path.

Charanam 5
viṣṇu vidhīśa svarūpiṇaṁ vijñānada pādukā śaraṇam । kṛṣṇa padāmbuja bambhara rādhā kṛṣṇa dāsa vara mānasa sadanam ॥
Word meanings
viṣṇu vidhīśa svarūpiṇam – the oneness of Vishnu and Shiva aspects
vijñānada pādukā śaraṇam – feet that grant wisdom
kṛṣṇa padāmbuja bambhara – a mind circling Krishna's feet like a bee
mānasa sadanam – who dwells in devotees' hearts
Summary
We bow to the Sadguru, immersed in Krishna bhakti, who grants wisdom.
Significance
Sadguru Swamigal is the confluence of devotion, wisdom and nama sankirtanam.

Overall essence
This kirtanam praises Sri Sadguru Swamigal as:
the grandfather of the nama sankirtanam tradition
the propagator of the path of bhajan
the form of devotion to Rama and Krishna
the compassionate Sadguru
the great soul who came to uplift devotees

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Sri Sadguru Swamigal gave the world nama sankirtanam as an ordered spiritual path.
His life held Rama-nama, the experience of Krishna and the joy of bhajan.
The bhajan tradition he shaped still awakens a taste for the Name in devotees' hearts today.
‘Bhajē sadgurum’ is not only a kirtanam;
it is a path to experiencing the Lord through devotion to the Guru.”

@@ 2.4 | Guru Trayam |
(A combined meditation on the three Gurus)
Bodhendra Swamigal
Sreedhara Venkatesa Ayyaval
Sri Sadguru Swamigal

Kirtanam
“Guru mauḷi maṇē”
Raga – Behag
Tala – Adi

Pallavi
guru mauḷi maṇē bhajēbhavantaṁ śaraṇāgata cintāmaṇē ॥
Word meanings
guru mauḷi maṇē – O crest-jewel among Gurus
bhajē bhavantam – I worship you
śaraṇāgata cintāmaṇē – the wish-fulfilling gem for those who surrender
Summary
I worship the three Gurus, who are a wish-fulfilling gem to those who take refuge in them.
Significance
A Guru is not merely a teacher; he is the power of grace that fulfils the devotee's spiritual life.

Charanam 1
kalyāṇaṁ mayi kalaya munīndrā- tulya vibhava bōdhēndra yatīndra ॥
Word meanings
kalyāṇaṁ mayi kalaya – please bless me with auspiciousness
munīndra tulya vibhava – whose glory equals that of the great sages
bōdhēndra yatīndra – Bodhendra, lord of ascetics
Summary
May Bodhendra Swamigal, whose glory equals that of the great sages, bless me with auspiciousness.
Significance
Bodhendra is revered as the great ascetic of the doctrine of the Name.

Charanam 2
sādhujana ślāghita satkīrtē śrīdhara veṅkaṭa kulaguru mūrtē ॥
Word meanings
sādhujana ślāghita – praised by the saintly
satkīrtē – of holy fame
śrīdhara veṅkaṭa – Sridhara Ayyaval
kulaguru mūrtē – who shines as the family Guru
Summary
Sridhara Ayyaval, praised by the saints, shines as our family Guru.
Significance
Ayyaval is the great soul who brought nama sankirtanam into family life as well.

Charanam 3
aṅkita harināma vāgabhirāma śaśāṅka vadana śrī veṅkaṭarāma ॥
Word meanings
aṅkita harināma – firmly stamped with Hari's name
vāgabhirāma – of sweet speech
śaśāṅka vadana – with a face like the moon
śrī veṅkaṭarāma – Sadguru Swamigal
Summary
We bow to Sadguru Swamigal, immersed in Hari's name, who gave sweet teaching.
Significance
Sadguru Swamigal shaped nama sankirtanam into a path of music, bhajan and experience.

Charanam 4
khaṇḍita mānasa śatru stōma paṇḍita pūjita kōdaṇḍa rāma ॥
Word meanings
mānasa śatru – the enemies of the mind (desire, anger and the rest)
khaṇḍita – who destroyed
paṇḍita pūjita – worshipped by the wise
kōdaṇḍa rāma – Lord Rama with His bow
Summary
We meditate on Kodanda Rama, who destroys the enemies of the mind and is worshipped by the wise.
Significance
The central deity of the three Gurus — the name of Sri Rama.

Charanam 5
nāma brahma parāyaṇa siṁha pāmarajana pāvana narasiṁha ॥
Word meanings
nāma brahma parāyaṇa – wholly established in the Name as Brahman
siṁha – brave like a lion
pāmarajana pāvana – who sanctifies even ordinary people
narasiṁha – compassion in the form of Narasimha
Summary
The compassionate Gurus, established in the Name, who uplifted even the simplest people.
Significance
The mission of the three Gurus — to carry the Name to everyone.

Overall essence
This kirtanam sees the trinity of nama sankirtanam Gurus —
Sri Bodhendra
Sridhara Ayyaval
Sri Sadguru Swamigal
— as one divine light.
It praises all three as:
those who proclaimed the glory of the Name to the world
those who made the path to God simple for ordinary people
forms of devotion, compassion and love of the Name

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In the nama sankirtanam tradition, the ‘Guru Trayam’ is a great spiritual principle.
Bodhendra established the doctrine of the Name.
Ayyaval carried it to the people with compassion.
Sadguru Swamigal established it in the world as the bhajan tradition.
Together these three are the divine Gurus who protected the path of the Name in Kali yuga.”

@@ 2.5 | Guru Abhang |
Gurudhyāyī
guru dhyāyī guru dhyāyī guru paratē sādhana nāhī ॥
kēlē sadguru pūjana hēci mājhē anuṣṭhāna ॥
guru sēvēcā saṅkalpa hēci mājhē pūrṇa tapa ॥
śāntīśvara sāṅgē varma guru tōcī parabrahma ॥

Meaning line by line
1. guru dhyāyī guru dhyāyī
Word meanings
guru dhyāyī – meditate on the Guru
guru paratē – other than the Guru
sādhana nāhī – there is no other practice
Summary
Meditating on the Guru is the highest practice; there is no path beyond it.
Significance
The highest state of devotion to the Guru on the path of bhakti.

2. kēlē sadguru pūjana
Word meanings
sadguru pūjana – worship of the Sadguru
hēci mājhē anuṣṭhāna – that itself is my daily observance
Summary
Worshipping the Sadguru is the dharma of my life.
Significance
Serving the Guru is not mere courtesy; it is a spiritual observance.

3. guru sēvēcā saṅkalpa
Word meanings
guru sēvā – service to the Guru
saṅkalpa – firm resolve
hēci mājhē pūrṇa tapa – that itself is my complete penance
Summary
Service to the Guru is the highest penance.
Significance
Penance is not only meditating in a forest; it is selfless service to the Guru.

4. śāntīśvara sāṅgē varma
Word meanings
śāntīśvara – the saint who composed this abhang
sāṅgē varma – tells the secret
guru tōcī parabrahma – the Guru is Parabrahman
Summary
This saint teaches that the Guru is the Supreme.
Significance
The essence of the Guru principle in “Guru Brahma, Guru Vishnu…”.

Overall essence of the abhang
The central idea of this abhang:
meditating on the Guru is the highest practice
worshipping the Guru is the daily observance
serving the Guru is penance
the Guru is Parabrahman
It is a fine example of the Marathi devotional tradition, expressing devotion simply and deeply.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This small abhang holds the whole Guru principle.
The truth of Parabrahman taught in the Vedas
is made simple by the path of devotion as ‘the Guru is the Supreme’.
Meditating on the Guru, serving the Guru, worshipping the Guru —
this abhang shows that these are the three pillars of a devotee's spiritual life.”

“Sadguru vāñcōni”
sadguru vāñcōni sāmpaḍē nā sōya dharāvē tē pāya ādhī ādhī ॥
āpaṇā sārikhē karitī tātkāḷa nāhī kāḷa vēḷa tayā lāgī ॥
lōha parisācī na sāhē upamā sadguru mahimā agādhacī ॥
tukā mhaṇē kaisē āndhaḷē hē jana gēlē visarūni kharyā dēvā ॥

Meaning line by line

1. sadguru vāñcōni sāmpaḍē nā sōya
Word meanings
sadguru vāñcōni – without the Sadguru
sāmpaḍē nā sōya – true good is not found
dharāvē tē pāya – one must hold his feet
ādhī ādhī – first, before everything
Summary
Without the Sadguru there is no spiritual progress; so first one must reach the Guru's feet.
Significance
The Guru is the first step of spiritual life.

2. āpaṇā sārikhē karitī tātkāḷa
Word meanings
āpaṇā sārikhē – like himself
karitī – he makes
tātkāḷa – at once
nāhī kāḷa vēḷa – without delay
tayā lāgī – by his grace
Summary
By his grace the Sadguru raises the disciple to be like himself.
Significance
A true Guru does not keep the disciple below him; he lifts him up.

3. lōha parisācī na sāhē upamā
Word meanings
lōha – iron
parisa – the philosopher's stone
upamā na sāhē – even that comparison falls short
sadguru mahimā agādhacī – the Sadguru's glory is immeasurable
Summary
The Sadguru cannot be compared even to the philosopher's stone that turns iron into gold.
Significance
The philosopher's stone turns iron into gold;
but the Sadguru raises a human being to a divine state.

4. tukā mhaṇē kaisē āndhaḷē hē jana
Word meanings
tukā mhaṇē – says Tukaram
āndhaḷē hē jana – how blind these people are
gēlē visarūni – they have forgotten
kharyā dēvā – the true God
Summary
Tukaram grieves for people who live forgetting the Guru, the true God.
Significance
A life that does not understand the Guru principle is spiritual darkness.

Overall essence of the abhang
The central idea of this abhang:
without the Sadguru there is no spiritual progress
the Guru's grace transforms the disciple
the Sadguru's glory has no comparison
the Guru is the true God

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In this abhang the glory of the Sadguru is told through very simple comparisons.
The philosopher's stone may turn iron into gold;
but the Sadguru turns a human being into the Lord's own nature.
That is why, in the devotional tradition, the Guru is revered as the God we can see.
Through this abhang Tukaram Maharaj reminds us
that ‘a life that forgets the Guru is spiritual blindness’.”

Hari bhakta santa
hari bhakta santa jhālē hō anēka tyā mājī niṣṭaṅka kāṁhī ēka (aika) ॥
śuka vāmadēva śaunaka nārada ajāmēḷa prahlāda vibhīṣaṇa ॥
dhruva parāśara vyāsa puṇḍalīka arjuna vālmīka ambarīṣa ॥
rukmāṅgada bhīṣma baka dālbhya sūta śibi hanumanta parīkṣiti ॥
kaśyapādi atri gautama vasiṣṭha bharadvāja śrēṣṭha mārkaṇḍēya ॥
balī kṛpācārya indra sūrya candra brahma āṇi rudra pūrṇa bhakta ॥
sarvāñcā hā ēka āhē mī kiṅkara mhaṇē jñānēśvara vaiṣṇavāsī ॥

Meaning line by line

1. hari bhakta santa jhālē hō anēka
Word meanings
hari bhakta santa – devotees of Hari and saints
jhālē hō anēka – many have appeared
tyā mājī – among them
niṣṭaṅka – with firm devotion
kāṁhī ēka – I too am a servant
Summary
There have been many devotees of Hari; Jnaneshwar humbly says that he too is a servant among them.
Significance
The mark of a true devotee — humility.

2. śuka vāmadēva śaunaka nārada
The great souls named
Shuka – teacher of the Bhagavatam
Vamadeva – a great rishi
Shaunaka – the sage of Naimisharanya
Narada – the Acharya of the path of devotion
Ajamila – raised up by the glory of the Name
Prahlada – devotee of Narasimha
Vibhishana – the crest-jewel of surrender
Summary
This list shows that devotion appeared in many people at many levels.

3. dhruva parāśara vyāsa puṇḍalīka
The great souls named
Dhruva – unshakeable devotion
Parashara – author of the Vishnu Purana
Vyasa – Veda Vyasa
Pundalika – devotee of Vitthala
Arjuna – who saw Krishna face to face
Valmiki – the great rishi of the Ramayana
Ambarisha – steadfast in the Ekadashi vow
Significance
King, sage, sinner, poet — anyone can become a devotee.

4. rukmāṅgada bhīṣma…
The great souls named
Rukmangada – steadfast in truth
Bhishma – sage who experienced the Lord
Dalbhya – a sage
Suta – teacher of the Puranas
Emperor Shibi – sacrifice
Hanuman – the supreme servant-devotee
Parikshit – who attained liberation by listening to the Bhagavatam
Significance
A life of devotion shows itself in many forms.

5. kaśyapādi atri…
The great souls named
Kashyapa
Atri
Gautama
Vasishtha
Bharadvaja
Markandeya
All of them sages in whom Vedic wisdom and devotion were united.

6. balī kṛpācārya…
The divine beings named
Mahabali – hero of giving
Kripacharya – devotion of an Acharya
Indra
Surya
Chandra
Brahma
Rudra
Summary
Even the gods are devotees of the Lord.
Significance
Devotion is not only for humans; it is the highest path for the gods too.

7. sarvāñcā hā ēka āhē mī kiṅkara
Word meanings
sarvāñcā – of all of them
kiṅkara – servant
jñānēśvara – Sri Jnaneshwar
vaiṣṇavāsī – living among the Vaishnavas
Summary
Jnaneshwar says, “I am the servant of all these great devotees.”
Significance
The highest state of devotion — the attitude of “servant of the Lord's servants”.

Overall essence of the abhang
This abhang:
remembers the lineage of devotees
shows that devotion has existed in every age
says that king, sage, sinner and god can all become devotees
ends in the humble attitude of a servant

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This abhang is like a garland of the lineage of devotees.
From Prahlada to Hanuman,
from Vyasa to Jnaneshwar,
the one thread that binds them all is devotion to Hari.
At the end, Jnaneshwar Maharaj gives us a great lesson —
the highest devotee never thinks himself great;
the one who lives as ‘servant of the Lord's devotees’ is the true devotee.”

Santa padācī jōḍa
Abhang
santa padācī jōḍa dē rē harī sādhu padācī jōḍa ॥
santa samāgamē ātma sukhācā sundara ugavē mōḍa dē rē harī ॥
suphaḷita karunī pūrṇa manōratha puravisī jīvicē kōḍa dē rē harī ॥
amṛta mhaṇē rē hari bhaktāñcā sēvaṭṭā karisī gōḍa dē rē harī ॥

Meaning line by line

1. santa padācī jōḍa dē rē harī
Word meanings
santa padā – the feet of the saints
jōḍa dē – please join me to
harī – O Lord Hari
Summary
O Hari! Join me to the feet of the saints.
Significance
The simple way to reach the Lord — the company of His devotees.

2. santa samāgamē ātma sukhācā
Word meanings
santa samāgama – satsang, the company of the holy
ātma sukhācā – of the bliss of the Self
sundara ugavē mōḍa – a beautiful sprouting takes place
Summary
In satsang the bliss of the Self begins to blossom.
Significance
Satsang makes the seed of spiritual life sprout.

3. suphaḷita karunī pūrṇa manōratha
Word meanings
suphaḷita – making it fruitful
pūrṇa manōratha – fulfilling the heart's wishes
jīvicē kōḍa – the secret and meaning of life
Summary
Satsang makes life meaningful.
Significance
True fulfilment lies only in a relationship with the Lord.

4. amṛta mhaṇē rē hari bhaktāñcā
Word meanings
hari bhaktāñcā – of the devotees of Hari
sēvaṭṭā – in the end
karisī gōḍa – you make sweet
Summary
The life of Hari's devotees becomes sweet as nectar in the end.
Significance
The final fruit of devotion to the Lord — bliss.

Overall essence of the abhang
This abhang beautifully expresses:
the glory of satsang
the need for the feet of the saints
the blossoming of the bliss of the Self
the sweetness of a devotee's life

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This abhang tells a great secret of the path of devotion —
instead of trying to reach the Lord directly,
hold on to the feet of His devotees.
Satsang is the first dawn of the bliss of the Self.
That is why all the great souls have praised ‘the company of the holy’ as the door to liberation.”

Guru mātā guru pitā
guru mātā guru pitā guru āmucī kula dēvatā ॥
thōra paḍatāṁ saṅkaṭē guru rakṣī māgē puḍhē ॥
kāyā vācā āṇi mana guru caraṇīṁ arpaṇa ॥
ēkā janārdanī śaraṇa guru ēka janārdana ॥

Meaning line by line

1. guru mātā guru pitā
Word meanings
guru mātā – the Guru is mother
guru pitā – the Guru is father
kula dēvatā – our family deity
Summary
The Guru is mother, father and family deity.
Significance
The highest feeling of devotion to the Guru.

2. thōra paḍatāṁ saṅkaṭē
Word meanings
thōra saṅkaṭē – in terrible troubles
guru rakṣī – the Guru protects
māgē puḍhē – behind and in front
Summary
The Guru protects us on every side.
Significance
The Guru's grace is the devotee's armour.

3. kāyā vācā āṇi mana
Word meanings
kāyā – body
vācā – speech
mana – mind
guru caraṇīṁ arpaṇa – offered at the Guru's feet
Summary
I offer body, mind and speech to the Guru.
Significance
Complete surrender.

4. ēkā janārdanī śaraṇa
Word meanings
janārdana – the Lord
guru ēka janārdana – the Guru is Janardana himself
Summary
The Guru is the very form of the Lord.
Significance
The principle that the Guru and the Lord are one.

Overall essence of the abhang
This abhang expresses the highest state of devotion to the Guru:
the Guru is mother and father
the Guru is protector
the Guru is the Lord
complete surrender to the Guru

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This abhang is the complete form of devotion to the Guru.
Here the devotee does not see the Guru as a mere teacher;
he sees him as mother, as father, as family deity,
and finally as the Lord Himself.
This is the highest experience of the devotional tradition —
the feeling that ‘the Guru is Janardana’.”

@@ 2.6.1 | “En manattudittavane” |
Kirtanam · Haridasa

Pallavi
en manattudittavane tapō vanattiruppavane en uḷattil unai oru kaṇattil ninaittāl anaittum tarubavane ॥
Word meanings
manattudittavane – You who arose in my mind
tapō vanattiruppavane – You who dwell in the forest of penance
oru kaṇattil ninaittāl – if one thinks of You even for a moment
anaittum tarubavane – You who grant every blessing
Summary
O Gurunatha, who has risen in my heart! Think of You for just a moment, and You grant every grace.
Significance
The glory of remembering the Guru: a single moment of meditation can change a life.

Charanam 1
sinattai venṟavane aṟamenum tanattil ninṟavane inda jagattin āṇḍavane ihapara sukhattai tarubavane ॥
Word meanings
sinattai venṟavan – one who has conquered anger
aṟamenum tanattil ninṟavan – one established in dharma, the true wealth
jagattin āṇḍavan – Lord of the world
ihapara sukham – well-being in this life and the next
Summary
The Guru, who has conquered anger and stands firm in dharma, grants well-being in this world and the next.
Significance
The true Guru is the very form of peace, dharma and compassion.

Charanam 2
cittam iraṅgādā en pittam teḷiyādā uttama gurunāthā āṇḍiḍum attane aruḷ nēsā ॥
Word meanings
cittam iraṅgādā – will Your heart not melt?
en pittam teḷiyādā – will my delusion not clear?
uttama gurunāthā – O noblest Guru
aruḷ nēsā – O loving one, full of grace
Summary
O Gurunatha! I pray that my ignorance be removed and that Your grace come to me.
Significance
The humble surrender of the devotee.

Charanam 3
appanukku upadēsam seyda ñāna subbanum nī tānō muppiḍi avalai muyanṟu pusitta kaṇṇappanum nī tānō ॥
Word meanings
appanukku upadēsam seyda – who taught even his father, Lord Shiva
ñāna subban – Lord Muruga (Swaminatha), the embodiment of wisdom
muppiḍi avalai pusitta – who accepted three handfuls of flattened rice, the humble offering of devotion
kaṇṇappan – recalling the devotion of Kannappa Nayanar
Summary
You are Muruga Himself, and the compassion that accepts the simple love of devotees.
Significance
The Guru, Muruga, Shiva and compassion — all are one.

Charanam 4
oppilā māmaṇiyē nī ippōvē vandaruḷvāy unai eppōvum pōṟṟiḍuvēn en appanē āṇḍiḍa vā ॥
Word meanings
oppilā māmaṇi – peerless gem
vandaruḷvāy – please come and bless
eppōdum pōṟṟiḍuvēn – I will praise You always
Summary
Come into my life now, bless me and take me as Your own.
Significance
The complete call of devotion to the Guru.

Charanam 5
viritta jaḍaiyuḍan karittōl urittu sirittu ninṟavan nī pin tiripurattai eritaru mugattai aḷittu en uḷḷattai parittavanum nī ॥
Word meanings
viritta jaḍai – the form of Shiva with spread matted locks
karittōl urittu – who wears the elephant's hide
tiripurattai erittavan – who burnt the three cities (Tripura Samhara)
uḷḷattai parittavan – who has stolen my heart
Summary
You who possess Shiva's valour and compassion — You have captured my heart.
Significance
Devotion that sees the Guru as Shiva Himself.

Charanam 6
kiruttigaiyil udittavā ennai tirutti amaikka vandavā maṅgaḷapuri tanil tōnṟiyavā paṇiyum haridāsanukkaruḷ seyyavā ॥
Word meanings
kiruttigaiyil udittavā – born under the Krittika star
ennai tirutti amaikka vandavā – You who came to correct and uplift me
haridāsanukku aruḷ seyyavā – please bless this servant of Hari
Summary
O Gurunatha, who came to reform and raise me up — please shower Your grace.
Significance
The purpose of the Guru's incarnation: the protection of souls.

Overall essence
This kirtanam praises the Guru as:
the deity who arose in the heart
the form of Shiva and Muruga
the embodiment of compassion
the Guru of wisdom who removes ignorance
the power of grace that reforms and uplifts the devotee

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In this kirtanam the devotee does not see the Guru as a mere human being.
He experiences him as Shiva, as Muruga, as an ocean of compassion.
The line ‘You grant everything if one thinks of You for a moment’
reveals the wondrous power of remembering the Guru.
This song shows that the Guru's grace is the inner lamp that transforms a life.”

@@ 2.6.2 | “Tuṅgātīra nivāsi” – Raga Senchurutti |
Raga – Senchurutti · Vidyatirtha Swamigal

Dhyana Slokam
parabrahma māyēśa kāruṇyasindhum
svamāyā dhṛtāṅgaṁ sadā bandhaśūnyam । kṛtādhyātmikābhyāsa siddhyāpti līlam
bhavācārya matkṛtsnamēkaṁ prapadyē ॥
Word meanings
parabrahma – the Supreme Brahman
māyēśa – the master of maya
kāruṇya sindhum – ocean of compassion
svamāyā dhṛtāṅgam – who took a human form through his own maya
bandha śūnyam – free of all bondage
ādhyātmika abhyāsa – spiritual practice
siddhi āpti līlam – who attained the perfection of wisdom with ease
bhavācāryam – my Acharya
prapadyē – I surrender
Summary
I surrender to my Gurunatha — an ocean of compassion, beyond maya, established in the knowledge of the Self.
Significance
This dhyana slokam makes us see the Guru not as a mere man but as the very form of Parabrahman.

Pallavi
tuṅgātīra nivāsi narahari vanasañcāri । āśritajana sukhakāri vidyā tīrtha virāgi ॥
Word meanings
tuṅgātīra nivāsi – who lives on the banks of the Tunga
narahari vanasañcāri – who roams the forest of Narasimha
āśritajana sukhakāri – who gives joy to those who take refuge
vidyātīrtha virāgi – the renunciate Vidyatirtha
Summary
We bow to Sri Vidyatirtha Swamigal, who lived on the banks of the Tunga and blessed all who sought refuge in him.
Significance
The Guru's dwelling itself becomes a holy place for devotees.

Charanam 1
vēdāraṇya vihāri sajjana hṛdaya vikāsi । paraśaktyākṛti dhāri vidyātīrtha virāgi ॥
Word meanings
vēdāraṇya vihāri – who lives in meditation in the forest of the Vedas
sajjana hṛdaya vikāsi – who makes the hearts of the good blossom
paraśaktyākṛti dhāri – who bears the aspect of Parashakti
Summary
The divine Guru who makes the hearts of the noble blossom.
Significance
The Guru's presence brings the flowering of wisdom in the devotee's mind.

Charanam 2
dayādi ṣaḍguṇa śāli brahmānanda vidhāyi । śrī gururantaryāmi vidyātīrtha virāgi ॥
Word meanings
dayādi ṣaḍguṇa śāli – full of the six virtues beginning with compassion
brahmānanda vidhāyi – who grants supreme bliss
antaryāmi – who dwells within
Summary
The compassionate Guru dwells in the disciple's heart and grants supreme bliss.
Significance
The Guru is not only outside; he is also the inner witness.

Charanam 3
pātaka puñjaka hāri bhasma vibhūṣaṇa dhāri । parapada bōdhanakāri vidyātīrtha virāgi ॥
Word meanings
pātaka puñjaka hāri – who destroys heaps of sins
bhasma vibhūṣaṇa dhāri – adorned with sacred ash
parapada bōdhanakāri – who teaches the path to the supreme abode
Summary
The Guru who removes sins and teaches the path to liberation.
Significance
The Guru's teaching guides life towards the supreme goal.

Charanam 4
vidita tattva sannyāsi tatpada niṣṭhita yōgi । ahētu karuṇāśāli vidyātīrtha virāgi ॥
Word meanings
vidita tattva – who has known the Truth
sannyāsi – the renunciate
tatpada niṣṭhita yōgi – the yogi established in the Supreme
ahētu karuṇāśāli – whose compassion needs no reason
Summary
The great yogi, established in the Supreme, who blesses without any cause.
Significance
The true Guru blesses without expecting anything in return.

Overall essence
This kirtanam praises Vidyatirtha Swamigal as:
the form of Parabrahman
an ocean of compassion
supreme among renunciates and yogis
one who makes devotees' hearts blossom
the teacher of the path to liberation

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In this kirtanam the Guru is not merely a wise man;
he is described as the living form of Parabrahman.
That great soul who lived on the banks of the Tunga
made a river of wisdom flow in the hearts of devotees.
His compassion needs no reason;
his teaching shows the way to liberation.
That is why the name ‘Vidyatirtha Virāgi’ shines for devotees as a mantra that gives wisdom and peace.”

@@ 2.6.3 | “Bhaja rē lōkagurum” – Raga Abheri |
Raga – Abheri · Adi Shankaracharya

Pallavi
bhaja rē lōkagurum manuja bhaja rē lōkagurum ॥
Word meanings
bhaja rē – worship, meditate upon
lōkagurum – the Guru of the world
manuja – O man
Summary
O man! Worship Adi Shankara, the Guru of the world.
Significance
This is a call: the highest purpose of human life is to reach the Guru of wisdom.

Charanam 1
āryāmbā mukha paṅkaja bhānuṁ āryājāni padāmbuja bhṛṅgam ॥
Word meanings
āryāmbā mukha paṅkaja bhānum – the sun that made the lotus face of his mother Aryamba bloom
āryājāni padāmbuja bhṛṅgam – a bee at the lotus feet of his own Guru
Summary
Shankara, who brought glory to his mother and stood firm in devotion to his Guru.
Significance
In Adi Shankara's life, devotion to mother and devotion to Guru both held the highest place.

Charanam 2
nityānitya vivēcana caturaṁ satyādvaya cicchintana niratam ॥
Word meanings
nitya anitya vivēcana – discrimination between the eternal and the transient
caturam – the expert
satya advaya cit cintana – meditation on the non-dual Supreme
niratam – ever absorbed
Summary
The sage established in discrimination between the eternal and the transient, immersed in non-dual contemplation.
Significance
The heart of Adi Shankara's teaching: “Brahman alone is real.”

Charanam 3
daṇḍa kamaṇḍalu maṇḍita pāṇiṁ paṇḍita pāmara vandita pādam ॥
Word meanings
daṇḍa kamaṇḍalu – the sannyasi's staff and water pot
maṇḍita pāṇi – held in his hands
paṇḍita pāmara – scholars and ordinary people alike
vandita pādam – feet worshipped by them
Summary
The sannyasi Guru worshipped by scholars and common people alike.
Significance
The grace of a true Guru is equal for all.

Charanam 4
śaṅkaram āśrita jana mandāraṁ kiṅkara bhāratī tīrtha susēvyam ॥
Word meanings
āśrita jana mandāram – like the wish-fulfilling Mandara tree to those who take refuge
kiṅkara – servant
bhāratī tīrtha – Acharyas such as Bharati Tirtha
susēvyam – the Guru they humbly serve
Summary
Jagadguru Shankara, who blesses those who seek refuge in him.
Significance
Adi Shankara is not just an individual sage; he is the foundation of the Guru parampara.

Overall essence
This kirtanam praises Adi Shankara as:
the Guru of the world
the sun of Advaita wisdom
the crest-jewel of devotion to the Guru
the embodiment of sannyasa dharma
the Acharya revered by all

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This kirtanam does not show Adi Shankara only as a philosopher;
it shows him as the Jagadguru who came to awaken the world spiritually.
In his life, devotion to mother, devotion to Guru, the detachment of sannyasa and Advaita wisdom all came together.
‘Bhaja rē lōkagurum’ is not just a song;
it is a spiritual call inviting human life to the path of wisdom.”

@@ 2.6.4 | “Śāradā pīṭha nilayam” – Raga Sindhu Bhairavi |
Raga – Sindhu Bhairavi · Sringeri Sharada Peetham · Abhinava Vidyatirtha Mahaswamigal

Pallavi
śāradā pīṭha nilayaṁ bhaja śaṅkara varyaṁ śāradā pīṭha nilayaṁ śaṅkarācārya varyam ॥
Word meanings
śāradā pīṭha nilayam – who resides at the Sharada Peetham
bhaja – worship
śaṅkara varyam – the noblest Shankaracharya
śaṅkarācārya varyam – the supreme Acharya
Summary
Worship the Shankaracharya who shines at the Sringeri Sharada Peetham.
Significance
It shows that the Guru parampara is the living continuation of Adi Shankara.

Charanam 1
śṛṅgēri puravāsaṁ śrita candramaulīśam abhimāna kṣētrajñam advaita śāstrajñam ॥
Word meanings
śṛṅgēri puravāsam – who lives in Sringeri
śrita candramaulīśam – who worships Sri Chandramouleeshwara
abhimāna kṣētrajñam – who knows the greatness of the holy kshetra
advaita śāstrajñam – master of the Advaita scriptures
Summary
The Jagadguru who worships Chandramouleeshwara at Sringeri and spreads the wisdom of Advaita.
Significance
A lineage of Acharyas in which wisdom and devotion meet as one.

Charanam 2
abhinava vidyātīrthaṁ ānanda guru mūrtim ॥
Word meanings
abhinava vidyātīrtham – Abhinava Vidyatirtha Mahaswamigal
ānanda guru mūrtim – the Guru who is the form of bliss
Summary
Abhinava Vidyatirtha is the Guru whose very nature is bliss.
Significance
The presence of a great soul gives devotees peace and joy.

Charanam 3
akhaṇḍa maṇḍita jñānānanda rūpaṁ taṁ haridāsa hṛdayē kalpita sarvajñam ॥
Word meanings
akhaṇḍa jñānānanda rūpam – the form of unbroken wisdom and bliss
haridāsa hṛdayē – in the heart of the devotee
kalpita sarvajñam – who shines as the all-knowing one
Summary
The all-knowing Guru who shines in the devotee's heart as unbroken wisdom and bliss.
Significance
The Guru is not only outside; he abides in the devotee's heart as well.

Overall essence
This kirtanam praises:
the glory of the Sringeri Sharada Peetham
the Shankaracharya parampara
Advaita wisdom
devotion to Chandramouleeshwara
the blissful form of Abhinava Vidyatirtha Mahaswamigal

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This kirtanam beautifully describes the glory of the Sringeri Guru parampara.
The Sharada Peetham established by Adi Shankara is not merely a math;
it is a spiritual centre where wisdom, devotion and detachment live.
Great souls like Abhinava Vidyatirtha Mahaswamigal
are the living light of that lineage.
To meditate on them
is to fill the mind with unbroken wisdom and bliss.”

@@ 2.6.5 | “Guru kirupai” – Raga Desh |
Raga – Desh

Pallavi
guru kirupai illāmal hari kirupai illai hari kirupai illāmal bhaktiyum illai ॥
Word meanings
guru kirupai – the Guru's grace
hari kirupai – the Lord's grace
bhakti – devotion to God
Summary
Without the Guru's grace there is no grace of the Lord;
without the Lord's grace true devotion does not blossom.
Significance
On the path of bhakti, the Guru is the first door.
The Guru's grace is the bridge to the Lord's blessing.

Charanam 1
bhakti illāmal muktiyum illai mukti illāmal duḥkha nivṛttiyum illai ॥
Word meanings
mukti – liberation from the bondage of birth
duḥkha nivṛtti – the end of sorrow
Summary
Without devotion there is no liberation;
without liberation there is no true end to sorrow.
Significance
This is the chain of spiritual progress:
Guru's grace → Hari's grace → devotion → liberation → end of sorrow.

Charanam 2
guru uruvum hari uruvum ōr uruvāgumē ōr uruvil un manadai niṟuttināl ॥
Word meanings
guru uruvum hari uruvum – the Guru and the Lord
ōr uru – are one
manadai niṟuttināl – if the mind is steadied in meditation
Summary
The Guru and Hari are not different;
meditating on one, we reach the other too.
Significance
The principle that the Guru and the Lord are one.

Charanam 3
ōr uruvāna anda ōṅkārap poruḷ sadguru ñānānanda brahmamāy oḷirndiḍumē ॥
Word meanings
ōṅkārap poruḷ – the Supreme meant by Pranava (Om)
sadguru – the true Guru
ñānānanda brahmam – Parabrahman, which is wisdom and bliss
oḷirndiḍumē – will shine forth
Summary
The Supreme meant by Om shines forth as the Sadguru, as Brahman of wisdom and bliss.
Significance
The Guru is not a mere man;
he is the living expression of the Parabrahman denoted by Om.

Overall essence
The central idea of this kirtanam:
the Guru's grace is the basis of everything
the Guru and Hari are one
liberation comes through devotion
the Sadguru is Brahman of wisdom and bliss

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This kirtanam explains the whole path of spiritual life very simply.
Life begins in the Guru's grace, grows in Hari's grace, blossoms as devotion and is fulfilled in liberation.
Here the Guru is not different from the Lord;
the Parabrahman called ‘the meaning of Om’ shines as the Sadguru.
That is why the great ones say:
‘One who has reached the Guru has reached the Lord Himself.’”

@@ 2.6.6 | “Bhaja bhaja mānasa” – Raga Tilang |
Raga – Tilang · Raghavendra Swami · Mantralayam

Pallavi
bhaja bhaja mānasa rāghavēndraṁ guru rāghavēndraṁ yati rāghavēndram ॥
Word meanings
bhaja bhaja – meditate again and again
mānasa – O mind
rāghavēndram – on Sri Raghavendra Swamigal
guru rāghavēndram – Raghavendra, the Guru
yati rāghavēndram – Raghavendra, the foremost of ascetics
Summary
O mind! Always worship Sri Raghavendra Swamigal.
Significance
This song is a call to chant the name, settling the mind in remembrance of the Guru.

Charanam 1
tuṅgā nadī tīraṁ puṇya sthala vāsaṁ mantrālaya kṣētraṁ brindāvana vāsam ॥
Word meanings
tuṅgā nadī tīram – the banks of the Tungabhadra river
puṇya sthala vāsam – who dwells in a holy place
mantrālaya kṣētram – the holy town of Mantralayam
brindāvana vāsam – who abides in the Brindavana
Summary
Meditate on Raghavendra, who blesses from the Brindavana at Mantralayam.
Significance
Even the Brindavana of a great saint becomes a living centre of grace for devotees.

Charanam 2
dīna jana priya gānarasa pūrṇaṁ prahlāda avatāraṁ kiñcita rūpam ॥
Word meanings
dīna jana priya – dear to the poor and humble devotees
gānarasa pūrṇam – full of music and the sweetness of devotion
prahlāda avatāram – the incarnation of Prahlada
kiñcita rūpam – an aspect of that incarnation
Summary
The great saint, an incarnation of Prahlada's aspect, who shows compassion to devotees.
Significance
Raghavendra Swamigal is revered as the embodiment of devotion, compassion and the grace of Narasimha.

Charanam 3
phālguṇa saṅgama guṇaśīla nāmaṁ mṛttikā kaliyuga parama auṣadham ॥
Word meanings
phālguṇa saṅgama – the holy confluence
guṇaśīla nāmam – a sacred name full of virtue
mṛttikā – the sacred earth (from the Brindavana)
kaliyuga parama auṣadham – the supreme medicine of Kali yuga
Summary
Raghavendra's sacred earth and his name are medicine for the sufferings of Kali yuga.
Significance
The Guru's name and the Guru's prasadam are like spiritual medicine for devotees.

Overall essence
This kirtanam praises Sri Raghavendra Swamigal as:
the foremost of ascetics
the great saint of Mantralayam
an aspect of the Prahlada incarnation
the compassionate protector of devotees
the healer of Kali yuga

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This kirtanam simply expresses the glory of Sri Raghavendra Swamigal's compassion.
Devotees experience him as a living saint who still blesses from the Brindavana at Mantralayam.
His name, his sacred earth, his grace — all are spiritual medicine for the people of Kali yuga.
That is why this song tells the mind again and again:
‘Bhaja bhaja mānasa rāghavēndram.’”

@@ 2.6.7 | “Jagadgurō śrī śaṅkara” – Raga Abheri (Namavali) |
Raga – Abheri · Type – Namavali · Adi Shankaracharya

jagadgurō śrī śaṅkarā, mukti pradāyaka śaṅkarā ॥
virāgi pūjita śaṅkarā, vibhūti bhūṣita śaṅkarā ॥
bhāṣyakāra śrī śaṅkarā, bhadrapradāyaka śaṅkarā ॥
sadguru mūrtē śaṅkarā, saṅkaṭa vāraka śaṅkarā ॥
śivāvatāra śaṅkarā, śiṣya hitaṅkara śaṅkarā ॥

Meaning line by line

1. jagadgurō śrī śaṅkarā
Word meanings
jagadgurō – O Guru of the world
mukti pradāyaka – giver of liberation
Summary
Adi Shankara, Guru to the whole world, who showed the path to liberation.
Significance
Adi Shankara is not only the Acharya of one tradition; he is a spiritual Guru for all.

2. virāgi pūjita śaṅkarā
Word meanings
virāgi pūjita – revered by renunciates full of detachment
vibhūti bhūṣita – adorned with sacred ash
Summary
Shankara shines as the very form of detachment, bearing the marks of Shiva.
Significance
Wisdom and detachment are the two pillars of Shankara's life.

3. bhāṣyakāra śrī śaṅkarā
Word meanings
bhāṣyakāra – author of the commentaries on Vedanta
bhadra pradāyaka – giver of auspiciousness and welfare
Summary
The Acharya who gave wisdom to the world through his commentaries on the Prasthana Traya.
Significance
Adi Shankara's commentaries remain the foundation of Vedanta to this day.

4. sadguru mūrtē śaṅkarā
Word meanings
sadguru mūrti – the form of the true Guru
saṅkaṭa vāraka – remover of troubles
Summary
As the Guru of wisdom, Shankara removes the sorrows of devotees.
Significance
A Guru does not only give knowledge; he also removes the darkness of life.

5. śivāvatāra śaṅkarā
Word meanings
śivāvatāra – incarnation of Lord Shiva
śiṣya hitaṅkara – who seeks the good of his disciples
Summary
The Jagadguru who came as Shiva's incarnation and uplifted his disciples.
Significance
In the tradition, Adi Shankara is revered as an incarnation of Shiva.

Overall essence
This namavali praises Adi Shankara as:
the Jagadguru
the sage who grants liberation
the crest-jewel of detachment
the great commentator
the incarnation of Shiva
the remover of devotees' sorrows

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This namavali condenses the whole glory of Adi Shankara into a few words.
He is not merely a philosopher;
he is the Jagadguru who showed the path to liberation.
He is not merely a sannyasi;
he is the great soul who came as Shiva's incarnation and spread the light of Vedanta across the world.
That is why devotees still call to him with love:
‘Jagadgurō śrī śaṅkarā.’”

@@ 2.6.8 | “Akhilānanda sandāyi” – Raga Yaman Kalyani |
Raga – Yaman Kalyani · Vidyatirtha Swamigal · Sringeri Sharada Peetham

Dhyana Slokam
akhilānanda sandāyi manōjña mukha paṅkajam jagadguruṁ jagat pūjyaṁ vidyātīrtha mahaṁ śrayē ॥
Word meanings
akhila ānanda sandāyi – who gives joy to all
manōjña mukha paṅkajam – whose lotus face captivates the mind
jagadgurum – the Guru of the world
jagat pūjyam – revered by the world
śrayē – I take refuge
Summary
I take refuge in Jagadguru Vidyatirtha, who gives joy to all.
Significance
This slokam shows that even the darshan of the Guru fills the devotee's mind with joy.

Slokam 1
bhidyatē hṛdaya granthiḥ dṛṣṭē yasminniti śrutiḥ । jagau parāvaraṁ śāntaṁ taṁ vidyātīrtha māśrayē ॥
Word meanings
hṛdaya granthi – the knots of the heart, the bondage of ignorance
dṛṣṭē yasmin – when he is realised
parāvaram – all that is high and low
śāntam – supreme peace
Summary
When he is realised, the knots of ignorance are cut and supreme peace is attained.
Significance
The Guru's darshan is the way to wisdom and inner freedom.

Slokam 2
naṭatyānana raṅgē hi yasya sākṣāt sarasvatī । natārti śamanē dakṣaṁ taṁ vidyātīrtha māśrayē ॥
Word meanings
sarasvatī naṭati – Goddess Saraswati dances on his tongue
natārti śamanē – in removing the sorrows of those who bow
dakṣam – who is skilled
Summary
The Guru of wisdom, on whose tongue Saraswati dwells, removes the sorrows of devotees.
Significance
The Guru's words are not mere words; they are divine speech.

Slokam 3
vaṭamūlaṁ parityajya śṛṅgādrau nivasan hi yaḥ । tattvaṁ bōdhayatē bhaktān dakṣiṇāsyaṁ tamāśrayē ॥
Word meanings
vaṭamūlam – Dakshinamurti, who sat at the foot of the banyan tree
śṛṅgādrau nivasan – now dwelling at Sringeri
tattvaṁ bōdhayatē – who teaches the Truth
Summary
The Gurunatha who, as an incarnation of Dakshinamurti, teaches the Truth from Sringeri.
Significance
This song sees the Jagadguru as the form of Dakshinamurti.

Slokam 4
viṣayāśāṁ parityajya vairāgyaṁ paramaṁ śritāḥ । munayō yatkṛpā bhājastaṁ vidyātīrtha māśrayē ॥
Word meanings
viṣayāśā – worldly desires
vairāgyam – detachment
yatkṛpā bhājāḥ – those who received his grace
Summary
By his grace, sages gave up worldly desires and became established in detachment.
Significance
It is the Guru's grace that brings true detachment.

Slokam 5
dyāvā bhūmī hi janayan dēva ēka iti śrutiḥ । yaṁ varṇayati sarvēśaṁ taṁ vidyātīrtha māśrayē ॥
Word meanings
dyāvā bhūmī – heaven and earth
dēva ēka – the one Supreme Being
sarvēśam – the Lord of all
Summary
The Gurunatha who shines as the form of that one Supreme Being spoken of in the Vedas.
Significance
The Vedantic principle: the Guru is Parabrahman.

Slokam 6
tīrē tuṅgā taṭinyā yastattvaṁ śiṣyān prabōdhayan । āstē dēśika rājaṁ taṁ vidyātīrthaṁ samāśrayē ॥
Word meanings
tuṅgā taṭinyā tīrē – on the banks of the Tunga river
śiṣyān prabōdhayan – teaching wisdom to disciples
dēśika rājam – king among Acharyas
Summary
The king among Acharyas, who taught wisdom to his disciples on the banks of the Tunga.
Significance
A living picture of the Sringeri Guru parampara.

Slokam 7
arthamapūrvaṁ śrutēr yastu yathāvadava bōdhayan । śiṣyān dharmapadā saktān kurutē tamahaṁ śrayē ॥
Word meanings
śrutēr artham – the true meaning of the Vedas
bōdhayan – who teaches
dharmapadā saktān – who establishes them on the path of dharma
Summary
The Gurunatha who explains the true meaning of the Vedas and establishes his disciples in dharma.
Significance
A true Guru does not only give knowledge; he also shapes a life of dharma.

Overall essence
This kirtanam praises Vidyatirtha Mahaswamigal as:
the form of bliss
an incarnation of Dakshinamurti
a knower of Vedanta
a teacher of dharma
a teacher of detachment
the Jagadguru on the banks of the Tunga

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This kirtanam shows the Jagadguru not merely as the head of a math,
but as the living form of Dakshinamurti.
That great soul, seated on the banks of the Tunga, taught his disciples the truths of Vedanta;
he did not just make them learned — he established them in dharma.
That is why every verse of this song ends with
‘taṁ vidyātīrtham āśrayē’ —
‘In that Gurunatha alone I take refuge.’”

@@ 2.6.9 | “Śaṅkara dēśika” – Raga Nata Namakriya (Namavali) |
Raga – Nata Namakriya · Type – Namavali · Adi Shankaracharya

Namavali
śaṅkara dēśika māmpāhi śiva guru tanaya māmpāhi ॥
sajjana pūjita māmpāhi sadguṇa śōbhita māmpāhi ॥
kālaḍiyudbhava māmpāhi kāma vivarjita māmpāhi ॥
āryāmbā suta māmpāhi ārti vināśana māmpāhi ॥
sarvajña gurō māmpāhi sarva śivaṅkara māmpāhi ॥

The meaning of “māmpāhi”
mām – me
pāhi – protect, save
So in every line the devotee prays: “O Shankara, protect me!”

Detailed meaning line by line

1. śaṅkara dēśika māmpāhi
Word meanings
śaṅkara – giver of auspiciousness
dēśika – the Acharya who teaches
māmpāhi – please protect me
Detailed meaning
O Jagadguru Shankara, who taught the world the path of wisdom — please protect me.
Spiritual meaning
The Guru's teaching is the light that frees us from the darkness of life.

2. śiva guru tanaya māmpāhi
Word meanings
śivaguru – Shivaguru, Adi Shankara's father
tanaya – son
Detailed meaning
O divine child, born as the son of Shivaguru — please protect me.
Spiritual meaning
Shankara's incarnation is regarded as a divine resolve.

3. sajjana pūjita māmpāhi
Word meanings
sajjana – the good, the saintly
pūjita – who is worshipped
Detailed meaning
O great soul, revered by saints and sages — please protect me.
Spiritual meaning
It is other great souls who first recognise and honour a true saint.

4. sadguṇa śōbhita māmpāhi
Word meanings
sadguṇa – noble qualities
śōbhita – adorned
Detailed meaning
O you who shine with virtues such as compassion, wisdom, sacrifice and detachment — please protect me.
Spiritual meaning
The Guru's life itself is a living scripture for the disciple.

5. kālaḍiyudbhava māmpāhi
Word meanings
kālaḍi – the holy town of Kaladi in Kerala
udbhava – who was born there
Detailed meaning
O Jagadguru, born in the holy land of Kaladi — please protect me.
Spiritual meaning
The birthplaces of great souls become holy places too.

6. kāma vivarjita māmpāhi
Word meanings
kāma – desire, craving for pleasure
vivarjita – who has completely given up
Detailed meaning
O embodiment of detachment, who has wholly conquered worldly desires — please protect me.
Spiritual meaning
Victory over desire is the foundation of the path of wisdom.

7. āryāmbā suta māmpāhi
Word meanings
āryāmbā – Shankara's mother
suta – son
Detailed meaning
O compassionate Shankara, born as the son of Aryamba — please protect me.
Spiritual meaning
Devotion to his mother held the highest place in Shankara's life.

8. ārti vināśana māmpāhi
Word meanings
ārti – sorrow, anguish of mind
vināśana – who destroys
Detailed meaning
O remover of the sorrows and inner darkness of devotees — please protect me.
Spiritual meaning
The Guru's grace brings peace of mind and spiritual rest.

9. sarvajña gurō māmpāhi
Word meanings
sarvajña – who knows everything
gurō – O Gurunatha
Detailed meaning
O Jagadguru, who knows the Vedas, the shastras and the truth of the Self — please protect me.
Spiritual meaning
The Guru's knowledge is not book learning; it is experienced wisdom.

10. sarva śivaṅkara māmpāhi
Word meanings
sarva – for all
śivaṅkara – who brings auspiciousness
Detailed meaning
O compassionate Shankara, who brings good to all beings — please protect me.
Spiritual meaning
A true saint lives not only for his disciples but for the welfare of the world.

Overall essence
This namavali praises Adi Shankara as:
the Jagadguru
the compassionate Acharya
the crest-jewel of detachment
a great soul full of devotion to his mother
the remover of sorrow
the one who brings good to the world

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In this namavali the devotee calls out ‘māmpāhi’ in every line —
‘O Shankara, protect me!’ — with complete surrender.
Here Shankara is not merely a philosopher;
he is seen as a loving mother, as the Guru of the world, as the remover of sorrow.
So this namavali becomes a deep prayer of surrender to the Guru,
rising from the devotee's heart.”

@@ 2.6.10 | “Śaṅkara pūjitē” – Raga Saramati (Namavali) |
Raga – Saramati · Type – Namavali · Sharadamba · Sringeri Sharada Peetham

Namavali
śaṅkara pūjitē śāradē sarva sahōdari śāradē ॥
abhīṣṭa varadē śāradē adbhuta caritē śāradē ॥
śṛṅga giristhē śāradē śruti pratipādyē śāradē ॥
maṅgala dāyini śāradē saṅgīta priyē śāradē ॥
kāmita varadē śāradē kōmala caraṇē śāradē ॥

The meaning of “Śāradē”
Śāradā / Śāradē – Goddess Saraswati, the deity of wisdom
Mother Sharadamba, who blesses at Sringeri
In every line the devotee lovingly calls out to the Mother.

Detailed meaning line by line

1. śaṅkara pūjitē śāradē
Word meanings
śaṅkara pūjitē – O you who were worshipped by Adi Shankara
śāradē – Goddess Saraswati, the form of wisdom
Detailed meaning
O Sharadamba, worshipped by Adi Shankaracharya — salutations to you.
Spiritual meaning
Sharadamba is the deity whom Shankara himself, who reached the summit of wisdom, worshipped.

2. sarva sahōdari śāradē
Word meanings
sarva – to all
sahōdari – sister, one bound to all in love
Detailed meaning
O Mother, who looks on everyone in the world with equal love!
Spiritual meaning
Divine love makes no distinctions.

3. abhīṣṭa varadē śāradē
Word meanings
abhīṣṭa – what is desired
varadē – giver of boons
Detailed meaning
O giver of boons, who fulfils the good wishes of devotees!
Spiritual meaning
Sincere prayer surely receives divine grace.

4. adbhuta caritē śāradē
Word meanings
adbhuta – wondrous
caritē – whose story and glory
Detailed meaning
O Mother, whose divine play and stories of compassion are wondrous!
Spiritual meaning
Divine glory can never be fully measured by human intellect.

5. śṛṅga giristhē śāradē
Word meanings
śṛṅgagiri – the holy town of Sringeri
sthē – who dwells there
Detailed meaning
O Mother, who blesses from the holy kshetra of Sringeri!
Spiritual meaning
The holy place and the deity are one inseparable spiritual experience.

6. śruti pratipādyē śāradē
Word meanings
śruti – the Vedas
pratipādyē – the Supreme that they proclaim
Detailed meaning
O embodiment of supreme wisdom, praised by the Vedas!
Spiritual meaning
Sharadamba is not just a goddess of learning; she is the source-power of Vedantic wisdom.

7. maṅgala dāyini śāradē
Word meanings
maṅgala – auspiciousness, goodness
dāyini – who gives
Detailed meaning
O Mother, who gives auspiciousness and well-being to devotees!
Spiritual meaning
Divine grace shows itself in life as peace and auspiciousness.

8. saṅgīta priyē śāradē
Word meanings
saṅgīta – music
priyē – who loves
Detailed meaning
O Goddess, who loves music, sound and devotional kirtans!
Spiritual meaning
Music is the highest form of devotional worship.

9. kāmita varadē śāradē
Word meanings
kāmita – what is wished for
varadē – giver of boons
Detailed meaning
O compassionate Mother, who fulfils the noble resolves of devotees!
Spiritual meaning
God accepts the pure wish of the devotee's heart.

10. kōmala caraṇē śāradē
Word meanings
kōmala – tender
caraṇē – whose holy feet
Detailed meaning
O Mother, whose holy feet are full of tenderness and compassion!
Spiritual meaning
The Mother's feet are the devotee's resting place of surrender.

Overall essence
This namavali praises Sharadamba as:
the deity worshipped by Shankara
the embodiment of wisdom
the giver of boons
the bringer of auspiciousness
the lover of music
the compassionate Mother

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In this namavali Sharadamba is not described merely as a goddess of learning;
she is the living Mother of Vedantic wisdom.
The deity whom Adi Shankara himself worshipped
still gives devotees wisdom, auspiciousness and compassion today.
The last line, ‘kōmala caraṇē śāradē’,
shows the devotee finally surrendering completely at the Mother's feet.”

@@ 2.6.11 | “Śrī śaṅkarācārya varyam” |
Adi Shankaracharya · Govinda Bhagavatpada · Padmapadacharya · Sringeri Sharada Peetham · Bharati Tirtha Mahaswamigal

Dhyana slokam / Stuti

1. śrī śaṅkarācārya varyaṁ sarva lōkaika vandyaṁ bhajē dēśikēndram ॥
Word meanings
śrī śaṅkarācārya varyam – the supreme Shankaracharya
sarva lōkaika vandyam – worshipped by all the worlds
bhajē – I meditate on, I worship
dēśikēndram – the greatest of Acharyas
Detailed meaning
I worship with devotion Jagadguru Shankaracharya, who is revered throughout the world.
Spiritual meaning
Shankara is not a regional Acharya; he is a universal spiritual Guru.

2. dharma pracārē'ti dakṣaṁ yōgi gōvinda pādāpta sannyāsa dīkṣam ॥
Word meanings
dharma pracārē ati dakṣam – supremely skilled in spreading dharma
yōgi gōvinda pāda – the yogi Govinda Bhagavatpada
āpta sannyāsa dīkṣam – who received sannyasa initiation from him
Detailed meaning
The Jagadguru who took sannyasa from Govinda Bhagavatpada and spread dharma in the world.
Spiritual meaning
Even a great soul honours and lives by the Guru parampara.

3. durvādi garvāpanōdaṁ padmapādādi śiṣyāḷi saṁsēvya pādam ॥
Word meanings
durvādi garva apanōdam – who removed the arrogance of false debaters
padmapādādi śiṣyāḷi – disciples such as Padmapada
saṁsēvya pādam – whose feet they served
Detailed meaning
The Gurunatha who defeated false arguments and whose feet were served by disciples like Padmapada.
Spiritual meaning
Wisdom is not just winning debates; it is the power to shape disciples.

4. śaṅkādri dambhōli līlaṁ kiṅkarāśēṣa śiṣyāḷi santrāṇa śīlam ॥
Word meanings
śaṅkā adri – the mountain of doubt
dambhōli līlam – who shattered it like a thunderbolt
santrāṇa śīlam – whose nature is to protect
Detailed meaning
The Acharya who shattered mountains of doubt like a thunderbolt and protected and guided his disciples.
Spiritual meaning
The Guru destroys the disciple's ignorance and gives spiritual confidence.

5. bālārka nīkāśa cēlaṁ bōdhitāśēṣa vēdānta gūḍhārtha jālam ॥
Word meanings
bālārka nīkāśa cēlam – whose ochre robe glows like the rising sun
vēdānta gūḍhārtha jālam – the deep secrets of Vedanta
Detailed meaning
He who shone like the rising sun and explained the secrets of Vedanta to the world.
Spiritual meaning
Wisdom is like the sun that dispels darkness.

6. rudrākṣa mālā vibhūṣaṁ candramaulīśvara ārādhanāvāpta tōṣam ॥
Word meanings
rudrākṣa mālā vibhūṣam – adorned with rudraksha beads
candramaulīśvara ārādhanā – worship of Shiva as Chandramouleeshwara
āpta tōṣam – who attained great joy from it
Detailed meaning
The Jagadguru who worshipped Chandramouleeshwara with devotion to Shiva and found bliss in it.
Spiritual meaning
Even a sage of Advaita lives with deep devotion.

7. vidrāvitāśēṣa dōṣaṁ bhadra pūga pradaṁ bhakta lōkasya nityam ॥
Word meanings
aśēṣa dōṣam vidrāvita – who drives away all faults
bhadra pūga pradam – who gives abundant good
Detailed meaning
The compassionate Acharya who removes the faults of devotees and gives them good.
Spiritual meaning
The Guru's grace brings purity of mind and spiritual progress.

8. pāpāṭavī citra bhānuṁ jñāna dīpēna hārdaṁ tamō vārayantam ॥
Word meanings
pāpāṭavī – the forest (darkness) of sin
jñāna dīpam – the lamp of wisdom
hārdam tamas – the darkness of the heart
Detailed meaning
Like the sun, he removes the darkness of the mind and of sin with the lamp of wisdom.
Spiritual meaning
Wisdom is an inner light.

9. dvaipāyana prīti bhājaṁ sarvadā pāpahāmōgha bōdha pradaṁ tam ॥
Word meanings
dvaipāyana – Veda Vyasa
prīti bhājam – dear to him
amōgha bōdham – unfailing teaching of wisdom
Detailed meaning
Dear to Veda Vyasa, he gives teaching that removes sins.
Spiritual meaning
Shankara carried forward the Vedantic tradition of Veda Vyasa.

10. rājādhi rājābhi pūjyaṁ ramya śṛṅgādri vāsaika lōlaṁ yatīḍyam ॥
Word meanings
rājādhi rājābhi pūjyam – worshipped even by emperors
śṛṅgādri vāsa – dwelling at Sringeri
yatīḍyam – praised by ascetics
Detailed meaning
The Sringeri Jagadguru, revered by kings and ascetics alike.
Spiritual meaning
Even kings bow before true spiritual greatness.

11. rākēndu saṅkāśa vaktraṁ ratna garbhēbha vaktrāṅghri pūjānu raktam ॥
Word meanings
