rākēndu saṅkāśa vaktram – whose face is like the full moon
pūjānu raktam – devoted to worship
Detailed meaning
The Gurunatha with a face serene as the full moon, absorbed in worship.
Spiritual meaning
Inner peace shines on the face as light.

Phalashruti
śrī bhāratī tīrtha gītaṁ śaṅkarārya stavaṁ yaḥ paṭhēd bhakti yuktaḥ । sō'vāpnuyāt sarvamiṣṭaṁ śaṅkarācārya varya prasādēna tūrṇam ॥
Word meanings
bhakti yukta – with devotion
sarvamiṣṭam – everything that is good
prasādēna – by the grace
Detailed meaning
Whoever recites this hymn with devotion will attain every good thing by the grace of Shankaracharya.

Overall essence
This hymn praises Adi Shankara as:
the Jagadguru
the propagator of dharma
the sun of Vedanta
a devotee of Shiva
the protector of disciples
the remover of ignorance

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This hymn does not show Adi Shankara merely as a philosopher.
It describes him as the protector of dharma, the sun of Vedanta, the compassionate Guru of his disciples.
His wisdom shatters mountains of doubt like a thunderbolt;
his compassion removes the darkness from devotees' minds.
That is why, at the end, this hymn declares with certainty:
if Shankaracharya's grace is received, everything the devotee needs comes of itself.”

@@ 4.1 | First Dhyana Slokam |
First Dhyana Slokam
“I bow to Sri Narayana Tirtha.
By drinking water sanctified by the dust of his holy feet, the mind becomes pure. Through that purity of mind, one comes to know the true nature of the Self.”
Key message
The presence of great souls purifies the mind.
When the mind is pure, knowledge of the Self dawns.
Devotion to the Guru is the doorway to spiritual progress.

Second Dhyana Slokam
“I bow to Sri Narayana Tirtha, the Sadguru who grants liberation.
Chanting his name even a little gives the merit of countless repetitions of ‘Om’. Worshipping him is equal to worshipping all the gods — Hari, Hara, Brahma and the rest. His teachings clearly explain the true meaning of the scriptures to the whole world.”
Key message
The Guru is the form of all the gods.
Remembering the Guru's name brings great merit.
A true Guru makes the essence of the scriptures clear.
The Guru's grace shows the path to liberation.

@@ 4.2 | “Ēhi mudaṁ dēhi” – Raga Ananda Bhairavi |
Meaning line by line
ēhi mudaṁ dēhi śrī kṛṣṇā
“Krishna! Come and give me joy.”
māṁ pāhi gōpāla bāla kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“O Gopala, child Krishna! Protect me.”
nandagōpa nandana śrī kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“O beloved son of Nandagopa, Krishna!”
yadu nandana bhakta candana kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“O you who were born in the Yadu clan! You who soothe devotees like cool sandal paste!”
kala gatiṁ darśaya śrī kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“Show us your sweet play and your graceful movements.”
tava karṇau calaya śrī kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“Wiggle your little ears and delight us.”
dhāva dhāva mādhava śrī kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“Madhava! Come running to us.”
navya navanīta māhara śrī kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“O Kanna, who steals the fresh butter!”
bhavya naṭanaṁ kuru śrī kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“Dance your beautiful divine dance.”
balabhadra sahita śrī kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“O Krishna, who shines together with Balarama!”
nāradādi munigēya kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“O Krishna, sung by sages such as Narada!”
śrīman nārāyaṇa tīrtha varada kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“O Krishna, who blessed Sri Narayana Tirtha!”
Brief meaning
In this kirtanam the devotee calls Bala Krishna with great affection.
“Come, Kanna! Give me joy, protect me, show me your play, come running, dance, play” — he calls with the love of a mother or devotee calling a child.
This is a most beautiful expression of vatsalya bhakti, the devotion of parental love.
The song shows that simply remembering Krishna's childhood play fills the mind with joy.
Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
When we call God with love, He comes to us.
The Lord's childhood play purifies the mind.
In Krishna bhakti there is no fear; only affection and joy.
Kanna who steals butter is really stealing the hearts of devotees.
Remembering His name and His play brings peace of mind and the sweetness of devotion.
The sweetness of this kirtanam is that we too can lovingly call the same Krishna whom great souls and sages have sung.

@@ 4.3 | “Kṛṣṇaṁ kalaya sakhi” – Raga Mukhari |
Meaning line by line
kṛṣṇaṁ kalaya sakhi sundaraṁ bāla kṛṣṇaṁ kalaya sakhi sundaram
“O friend! Meditate on the beautiful child Krishna; experience Him.”
kṛṣṇaṁ gataviṣaya tṛṣṇaṁ jagatprabhu viṣṇuṁ surārigaṇa jiṣṇum
“Meditate on Krishna, who is free of worldly desires, who is Vishnu the Lord of the world, and who conquers the demons.”
dhīraṁ bhavajaladhi pāraṁ sakala vēda sāraṁ samasta yōgidhāram
“Meditate on Krishna, who carries us across the ocean of samsara, who is the essence of all the Vedas, and who is the support that brings all yogis safely to the shore.”
śṛṅgāra rasabhara saṅgīta sāhitya gaṅgā laharī khēla saṅgam
“Meditate on Krishna, in whom sweetness, music, literature and devotion surge like the waves of the Ganga.”
rāmēṇa jagadabhi rāmēṇa balabhadra rāmēṇa samavāpta kāmēṇa
“Meditate on Krishna, who delights the world, who is with Balarama, and who fulfils the wishes of all.”
arthaṁ śithilīkṛta anarthaṁ śrī nārāyaṇa tīrthaṁ paramapuruṣārtham
“Meditate on Krishna, who destroys all that is harmful and gives life its true purpose — the very form of the supreme goal praised by Sri Narayana Tirtha.”
Brief meaning
In this kirtanam one devotee calls to her friend: “Meditate on the beautiful child Krishna.”
Krishna is not merely a child; the song describes Him in many dimensions — the support of the world, the essence of the Vedas, the God of yogis, the form of devotion, music and sweetness, the one who carries us across the ocean of samsara.
It is a wonderful kirtanam in which the sweetness of His childhood play and the glory of the Supreme are blended together.
Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
Thinking of Krishna lifts the mind above worldly desires.
Even in the form of a child, the Lord is the Supreme.
It shows that the essence of the Vedas is the experience of God.
Music, literature and devotion all culminate in Krishna bhakti.
The only boat to cross the ocean of samsara is surrender to the Lord.
The call “sakhi” (friend) shows the intimacy of devotion and the secret of experiencing Him.
Krishna bhakti shows the way to liberation, the supreme goal of life.

@@ 4.4 | “Pāhi pāhi jaganmōhana” – Raga Nata Namakriya |
Meaning line by line
pāhi pāhi jaganmōhana kṛṣṇa paramānanda śrī kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“O Krishna, who enchants the whole world! Protect me. O form of supreme bliss!”

dēvakī vasudēva nandana kṛṣṇa divya sundara śrī kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“O son of Devaki and Vasudeva! O Krishna of divine beauty!”

nanda yaśōdā nandana kṛṣṇa indu vadana śrī kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“O son who delighted Nanda and Yashoda! O Krishna, whose face is like the moon!”

kiṅkiṇi racita ghaṇaṅ ghaṇa kṛṣṇa krīḍālōla śrī kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“O Krishna, playing on while the little bells on your feet jingle!”

kuṅkuma paṅka vipaṅkila kṛṣṇa gūḍha mahima śrī kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“O you adorned with kumkum! O Krishna of deep, hidden glory!”

taraḷita kuṇḍala maṇḍita kṛṣṇa tāṇḍavalōla śrī kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“O you adorned with earrings that swing as you dance! O Krishna who delights in dancing!”

sādhu sādhu naṭavēṣa śrī kṛṣṇa satyasandha śrī kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“O Krishna in the wondrous guise of a dancer! O upholder of truth!”

pālita nārāyaṇa tīrtha kṛṣṇa parama pāvana śrī kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa
“O Krishna, who protected Narayana Tirtha! O most holy one!”
Brief meaning
In this kirtanam the devotee praises Krishna as “Jaganmohana” — the one whose beauty and bliss enchant the whole world.
While delighting in the beauty of His play — the child's games, the jingle of the bells, the swing of the earrings, the dance, the moon-like face — the song shows that He is the Supreme and the protector of devotees.
It is a wonderful kirtanam that unites the sweetness of His childhood play with the glory of the Supreme Self.
Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
Krishna's beauty is not just outward beauty; it is the form of bliss.
His childhood play naturally draws the mind to the Lord.
Though He appears as a playing child, He is the Supreme.
“Jaganmohana” means the one who divinely captivates the minds of the world.
Delighting in the Lord's play is a path to purity of mind and growth in devotion.
True joy lies in remembering the Lord.
This kirtanam gives the confidence that the same Krishna who protected the great souls will protect us too.

@@ 4.5 | “Nanda nandana gōpāla jaya” – Raga Bilahari |
Meaning line by line
nanda nandana gōpāla jaya navanīta cōra gōpāla
“O Gopala, son of Nanda! O Kanna, the butter thief! Victory to you.”

kandarpa śatakōṭi sundara sukhakara mandahāsa śrī gōpāla
“O you whose beauty surpasses that of a hundred crore Manmathas! O you who give joy with your sweet smile!”

brindāvana gōbṛnda yamunānanda candrānana śrī gōpāla
“O you who shine in Brindavan among the people of Gokula! O you who delight the Yamuna! O you whose face is like the moon!”

līlā mānuṣa bālaka vēṣa balānuja vigraha gōpāla
“O child Krishna, who plays in the guise of a human child! O younger brother of Balarama!”

muraḷīdhara muni bṛnda vicintita mukta janāśraya gōpāla
“O bearer of the flute! O God on whom the sages meditate! O refuge of the liberated!”

nanda sunandādi vandita caraṇāravinda mahōnnata gōpāla
“O exalted Gopala, whose lotus feet are worshipped by Nanda, Sunanda and others!”

gōpī manmatha gōparipālaka gōvardhanōddhara gōpāla
“O you who captivate the hearts of the gopis! O protector of the cowherds! O lifter of Govardhana hill!”

rāsa maṇḍala naṭa dāsa janāvana vāsavādi nuta gōpāla
“O dancer of the Rasa Lila! O protector of your servants! O you praised by Indra and the other gods!”

mūla kāraṇa mōha vāraka lakṣmī lōla parādhīśa gōpāla
“O root cause of the world! O remover of delusion! O Lord of Lakshmi! O Supreme!”

ghōra bhavārṇava tāraka pālita nārāyaṇa tīrtha gōpāla
“O you who carry us across the terrible ocean of samsara! O Gopala who protected Narayana Tirtha!”

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Krishna is praised as Bala Gopala, as the Supreme who lifted Govardhana hill, as the beautiful one who captivates the hearts of the gopis, and as the protector of devotees.
His childhood play, the Rasa Lila, His grace to devotees and His nature as the Supreme — all are beautifully joined in a single kirtanam.

Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
Krishna's childhood play makes devotion sweet.
Kanna who steals butter is really stealing the hearts of devotees.
He who lifted Govardhana also lifts the burden of devotees' sorrows.
The Rasa Lila is not an ordinary dance; it symbolises the blissful union of the individual soul with the Supreme.
Krishna is not only the form of beauty; He is the Supreme who removes delusion.
This song shows that surrender to the Lord is the way to cross the ocean of samsara.
The special beauty of this kirtanam is that a simple devotee too can lovingly experience the same Krishna on whom the sages meditate.

@@ 4.6 | “Nanda nandanaṁ nijabhakta” – Raga Tilang |
Meaning line by line
nanda nandanaṁ nija bhakta candanam ākalaya
“Meditate on Krishna, the son of Nanda; experience Him who soothes true devotees like cool sandal paste.”

nandādi munīndra bhāvya nandam ākalaya
“Experience Krishna, on whom Nanda and the great sages meditate and rejoice.”

sundarānana candra śrī mandiram ākalaya
“Meditate on Him whose beautiful face is like the moon and whose body is the abode of Lakshmi.”

śṛṅgāra rasa pravāha śrīnidhim ākalaya
“Experience Krishna, the treasure from whom sweetness, love and devotion overflow.”

kalabha sundara gamana kalita lalitam ākalaya
“Meditate on Krishna, whose gait is gentle and sweet like the graceful walk of a young elephant.”

parijana rakṣaṇa baddha parikaram ākalaya
“Experience Krishna, who has made protecting His devotees His very duty.”

vijaya gōpāla matula vikhyātam ākalaya
“Meditate on Vijaya Gopala, who shines with unequalled fame.”

balabhadra bhadrada bāhu bandham ihākalaya
“Experience Krishna in His loving bond with Balarama.”

mathurā māninī jana manasijam ākalaya
“Meditate on Krishna, who like Manmatha captivates the hearts of the women of Mathura.”

śiva nārāyaṇa tīrtha sēvya divya padam ākalaya
“Experience Krishna, whose divine feet are served by great souls like Shiva Narayana Tirtha.”

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Krishna is described as cool sandal paste for devotees, as the form of beauty, as the compassionate protector of devotees, and as the Supreme on whom great souls meditate.
It is a devotional song rich in experience, asking us to savour everything — the beauty of His face, the beauty of His walk, His love for devotees and the sweetness of His play.

Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
Krishna bhakti gives the mind coolness and peace, like sandal paste.
The Lord's beauty is not just outward beauty; it is an experience of bliss.
Protecting devotees is the Lord's natural dharma.
We too can experience through devotion the same Krishna whom great souls and sages experienced.
The play of child Krishna softens the mind and settles it in devotion.
The central message of this kirtanam is “ākalaya” — hold Krishna in the mind and experience Him.
Thinking of God brings sweetness, peace and protection into life.

@@ 4.7 | “Ālōkayē rukmiṇī kalyāṇa gōpālam” – Raga Kambhoji |
Meaning line by line
ālōkayē rukmiṇī kalyāṇa gōpālam
“I behold Gopala at His wedding with Rukmini.”
(Radha Kalyana Madhava, Sita Kalyana Raghava — all the divine wedding forms shine as one in Him.)

nīlamēgha nibhākāraṁ bālārka samāna cēlam
“Him whose form is like a dark rain cloud and whose yellow silk glows like the rising sun.”

nīlāmbarānujaṁ gōpālakaṁ nīlālakāntam
“Gopala, the younger brother of Balarama, with beautiful dark curls.”

dvārakāpura maṇḍapē dvādaśāditya sannibhē
“Him who shines in the hall at Dwaraka with the brilliance of twelve suns.”

bhūri ratna siṁhāsanē bhūsuragaṇē
“Seated on a throne studded with countless gems, surrounded by Vedic scholars.”

vīrāsanē sukhāsīnaṁ viśva maṅgala dāyinam
“Seated at ease in the hero's posture, bringing auspiciousness to the world.”

dhīra yōgi saṁsēvanaṁ dēvakī vasudēva sūnum
“Krishna, the son of Devaki and Vasudeva, served by steadfast yogis.”

aṣṭa mahiṣī samētam amara nāri susēvitam
“Him who shines with His eight queens and is served by celestial maidens.”

tuṣṭa puṣṭa janāvṛtaṁ tumburu gītam
“Surrounded by happy and prosperous devotees, and sung by Tumburu.”

iṣṭa jana samāśritam īśvaram aparājitam
“The Lord in whom loving devotees take refuge, the one who can never be defeated.”

dṛṣṭa sarvalōka jātaṁ dēvarājādi vinutam
“Him who is seen by all the worlds and praised by Indra and the other gods.”

śaṅkha dundubhi nāditē sadasō gandharvādi gītē
“Him who shines amid the auspicious sound of conches and drums, while the Gandharvas sing.”

puṅgānupuṅga nigama buddha janāvṛtē
“Surrounded by those wise in the Vedas.”

paṅkajēkṣaṇa rambhādi paṭu naṭana vinōditē
“Krishna, delighted by the dance of lotus-eyed celestial maidens such as Rambha.”

kuṅkuma kēsara varṣitē kōmalākāra caritē
“Showered with saffron, with a tender and graceful form.”

divya kirīṭa kuṇḍala dīpta pītāmbaradharam
“Wearing a divine crown, earrings and radiant yellow silk.”

navya muktāmaṇi hāraṁ nānāvihāram
“Wearing fresh pearl garlands, performing many kinds of play.”

avyaya bhūti vistāram amara nārī surādhāram
“The source of imperishable wealth and the support of the gods themselves.”

bhavya kaustubha kandharaṁ bhakta mānasa sañcāram
“Whose chest is adorned with the Kaustubha gem, and who moves in the minds of devotees.”

hēma muktāmaṇi chatraṁ hīra ratna kaṭisūtram
“Surrounded by ornaments of gold, pearl and diamond.”

cāmara vyajanāvṛtaṁ śaradindu vaktram
“Fanned with chamaras, with a face like the autumn moon.”

kāmakōṭi samagātraṁ kanakāṅgadādi vicitram
“With a body more beautiful than a crore of Manmathas, adorned with golden armlets.”

śyāma sundaraṁ pavitraṁ śaraṇāgatōddhāra gōtram
“Krishna, the dark and beautiful one, the holy one, who saves those who surrender.”

candrānanā bhaiṣmī bhāmā jāmbavati satyā mitravindā bhadrā lakṣmaṇā kāḷindī nāyakam
“The Lord of His queens — Rukmini, Satyabhama, Jambavati, Mitravinda, Bhadra, Lakshmana, Kalindi.”

nanda nandanam āśrita bṛnda bṛndāraka vandyam
“The son of Nanda, worshipped by the devotees who seek refuge and by the gods.”

nandita nārāyaṇa tīrthā nandadam ānanda kandam
“Krishna, who delighted Narayana Tirtha, the very root of bliss.”

Brief meaning
This kirtanam describes the divine darshan of Sri Krishna seated majestically on His throne at His wedding with Rukmini.
His beauty, His royal splendour, the divine grandeur, His grace to devotees and His nature as the Supreme praised by the gods are all beautifully woven together.
This is not just a wedding scene; it is also seen as a symbol of the divine wedding in which the individual soul unites with the Supreme.

Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
Krishna is not only Bala Gopala; He is also the supreme king of kings at Dwaraka.
Rukmini Kalyanam signifies the final union of devotion.
The Lord's beauty, splendour and compassion all coexist in perfect harmony.
He who moves in the minds of devotees is the true Supreme Self.
Protecting those who surrender is the Lord's nature.
This kirtanam is full of the joy of darshan — the listener feels as if present in the wedding hall.
A wonderful kirtanam that tells us the source of bliss is the experience of the Lord.

@@ 4.8 | “Kalyāṇa kṛṣṇā” – Raga Khamas |
Meaning line by line
kalyāṇa kṛṣṇa kamanīya kṛṣṇa
“O auspicious Krishna! O Krishna of captivating beauty!”

kāḷiya nartana śrī kṛṣṇa
“O heroic Krishna, who danced on the serpent Kaliya!”

gōvardhana giridhāri murāri gōpiyar mana sañcāri
“O lifter of Govardhana hill! O slayer of Mura! O you who ever move in the hearts of the gopis!”

brindāvana śrī tuḷasi māla pītāmbaradhāri murāri
“O Murari, who shines in Brindavan wearing a tulasi garland and yellow silk!”

śyāma sundara jagadōddhārā sajjana paripāla dayāḷā
“O dark and beautiful one! O saviour of the world! O compassionate protector of the good!”

nandakumāra navanītacōra nāda vēṇulōla kṛpāḷā
“O son of Nanda! O Kanna, the butter thief! O you who delight in the sweet sound of the flute! O merciful one!”

Brief meaning
This kirtanam briefly and sweetly describes many of Krishna's divine deeds and His beauty.
The hero who danced on Kaliya, the protector who lifted Govardhana, the form of love who captivated the gopis, the child who steals butter, the saviour of the world — all these forms merge here into one beautiful Krishna.

Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
Krishna is at once a hero, the form of compassion, a child and the Supreme.
“Kaliya nartanam” signifies the Lord's victory over ego and poisonous tendencies of the mind.
“Govardhana giridhari” shows that the Lord will do anything to protect His devotees.
Krishna who lives in the hearts of the gopis is really the Supreme Self who abides in the minds of devotees.
The sound of the flute is the Lord's divine call; the mind that hears it forgets the world and seeks God.
Kanna who steals butter is really stealing the hearts of devotees.
This kirtanam simply conveys the Lord's beauty, play, compassion and protecting nature.

@@ 4.9 | “Parama puruṣamanuyāma” – Raga Behag |
Meaning line by line
parama puruṣamanuyāma vayaṁ sakhi parama puruṣamanuyāma
“O friend! Let us follow Krishna, the Supreme Person; let us reach Him alone.”

surucira hāsaṁ sundara nāsaṁ taruṇāruṇa kiraṇādhara sarasam
“Krishna with the sweetest smile, the beautiful nose, and lips red like the rising sun.”

nandakumāraṁ nagavara dhīraṁ brindāvana bhuvi vividha vihāram
“Krishna, the son of Nanda, the hero who lifted Govardhana, who performs many kinds of play in Brindavan.”

bṛndāraka gaṇa vandita caraṇāravindaṁ miḷita maṇi madhukara nikaram
“Him whose lotus feet are worshipped by the gods, with devotees gathered round them like bees swarming over jewels.”

bhāvuka caraṇaṁ bhavasantaraṇaṁ bhavya sēvaka jana bhāgya vitaraṇam
“Krishna, who is the refuge of devotees, who carries them across the ocean of samsara, and who bestows good fortune on His noble servants.”

avyaya vimala vibhūti vijṛmbhita divya maṇi racita vividhābharaṇam
“Him who wears ornaments set with divine gems, radiant with imperishable, pure splendour.”

paramōdāraṁ pāpa vidūraṁ smarasāyaka sragdharam ati caturam
“Krishna, supremely generous, the remover of sins, the beautiful one wearing garlands like Manmatha.”

viracita muraḷī gīta rasāmṛta bharita ghanaṅghana kaustubha hāram
“Him who pours out nectar through the music of His flute, whose chest is adorned with the Kaustubha gem.”

yuvatī gītaṁ yōgi sulalitaṁ kavijana mānasa kamala vilasitam
“Krishna, sung by young maidens, meditated on by yogis, shining in the lotus hearts of poets.”

śiva nārāyaṇa tīrtha viracitaṁ śrī gōpāla dayārasa miḷitam
“Sri Gopala, full of the nectar of compassion, sung by Shiva Narayana Tirtha.”

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam devotees call to one another: “Come! Let us reach Krishna, the Supreme Person.”
Krishna's beauty, smile, flute music, play, compassion, protection of devotees, being the object of yogis' meditation and the joy of poets' hearts — all these dimensions are beautifully described.
It is a joyful devotional kirtanam that invites us to experience the Lord.

Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
“Parama Purusha” means the Supreme who is above all.
Krishna bhakti is not only a personal experience; it is also inviting others into that joy.
The sound of the flute signifies the Lord's divine call.
The Lord's feet are the boat that helps us cross the ocean of samsara.
A simple devotee can reach through love the same Krishna on whom yogis meditate.
Krishna is the centre that draws poets, devotees, yogis and gods alike.
This kirtanam expresses the height of “experienced devotion” — savouring the Lord and accepting Him as the goal of life.

@@ 4.10 | “Jaya jaya gōkulabāla” – Raga Kurinji |
Meaning line by line
jaya jaya gōkulabāla jaya sakalāgama mūla
“O protector of Gokula, victory to You! O root cause of all the scriptures, victory to You!”

dayayā māṁ gōpāla dīnaṁ pālaya bāla
“O Gopala! Protect poor me with Your compassion.”

dēvamayā caritamidaṁ dīnadhiyā aparādham
“With my small understanding I fail to know Your divine deeds fully, and I err.”

śrī vasudhādhava kṛpayā kēvalamēva dhunīhi
“O Lord of Lakshmi! By Your compassion alone, wash away my faults and sins.”

īkṣē tāvaka dēhē īśa jaganti nirīhē
“O Lord! In Your desireless divine form I see all the worlds.”

vīkṣā śikṣita mōhē vēdāntātula gēhē
“By Your gracious glance remove my delusion and show me the highest state of Vedanta.”

jagadaṇḍa kōṭi tanō jagadu darāntara sutanō
“O You whose body holds crores of universes! All those worlds are contained within You.”

agaṇita saṅkhyāta manō aparimitāśrita dhēnō
“O Krishna, the support of countless minds and the protector of boundless numbers who surrender!”

vitara mayīśvara karuṇāṁ vighaṭaya mōhāvaraṇam
“O Lord! Pour Your compassion on me and remove the veil of delusion.”

satataṁ mē bhava śaraṇaṁ sādhaya saṁsṛti taraṇam
“Be my refuge always, and help me cross this ocean of samsara.”

iti vidhinā parigītaṁ śruti vacasā hari caritam
“Thus is the glory of Hari, sung by the Vedas and by Brahma.”

yati nārāyaṇa kathitaṁ yadukula bhūṣaṇa muditam
“This is the kirtanam sung by Narayana Tirtha, the best of ascetics, in praise of Krishna, the jewel of the Yadu clan.”

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam the devotee praises Krishna as “Gokulabala”, “the root cause of the Vedas” and “the support of the universes”, and prays to be protected by His compassion.
The song speaks not only of the sweetness of child Krishna but also of Him as the Supreme, the summit of Vedanta and the support of the worlds.
It is a kirtanam in which devotion, surrender and Vedantic wisdom are blended together.

Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
This song stresses that child Krishna is the Supreme.
Without the Lord's compassion the veil of delusion cannot be removed.
The final essence of the Vedas is surrender to the Lord.
The truth of the cosmic form — that the whole world is contained within the Lord — is told here indirectly.
True devotion is admitting one's faults and surrendering to the Lord.
The only support for crossing the ocean of samsara is the Lord's grace.
The deep message of this kirtanam: we may delight in Krishna as Bala Gopala, and at the same time we must meditate on Him as Parabrahman.

@@ 4.11 | “Vīkṣēhaṁ kadā” – Raga Ahiri |
Meaning line by line
vīkṣēhaṁ kadā gōpāla mūrtim
“When shall I behold the divine form of Gopala Krishna?”

sākṣāt madana kōṭi saundarya rasapēṭim
“The treasure-chest of beauty, surpassing the beauty of a crore of Manmathas.”

mṛgamada lalita tilaka vilasat viśāla phālam
“With a broad forehead adorned with a musk tilak.”

sakala guṇa sampatyā sahitam ananta līlam
“Krishna, full of every virtue, whose divine play is endless.”

brindāvana vihāraṁ baddha mayūra barham
“Krishna who plays in Brindavan, wearing a peacock feather.”

nanda nandanam akhila ānandakara vigraham
“The son of Nanda, whose divine form gives joy to all.”

kālamēgha śarīraṁ kalita vidyut vasanam
“With a body like a dark rain cloud and garments shining like lightning.”

kēḷi kalā kutuka kiñcit kuñcitādharam
“Eager for play, with sweet lips gently curved.”

taddhimi dhimi dhimiti tāṇḍava lōlam akhila
“Dancing joyfully to the rhythm of ‘taddhimi dhimi’.”

tāpatrayōpa śama kṣama karuṇā kaṭākṣam
“Whose compassionate glance can remove the three kinds of suffering — of body and mind, from other beings, and from nature.”

sarasa muraḷī gīta sarasa sudhālaharī
“Who pours out waves of nectar through the sweet music of His flute.”

nirata nārāyaṇa tīrtha nirmala mānasa haṁsam
“Krishna, like a swan playing in the pure lake of Narayana Tirtha's mind.”

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam the devotee longs for the divine darshan of Krishna.
His beauty, the peacock feather, the music of the flute, His rain-cloud colour, His dance and His compassionate glance — all these divine features are lovingly described.
It is not mere description; it is a deep expression of devotion that longs to see the Lord.

Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
The peak of true devotion is the longing: “When shall I see the Lord?”
Krishna's beauty is not outward beauty; it is an experience of bliss.
The sound of the flute is regarded as the Lord's gracious call.
The peacock feather, the musk tilak and the rain-cloud colour all symbolise divine sweetness and simplicity.
The Lord's compassionate glance removes the three kinds of suffering in life.
When the devotee's mind becomes pure, the Lord dwells in it like a swan.
This is a wonderful song full of longing for darshan and the yearning of devotion.

@@ 4.12 | “Gōvardhana giridhara” – Raga Darbari Kanada |
Meaning line by line
gōvardhana giridhara gōvinda gōkulapālaka paramānanda
“O Govinda, who lifted Govardhana hill! O protector of Gokula! O form of supreme bliss!”

śrīvatsāṅkita śrīkaustubha dhara bhāvaka bhayahara pāhi mukunda
“O You who bear the Srivatsa mark and the Kaustubha gem! O remover of devotees' fear! Mukunda, protect me.”

amita kalyāṇaguṇa agaṇita līla aparimitānanda ghana nandabāla
“O son of Nanda, full of countless good qualities, endless divine play and boundless bliss!”

śamita daitya dambha śāntyādi mūla vimala mānasa vṛtti nivasana śīla
“O destroyer of the demons' pride! O root of noble qualities such as peace! O You who love to dwell in pure minds!”

ānandāmṛta vāridhi khēla alaghu parākrama anupama līla
“O You who play in the ocean of the nectar of bliss! O You of matchless valour and unequalled play!”

śrī nandātmaja śritajanapāla śrīkara kisalaya lālana lōla
“O son of Nanda! O protector of those who surrender! O You who show love with hands tender as new leaves!”

pāṭita suraripu pādapa bṛnda pāvana carita parāmṛta kanda
“O destroyer of the enemies of the gods! O source of nectar through Your holy deeds!”

nāṭya rasōtkaṭa nānābharaṇa nārāyaṇa tīrtha arcita caraṇa
“O You who delight in the joy of dance, adorned with many ornaments, whose feet are worshipped by Narayana Tirtha!”

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Krishna is praised as “Govardhana Giridhara” — the Supreme who lifted a whole mountain to protect His devotees.
His valour, compassion, blissful play, His dwelling in devotees' minds and His destruction of the demons' pride are beautifully described.
It is a kirtanam that reveals the depth of Krishna bhakti, which gives both protection and bliss.

Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
“Govardhana Giridhara” signifies the Lord who takes up any burden for His devotees.
Krishna is not only a hero; He is the compassionate one who dwells in devotees' minds.
Destroying ego and granting peace is the Lord's divine play.
The Lord's stories are like nectar; listening to them purifies the mind.
The mind of a true devotee is a temple where the Lord dwells.
Krishna's play is like an ocean of the nectar of bliss — experiencing it brings endless joy.
The essence of devotion is to fix the mind at the Lord's feet, as Narayana Tirtha did.

@@ 4.13 | “Mādhava māmava dēva” – Raga Neelambari |
Meaning line by line
mādhava māmava dēva kṛṣṇa – yādava kṛṣṇa yadukula kṛṣṇa
“O Madhava, protect me! O Krishna born in the Yadava clan! O pride of the Yadu clan!”

sādhu janādhāra sārvabhauma – mādhava māmava dēva
“O supreme king, support of the saintly! Madhava, protect me.”

ambuja lōcana kambu śubhagrīva – bimbādhara candra bimbānana
“O You with lotus eyes, a beautiful neck like a conch, lips red as the bimba fruit and a face like the moon!”

cāmpēya nāsāgra lagna sumauktika – śārada candra janita madana
“O You with a pearl on a nose like the champaka flower! O You with the charm of Manmatha, like the autumn moon!”

śaṅkha cakra padma śārṅga khaḍga – vaijayanti kaustubhādi bhūṣa
“O You who bear the conch, discus, lotus, the bow Sharnga and the sword, adorned with the Vaijayanti garland and the Kaustubha gem!”

svīkṛta buddhyādi tattva samanvita – divya maṅgaḷa gōpa bāla vēṣa
“O You who are the support of the principles beginning with intellect, and who took the divine guise of a cowherd boy!”

kapaṭa mānuṣa dēha kalpita jagadaṇḍa kōṭi mōhita bhāratī ramaṇa
“Though You seem to have taken a human body, You hold crores of universes!”

apagata mōha tadudbhava nijajana – karuṇādhṛta dēha sulakṣaṇa
“O You of auspicious marks, who remove delusion and out of compassion took a body for Your devotees!”

taraḷa kuṇḍala ravi maṇḍala vikasita – nijajana mānasa paṅkēruha
“O You with swinging earrings, shining like the sun, who blossom in the lotus hearts of devotees!”

karuṇā hāsa sudhānidhi kiraṇa – śamita bhavatāpa janamōha
“O You who, with a smile full of compassion, remove the sorrows of samsara and people's delusion!”

muraḷī gāna rasāmṛta pūrita – vraja yuvatī mānasārṇava bhō
“O You who fill the ocean-like hearts of the maidens of Vraja with the nectar of Your flute!”

sarasa guṇārṇava tīrṇa bhavārṇava – satataṁ gīta kīrti maṇḍala bhō
“O ocean of sweet qualities! O You who help us cross the ocean of samsara! O You whose fame is ever sung!”

āgama giri śikharōdita satyacit advaya lakṣaṇa sukha bhānō
“O sun of non-dual Existence-Consciousness-Bliss, rising on the summit of the mountain of the scriptures!”

bhōgi kulōttama bhōga śayana – dugdha sāgarajā lakṣaṇāḍhya tanō
“O Supreme who reclines on Adishesha! O You who shine with Lakshmi, born of the milk ocean!”

indirayā saha sundara kṛṣṇa – purandarādi vandya padakamala
“O beautiful Krishna, shining with Lakshmi! O You whose lotus feet are worshipped by Indra and the gods!”

nanda nandana yōgivarya durandhara – nārāyaṇa tīrtha mati vihāra
“O son of Nanda! O You on whom the best of yogis meditate! O You who play in the mind of Narayana Tirtha!”

Brief meaning
This kirtanam describes Krishna not only as Bala Gopala but also as the form of Parabrahman.
His divine beauty, His weapons, His compassion, the music of His flute, His dwelling in devotees' minds and His removal of the sorrows of samsara are all beautifully celebrated.
It is an exalted kirtanam uniting devotion, Vedanta and the experience of divine beauty.

Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
This song deeply conveys that child Krishna is the Supreme.
The Lord's human incarnation is for compassion alone; it is not an ordinary human birth.
The music of the flute transforms devotees' minds divinely.
The Lord's compassionate smile is the medicine that removes the sorrow of samsara.
The Vedantic truth of “Sat-Chit-Ananda” is experienced in the form of Krishna.
The devotee's mind is the Brindavan where the Lord plays.
This kirtanam shows that the sweetness of devotion and Vedantic wisdom are one.

@@ 4.14 | “Pāhi pāhimām” – Raga Jonpuri |
Meaning line by line
pāhi pāhimāṁ parama kṛpāḷō
“O most compassionate one! Protect me.”

dēhi mē tvayi dēva śubhabhaktim
“O Lord! Give me pure devotion to You.”

asura saṁhāra śrī vijayagōpāla
“O heroic Vijaya Gopala, who destroyed the demons!”

amara saṁrakṣaṇa śrī vijayagōpāla
“O Vijaya Gopala, who protects the gods!”

vasudēva tanaya śrī vijayagōpāla
“O Vijaya Gopala, son of Vasudeva!”

vāsavādi nuta śrī vijayagōpāla
“O Vijaya Gopala, praised by Indra and the other gods!”

saṅgīta lōla śrī vijayagōpāla
“O Vijaya Gopala, who delights in music!”

sadguṇa śīla śrī vijayagōpāla
“O Vijaya Gopala, full of all good qualities!”

śṛṅgāra rasa pōṣa śrī vijayagōpāla
“O Vijaya Gopala, who nourishes the sweetness of devotion and love!”

śritajana pāla śrī vijayagōpāla
“O Vijaya Gopala, protector of those who surrender!”

dhīraguṇōdāra śrī vijayagōpāla
“O Vijaya Gopala, of noble mind and excellent qualities!”

dhēnukādi haraṇa śrī vijayagōpāla
“O Vijaya Gopala, who destroyed demons such as Dhenuka!”

nārada munigēya śrī vijayagōpāla
“O Vijaya Gopala, sung by the sage Narada!”

nārāyaṇa tīrtha śrī vijayagōpāla
“O Vijaya Gopala, praised by Narayana Tirtha!”

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam the devotee calls Krishna “the supremely compassionate one” and prays: “Protect me; grant me steady devotion to You.”
Krishna is described as the destroyer of demons, the protector of gods and devotees, the sweet Bala Gopala who delights in music, and the form of all good qualities.

Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
The highest prayer of devotion is “grant me devotion” — not worldly desires.
Krishna is the protector of devotees.
Destroying demons signifies destroying not only outer evils but also inner ego, desire and anger.
Music, devotion and love are all pathways to experiencing Krishna.
“Vijaya Gopala” signifies the Supreme who always gives victory to dharma.
Krishna is the compassionate one who never abandons those who surrender.
This kirtanam simply expresses devotion, compassion, protection and bliss.

@@ 4.15 | “Jaya jaya bālagōpāla” – Raga Varamu |
Meaning line by line
jaya jaya bālagōpāla, jaya jaya madana gōpāla
“O Bala Gopala, victory to You! O Gopala, more beautiful than Manmatha, victory to You!”

jaya jaya vijayagōpāla, jaya jaya kalyāṇa gōpāla
“O Vijaya Gopala, who always gives victory! O auspicious Kalyana Gopala, victory to You!”

nirupama guṇa ratnākāśa, nirmala kīrti pūritāśa
“O You who are the sky holding the jewels of matchless virtues! O You who fill the world with pure fame!”

śaranidhi madhya nivēśa, sārasa nētra lakṣmīśa
“O You who dwell in the milk ocean! O lotus-eyed Lord of Lakshmi!”

brahmādi sura pūjyapāda, pātaka jāla vibhēda
“O You whose feet are worshipped by Brahma and the gods! O destroyer of the mass of sins!”

brahmaṇya dēva dhṛta vēda, balabhadrāvaraja bhakta varada
“O Lord of dharma who upholds the Vedas! O younger brother of Balarama! O giver of boons to devotees!”

parijana hṛdaya prabhāva, paricita muraḷī rāva
“O You who rule the hearts of devotees! O You who are known by the sweet sound of the flute!”

carita puṇyaika sēva, śiva nārāyaṇa tīrtha dēva
“Serving Your stories is the greatest merit. O God praised by Shiva Narayana Tirtha!”

Brief meaning
This kirtanam praises Krishna as Bala Gopala, as the form of beauty, as the giver of boons to devotees and as the protector of the Vedas.
His flute music, His good qualities, His effect on devotees' hearts and His power to destroy sins are all beautifully praised in this song.

Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
The cry “jaya jaya” in praising Krishna shows the flood of joy in devotion.
The Lord's qualities are like countless jewels.
The sound of the flute is the Lord's call of compassion.
Hearing and singing the Lord's stories is the greatest merit.
Krishna protects the Vedas; at the same time He mingles with devotees as the simple Bala Gopala.
Living in the hearts of devotees is the Lord's true temple.
This kirtanam expresses the glory of devotion, bliss and remembrance of the Name.

@@ 5.1 | “Gōvinda kṛṣṇa pāhi” – Raga Yadukula Kambhoji |
Meaning line by line
gōvinda kṛṣṇa pāhi yaduvīra
“O Govinda! O Krishna! O hero of the Yadu clan! Protect me.”

rāja gōpālakṛṣṇa pāhi yaduvīra
“O Rajagopala Krishna! O hero of the Yadu clan! Protect me.”

rādhāmana kumuda pūrṇacandrā
“O full moon who makes the lily of Radha's heart blossom!”

puṇyagāthā nandita munijana sāndrā
“Your holy stories delight the sages.”

kamalāvallabha divya kañjanētra
“O Lord of Lakshmi! O You with divine lotus eyes!”

gōpa yamalārjuna bhañjana nīlagātra
“O Gopala who broke the twin Arjuna trees! O You with a dark blue body!”

cañcalamaṇi kuṇḍala cāruhāsa
“O You who shine with swinging jewelled earrings and a sweet smile!”

caṇḍa cāṇūra muṣṭikāsura damanahāsa
“O hero who destroyed fierce demons like Chanura and Mushtika!”

kāḷiya phaṇaraṅga nāṭyalōla
“O You who danced joyfully on the hoods of the serpent Kaliya!”

bāla kēḷi viśārada manōjña līla
“Skilled in childhood games, performing play that captivates the mind.”

tāpādi rahita tamāla nīla
“O You who are untouched by the sorrows of samsara, dark like the tamala tree!”

vijaya gōpāla gōpijana kēḷilōla
“O Vijaya Gopala! You who delight in play with the gopis!”

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Krishna is described as the hero of the Yadu clan, the moon of Radha, the hero who destroyed the demons, and Gopala who delights in childhood play.
With deeds such as dancing on Kaliya, breaking the twin Arjuna trees and destroying Chanura and Mushtika, together with His sweet smile and childlike beauty, this song becomes full of joy.

Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
Krishna is at once a hero and a sweet child.
The description of Him as the moon of Radha's heart shows the supreme joy of devotion.
Dancing on Kaliya signifies the Lord's victory over ego.
Breaking the twin Arjuna trees signifies freeing the soul from the bonds of karma.
His childhood play naturally draws the devotee's mind to God.
Hearing Krishna's stories gives joy even to the sages.
In this kirtanam devotion, valour, play and beauty — all aspects of Krishna — are harmoniously presented.

@@ 5.2 | “Kṛpayā māmuddhara” – Raga Madhyamavati |
Meaning line by line
kṛpayā māmuddhara śrīkṛṣṇa – harē mukunda
“O Sri Krishna! O Hari! O Mukunda! By Your compassion, lift me up and save me.”

drupada nṛpatanayā duritahara caraṇa – harē mukunda
“O You whose feet removed the sorrows of Draupadi! O Mukunda!”

kapaṭaripu haraṇa kaustubhābharaṇa
“O destroyer of deceitful demons! O You who wear the Kaustubha gem!”

dhīra vāridhi gambhīra, sakala lōkādhāra
“O You whose majesty is deep as the ocean! O support of all the worlds!”

haiyaṅgavīna cōra, gōpa sundari jārākāra
“O Kanna who steals fresh butter! O beautiful one who captivates the hearts of the gopis!”

vijitamāra manō vidūra
“O You whose beauty conquers even Manmatha! O Supreme, beyond the easy reach of the mind!”

tāraka viracita hāra virājita
“O You adorned with garlands shining like the stars!”

dānava darpa vibhañjana śūra
“O hero who breaks the pride of the demons!”

amarāri bhañjanādi bhīma, hē mādhava
“O mighty hero who destroys the enemies of the gods! O Madhava!”

kamalā manamōhana kāma
“O beautiful one who captivates even Lakshmi's heart!”

ati tāmasa yamalārjuna śāpa virāma
“O compassionate one who ended the curse of the twin Arjuna trees!”

yamunā vihāra ramaṇījana hṛdayākarṣaṇādbhuta
“O wondrous beauty who plays by the Yamuna and draws the hearts of the maidens!”

kalita mṛduvacana vāgabhirāma
“O You who charm with sweet and gentle words!”

agajā sannuta kīrti jāla, ādyanta rahita
“O You whose fame is praised even by Parvati! O You who have neither beginning nor end!”

nigamāvaḷi vidhēya līla, atyanta bhakta sukha mārga pradāna suśīla
“O You whose play is sung by the Vedas! O noble one who shows devotees the path of bliss!”

kāḷiya śirasi takiṭa dhimi dhimiti… naṭana vilōla
“O You who danced joyfully, keeping rhythm on the head of Kaliya!”

pītāmbaradharābhra nīla
“O You, dark as a rain cloud, wearing yellow silk!”

bhakta pāṇḍava dūta duryōdhanādi kāla
“O You who went as messenger for the Pandavas! O You who were death to the unrighteous like Duryodhana!”

sakala lōkātīta kastūri tilaka phāla
“O Supreme beyond all worlds! O You whose forehead is adorned with a musk tilak!”

dhṛta vanamāla pātaka tatihara pāvana guṇakula
“O You who wear the forest garland! O treasury of holy qualities that remove sins!”

vēda gaṇēḍita vijaya gōpāla
“O Vijaya Gopala, praised by the Vedas!”

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam the devotee prays to Krishna: “By Your compassion, lift me up and protect me.”
Krishna's compassion, valour and beauty, and many of His deeds — dancing on Kaliya, saving Draupadi, going as messenger for the Pandavas — are praised here.
It is a kirtanam that expresses the devotee's surrender and Krishna's compassionate, protecting nature.

Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
“Kṛpayā māmuddhara” — the soul can rise only by the Lord's grace.
Krishna removes the sorrows of devotees; the saving of Draupadi is proof of this.
Dancing on Kaliya signifies the Lord's victory over ego and poisonous tendencies of the mind.
Though the Lord's deeds look like play, they hold deep truth within.
Showing devotees the path of bliss is Krishna's compassion.
The Supreme praised by the Vedas is at the same time the child Krishna who steals butter.
This kirtanam unites compassion, surrender, heroic play and the sweetness of devotion.

@@ 5.3 | “Pāhi madanagōpāla” – Raga Todi |
Meaning line by line
pāhi madanagōpāla mukunda pālita mucukunda
“O Gopala, more beautiful than Manmatha! O Mukunda, who protected Muchukunda! Protect me.”

1. nanda nandana nandita munijana mandahāsa vadana harē kṛṣṇa
“O son of Nanda! O delight of the sages! O Krishna with a sweetly smiling face!”

2. daṇḍita ripujana aṇḍaja vāhana puṇḍarīka nayana harē kṛṣṇa
“O destroyer of enemies! O You who ride on Garuda! O lotus-eyed Krishna!”

3. patita pāvana natajanāvana satatānanda ghana harē kṛṣṇa
“O You who sanctify even the fallen! O protector of those who surrender! O Krishna, the form of everlasting bliss!”

4. arjuna cēla sajjana pāla parjita ripujāla harē kṛṣṇa
“O friend of Arjuna! O protector of the good! O destroyer of enemies!”

5. indirā ramaṇa mandara bharaṇa vandita dēva gaṇa harē kṛṣṇa
“O Lord of Lakshmi! O You who bore Mount Mandara! O You worshipped by the gods!”

6. kuñjara pōṣaṇa mañjuḷa bhāṣaṇa mañjīra padayugaḷa harē kṛṣṇa
“O protector of the elephant (Gajendra)! O You of sweet speech! O Krishna with anklets on Your feet!”

7. kāmita phalada sāmaja varada vāma muraḷīninada harē kṛṣṇa
“O giver of the fruits we desire! O You who blessed the king of elephants! O Krishna of the sweet flute!”

8. ratipati janaka śrita saṁrakṣaka nuta yadukula tilaka harē kṛṣṇa
“O father of Manmatha! O protector of those who surrender! O jewel of the Yadu clan!”

9. vāli mardana vānara pōṣaṇa vāridhi bandhana harē rāma
“O Rama, who slew Vali! O protector of the vanaras! O You who built the bridge across the ocean!”

10. vidhinuta śīla vijayagōpāla vēda nikarapāla harē kṛṣṇa
“O Vijaya Gopala of noble nature, praised by Brahma! O protector of the Vedas!”

Brief meaning
This kirtanam praises Krishna through the deeds of several incarnations.
Krishna's beauty, compassion, protection of devotees, the saving of Gajendra, standing by Arjuna and the music of His flute are all celebrated here.
By including a deed of the Rama incarnation in one place, Narayana Tirtha shows that all incarnations are expressions of the one Supreme.

Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
Krishna and Rama are not different; they are incarnations of the one Narayana.
“Patita pāvana” — however far one has fallen, the Lord will save.
Standing by Arjuna shows that God becomes the devotee's friend.
The saving of Gajendra is a sign of the Lord's immediate compassion.
The sound of the flute transforms the devotee's mind divinely.
The Lord's feet give everlasting bliss to those who surrender.
This kirtanam beautifully unites protection of devotees, the secret of the incarnations, compassion and bliss.

@@ 5.4 | “Dēva dēva dēvakītanaya” – Raga Kedaram |
Meaning line by line
dēva dēva dēvakītanaya pāhimām
“O God of gods! O son of Devaki! Protect me.”

1. dēva kārya durandhara dīna mandāra
“O You who accomplish the tasks of the gods! O You who bless the suffering like the wish-fulfilling Mandara tree!”

2. vasudhā bhayavāraka vardhita lōka
“O You who remove the fear of the earth and protect the world!”

3. vasudēva dēvakī putra vārija nētra
“O son of Vasudeva and Devaki! O lotus-eyed one!”

4. nandagōpa sukumāra navanīta cōra
“O tender child of Nandagopa! O Kanna who steals butter!”

5. pūtanā saṁhāra dadhi pūritōdara
“O slayer of Putana! O child whose belly is full of curd and butter!”

6. śakaṭāsura bhañjana sādhurañjana
“O destroyer of Shakatasura! O You who give joy to the good!”

7. mātṛ darśita svarūpa mādhavarūpa
“O Madhava, who showed His cosmic form to His mother!”

8. bālabhāva nānāvidha līlācatura
“O You who are skilled in many kinds of childhood play!”

9. khaṇḍita tṛṇāvarta kañjavadana
“O lotus-faced one, who destroyed the demon Trinavarta!”

10. yamalārjuna bhañjana yadu rañjana
“O You who broke the twin Arjuna trees! O delight of the Yadu clan!”

11. rauhiṇēya sahacara ramya vihāra
“O You who play sweetly together with Balarama!”

12. gōvatsa paripālana guñjābharaṇa
“O protector of the calves! O You adorned with a garland of gunja berries!”

13. vatsāsura saṁhāra vanamālādhara
“O slayer of Vatsasura! O wearer of the forest garland!”

14. brindāvana gōpijana bṛndamōhana
“O Krishna who enchants the hearts of the gopis of Brindavan!”

15. muraḷīgāna vilōla mōhana līlā
“O You who delight in the music of the flute and perform enchanting play!”

16. nāradādi munigēya naraka vijaya
“O You sung by Narada and other sages! O conqueror of Narakasura!”

17. akrūra vandita pāda arjuna sūta
“O You whose feet Akrura worshipped! O charioteer of Arjuna!”

18. cāṇūra malla mardana cāru nartana
“O slayer of the wrestler Chanura! O beautiful dancer!”

19. kuvalayāpīḍa bhēdana kuṅkumāruṇa
“O conqueror of the elephant Kuvalayapida! O You who glow red like kumkum!”

20. kaṁsāsura saṁhāra karuṇā sāgara
“O slayer of Kamsa! O ocean of compassion!”

21. vasudēvādi śōkahara vārijōdara
“O You with a lotus navel, who removed the grief of Vasudeva and others!”

22. ugrasēnādi rājya dāna ucita kāraṇa
“O just one, who restored the kingdom to Ugrasena!”

23. sāndīpinī varaprada sādhita vēda
“O You who gave a boon to the sage Sandipani! O You who establish the Vedas!”

24. dvārakā nāyaka bhakta bandha mōkṣaṇa
“O Lord of Dwaraka! O You who cut the bonds of devotees!”

25. rukmiṇī kalyāṇa kṛṣṇa rukmi bhañjana
“O Krishna who married Rukmini! O conqueror of Rukmi!”

26. satyabhāmā jāmbavati kālindī priya
“O beloved of Satyabhama, Jambavati and Kalindi!”

27. ṣōḍaśa strī sahasrēśa sundara kēśa
“O Lord of sixteen thousand women! O You with beautiful hair!”

28. kuntīsuta paripāla kuruvaṁśa kāla
“O protector of the Pandavas! O destroyer of the unrighteousness of the Kuru line!”

29. gāṅgēya mōkṣa pradāna garuḍa kētana
“O You who gave liberation to Bhishma! O You whose flag bears Garuda!”

30. uddhava tattvabōdhana ugra śāsana
“O You who taught the Truth to Uddhava! O You who uphold the strict law of dharma!”

31. dharaṇi bhāra haraṇa dharma kāraṇa
“O form of dharma, who came to remove the burden of the earth!”

32. viśada guṇālavāla vijayagōpāla
“O Vijaya Gopala, shining with countless good qualities!”

Brief meaning
This great kirtanam briefly tells the whole story of Krishna's incarnation.
His childhood play, the destruction of demons, His joy with the gopis, the establishing of dharma in the Mahabharata, the protection of devotees and His teaching — all aspects of the Krishna avatara are beautifully woven together.
It is arranged like “a summary of the story of Krishna's incarnation”.

Significance / essence to share in an upanyasam
Krishna's incarnation is not just play; it is a divine plan for establishing dharma.
From child Krishna stealing butter to the teaching at Kurukshetra, all are divine acts with spiritual meaning.
The Lord incarnates to remove the sorrows of devotees and to destroy unrighteousness.
He who gives love through the flute and He who destroys unrighteousness with the discus are the same Krishna.
Teaching the Truth to Uddhava is the summit of divine wisdom.
“Dharaṇi bhāra haraṇam” — when dharma declines in the world, the Lord incarnates.
This kirtanam unites devotion, history, Vedanta and the secret of the incarnation in one song.

@@ 6.1 | “rāmā daya jūḍavē” – Raga Keeravani |
Meaning line by line
This whole kirtanam is a prayer of surrender, melting with longing for compassion.
Bhadrachala Ramadas lived his whole life in the name of Rama.
Even in the suffering of prison he did not let go of Rama.
In this song we see:
a plea for compassion,
firm devotion to Rama,
complete surrender,
and the faith that “You alone are my support”.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
rāmā daya jūḍavē bhadrācala dhāmā nannu brōvavē
O Rama of Bhadrachalam! Look upon me with kindness! Protect me!
“daya jūḍavē” = look with compassion.
“brōvavē” = protect.
This is the devotee's direct cry.

jānaki rāma kalyāṇa rāma kōdaṇḍa rāma daya jūḍavē
O Janaki Rama! O Kalyana Rama! O Kodanda Rama! Be kind to me!
Calling on Rama's many divine forms,
the devotee asks for compassion.

paṭṭābhi rāma daya jūḍavē
O Rama crowned as king! Be kind to me!
Rama is:
the king of dharma,
the protector of devotees.

Charanam 1
rāmā daya jūci rakṣiñci mamu nēlu
O Rama! Look upon us with kindness, protect us and sustain us.
“nēlu” = to keep alive, to support.

rāmā raṇaraṅga bhīma
O Rama, mighty hero of the battlefield!

jagadabhi rājīvadaḷa lōcana
O Lord of the world, with eyes like lotus petals!
A form that unites valour and compassion.

bhakta parādhīna
One who is bound by His devotees.
A most beautiful principle of devotion:
the Lord is a servant to His devotees' love.

bhavamōcana
One who frees us from samsara.

Charanam 2
rājarājakula rājarājārcita
Worshipped even by the king of kings.

rājita vaibhava rāja lalāma
The king of kings, shining with glory.

tāṭaka saṁhāraṇa
Who destroyed Tataka.

mēṭi kōṭi rākṣasa mada haraṇa
Who destroyed the pride of countless rakshasas.
Rama destroys
both the demons outside
and the ego within.

Charanam 3
nīṭutō śrī rāmakōṭi vrāsiti
I wrote the name “Sri Rama” a crore times.
Ramadas's devotion to the Name.

nīku sāṭilēdani sārē sārēku vēḍiti
Again and again I prayed, saying there is none equal to You.

dikku nīvani nammiti
I trusted that You alone are my support.
“dikku” = refuge.

nī pādamula makkuva goni mrokkiti
With love for Your feet, I bowed to them.

cikkulu peṭṭaku śrī rāmadāsuni
Do not trouble this Ramadas any further.
The voice of a devotee melting in the pain of prison.

cakkagā brōvavē cakkani jānaki
O beautiful Janaki's Rama! Protect me well!

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas:
asks Rama for compassion,
reveals the glory of Rama's name,
surrenders completely, saying “You alone are my refuge”,
and prays to Rama, who never abandons His devotee.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The bond between devotee and God
Ramadas speaks to Rama
not only with reverence
but also with the intimacy of one who belongs.

2. The glory of the Name
“rāmakōṭi vrāsiti”
Rama's name
purifies the mind
and stands by us in suffering.

3. Bhakta parādhīna
This is a very high idea of devotion:
the Lord is bound by His devotee's love.

4. Devotion even in the pain of prison
Ramadas
did not lose faith in hardship;
he turned his suffering itself into prayer.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Even when the world abandoned him, Ramadas did not let go of the One — Rama.
That is why his weeping and his faith are blended together:
‘rāmā daya jūḍavē… You alone are my refuge…’”

Summary meaning
O Rama, please show your compassion. O Lord of Bhadrachalam, protect me. O Rama of Sita, O auspicious Rama, O Kodanda Rama, O crowned king Rama—protect me. You are moved by your devotees’ love and free them from suffering. You are the king of kings, destroyer of demons. You alone are my refuge. Please protect your devotee Ramadasu

@@ 6.2 | “rāmajōgi mandu gonarē” – Raga Khamas |
This kirtanam is a very sweet song of teaching.
Here Bhadrachala Ramadas calls Rama's name a “medicine”.
“rāma jōgi mandu” = the medicine of Rama's name.
This medicine is
not for the body,
but for the mind and the soul.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
rāmajōgi mandu gonarē pāmarulāra
O simple people! Take the medicine of Rama-jogi!
“mandu” = medicine.
Here
Rama's name is called a medicine.

rāmajōgi mandu mīru prēmatō bhujiñcinapuḍē
Take that medicine of Rama's name with love.
Mere utterance is not enough;
remembrance of the Name must be with love.

Charanam 1
kāma krōdha lōbhamulanu kaḍaku pāradōlē mandu
A medicine that uproots desire, anger and greed.
The real diseases of man are
desire,
anger,
greed.
Rama's name is
the medicine that purifies the mind.

kāṭuka koṇḍala vaṇṭi karmamuleḍa-bāpē mandu
A medicine that dissolves even mountains of karma.
However great the sin,
remembrance of the Name can dissolve it.

Charanam 2
sāṭilēdu jagamulandu svāmi rāmajōgi mandu
There is no equal to this medicine in the world.
Rama's name is
simple,
yet of boundless power.

vēṣadhāri māyalanē vedala vadaliñcēdi mandu
A medicine that removes false outward guises and illusions.
Devotion is
not for outward show,
but for inner purity.

pāṣaṇḍa durmārgulanu pāvanula jēyiñcē mandu
A medicine that purifies even those on the wrong path.
The Name
has the power to transform anyone.

Charanam 3
mudamutō bhadrādriyandu muktini pondiñcē mandu
A medicine that joyfully grants liberation at Bhadrachalam.
A remembrance of the Bhadrachalam temple.

sadayuḍaina rāmadāsu sadbhaktitō golicē mandu
The medicine that the compassionate Ramadas took with true devotion.
He
experienced this medicine himself
and offers it to others too.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas:
calls Rama's name a spiritual medicine,
says it removes the diseases of the mind — desire, anger and karma,
and teaches that remembering the Name with love brings liberation.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The Name is medicine
We look for medicine for the body.
But
stress,
anger,
greed
also need medicine.
Ramadas says:
“Rama's name is that medicine.”

2. Chanting with love
“prēmatō bhujiñcinapuḍē”
Not mechanical chanting,
but remembrance of the Name joined with love.

3. Inner purification
This song is
not about outward ritual,
but about inner change.

4. The Name is for everyone
“pāmarulāra”
Devotion is
not only for scholars,
but for everyone.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Man searches the world for many medicines.
But what is the medicine for peace of mind?
Ramadas smiles and says —
‘rāmajōgi mandu gonarē!’
That medicine is not found in a shop… it is found in remembering the Name.”

Summary meaning
Ramadasu says that the greatest “medicine” for human suffering is the devotion of a Rama-yogi. When a person lovingly accepts this path, it destroys desire, anger and greed, removes heavy karmas, drives away false and deceitful people, transforms the wicked into pure souls, and finally grants liberation. He concludes that this is the sacred medicine he offers with pure devotion.

@@ 6.3 | “hari hari rāma” – Raga Kanada |
This kirtanam is a deep prayer of surrender.
Here Bhadrachala Ramadas melts before Rama and says:
“Rama! Do not forget me!”
This song beautifully expresses:
the glory of remembering the Name,
Rama's compassion,
the devotee's fear,
and divine protection.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
hari hari rāma nannaramara jūḍaku
Hari! Hari! Rama! Do not forget me!
“jūḍaku” = do not leave me unnoticed.
This is
a call that mixes the devotee's fear
with the right of one who belongs.

niratamu nī nāma smaraṇayē maranu
I will never forget to remember Your name.
The devotee's resolve:
“I will not forget You;
do not You forget me.”

Charanam 1
daśaratha nandana
O son of Dasharatha!

daśamukha mardana
O slayer of ten-headed Ravana!
He destroys both the Ravana outside
and the Ravana of ego within.

paśupati rañjana
O You who are dear to Shiva!
The unity of Hari and Hara.

pāpa vimōcana
The remover of sins.

Charanam 2
maṇimaya bhūṣaṇa
O You adorned with jewels!

mañjuḷa bhāṣaṇa
O You of sweet speech!
Rama's
gentleness
and soft nature.

raṇa jaya bhīṣaṇa
The mighty hero who brings victory in battle.

raghukula pōṣaṇa
Who protected and nurtured the Raghu clan.

Last charanam
patita pāvana nāma
One whose name sanctifies even the fallen.
The glory of the name “Rama”.

bhadrācaladhāma
O Rama who shines at Bhadrachalam!
A remembrance of the Bhadrachalam temple.

satatamu śrī rāmadāsu nannēlumā
Always protect and guide this Ramadas!
“nannēlumā”:
support me,
guide me,
do not abandon me.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas
stays firm in Rama's name,
prays to Rama not to forget him,
and praises Rama as
the remover of sins,
the protector of devotees,
the embodiment of compassion.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. “I will not forget You; do not You forget me”
This line is the heart of devotion.

2. The power of remembering the Name
“niratamu nī nāma smaraṇayē”
Constant chanting of the Name
steadies the mind
and removes fear.

3. Rama — gentleness and valour
In this song
sweet speech
and heroic battle
come together.

4. Patita pāvana
Rama is
not for perfect people,
but for people with faults.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“What is a devotee's greatest fear?
The fear: ‘Has God forgotten me?’
That is why Ramadas calls out like a child —
‘hari hari rāmā… nannaramara jūḍaku!’”

Summary meaning
In this song Ramadas cries out to Rama, begging Him to look at him with compassion. He admits that he forgets Rama’s name constantly, even though the Name itself purifies the fallen. He praises Rama as the son of Dasharatha, the destroyer of Ravana, the delight of Shiva, the remover of sins, the sweet-speaking, gem-adorned warrior who protects the Raghu dynasty. Finally, Ramadas pleads that the Lord of Bhadrachalam must always protect His devotee.

@@ 6.4 | “śrī rāmula divyanāma” – Raga Saveri |
This kirtanam is a very deep teaching on devotion.
Here Bhadrachala Ramadas tells a great truth:
“Great sacrifices and hard penances are not necessary.
True remembrance of Rama's name and a good heart are enough.”
This song very simply explains:
the simple path of devotion,
the glory of the Name,
service to the Lord's devotees,
a life of dharma.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
śrī rāmula divyanāma smaraṇa jēyucunna cālu
Just remembering the divine name of Sri Rama is enough.
“cālu” = enough.

ghōramaina tapamulanu kōra nēṭikē manasā
O mind, why do you seek hard penances?
Devotion is
simple,
and possible for everyone.

Charanam 1
tāraka śrī rāma nāma dhyānamu jēsina cālu
Meditating on Rama's name, which grants liberation, is enough.
“tāraka nāma”:
the name that carries us across the ocean of births.

vēru vēru daivamulanu veduka nēṭikē manasā
O mind, why wander searching for many gods?
The mind
wanders restlessly.
The saint says:
“Settle in one Name.”

Charanam 2
bhāgavatula pāda jalamu paina callukonna cālu
Just sprinkling on your head the water that has washed the feet of the Lord's devotees is enough.
The glory of serving the Lord's devotees.

bhāgīrathiki pōvalanucu bhrānti yēṭikē
Why the confusion about having to go to the Ganga?
A reference to the river Ganga.
True holiness lies
in the company of devotees.

bhāgavatula vāgamṛtamu pānamu jēsina cālu
Just drinking in the nectar-like words of the Lord's devotees is enough.

bāgu mīrinaṭṭi amṛta pāna mēṭikē manasā
O mind, what nectar could be greater than that?
The glory of satsang.

Charanam 3
parula hiṁsa sēyakunna parama dharmamantē cālu
Not causing harm to others is the highest dharma — that is enough.
A very simple yet lofty teaching on dharma.

parulanu rakṣintunani palkanēṭikē
Why boast, “I will save the world”?
Dharma without ego.

dorakoni parula dhanamu dōcaka yuṇḍitē cālu
Not stealing others' wealth is enough.

gurutugānu gōpuramu kaṭṭanēṭikē manasā
O mind, must one build a great temple tower for that?
More than outward grandeur,
honesty matters.

Charanam 4
atithi vacci yākalanna anna minta iḍina cālu
Giving a little food to a hungry guest is enough.
The glory of feeding others.

kratuvu sēya valayu nanē kāṅkṣa yēṭikē
Why the wish to perform great sacrifices?
Simple kindness is itself the great sacrifice.

Last charanam
satatamu mā bhadragiri svāmi rāmadāsuḍaina
I am Ramadas, who always remembers Rama of Bhadragiri.
A reference to the Bhadrachalam temple.

itara matamulani yēṭi vetala nēṭikē manasā
O mind, why wander on other paths?
Steadiness of mind.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas
says that Rama's name is enough.
More than hard penances,
remembering the Name,
serving the Lord's devotees,
feeding the hungry,
and non-violence
are what matter, he says.
He teaches that simple devotion is the highest path.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The simple path of devotion
The heart of this song:
“cālu” — “enough.”
The Lord
does not ask for hard practices;
He wants only a sincere heart.

2. Remembering the Name = the saving mantra
Rama's name gives
purity of mind,
freedom from fear,
and liberation.

3. The glory of satsang
“bhāgavatula vāgamṛtam”
The devotees'
words,
company
and service
are all spiritual nectar.

4. True dharma
The great teaching Ramadas gives:
“Do not cause harm to others.”
That itself is the supreme dharma.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Man seeks great sacrifices, hard penances and rare achievements.
But Ramadas gently says —
‘Remembering Rama's name is enough…
giving a handful of food to the hungry is enough…
living with a good heart is enough…’
That is the true path of devotion.”

Summary meaning
In this kriti, Ramadas teaches his mind the essence of spirituality:
You don’t need severe penances, pilgrimages, yajnas, or running behind many gods. The simple and powerful remembrance of Rama’s name is enough to purify and uplift. The grace of true devotees, their words, and their presence are more powerful than Ganga or heavenly nectar. Not harming others and living with kindness is the highest dharma. Feeding the hungry is greater than building temples for show. He concludes that one must remain a humble servant of Lord Rama and need not chase other complicated paths.

@@ 6.5 | “palukē baṅgāra māyenā” – Raga Ananda Bhairavi |
This kirtanam is a deeply moving cry of devotion.
Here Bhadrachala Ramadas grieves before Rama:
“I keep calling You…
why do You not answer?”
This song expresses:
the devotee's longing,
the love that speaks to God with the right of one who belongs,
the glory of remembering the Name,
and a heart waiting for compassion.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
palukē baṅgāra māyenā kōdaṇḍapāṇi
O Rama, bearer of the Kodanda bow! Have Your words turned to gold?
“Is it so rare for You to speak?”
A playful complaint, full of the right of love.

palukē baṅgāramāyē pilacina palukavēmi
When I call, why do You not answer?
The devotee's longing:
“I am calling You;
why are You silent?”

Charanam 1
kalalō nī nāmasmaraṇa marava cakkani taṇḍri
O sweet Father, I do not forget Your name even in my dreams!
“taṇḍri” = father.
For Ramadas,
Rama is not only God
but Father.

iravuga isukalōna poralina uḍuta bhaktiki
To the devotee who rolled weeping in the sand —
the devotee's suffering and humility.

karuṇiñci brōcitivani neranammitini taṇḍri
I firmly trusted that You would protect me with compassion.
Complete faith.

Charanam 2
rāti nātigā jēsi bhūtalamuna prakhyāti jenditivani
O You who became famous on earth for turning a stone into a woman!
This refers to the liberation of Ahalya.

prītitō nammiti taṇḍri
With love I trusted You, Father!

Charanam 3
enta vēḍina nīku suntaina daya rādu
However much I plead, does not even a little compassion come to You?
This is not anger —
it is the right of love.

pantamu sēya nēnentaṭi vāḍanu taṇḍri
Who am I to be stubborn with You, Father?
In the end
the ego melts,
and only surrender remains.

Last charanam
śaraṇāgata trāṇa birudāṅkituḍavu gāvā
Are You not famed as the protector of those who surrender?
Rama's glory:
protecting those who take refuge.

karuṇiñcu bhadrācala vara rāmadāsa pōṣa
O Rama of Bhadrachalam, sustainer of Ramadas! Be compassionate!
A reference to the Bhadrachalam temple.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas
calls to Rama with the right of one who belongs,
asks, “I have not forgotten You; why do You not answer?”
and finally surrenders and prays for compassion.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The right of love in devotion
This song is
not devotion of fear,
but devotion of love.
The devotee
complains even to God with affection.

2. The state of remembering the Name
“Not forgetting the Name even in dreams”
This is
not ordinary chanting,
but devotion that has become one's very breath.

3. The liberation of Ahalya
“He who turned a stone into a woman”
That is,
compassion that melts even a heart of stone.

4. The peak of surrender
In the end:
“Who am I to quarrel with You?”
There the ego melts.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“A true devotee sometimes weeps before God… sometimes complains…
but never leaves Him.
That is Ramadas's state.
That is why he asks, melting —
‘palukē baṅgāramāyēnā…?’
‘Can You not speak even one word, Rama?’”

Summary meaning
In this kriti, Rāmadāsa cries emotionally to Lord Rama. He says, “O Kodandapani, if Your words can transform things into gold, why won’t my words change my fate?” He prays that even in dreams he should never forget Rama’s name. He describes the suffering of a devotee rolling in sand at midnight and says he fully believes the Lord will protect him. He begs for compassion, wonders why despite all his pleading the Lord’s grace doesn’t come quickly, and humbly admits he has no right to argue. He reminds Rama that He is the protector of those who surrender, and finally prays for mercy and protection of Rāmadāsa.

@@ 6.6 | “ētīruga nanu” – Raga Nata Namakriya |
This kirtanam is a deeply moving song of surrender.
Here Bhadrachala Ramadas asks Rama:
“Rama! How will You save me?”
This question comes
not from doubt,
but from longing filled with complete faith.
In this song
the suffering of samsara,
his own sense of guilt,
the prayer for compassion,
and surrender
are all beautifully expressed.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
ētīruga nanu daya jūcedavō inavaṁśōttama rāma
O noble Rama, born of the solar dynasty! In what way will You show me compassion?
“ētīruga” = in what way?
This is
the cry of a devotee longing for help.

nā taramā bhavasāgara mīdanu naḷina daḷēkṣaṇa rāma
O lotus-eyed Rama! Can I cross this ocean of samsara on my own?
“bhavasāgaram”:
the ocean of life, full of
births,
sorrow,
and confusion of mind.

Charanam 1
śrī raghunandana sītāramaṇa
O joy of the Raghu clan! O Lord of Sita!

śritajana pōṣaka rāma
O Rama, who protects those who surrender to You!
The devotee thinks
not of his own worthiness,
but of Rama's nature.

kāruṇyālaya bhakta varada
O abode of compassion! O giver of boons to devotees!

ninu kannadi kānupu rāma
Other than You I see no one, Rama!
Complete surrender.

Charanam 2
muripemutō nā svāmiyu nīvani munduga delpiti rāma
With love I declared long ago, “You alone are my Lord,” Rama!
The devotee's firm resolve.

maravaka ikanabhimāna-muñcu nī marugu joccitini rāma
Not forgetting that love, I have come to Your feet, Rama!
The devotee
leaves the world
and turns toward God.

Charanam 3
krūra karmamulu nēraka jēsiti nēramulencaku rāma
Unknowingly I did cruel deeds; do not count my faults, Rama!
A person's honest confession.

dāridryamu parihāramu cēyavē daiva śikhāmaṇī rāma
O Rama, crest-jewel among gods! Remove my poverty!
This is
not only material poverty,
but spiritual emptiness too.

Last charanam
vāsavanuta rāmadāsa pōṣaka vandanam ayōdhyā rāma
O Rama of Ayodhya, praised even by Indra! Sustainer of Ramadas! Salutations to You.

dāsārcita mākabhaya-mosaṅgavē dāśarathī raghurāma
O Raghurama, son of Dasharatha, worshipped by Your servants! Grant us fearlessness!
“abhayam”:
freedom from fear,
spiritual protection.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas
says he cannot cross the ocean of samsara alone,
surrenders completely to Rama,
confesses his faults,
and finally prays for fearlessness.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The ocean of samsara
This life is an ocean full of
desire,
fear,
sorrow,
ego.
The devotee says:
“I cannot cross it alone.”

2. Surrender = true humility
“nēramulencaku”
A true devotee
does not hide his faults.

3. God alone is the refuge
“ninu kannadi kānupu”
This is
single-minded devotion,
surrender to Him alone.

4. Spiritual poverty
“dāridryam”
Man's greatest poverty
is not lack of money,
but lack of remembrance of God.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In the ocean of life, man tries to swim in many directions.
But one day he grows tired.
Only then does he realise —
‘I cannot cross this ocean… Rama alone must save me.’
That cry of complete surrender is —
‘ētīruga nanu daya jūcedavō rāmā…’”

Summary meaning
In this kriti, Rāmadāsa prays deeply to Rama, asking if the Lord will show compassion when he approaches. He wonders whether he can ever cross the ocean of worldly life without Rama’s help. He praises Rama as the protector of devotees and laments that he is not able to see His presence. He confesses his past mistakes, asks Rama not to count them as sins, and begs for relief from poverty and suffering. He offers salutations to Ayodhya Rama, praised by Indra, and ends by asking the Lord to remove all his fears and protect him as always.

@@ 6.7 | “rāmakṛṣṇa gōvinda” – Raga Sindhu Bhairavi |
This whole kirtanam is the rapture of nama sankirtanam.
Here Bhadrachala Ramadas sings many divine names —
Rama,
Krishna,
Narayana —
as one flowing stream of joy.
The heart of this song:
“The Name is my refuge.
The Name is the Lord's presence.”
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
rāmakṛṣṇa gōvinda nārāyaṇa
Rama! Krishna! Govinda! Narayana!
Many names —
one Supreme.

harē rāmakṛṣṇa gōvinda nārāyaṇa
O Hari! Rama! Krishna! Govinda! Narayana!
This is
chanting,
sankirtanam,
a stream of joy.

Charanam 1
kṛṣṇarāma gōvinda nārāyaṇa
Krishna! Rama! Govinda! Narayana!
Chanting the names in turn
settles the mind in thought of God.

rāmakṛṣṇāyani prēmatō pilicitē
When I call “Rama! Krishna!” with love,

mōmaina jūpavēmi nārāyaṇa
why do You not show me Your face, Narayana?
The devotee's longing:
“I call You with love;
why do You not appear?”

Charanam 2
aṇḍaja vāhana
O You who ride on Garuda!

puṇḍarīkākṣa
O lotus-eyed one!

nī daṇḍa jērinānayya nārāyaṇa
I have reached Your feet, Narayana!
“daṇḍa jērinānu”:
I have come to Your refuge.

Charanam 3
mādhava viṣṇu madhusūdana śrīdhara
Madhava! Vishnu! Madhusudana! Sridhara!
Each name is
a divine deed,
a truth.

śēṣa śayana śrīman nārāyaṇa
O Sriman Narayana, reclining on Adishesha!

Charanam 4
vāsudēva mukunda vanamālī cakradhara
Vasudeva! Mukunda! Wearer of the forest garland! Bearer of the discus!

narasiṁhācyuta nārāyaṇa
Narasimha! Achyuta! Narayana!
All the incarnations are
the one truth of Narayana.

Last charanam
rāmadāsuni brōva prēmatō
To protect Ramadas with love,

bhadrācala dhāmuḍavaina śrīman nārāyaṇa
O Sriman Narayana, who shines at Bhadrachalam!

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas
sings many divine names in rapture,
longs for the Lord to give darshan when the Name is spoken with love,
and finally surrenders to Narayana.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The joy of nama sankirtanam
This song is
not only music,
but the experience of the sweetness of the Name.

2. Many names — one Supreme
Rama,
Krishna,
Narayana,
Madhava…
The names are many;
God is one.

3. When we call with love
“prēmatō pilicitē”
The Lord must be called
not out of fear,
but out of love.

4. Surrender
“nī daṇḍa jērinānayya”
In the end,
a person must reach not the world
but the feet of God.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Sometimes a devotee needs no philosophy…
one Name is enough.
And sometimes even one Name is not enough —
‘Rama… Krishna… Govinda… Narayana…’ — the Name itself becomes a flood of joy.
That rapture of the Name is this kirtanam.”

Summary meaning
This kriti is a beautiful nāmavali where Bhadrāchala Rāmadāsa calls out to the Lord using many sacred names: Rama, Krishna, Govinda, Narayana. He pleads that when a devotee calls with love, the Lord should immediately show compassion. Rāmadāsa then surrenders completely to the lotus-eyed Lord who rides Garuda. He praises the Lord with many divine names—Madhava, Vishnu, Sridhara, Narasimha, Acyuta—and finally says, “Come with love and protect Your servant Rāmadāsa, O Bhadrachala Narayana!”

@@ 6.8 | “gōpikā ramaṇa” – Raga Shankarabharanam |
This kirtanam is an auspicious garland of divine names.
Here Bhadrachala Ramadas praises Krishna and Rama together.
The heart of this song:
“O One Supreme, shining in all the incarnations! Protect me.”
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
gōpikā ramaṇa
O beloved of the gopis!
A remembrance of the gopis —
the love between the Supreme Self and the individual soul.

gōkulōtsava
O festival of joy for Gokula!
Wherever Krishna is, there is celebration.

gōvinda jaya pāhi mām
O victorious Govinda! Protect me.

Charanam 1

O son of Nandagopa!

indu vadana sundara

pāhi mām

Charanam 3

dīna mandāra

Charanam 5

O Krishna, born of the lunar dynasty!

kundaradana

Last charanam
bhadra guṇa

subhadrā pūrvaja
O elder brother of Subhadra!

bhadrādrīśvara
O Lord of Bhadradri!

pāhi mām
Protect me!

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas sings together
Krishna's beauty,
Rama's compassion,

In the end he surrenders, saying “pāhi mām”.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The oneness of the incarnations
In this song

2. The Lord = a festival
“gōkulōtsava”
Where the Lord is
is not only a temple,

3. Pāhi mām
Again and again:

This is the heart of devotion.

4. Beauty and compassion
In this kirtanam
beauty,

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“God does not come to a devotee in only one form.
Sometimes as Rama,
sometimes as Krishna,
sometimes as Govinda.
But the devotee's prayer is always the same —

@@ 6.9 | “murahara nagadhara” – Raga Senchurutti |
This kirtanam is arranged like a great namavali (garland of names).
Here Bhadrachala Ramadas praises many divine forms together —
Krishna,
Rama,
Narayana.
This whole song is full of
the sweetness of the Name,
the glory of the incarnations,
the Lord's love for devotees,
and the beauty of the Supreme.

Pallavi

“nagadhara” → Krishna who held up Govardhana hill.
Many divine deeds — one Supreme.

Charanam 1

Who protects devotees with love.

This song is

Rama,
Krishna,
Narayana —

4. Vedanta and devotion
“antaryāmin”
“saccidānanda”
Here
devotion
and Vedanta
