A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“For a true devotee, God is not in only one name.
Every divine deed he remembers becomes a name.
So Ramadas calls out in rapture —
‘Govinda… Mukunda… Madhava… Rama…’
In the end all the names merge into the one Supreme.”

Summary meaning
This song is a beautiful garland of names of the Lord, describing His qualities, feats, beauty, compassion, and cosmic nature. Each line praises a different aspect — from Rāma, Krishna, Govinda, Mukunda — all merged into one Supreme Being who protects devotees and destroys evil.

@@ 6.10 | “pāvana rāma nāma” – Raga Kapi |
This whole kirtanam is one longing.
Bhadrachala Ramadas asks again and again:
“When will that spiritual state come to me?”
This song expresses a deep longing for
the sweetness of Rama's name,
service to the Lord,
purity of mind,
and steady devotion.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
pāvana rāma nāma sudhārasa pānamu jēsēdennaṭikō
When shall I drink the nectar of the holy name of Rama?
“sudhā rasam”:
bliss like nectar.
Rama's name is
not a word,
but an experience.

Charanam 1
sēviñciyu śrī hari padāmbulu cittamuñcē dennaṭikō
When will I be able to fix my mind at the holy feet of Sri Hari?
A longing for steadiness of mind.

Charanam 2
dāsula gani santōṣamuna tava dāsōhamanu dennaṭikō
When will I be able to rejoice on seeing the devotees and say, “I am Your servant”?
True devotion:
humility,
love for the Lord's devotees.

Charanam 3
bhūsutakunu atiprāṇa prathamam puruṣōttamu ganu dennaṭikō
When will I realise You as the Supreme Person, dearest to the daughter of the Earth (Sita)?

Charanam 4
cañcala guṇamula māni
Giving up restless qualities,

sadā niścalamati nuṇḍē dennaṭikō
when will I always remain with a steady mind?
The great challenge of devotion:
the wandering mind.

Charanam 5
pañca tatvamula tāraka nāmamu paṭhiyiñcuṭa nā kennaṭikō
When will I fully chant the saving Name that carries us beyond this world of five elements?
“tāraka nāma”:
the name that carries us across the ocean of births.

Charanam 6
vañcana lēkanu bhadrādrīśuni varṇana jēsē dennaṭikō
When will I sing of the Lord of Bhadradri without any deceit?
True devotion is
not outward drama,
but honesty of heart.

Last charanam
añcitamugā rāmadāsuḍa nanukoni ānandiñcē dennaṭikō
When will I truly feel “I am Ramadas” and rejoice?
This is
the peak of devotion,
the bliss of the servant's attitude.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas
longs to experience the sweetness of Rama's name,
prays for steadiness of mind,
wishes to remain in the attitude of a servant,
and longs for the day when true devotion will come.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Devotion = experience
Rama's name is
not mere chanting,
but “sudhā rasam”.

2. The state of mind
“niścalamati”
The great problem of today's man:
a restless mind.
Devotion
steadies the mind.

3. The servant's attitude
“tava dāsōham”
True bliss is
the feeling “I belong to God”.

4. True devotion
“vañcana lēkanu”
Devotion done not for show
but with honesty of heart is the highest.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Here Ramadas does not ask God for wealth… he does not ask for fame…
he asks for only one thing —
‘When will the day come when I truly taste the sweetness of Rama's name?’
That very longing made him Ramadas.”

Summary meaning
In this song, Ramadasa says:
“How blessed I am to drink the nectar of Rama’s sacred name! How wonderful to serve Hari’s feet, to call myself His servant, and to keep my mind steady! Chanting the Taraka Mantra, speaking of the Bhadrachala Lord without deceit, and surrendering fully as Rama Dasa — all of this fills my life with indescribable joy.”

@@ 6.11 | “gōvinda rāma harē” – Raga Kanada |
This kirtanam is a joyful nama sankirtanam.
Here Bhadrachala Ramadas sings together many divine forms —
Rama,
Krishna,
Gopala Krishna.
The heart of this song:
“Rama and Krishna are not different —
they are many divine deeds of the one compassionate Lord.”
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
gōvinda rāma harē jaya jaya
Govinda! Rama! Hari! Victory! Victory!

gōpālakṛṣṇa harē jaya jaya
Gopala Krishna! Hari! Victory! Victory!
This is
nama sankirtanam,
a flood of the joy of devotion.

Charanam 1
sākēta nāyaka
O Lord of Ayodhya!

nāgēśa sannuta
O You praised by Shiva!

rākēndu bimbānana
O Rama with a face like the full moon!

Charanam 2
dēvakī nandana
O son of Devaki!

tāpasa rakṣaka
Protector of the sages.
Rama and Krishna are both
protectors of dharma.

bhāvaja kōṭi sama
More beautiful than a crore of Manmathas.

Charanam 3
gautama sannuta
Praised by Gautama.

sītāmanōhara
Who captivated Sita's heart.

tāta nidēśakara
Who honoured His father's command.
Rama's steadfastness in dharma.

Charanam 4
pūtanā māraṇa
O Krishna who destroyed Putana!

nūtana dadhi navanīta kṛtavadana
O child Krishna, face smeared all over with fresh curd and butter!
The sweetness of parental love.

Charanam 5
citrakūṭācala bhadra gṛhālaya
O You who shine at Chitrakuta and at Bhadragiri!

vṛtra mahita guṇa
Whose qualities are praised even by the gods.

Charanam 6
bṛndāvanāntara sundara rūpa
O Krishna of beautiful form, shining in Vrindavan!

sanandana nandanuta
Praised by Sanaka and the other sages.

Last charanam
bhadrānvita
Full of auspicious qualities.

balabhadrānuja kṛṣṇa
O Krishna, younger brother of Balarama!

bhadrādri raghu puṅgava harē rāma
O Rama, lion of the Raghu clan, shining at Bhadradri!

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas sings together
Rama's dharma,
Krishna's play,
and Narayana's supremacy,
and becomes immersed in the joy of nama sankirtanam.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The oneness of Rama and Krishna
This song says:
“The incarnations are many; the Supreme is one.”

2. Many flavours in devotion
Rama → dharma.
Krishna → divine play.
Both → compassion.

3. Nama sankirtanam
The repeated “jaya jaya”
uplifts the mind
and awakens devotion.

4. Parental love and Vedanta
On one side:
Krishna who steals butter.
On the other:
the Supreme praised by the sages.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“God sometimes comes to the devotee as Rama of dharma…
sometimes as the child Krishna stealing butter…
but in whatever form He comes, the devotee sings only one thing —
‘gōvinda rāma harē jaya jaya!’”

Summary meaning
In this kirtan, Ramadasa glorifies Lord Rama and Lord Krishna in all their forms and pastimes:
• As Govinda and protector of cows, as beloved of Sita, as son of Devaki, as the butter-stealing child, and as the mighty hero destroying demons.
• He recalls the Lord in Vrindavan, in Chitrakuta, and in Bhadrachala, highlighting His divine leelas, protection, and accessibility to devotees.
• The song is a vivid celebration of both Rama and Krishna, merging their divine stories into one continuous devotion.

@@ 6.12 | “kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa” – Raga Yadukula Kambhoji |
This whole kirtanam is full of
nama sankirtanam of Krishna,
the experience of His form,
the sweetness of His play,
and the rapture of devotion.
Here Bhadrachala Ramadas sings with great joy of
Krishna's beauty,
compassion,
valour,
and divine play.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa gōvinda kṛṣṇa
Krishna! Krishna! Govinda! Krishna!

gōvinda gōvinda gōpālakṛṣṇa
Govinda! Govinda! Gopala Krishna!
This is
not an ordinary song,
but the rapture of chanting the Name.

Charanam 1
rājagōpāla
O king of the cowherds!

ramaṇīya vēṣa
O You of most beautiful appearance!

rādhā vallabha
O beloved of Radha!

kamalāvallabha

kamalāyatākṣa

Charanam 4
gajarāja rakṣaka

gambhīra hṛdaya
With a heart deep in compassion.

gati jita gajarāja

Charanam 5
candana carcita cārukaḷēbara
O You whose beautiful body is anointed with sandal paste!

campaka nāsika
With a nose beautiful as the champaka flower.
Devotion that savours His form.

Charanam 6
rāsakrīḍā ramaṇī vallabha
The beloved of the gopis in the Rasa play.
Krishna's Rasa Lila.

ratipati janaka
Whose beauty is the very source of Manmatha.

Last charanam
bhavya guṇālaya
A temple of noble qualities.

bhava duḥkha hara
Who removes the sorrow of samsara.

bhadrādrivāsa gōpālakṛṣṇa
O Gopala Krishna, shining at Bhadradri!

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas sings, like a namavali,
Krishna's beauty,
divine play,
compassion,
valour,
and His nature as protector of devotees.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The joy of nama sankirtanam
Saying “kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa gōvinda” again and again
purifies the mind
and awakens devotion.

2. Devotion that savours His form
In this song
the eyes,
the crown,
the sandal paste,
the divine body
are all savoured.
The devotee experiences God as beauty.

3. Compassion and valour
He destroys the demons,
and at the same time saves Gajendra.

4. Relief from the sorrow of samsara
“bhava duḥkha hara”
The Lord's name
dissolves the pain of the mind.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Though the Vedas speak of Krishna as philosophy,
Ramadas sees Him as an experience full of love.
So he does not speak philosophy…
in rapture he just keeps saying the names —
‘Krishna… Govinda… Gopala Krishna…!’”

Summary meaning
This kirtan glorifies Krishna in multiple aspects:
• As Govinda, protector of cows, the beloved of Radha, with lotus- like eyes, and the vanquisher of Kamsa.
• As the divine one who receives countless offerings, destroys demons, and even subdues mighty elephants.
• His beauty, grace, divine dance (Rasa), and love for the gopis are celebrated.
• He is the remover of worldly sorrows and resides in Bhadrachala, symbolizing divine presence and protection for devotees.

@@ 6.13 | “nārāyaṇa harē gōvinda” – Raga Ananda Bhairavi |
This whole kirtanam is complete surrender in the words
“harē! mām pāhi!”
— “Protect me!”
Here Bhadrachala Ramadas sings many forms together —
Narayana,
Rama,
Krishna —
and experiences the Lord's
compassion,
valour,
love for devotees,
and beauty.

the devotee's complete surrender.

Charanam 1
ālōla tuḷasī vanamāla

Charanam 6

kamanīya
Most beautiful.

O lotus-eyed one, sung by Your devotees!

Last charanam
bhaktajana hṛdaya nivāsa
Who dwells in the hearts of devotees.
A line where Vedanta and devotion meet.

bharatāgraja
Elder brother of Bharata.

bhadrācala vāsa
O Rama who lives at Bhadrachalam!

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas sings of
Narayana's many divine forms,
the compassion that protects devotees,
and the glory of the incarnations,
and surrenders, saying “protect me”.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The attitude of surrender
In every line:
“mām pāhi”
This is
the inner voice of the devotee.

2. The Lord = protector of devotees
He protects Prahlada.
He raises up Vibhishana.

3. The secret of the incarnation
“kāraṇa mānuṣa”
Though the Lord comes as a man,
His purpose is liberation.

4. The dweller in the devotee's heart
Not only the temple —
the devotee's heart is His home.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Here Ramadas does not call the Lord as just a deity…
he calls Him as the one support of life.
So in every line, in a voice mixed with tears, he says —
‘harē… mām pāhi… protect me!’”

@@ 6.14 | “rāma rārā” – Raga Khamas |
This whole kirtanam is one melting call:
“Rama! Come! Come to save me!”
Here Bhadrachala Ramadas
recalls the deeds of Rama's life
and calls that same Rama to come into his own life.
This song holds
the main events of the Ramayana,
the devotee's surrender,
and the feeling: “You helped everyone; You must help me too.”
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
rāma rārā sītārāma rārā
Rama! Sita-Rama! Come! Come!

rāma rārā nannu brōva rārā
Come to save me!
This is
not just a kirtanam,
but the devotee's tearful cry.

Charanam 1
rāma rāma ninu cāla rājulandaru piluva vacciri
Rama! Many great kings called You, and You went.
“You went to everyone —
will You not come to me too?”

kṣēma mosaga bhadra śaila dhāmuḍavai velasina taṇḍri
O Father, who shines at Bhadrachalam to grant welfare to devotees!

Charanam 2
muni yāgamu gāci
Who protected the sacrifice of the sages.

śilanu maguva jēsi
Who turned Ahalya, who had become stone, back into a woman.
Rama is
the one who lifts curses.

janakuniṇṭa śivuni villu viraci
Who broke Shiva's bow in Janaka's house,

sītanu cērukonna svāmi
and so won Sita as His bride.

Charanam 3
tapasi vēṣamunu dharinci
Who took the guise of an ascetic,

taṇḍri koraku vanamunakēgi
and went to the forest for His father's word.
Rama's steadfastness in dharma.

tapana sutuni gāci
Who protected Sugriva, son of the Sun.

udadhi dāṭi rāvaṇuni druñcina
Who crossed the ocean and destroyed Ravana.

Last charanam
laṅka vibhīṣaṇuni kosagi
Who gave Lanka to Vibhishana,

lalana gūḍi ayōdhyanu śaṅkalēka nēlukonna
and ruled Ayodhya together with Sita, free of all fear.

dayatō bhadrācala nivāsa
O Rama who lives at Bhadrachalam with compassion!

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas
recalls the deeds of the Ramayana,
tells how Rama protected many,
and finally calls:
“You who protected everyone
must come to protect me too.”

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The Ramayana = a story of compassion
He raised Ahalya.
He protected the sages.
He accepted Vibhishana.
Rama came not to punish —
but to save.

2. Surrender
“rārā”
This one word
is the devotee's whole longing.

3. Dharma and compassion
Rama
protects dharma,
and at the same time is the form of compassion.

4. Where the Lord must come
For Ramadas,
it is not only Ayodhya —
the devotee's heart is the true Bhadrachalam.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Ramadas does not call Rama merely as a hero of history…
he calls: ‘O You who raised Ahalya… O You who accepted Vibhishana…
come once to me too and bless me!’
That melting cry is —
‘rāma rārā!’”

Summary meaning
This song is a heartfelt plea to Lord Rama to come quickly. Ramadasa recalls all the major events of Rama’s life: kings summoning him, crossing the ocean, protecting Sita, defeating Ravana, and returning to Ayodhya. It reflects devotion, awe, and the bhava of longing to experience Rama’s divine presence.

@@ 6.15 | “ālōla tuḷasi” – Raga Bhairavi |
This kirtanam beautifully unites
nama sankirtanam,
devotion to Rama,
the glory of Vishnu,
and the devotee's surrender.
Here Bhadrachala Ramadas experiences
Rama,
Narayana
and Krishna
as one Supreme.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
ālōla tuḷasi vanamāla bhūṣaṇa
O You who wear a swaying garland of tulasi!
Tulasi is
the emblem of devotion to Vishnu.

śrī rāma rāma harē
Sri Rama! Hari!
The rapture of nama sankirtanam.

Charanam 1
śrīman raghu rāma
O auspicious Rama of the Raghu clan!

śrīman nārāyaṇa kṛṣṇa gōvinda
Narayana! Krishna! Govinda!
Many names —
one Supreme.

jagannātha puruṣōttama pālaya
O Lord of the world! O Supreme Person! Protect me!

Charanam 2
nanda nandana
O son of Nandagopa!

indu vadana
O You with a face like the moon!

Charanam 3
kṣīrābdhi śayana
O Narayana, reclining in the milk ocean!

kṣārābdhi bandhana
O Rama, who bridged the salt ocean!
Rama's building of the bridge.

Charanam 4
daśaratha bāla
O son of Dasharatha!

daśamukha kāla
Death to ten-headed Ravana.

Charanam 5
dhanya cāritra
Whose story is holy.

vanya vanamāla
Who wears a garland of forest flowers.
A remembrance of Rama in exile in the forest.

Charanam 6
tāṭakāntaka
Who destroyed Tataka.

pāṭitāsura
Who struck down the demons.

Charanam 7
vāli nāśaka
Who slew Vali.

Charanam 8
bhakta pālaka
Protector of devotees.

mukti dāyaka
Giver of liberation.

Charanam 9
kaṅkaṇa bhūṣaṇa
Adorned with beautiful bracelets.

paṅkaja nayana
Lotus-eyed.

Last charanam
bharatānanda
The elder brother who is Bharata's joy.

bhadrādri vāsa
O Rama who lives at Bhadradri!

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas experiences together many forms —
Rama,
Krishna,
Narayana.
He joyfully sings of
the glory of the incarnations,
the protection of devotees,
and nama sankirtanam.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The sweetness of nama sankirtanam
“rāma rāma harē”
said again and again
purifies the mind
and nourishes devotion.

2. The oneness of the incarnations
Rama,
Krishna,
Narayana —
all three are the one Supreme.

3. Protection of devotees
The slaying of Tataka,
of Ravana,
of Vali —
all are the protection of dharma.

4. Giver of liberation
“mukti dāyaka”
The Lord
not only removes suffering,
but in the end gives liberation too.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“For Ramadas the Lord is not in one form only…
He is Narayana wearing the tulasi garland,
Krishna who steals butter,
and Rama who protects dharma.
So he joyfully pours out the names —
‘śrī rāma rāma harē!’”

Summary meaning
This kirtan praises Lord Rama in all his glory and roles: the divine protector of devotees, the slayer of demons, the noble prince, the beautiful son of Nanda, and the supreme protector residing at Bhadrachala. Ramadasa evokes deep bhakti, invoking Rama’s grace and protection.

@@ 6.16 | “kamalā vallabha gōvinda” – Raga Shankarabharanam |
This whole kirtanam is a nama sankirtanam that joyfully experiences
the sweetness of the name “Govinda”,
Krishna's beauty,
His divine play,
and His love for devotees.
Every line is filled with the surrender of
“mām pāhi”
— “Protect me!”
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
kamalā vallabha gōvinda
O Govinda, beloved of Goddess Lakshmi!

mām pāhi kalyāṇa kṛṣṇa gōvinda
O auspicious Krishna Govinda! Protect me!
“kalyāṇa”:
who gives auspiciousness,
who uplifts life.

Charanam 1
kamanīyānana gōvinda
O Govinda with a beautiful face!
The devotee
savours divine beauty.

Charanam 2
kañja vilōcana gōvinda
O lotus-eyed Govinda!

kāḷiya mardana gōvinda
Krishna,
who subdued the serpent Kaliya.
The destruction of ego.

Charanam 3
yaśōda bāla gōvinda
O child Krishna of Yashoda!

yadukula tilaka gōvinda
The jewel of the Yadu clan.

nitya mahōtsava gōvinda
Who is an everlasting festival of joy.
Where the Lord is,
there is always joy.

Charanam 4
nityānanda gōvinda
The form of eternal bliss.

vēṇu vinōla gōvinda
Absorbed in the music of the flute.
Krishna is
the form of music.

vijayagōpāla gōvinda
Gopala, who always gives victory.

Charanam 5
bhakta vatsala gōvinda
Who loves His devotees without limit.

bhāgavata priya gōvinda
Dear to the Lord's devotees.

bharatānanda gōvinda
The joy of Bharata.

bhadrādrivāsa gōvinda
O Govinda who lives at Bhadradri!

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas experiences, as nama sankirtanam,
Krishna's beauty,
His childhood play,
the dance on Kaliya,
His love for devotees,
and His nature as bliss.
In every line
he surrenders to the Lord, saying
“mām pāhi”.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The joy of nama sankirtanam
The very name “Govinda”
awakens devotion
and gives peace of mind.

2. Krishna = the form of bliss
The music of the flute,
His childhood play,
the everlasting festival —
all show
that “the Lord is bliss itself”.

3. The inner meaning of the dance on Kaliya
Kaliya is
poison,
ego,
turmoil of mind.
Krishna
dances on it and subdues it.

4. Bhakta vatsala
The Lord
is not only mighty,
but also a mother who loves the devotee.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“For Ramadas, Krishna is not mere philosophy…
He is the child playing the flute,
the hero who subdues Kaliya,
and the motherly compassion that protects the devotee.
So he melts again and again —
‘Govinda… mām pāhi!’”

@@ 6.17 | “ghōra bhavārṇava” – Raga Nata Namakriya |
This kirtanam speaks deeply about
the glory of the Name,
surrender,
the Lord's compassion,
and rescue from the sorrow of samsara.
The key message:
“The name of Govinda is the boat that carries us across this terrible ocean of births.”
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
ghōra bhavārṇava tāraka nāmadēya
O You whose name carries us across the terrible ocean of samsara!
“bhavārṇavam”:
the ocean of births,
a life of sorrow.
“tāraka”:
the one who brings us to the shore.

nīraja nētra śrī nārāyaṇa harē
O lotus-eyed Narayana! O Hari!

Refrain
gōvinda gōvinda gōvinda
Govinda! Govinda!
Chanting the Name again and again.

gōvinda gōpāla nārāyaṇa harē
Gopala! Narayana! Hari!

Charanam 1
ārti hara
Remover of sorrows.

amala kīrti vidhāyaka
Giver of pure fame.

mūrti parigraha
Who takes a form for the sake of devotees.
The secret of the incarnation.

Charanam 2
māṇikya kōṭīra
Who wears a ruby crown.

vāṇīśa vandya pada
Whose feet even Brahma worships.

vēṇi nibaddha barha
O Krishna with a peacock feather in His hair!

Charanam 3
kara dhṛta kōdaṇḍa
O Rama, bearing the Kodanda bow!

karuṇārasa bhāṇḍa
O treasure-house of compassion!

karivarāvana śauṇḍa
The hero who saved Gajendra.

Charanam 4
anātha bandhō
Kinsman of the helpless.

śrīnātha jagadīśa
Lord of Lakshmi and Lord of the world.

vināta kētana
Refuge of those who surrender.

Charanam 5
mādhava jagadīśa
Madhava! Lord of the world!

mādhavānanda rūpa
The form of bliss.

rādhā samēta
O Krishna, together with Radha!

Last charanam
bhīmāgha saṅgha hara
Who clears away great heaps of sin.

nāma vaibhava jaya
Victory to the glory of Your name!

rāma bhadrādrīśa
O Rama, Lord of Bhadradri!

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas
calls the sorrow of samsara “the ocean of births”,
and says that what saves us from it is
“the name of Govinda”.
Experiencing together the many forms —
Rama,
Krishna,
Narayana —
he becomes absorbed in nama sankirtanam.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The ocean of births
“ghōra bhavārṇava”
Life is
not only happiness,
but an ocean full of fear, sorrow and attachments.

2. The Name is the boat
“tāraka nāmadēya”
The saint says:
not sacrifice,
not yoga,
not hard penance —
chanting the Name is enough.

3. The secret of the incarnation
“mūrti parigraha”
The Lord
takes a form only to bestow grace.

4. Kinsman of the helpless
A person
may feel alone in the world.
But
“anātha bandhō harē”
— the Lord will never abandon us.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“For Ramadas, this worldly life is a ‘ghōra bhavārṇava’ — a terrible ocean.
But the one boat he found to cross it is
the name ‘Govinda!’
So again and again he holds on to it —
‘gōvinda gōvinda gōpāla nārāyaṇa harē!’”

@@ 6.18 | “nārāyaṇaṁ bhajē” – Raga Mayamalavagowla |
This whole kirtanam is the devotional teaching:
“One should worship Narayana.”
Here Bhadrachala Ramadas praises in order Narayana's
compassion,
protection of the world,
protection of devotees,
beauty,
and supremacy.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
nārāyaṇaṁ bhajē nārāyaṇam
I worship Narayana!

lakṣmī nārāyaṇaṁ bhajē nārāyaṇam
I worship Lakshmi-Narayana!
Narayana together with Lakshmi.
This is
the experience of the perfect divine couple.

Charanam 1
bṛndāvana sthitiṁ nārāyaṇam
Narayana who shines in Vrindavan.

dēva bṛnda bahustutam
The Supreme praised by the hosts of gods.

Charanam 2
dinakara karāmbaram
Whose garments shine like the sun.

divya kanaka kaṅkaṇa dharam
Who wears divine golden bracelets.

Charanam 3
paṅkaja vilōcanam
Lotus-eyed.

bhakta saṅkaṭa vimōcanam
Who removes the troubles of devotees.

Charanam 4
karuṇāpayōnidhim
The ocean of compassion.

bhavya śaraṇāgata nidhim
The treasure of those who surrender.
The principle of surrender.

Charanam 5
rakṣita jagatrayam
Who protects the three worlds.

cakra śikṣitāsuracayam
Who subdued the demons with His discus.

Charanam 6
ajñāna nāśakam
Who destroys ignorance.

bhakta vijñāna bhāsakam
Who gives the light of wisdom to devotees.

Charanam 7
śrīvatsa bhūṣaṇam
Who bears the Srivatsa mark on His chest.

nanda gōvatsa pōṣaṇam
Krishna, who tended Nandagopa's cows and calves.

Charanam 8
śṛṅgāra nāyakam
The Lord of beauty.

pada gaṅgā vidhāyakam
From whose feet the Ganga arose.

Charanam 9
śrīkaṇṭha sahacaram
The companion and friend of Shiva.
The feeling of the oneness of Hari and Hara.

divya vaikuṇṭhapura caram
Who shines in divine Vaikuntha.

Last charanam
kari mukti dāyakam
Who gave liberation to Gajendra.

bhadragiriratna nāyakam
The jewel-like Lord of Bhadragiri.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas praises in order
Narayana's divine form,
compassion,
protection of devotees,
gift of wisdom,
and gift of liberation.
In the end he conveys:
“Worshipping such a Narayana is the goal of life.”

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Worship = a way of life
“nārāyaṇaṁ bhajē”
The saint
is not just singing;
he is teaching a way of life.

2. The Lord = an ocean of compassion
“karuṇāpayōnidhi”
The Lord's greatest quality is
compassion.

3. The destruction of ignorance
The root cause of worldly sorrow is
ignorance.
Remembering Narayana
fills the heart with light.

4. The treasure of surrender
“śaraṇāgata nidhi”
The Lord
never abandons those who surrender.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“For Ramadas, Narayana is not just a deity…
He is an ocean of compassion,
the remover of devotees' sorrows,
the sun of wisdom that dispels the darkness of ignorance.
So he gives, as the one teaching for life —
‘nārāyaṇaṁ bhajē!’”

Summary meaning
This kirtan is a devotional praise of Lord Narayana in His multiple divine forms: the protector of devotees, remover of obstacles, bestower of knowledge, adorned with Srivatsa, caretaker of cows, companion of Lakshmi, and residing in Vaikuntha and Bhadrachala. The emphasis is on Bhakta Raksha (protection of devotees), liberation (Mukti), and divine beauty.

@@ 6.19 | “narahari dēva” – Raga Yaman Kalyani |
This is a nama sankirtanam kirtanam that experiences together many incarnations —
Narasimha,
Narayana,
Rama,
Krishna.
In it
the sweetness of Rama's name,
the Lord's compassion,
the glory of the incarnations,
and the protection of devotees
are all united.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
nara hari dēva janārdana
O Lord Narahari! O Janardana!
The Supreme who protects devotees.

kēśava nārāyaṇa kanakāmbara dhārī
Keshava! Narayana! O You who wear golden silk!

The sweetness of Rama's name
rāma rāma rāma śrī raghu rāma
Rama! Rama of the Raghu clan!
This refrain is
the joy of chanting the Name,
the state of absorption in Rama's name.

Charanam 1
ravi kulābharaṇa
The ornament of the solar dynasty.

ravi suta sakhya
Friend of Sugriva, son of the Sun.

rākṣasa saṁhāra
Who destroyed the rakshasas.

rāja sēvita
Worshipped even by kings.

Charanam 2
pannaga śayana
Narayana, reclining on Adishesha.

patita pāvana
Who purifies even the fallen.

karuṇā sāgara
Ocean of compassion.

Charanam 3
bandhu janaka
Father to kin and to the world.

tripurāntaka sāyaka
Who became the arrow of Shiva (in the burning of Tripura).
The oneness of Hari and Hara.

sītā nāyaka
The Lord of Sita.

śrī raghu nāyaka
Lord of the Raghu clan.

Charanam 4
sundara śrīdhara
Beautiful Sridhara.

mandarōddhāra
Who bore Mount Mandara.
A remembrance of the churning of the milk ocean.

makuṭa bhūṣaṇa
Adorned with a crown.

Charanam 5
nanda nandanā
Son of Nanda.

nanda mukundā
Mukunda, who gives joy.

brindāvana vihārī gōvinda
O Govinda who plays in Vrindavan!

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas experiences all the incarnations —
Narasimha,
Narayana,
Rama,
Krishna —
as one Supreme.
Absorbed in Rama's name,
he sings of the Lord's compassion
and His protection of devotees.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The oneness of the incarnations
Many different forms —
Narahari,
Rama,
Govinda —
but the Supreme is one.

2. Absorption in Rama's name
“rāma rāma rāma”
This is
not just music,
but a state of chanting.

3. Patita pāvana
However far one has fallen,
the Lord will save.

4. Compassion matters most
The saint
experiences compassion even more than power.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“To Ramadas the Lord appears in many names —
Narahari, Narayana, Raghurama, Govinda…
but in every form he sees one thing —
the compassion that protects the devotee.
So in the end he dissolves in the Name —
‘rāma rāma rāma… śrī raghu rāma!’”

Summary meaning
This kirtan praises Lord Rama/Krishna in multiple forms: as Narahari (man-lion form), Janardana, Keshava, and the compassionate protector. It highlights his royal lineage, feats like destroying demons, rescuing devotees, lifting Mandhara hill, and divine beauty. The song is an appeal to the Lord as both protector and savior, emphasizing Bhakta Raksha and compassion.

@@ 6.20 | “caraṇamulē nammiti” – Raga Kapi |
This whole kirtanam is the attitude of surrender:
“Rama! I trust only in Your feet.”
Though it is in very simple words,
it deeply expresses
complete devotion,
trust in a support,
surrender,
and the attitude of a servant.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
caraṇamulē nammiti rāmā
Rama! I trust only in Your feet.

caraṇamulē nammiti
I hold on to Your feet alone.
Complete surrender, feeling “there is no other way”.

Charanam 1
vāridhi gaṭṭina vara bhadrācala varadā
O giver of boons at Bhadrachalam, who built the bridge across the ocean!

varadā varadā varadā
O giver of boons! Varada!
Again and again:
a pitiful cry,
a call mixed with faith.

Charanam 2
vanamuna rātini vanitaga jēsina
Who turned a stone in the forest into a woman.
This is
the uplifting of Ahalya.
Rama's feet
give life even to stone.

caraṇamu caraṇamu caraṇamu nī divya
Your divine feet! Feet! Feet!
The saint
becomes absorbed in the glory of Rama's feet.

Charanam 3
pādāravindamē ādhāramani
Your lotus feet alone are my support.

nēnu paṭṭiti paṭṭiti paṭṭiti
I have held on! I have held on tight!
The devotee's complete commitment.

Last charanam
bāguga nannēlu bhadrācala rāma
O Rama of Bhadrachalam! Protect me well.

dāsuḍa dāsuḍa dāsuḍa
I am Your servant! Your servant!
The peak of the servant's attitude.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas says:
“Rama's feet are the only support of life.”
Thinking of Rama's compassion,
praising the feet that uplifted Ahalya,
he surrenders completely, saying “I am Your servant”.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The principle of surrender
“caraṇamulē nammiti”
This is
the heart of the Vaishnava tradition:
“Not I — You must save me.”

2. The glory of the holy feet
What redeemed Ahalya was
the touch of Rama's feet.
The saint says:
that very foot is my support.

3. The simplicity of devotion
In this kirtanam
there is no difficult Vedanta,
no big philosophical words.
But
there is deep devotion.

4. The servant's attitude
“dāsuḍa dāsuḍa”
The saint's identity:
“I am Your servant.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Ramadas had few supports in life.
No wealth… no power…
but he had one thing —
‘unshakeable trust in Rama's feet.’
That is why he cries like a child —
‘caraṇamulē nammiti rāmā!’”

Summary meaning
This song expresses complete surrender and trust in Lord Rama’s feet, specifically at Bhadrachala. Ramadasu emphasizes that Rama’s feet are the ultimate support, capable of saving devotees, even women in danger at night. The song portrays devotion, protection, and the intimate bond between the devotee and the Lord.

@@ 6.21 | “narasiṁha dēvara” – Raga Behag |
This kirtanam beautifully praises Narasimha's
fierce form,
protection of devotees,
compassion,
and grace that comes into the heart.
Above all it carries the devotional feeling:
“The same Narasimha who protected Prahlada must protect me too.”
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
narasiṁha dēvara nannu brōvu śrīdhara
O Lord Narasimha! O Sridhara! Protect me!

Charanam 1
dānava saṁhāra
Destroyer of demons.

dāsābja bhāskara
Like the sun to the lotus of His devotees.
A beautiful comparison:
the devotee's mind = the lotus,
Narasimha = the sun.

mānasa pañjaramandu dayimpurā
Come into the cage of my mind and bless me.
Not only in the temple outside,
He must bless within the heart too.

Charanam 2
paśibāluḍaina prahlāduni bhaktiki
For the devotion of the little boy Prahlada,

niśicara hiraṇyakaśipuni druñcina
who destroyed the demon Hiranyakashipu.
The Lord incarnates to protect His devotee.

Charanam 3
narahari rūpa
Who took the form of Narahari —
man and lion together.

śrī guru pūrṇānanda
The Guru who is the form of complete bliss.

nāda-rūpuḍai vacci
Coming in the form of sound —
the Lord who blesses through
music,
the Name,
and sacred sound.

nannādarimpurā
Embrace me and protect me.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas,
remembering Narasimha who protected Prahlada,
prays: “With the same compassion, protect me too.”
He experiences Narasimha
not only as an outer deity,
but as the Supreme in the form of inner sound within the mind.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Protection of devotees
The story of Prahlada:
the Lord never abandons His devotee.

2. Compassion within fierceness
Narasimha is
fierce to the demon,
compassionate to the devotee.

3. The temple of the mind
“mānasa pañjaram”
The true temple is
the devotee's mind.

4. The Supreme in the form of sound
“nāda rūpuḍai”
Through music,
nama sankirtanam
and devotion,
the Lord reveals Himself.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Prahlada saw the Lord in a pillar.
Ramadas saw Narasimha in music and sacred sound.
So he prays for one thing —
‘O Narasimha who protected Prahlada…
come into my mind and bless me!’”

Summary meaning
This song is a devotional plea to Lord Narasimha. Ramadasu recalls the protection of Prahlada, asking Narasimha to destroy evil forces and free the devotee from the mental cage of fear and doubt. The song emphasizes divine protection, compassion, and the power of Narasimha as a guardian of dharma.

@@ 6.22 | “adigō bhadrādri” – Raga Varali |
This is a kirtanam of rapturous experience of the Bhadrachalam temple.
It is not mere description of a temple —
it is a divine experience sung in a devotee's rapture:
“Here is the Vaikuntha of Kali yuga!”
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
adigō bhadrādri gautami idigō cūḍaṇḍi
Look! Here is Bhadradri on the banks of the Gautami!
“Gautami” = the Godavari.
A call of devotional rapture:
“Come! See this divine sight!”

Charanam 1
mudamutō sītā mudita lakṣmaṇulu
Joyfully, with Sita and Lakshmana beside Him,

kadasi koluvugā raghupati yuṇḍeḍi
the Lord of the Raghus sits there in His court.

Charanam 2
cāru svarṇa prākāra gōpura dvāramulatō
With beautiful golden walls and temple-tower gateways,

sundaramai yuṇḍeḍi
it shines in wondrous beauty.
The temple = a divine city.

Charanam 3
anupamānamai ati sundaramai
Of matchless beauty.

tanaru cakramuna dhaga dhaga meriseḍi
The shining discus (Sudarshana) blazes there.
“dhaga dhaga” —
a scene glittering with light.

Charanam 4
kaliyuga manduna gala vaikuṇṭhamu
This is the Vaikuntha that has appeared in Kali yuga.
Bhadrachalam = Vaikuntha on earth.

alarucunnadi nayamuga mrokkuḍi
It shines beautifully — bow down to it!

Last charanam
śrīkaramugā nila rāmadāsuni
Bringing auspiciousness to Ramadas,

prākaṭamugā brōcē prabhu vāsamu
it is the dwelling of the Lord who protects visibly.
For Ramadas,
Bhadrachalam is not just a temple —
it is Rama, living and present.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas,
in the rapture of seeing the Rama of Bhadrachalam face to face,
praises that holy place as the Vaikuntha of Kali yuga.
He shares with the devotees
the darshan of Rama, Sita and Lakshmana,
the beauty of the temple,
the blaze of the discus,
and divine bliss.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The experience of a holy place
For a devotee,
a temple = a living divine world.

2. The Vaikuntha of Kali yuga
“kaliyuga manduna gala vaikuṇṭhamu”
This feeling
applies not only to Bhadrachalam,
but to any place filled with devotion.

3. The Lord's presence
Ramadas
experiences: “Rama is there.”
This is
not mere imagination,
but a devotee's experience.

4. The true fruit of temple darshan
A temple should be seen
not as sightseeing,
but as an experience of Vaikuntha.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“When Ramadas looked at Bhadrachalam, he did not see a stone building.
He saw —
the Vaikuntha of Kali yuga, where Sita, Rama and Lakshmana live.
So in rapture he calls the whole world —
‘adigō bhadrādri… idigō cūḍaṇḍi!’
‘Come! See this divine darshan!’”

@@ 6.23 | “śrī rāma nāmamē” – Raga Atana |
This whole kirtanam is a teaching on the glory of the Name:
“Sri Rama's name alone is the support of life.”
It explains, very simply and very deeply,
the power of nama japa,
the destruction of sin,
purity of mind,
and the path to liberation.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
śrī rāma nāmamē jihvaku sthiramai yunnadi
Sri Rama's name alone rests firmly on my tongue.
A name settled on the tongue = constant remembrance.

Charanam 1
śrī rāmula karuṇayē lakṣmīkaramai yunnadi
Sri Rama's compassion alone gives every auspiciousness.
“lakṣmīkaram”:
that which gives
prosperity,
auspiciousness,
and joy.

Charanam 2
ghōramaina pātakamulu goṭṭēnannadi mammu
It destroys even terrible sins.

cērakunda āpadalanu ceṇḍēnannadi
It keeps dangers from coming near us.
Rama's name = a protective armour.

Charanam 3
vadalani durviṣaya vāñca vadalamannadi
It makes us give up the clinging desires for sense-objects.
Remembering the Name
purifies the mind.

nā madilō hari bhajana sampatkaramai yunnadi
In my mind, Hari bhajan has become my wealth.
True wealth is
singing the Lord's praise.

Last charanam
mukti mārgamunakidi mūlamannadi
This is the very root of the path to liberation.
Rama's name = the path to moksha.

viraktuḍu bhadrācala rāmadāsuḍannadi
So says, with devotion, Ramadas of Bhadrachalam, who has become detached.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas praises the glory of Rama's name.
He says Rama's name
destroys sins,
removes dangers,
reduces worldly desires,
grows the Lord's bhajan in the mind,
and in the end gives liberation.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The Name alone is the support
“śrī rāma nāmamē”
For Ramadas,
not yoga,
not great austerity —
Rama's name is enough.

2. The Name = purity of mind
“durviṣaya vāñca vadalam”
To conquer worldly desires,
the Lord's name is the simple path.

3. Bhajan = true wealth
“hari bhajana sampat”
Today
we think money is wealth.
Ramadas says:
“Hari bhajan is wealth.”

4. The path to moksha
“mukti mārgamunakidi mūlam”
Remembering the Name is
not ordinary devotion,
but a direct path to liberation.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“There were many hardships in Ramadas's life.
But one thing never left him —
‘Rama's name.’
So he says from experience —
‘If Sri Rama's name stays firm on the tongue…
life itself becomes auspicious!’”

@@ 6.24 | “tāraka mantramu” – Raga Dhanyasi |
This kirtanam is an experience of great bliss and devotion:
“I have found the Taraka mantra; now life is victorious.”
“Taraka mantra” means
the mantra that carries us across the ocean of births.
Here the focus is the glory of the Lord's name —
“Rama nama”,
“Narayana nama”.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
tāraka mantramu kōrina dorikenu
The Taraka mantra I sought has been found!

dhanyuḍanaitini ōrannā
I have become blessed!
Finding the Name is the greatest blessing of life.

Charanam 1
mīrina kāluni dūtala pāliṭi mṛtyuvuyani nammukayunnā
There is no more fear of Yama's messengers.
The saving Name
takes us beyond the fear of death.

Charanam 2
maccikatō nitarāntarammula māyalalō paḍabōkannā
Do not fall into the illusions of the world!

heccuga nūṭa yenimidi tirupatulelami tiruga pani lēdannā
There is no need to wander round all the 108 holy shrines.
Remembering the Name is enough.

Charanam 3
muccaṭagā tā puṇya nadulalō munuguṭa pani yēmiṭikannā
What need is there to bathe in sacred rivers?
The Name itself is the holy water that washes away sin.

vacceṭi paruvapu dinamulalō suḍi vaḍu ṭalu mānakayunnā
Do not waste your life.

Charanam 4
yenni janmamula yerukato jūcina yēkō nārāyaṇuḍannā
In however many births one looks, there is only the one Narayana.
In everything, the one Supreme.

anni rūpulaiyunna parātparu
The Highest of the high, present in all forms.

Charanam 5
yenni janmamula jēsina pāpamu ī janmamutō viḍunannā
The sins of many births will leave with this very birth.
The glory of the Name.

anniṭikini kaḍasāri janmamidi
Let this be the last birth.
A longing for liberation.

Last charanam
dharmamu tappaka bhadrādrīśuni tana madilō nammucununnā
Without straying from dharma, I firmly trust the Lord of Bhadradri in my heart.

marmamu telisina rāmadāsu hṛn-mandiramunanē yunnā
Ramadas, who knew this secret, dwells in the temple of the heart.
The true temple is
the devotee's heart.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas expresses his joy at having found
the Taraka mantra — the name of Narayana / Rama.
He says:
remembering the Name is enough;
pilgrimages and great practices are not necessary;
the Name itself destroys sin;
the Name itself is the path to liberation.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The Taraka Name
“tārakam” =
that which carries across.
Across what?
The ocean of births.

2. The Name above outer practices
Ramadas
does not reject pilgrimage.
But he says:
“Without the Name, they are not complete.”

3. Advaitic vision
“ēkō nārāyaṇuḍannā”
In all forms,
one Supreme.

4. The temple of the heart
“hṛn mandiramu”
True devotion
must take place within.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“All his life Ramadas sought one treasure —
the Taraka Name.
Once he found it, he says:
‘No more fear of Yama… no more fear of birth…
Narayana's name is enough!’
So this kirtanam is not just a song —
it is the voice of joy of a devotee who has seen liberation.”

@@ 6.25 | “takkuvēmi” – Raga Saurashtram |
The central message of this kirtanam:
“When the Lord is right here with us,
what do we lack?”
It is a wonderful kirtanam expressing
complete faith,
the Lord's protection,
the glory of His incarnations,
and the fearlessness of the devotee.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
takkuvēmi manaku rāmuṇ-ḍokkaḍuṇḍu varaku
As long as we have Rama alone, what do we lack?
This is
the devotee's complete faith.

Charanam 1
prakka tōḍugā bhagavantuḍu
When the Lord is at our side as our companion,

tana cakradhāriyai centanē yuṇḍaga
holding His discus (Sudarshana), close beside us —
why fear?

Charanam 2
mruccu sōmakuni munu jampinayā matsya mūrti
When we have the Fish incarnation who once slew the demon Somaka —

Charanam 3
mandara giri mōsina kūrmāvatāruni
the Tortoise incarnation who bore Mount Mandara (at the churning of the ocean),

kṛpa manakuṇḍaga
when His grace is with us —

Charanam 4
hiraṇyakaśipuni iru cekkalugā paracina narahari
when Narahari, who tore Hiranyakashipu into two, is beside us —

Charanam 5
bhūmi svargamunu ponduga golicina vāmanuṇḍu
when we have Vamana, who measured earth and heaven —

Charanam 6
daśagrīvu munu daṇḍiñcina daśaratha rāmuni
when we have Dasharatha's Rama, who punished ten-headed Ravana —

Charanam 7
duṣṭa kaṁsuni druñcinaṭṭi śrī kṛṣṇuḍu
when Sri Krishna, who destroyed the wicked Kamsa, blesses us —

Last charanam
rāmadāsu nila rakṣiñcedanani
“I will protect Ramadas” —

prēmatō palkina prabhuviṭa nuṇḍaga
when the Lord who said this with love is with us!
The Lord is
not far away;
He is with us.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas,
remembering the many incarnations of Vishnu, says:
“When such a Lord is with us, there is nothing in life to fear.”

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Faith in the Lord
“takkuvēmi?”
The devotee's state of mind:
“The Lord is here… that is enough.”

2. Remembering the incarnations
Each incarnation is
a memory of protection.

3. Devotion that removes fear
This kirtanam
dissolves
stress,
fear,
and insecurity.

4. The Lord = on our side
“prakka tōḍugā”
The Lord is
not only a judge,
but a companion who stands by our side.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In the world, people always ask, ‘What am I lacking?’
But Ramadas asks the opposite —
‘When Rama is here, what do we lack?’
Narasimha protects…
Rama guides…
Krishna blesses…
So for the devotee, there is no fear at all!”

@@ 6.26 | “kaṇṭinēḍu mā rāmula” – Raga Nadanamakriya |
The central feeling of this kirtanam:
“Today I have seen my Rama!”
It is
the devotee's rapture of darshan,
the joy that cools the eyes,
the experience of the Lord in His temple form (archavatara).
The whole song expresses
the state of the devotee's heart after seeing Rama.
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Pallavi
kaṇṭi nēḍu mā rāmula
Today I saw our Rama!

kanugoṇṭi nēnu mā rāmula
Today my eyes received the darshan of our Rama!
Not just “I saw” —
“my eyes were filled with joy”.

Charanam 1
bhaktagaṇamula brōcu mā
Our protector of the hosts of devotees,

iṇṭi vēlupu bhadragiri nunna vānini
the light of our home, who dwells on Bhadragiri —
The Lord is
not only in the temple,
He is the light of the home.

Charanam 2
celuvoppu cunnaṭṭi sītā samētuḍai
Beautifully, together with Sita,

koluvu dīrina mā kōdaṇḍa rāmuni
our Kodanda Rama, seated in His royal court —

Charanam 3
taraṇi kula tilakuni
the jewel-mark of the solar dynasty,

ghana nīla gātruni
whose body has the colour of a dark rain-cloud —
A description of Rama's beauty.

karuṇā rasamu guriyu kanudōyi galavāni
whose two eyes rain the nectar of compassion —
The Lord's eyes
do not pass judgement;
they give compassion.

Charanam 4
kurumuñji mutyāla saramulu merayagā
with strings of pearls shining,

muripempu cirunavvu momu galiginavāni
whose face has a captivating smile —
The experience of darshan of the temple deity.

Charanam 5
karaku baṅgāru cēla kānti jagamulu gappa
the glow of His golden silk spreading over the worlds,

śara cāpamulu kēla dharayiñcu svāmini
the Lord holding bow and arrow in His hands —
Rama is
both beauty
and protection.

Last charanam
dharaṇipai śrī rāmadāsunēleḍu vāni
who guides Sri Ramadas on this earth,

parama puruṣuṇḍaina bhadragiri svāmini
the Supreme Person, the Lord of Bhadragiri.
Ramadas's life
centres on the darshan of Rama.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Bhadrachala Ramadas shares the joy of seeing the Rama of Bhadrachalam.
He experiences
Rama's beauty,
compassion,
His divine form together with Sita,
and His protection of devotees.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The joy of darshan
This song is
not the information “I saw”,
but the rapture of “I experienced”.

2. The principle of archavatara
The Lord is
not a statue,
but living compassion.

3. The Lord's eyes
“karuṇā rasamu guriyu”
In an upanyasam one can say:
“The very look with which the Lord sees us is grace.”

4. Darshan of Rama = fulfilment of life
Ramadas had
no wealth,
no kingdom,
but he had the darshan of Rama.
That itself is supreme bliss.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Some moments in a devotee's life cannot be told in words.
For Ramadas, that moment was the darshan of Rama.
So he says with delight:
‘Today my eyes have seen Rama!’
That joy of darshan is the very life of this kirtanam.”

@@ 7.1 | “narajanma bandāga” – Raga Yadukula Kambhoji |
The central message of this kirtanam is the deep glory of the Name:
“When we have been given a human birth, and a tongue that can speak, how can we not always say ‘Krishna’?”

narajanma bandāga nālige iruvāga kṛṣṇā ena bāradē
When a human birth has come to us,
when we have a tongue that can speak,
can we not say “Krishna”?

kṛṣṇā yendarē sakala kaṣṭavu parihāra kṛṣṇā ena bāradē
Just saying “Krishna” removes all troubles;
that being so, can we not say “Krishna”?

kṛṣṇā – yē – kṛṣṇā – gōpāla kṛṣṇā – rādhā kṛṣṇā
Krishna! Gopala Krishna! Radha Krishna!
(An outpouring of the joy of nama sankirtanam.)

malagiddu mai muridu ēḷuttalommē kṛṣṇā ena bāradē
When we wake from sleep, at least in that moment of stretching and rising,
can we not say “Krishna”?

suḷitāḍuta manayoḷagādaru vommē kṛṣṇā ena bāradē
Even while moving about the house,
can we not say “Krishna” at least once?

snāna pāna japa tapagaḷu māḍuta kṛṣṇā ena bāradē
While bathing, while eating and drinking, while doing japa and tapas,
can we not say “Krishna”?

śālyānna ṣaḍrasa tindu tṛptavanāgi kṛṣṇā ena bāradē
Even after eating a meal of fine rice with all six tastes and feeling satisfied,
can we not say “Krishna”?

kandana bigi bigidu appi muddāḍuta kṛṣṇā ena bāradē
Even while hugging a child tightly and cuddling it,
can we not say “Krishna”?

nimma mandagāminiyoḷu sarasalāḍutalomme kṛṣṇā ena bāradē
Even while talking lovingly with your wife,
can we not say “Krishna” at least once?
What the Dasa means here is:
“Spirituality is not running away from life;
the Lord's name must be mixed into every moment of life.”

durita rāśigaḷanu taridu bisāḍuva kṛṣṇā ena bāradē
Can we not say the name “Krishna”,
which cuts down and throws away mountains of sin?

garuḍa gamana namma purandara viṭhalana kṛṣṇā ena bāradē
Can we not say “Krishna”, the name of our Purandara Vittala
who rides on Garuda?

Brief meaning
In this song Purandara Dasa says:
a human birth is very rare.
While we have a tongue, saying the Lord's name is the simplest sadhana.
Not only alone in the pooja room —
when rising,
when walking,
when eating,
when with the family,
even in moments of love —
the name “Krishna” should keep flowing.
Remembering the Name is the simple path to liberation in Kali yuga.

Key significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The simple sadhana for Kali yuga – remembering the Name
Not everyone can do Vedic study, yagas or hard tapas.
But anyone can say “Krishna”.

2. Spirituality and life are not two
Nowhere in this song does the Dasa say “leave your home”.
What he says is:
“Do not look for the Lord by leaving life;
bring the Lord into life.”

3. The Name purifies the mind
When sorrow comes, the mind breaks.
But remembering the Name gives the mind strength to bear it.

4. The highest use of the tongue
The tongue
can be used to quarrel,
can be used to complain,
or can say “Krishna”.
The inner call of the song is to sanctify this rare tongue with the Lord's name.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“We need no separate time to think of the Lord.
Wherever the mind is, the Name can be there.
That is why the Dasa asks —
‘narajanma bandāga… kṛṣṇā ena bāradē?’”

@@ 7.2 | “viṭhala salahō” – Raga Chakravakam |
The central feeling of this kirtanam:
“Wherever I look, it is Vittala.
He is our protection, our relation, our support — the Supreme filling within and without.”
This is a wonderful kirtanam of experience —
the state in which the devotee sees only the Lord in every direction.

Pallavi
viṭhala salahō svāmi nammanu
O Vittala! O Lord, protect us!

viṭhala jagadantaryāmi
O Vittala! The indweller who fills the whole world!
“He is not only in the temple;
He is in all beings and in all places.”

hindē viṭhala mundē viṭhala
Vittala behind, Vittala in front.

hindē mundē viṭhala viṭhala
On every path of my life, it is Vittala.
The devotee has no fear —
the faith that “wherever I go, He is with me”.

eḍagē viṭhala balagē viṭhala
Vittala on the left, Vittala on the right.
His protection in every direction.

mēlē viṭhala keḷagē viṭhala
Vittala above, Vittala below.
He is in the sky and on the earth.
He is in the high and in the low.

oḷagē viṭhala horagē viṭhala
Vittala within, Vittala without.
This is the highest philosophy of this song.
Within — He is in the soul.
Without — He is the world.
Like the Upanishadic words:
“antar bahiś ca tat sarvam…”

tandē viṭhala tāyi viṭhala
Father is Vittala, mother is Vittala.
All worldly relations may change;
but the Lord alone is the eternal support.

bandhu viṭhala baḷaga viṭhala
Kinsman is Vittala, one's circle is Vittala.
True support is the relationship with the Lord.

bandhu baḷaga purandara viṭhala
Purandara Vittala, who is every relation.

Brief meaning
In this song Purandara Dasa says:
the Lord is not just a figure alone in a temple.
He is
in front and behind,
above and below,
within and without,
as mother, father and relations —
filling everywhere.
If the devotee lives in this awareness, loneliness, fear and insecurity all go away.

Key significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The principle of the Indweller (antaryami)
The one word “jagadantaryāmi” is a great Vedanta.
The Lord is
outside as the world,
inside as the witness.

2. The devotee's spiritual security
Singing this song gives the feeling of a child holding its mother's hand.
“When Vittala is everywhere, what do I fear?”

3. A bridge between Advaitic awareness and devotion
In very simple words, this song speaks of the great principles of
the cosmic form (vishvarupa),
the indwelling presence,
all-pervasiveness.

4. Human relations are impermanent — the Lord's love is eternal
Human relations
may change,
may drift away,
may break.
But when the Dasa says
“tandē viṭhala, tāyi viṭhala”,
he experiences the Lord as an eternal relation.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“What is the peak of devotion?
Eyes closed — the Lord…
eyes open — the Lord…
within — the Lord… without — the Lord…
It is in that experience that the Dasa sings —
‘viṭhala salahō… jagadantaryāmi!’”

@@ 7.3 | “dēvaki nandana” – Raga Janasammodini |
This whole kirtanam is an auspicious song describing the many divine qualities of Lord Krishna, the lilas of His incarnation, and His compassion for devotees.
It is an experience like a namavali —
every word is a lila, a principle, an experience.

dēvaki nandana nanda mukunda
O son of Devaki!
O joy of Nandagopa!
O Mukunda — giver of liberation!
The one Krishna is
a son to Devaki,
the darling of Nanda,
the giver of liberation to devotees.

nandita munijana nityānanda
O form of eternal bliss, praised by the sages!
What yogis meditate on and jnanis experience is that very form of bliss.

nigamōddhāra navanīta cōra
O You who reveal the true meaning of the Vedas!
O child who steals butter!
How beautiful:
on one side, the Supreme of Vedanta;
on the other, Yashoda's butter thief.

khagapati vāhana jagadōddhāra
O You whose vehicle is Garuda!
O You who save the whole world!

makara kuṇḍaladhara mōhana vēṣa
O enchanting form wearing fish-shaped earrings!
The Lord's beauty is the blissful power that draws the devotee.

rukmiṇi vallabha pāṇḍava pōṣa
O beloved of Rukmini!
O You who protected the Pandavas!
The Lord is
the ideal of married love,
the protector of dharma.

śaṅkha cakradhara śrī gōvinda
O Sri Govinda who bears the conch and discus!
Conch — protection.
Discus — destruction of adharma.

paṅkaja lōcana paramānanda
O lotus-eyed form of supreme bliss!
His holy eyes are the form of compassion.

kaṁsa mardana kaustubhābharaṇa
O hero who crushed Kamsa!
O You who wear the Kaustubha gem!
Compassion to devotees,
sternness to adharma.

haṁsa vāhana pūjita caraṇa
O You whose holy feet are worshipped by Brahma, who rides the swan!
Krishna is the Supreme whom even Brahma bows to.

varada bēlāpura cenna prasanna
O giver of boons!
O beautiful one who blesses at Belapura!
O You who are always gracious!
This is also taken as a reference to a holy place the Dasa experienced.

purandara viṭhala guṇa paripūrṇa
O Purandara Vittala!
O Perfect One, full of every auspicious quality!

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Purandara Dasa sings briefly of all of Lord Krishna's qualities:
His childhood lilas,
the principle of the Supreme,
protection of devotees,
establishment of dharma,
beauty,
compassion,
and valour.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Krishna – simplicity and the Supreme
The two words “navanīta cōra” and “nigamōddhāra” are enough.
The very child who steals butter
is the Supreme of the Vedas!
That is the wonder of the Krishna avatara.

2. The Lord's many relationships
Son to Devaki,
darling to Nanda,
Lord to Rukmini,
friend to the Pandavas,
bliss to the sages,
protector to the world.
The one Supreme is every relationship.

3. The many states of devotion
In this song
sweetness (madhurya),
detachment,
heroism,
Vedanta,
and devotion
are all mixed together.

4. Experiencing the auspicious qualities
In the final words “guṇa paripūrṇa” are contained
His compassion,
accessibility (saulabhya),
generosity,
valour,
beauty,
and wisdom.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Krishna cannot be seen from only one angle.
A child to Yashoda…
the Supreme to the Vedas…
protector to the Pandavas…
bliss to the devotees…
That is why the Dasa has placed a whole world in every name.”

@@ 7.4 | “rāgi tandīrā” – Raga Ahir Bhairavi |
On the surface this kirtanam looks like the song of a mendicant saying “bring me ragi”.
But its inner meaning is very high.
It is a Dasa's spiritual teaching —
a song that tells in simple village language:
“What is true good fortune?
What kind of life should we live?”

Opening remembrance of the Name
viṭhala viṭhala viṭhala pāṇḍuraṅga
O Panduranga Vittala! Vittala!
First the remembrance of the Name — then the teaching.
The Dasas always show this.

rāgi tandīrā bhikṣakē rāgi tandīrā
Bring ragi for this mendicant!
Outer meaning:
a mendicant is asking for food.
Inner meaning:
“Devotees! Bring the food of a life of dharma.”

yōgyarāgi bhōgyarāgi bhāgyavantarāgi nīvu
Be
worthy,
enjoyers of what is good,
and fortunate.
True “good fortune” is not money alone; it is also good qualities.

annadānava māḍuvarāgi anna chatra vaniṭṭavarāgi
Be those who give food,
and those who run free feeding houses.
Among all charities, the highest is giving food.

anya vārtegaḷa biṭṭavarāgi anudina bhajanaya māḍuvarāgi
Give up idle talk,
and be those who do the Lord's bhajan every day.
“anya vārte” — talking about others, quarrels, finding fault.

mātā pitaranu sēviparāgi pāpa karmava biṭṭavarāgi
Be those who serve mother and father,
and those who have given up sinful deeds.
The foundation of devotion to God is service to mother and father.

nītimārgadali bāḷuvarāgi khyātiyali mikilādavarāgi
Be those who live on the path of righteousness,
and those who rise high in good name.
If you want a good name, you must live in dharma.

siri ramaṇana sadā smarisuvarāgi guruvigē bāgōvantavarāgi
Be those who always remember Vishnu, the beloved of Lakshmi,
and those who bow humbly to the Guru.
Devotion to the Guru is essential for both devotion and knowledge.

karagadē saṁsāra nīguvarāgi
Be those who cross over samsara without dissolving in it.
The wisdom of detachment — living in the world without drowning in it.

purandara viṭhalana sēviparāgi
Be those who serve Purandara Vittala.
At the end of the song the Dasa gives the answer:
“Serving Vittala is the essence of life.”

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Purandara Dasa lists what a truly good life should be:
giving food,
the Lord's bhajan,
service to mother and father,
a life of dharma,
devotion to the Guru,
good conduct,
remembrance of the Lord.
These are the true wealth of human life.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. High dharma in simple language
There are no big Vedantic words in this song.
But it has
dharma,
devotion,
devotion to the Guru,
service to society,
and detachment.
That is the greatness of the Dasas.

2. Devotion is also a way of life
Doing bhajan alone is not enough.
The Dasa says:
one must also give food,
serve one's parents,
live honestly.
That is complete devotion.

3. “anya vārte” – a teaching that applies even today
Today
gossip,
criticism on social media,
and idle arguments
spoil peace of mind.
The Dasa says:
“Leave that and remember the Name.”

4. Living in samsara without dissolving in it
There is no need to run away from family life.
But:
“Do not drown in it and forget the soul.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“If you ask how a good person should be,
the Dasa has answered it in this one song.
Devotion in the heart…
dharma in conduct…
compassion in the hand…
Vittala's name on the tongue…
This is the life the Dasa shows us.”

@@ 7.5 | “kaliyugadalli harināmava” – Raga Shivaranjani |
This kirtanam is a wonderful song that explains, very simply and very deeply, the glory of remembering the Name in Kali yuga.
Its central message:
“In this Kali yuga, difficult yogas and austerities are not needed.
The Lord's name is enough.”

kaliyugadalli harināmava nenadarē kula kōṭigaḷuddharisuvavu
In Kali yuga, just by remembering Hari's name,
crores of generations of one's family are uplifted.
The devotional vision: “If one person truly remembers the Name, its merit spreads to the whole family.”

sulabhada bhaktige sulabha nendenisuva jalaruhanābhana nene manavē
O mind, remember Vishnu, the lotus-naveled one, who is easily reached by simple devotion!
The Lord is not reached only through difficult practices.
Love is enough.

snānavanariyenu maunavanariyenu dhyānavanariyenendu alaiya bēḍā
Do not worry, saying:
“I do not know the proper rules of bathing,
I do not know how to keep silence,
I do not know meditation.”
The Dasa removes spiritual fear.

jānakī vallabha daśaratha nandana gānalōlana nene manavē
Rama —
Lord of Janaki,
son of Dasharatha,
lover of music —
remember Him, O mind!
The Lord delights in the very simplicity of our devotion.

mantravanariyenu yantravanariyenu tantravanariyenendu alaiya bēḍā
Do not wander about saying:
“I do not know mantras,
I do not know yantras,
I do not know tantras.”
Complicated secrets are not needed to reach the Lord.

santata nityānandana nāmava cintagaḷillade nene manavē
O mind, remember always, without worry,
the name of the One whose form is eternal bliss!
Remembering the Name is medicine for peace of mind.

japavondariyenu tapavondariyenu upavāsavanariyenendu alaiya bēḍā
Do not grieve, saying:
“I do not know japa,
I do not know tapas,
I do not know fasting.”
Lack of qualification is no bar to devotion; what matters is the sincerity of the mind.

apāra mahima śrī purandara viṭhalana upāyadindali nene manavē
O mind, remember Purandara Vittala, whose glory is beyond measure,
in some way or other!
In the end the Dasa says:
“Do not fear whether the method is right or wrong; it is enough to remember the Lord.”

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Purandara Dasa says:
in Kali yuga the simple path is remembering the Name.
Do not worry even if you have no knowledge of yoga, tapas, mantra or tantra.
It is enough to remember the Lord with a sincere mind.
Hari's name is the simple path to liberation.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The dharma of Kali yuga – nama sankirtanam
This whole song says that in Kali yuga:
“The Name is the means, the Name is the goal.”

2. A song that removes spiritual inferiority
Many people think:
“I don't know mantras,”
“I don't know the shastras,”
“I am no great practitioner.”
The Dasa says:
“That does not make the Lord far away.”

3. The Lord is easily reached by simple devotion
“sulabhada bhaktige sulabha” — this one line is very important.
The Lord is
not caught by intellect,
but reached by love.

4. Remembering the Name – medicine for the mind
“cintagaḷillade nene manavē”
In today's times, for
anxiety,
stress,
fear,
and loneliness,
remembering the Name is a spiritual support that gives peace of mind.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In Kali yuga the Lord has not locked the door.
He has made the door simple.
It doesn't matter if you don't know mantras…
It doesn't matter if you don't know yoga…
The Dasa comforts us that one sincere ‘Rama’, ‘Krishna’, ‘Vittala’ is enough.”

@@ 7.6 | “dāsana māḍikō” – Raga Nadanamakriya |
This kirtanam is a deep prayer of surrender.
Here the Dasa asks the Lord for nothing — not wealth, not fame, not power.
What he asks is:
“Accept me as Your servant.”
This is the highest state of mind in devotion.

Pallavi
dāsana māḍikō yenna – svāmi
O Lord! Make me Your servant.
Complete surrender: “I am not my own; I am Yours.”

sāsira nāmada vēṅkaṭaramaṇā
O Venkataramana, who has a thousand names!
The Dasa also remembers the glory of the Lord's names.

Charanam 1
durbuddhigaḷanella biḍisō – ninna karuṇa kavacavenna haraṇakke toḍisō
Remove all my evil thoughts,
and clothe me in the armour of Your compassion.
More than enemies outside, the enemies within —
ego,
jealousy,
anger,
desire —
are the greater foes.

caraṇa sēve yenagē koḍisō
Grant me service at Your holy feet.
The devotee's desire — service to the Lord (kainkaryam).

abhaya kara puṣpavenna śiradalli muḍisō
Place on my head the flower of Your hand that grants fearlessness.
A longing that the Lord should bless saying “Do not fear”.

Charanam 2
druḍa bhakti ninnalli bēḍi – dēvā
O Lord! I ask for firm devotion to You.
Not a passing emotion; steady devotion.

aḍi geragu vanayya anudinavu pāḍi
Bless me to bow at Your feet and sing Your praise every day.
The daily practice of devotion:
prostration,
nama sankirtanam.

kaḍegaṇṇinde yenna nōḍi
Look at me from the corner of Your eye.
A single glance of grace from the Lord can change a life.

biḍuve koḍu ninna dhyānava mana śuci māḍi
Purify my mind and grant me meditation on You.
Without purity of mind, true meditation does not come.

Charanam 3
mora hokkavara kāyva birudu – enna mareyade rakṣaṇa māḍayya poredu
Is it not Your glory to protect those who take refuge in You?
In the same way, do not forget me — protect me.
“Protecting those who surrender” — the central principle of the Vaishnava faith.

duritagaḷellava taridu
Cut away all my sins and sorrows.

siri purandara viṭhala ennanu poredu
O Purandara Vittala! Embrace me and protect me.
“poredu” — to hold and protect with compassion.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Purandara Dasa
surrenders completely to the Lord.
He prays that
evil thoughts should go,
the mind should be purified,
there should be firm devotion,
service to the Lord,
and in the end, the Lord's protection.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. What does a true devotee ask for?
Not money, position or fame.
The Dasa asks for
right understanding,
devotion,
meditation,
service to the Lord.
This is the highest prayer.

2. The armour of compassion
“The armour of compassion” is a wonderful image.
In the world we look for
insurance,
security,
protection.
The Dasa says:
“The Lord's compassion is the true protection.”

3. The principle of surrender
“mora hokkavara kāyva birudu”
That is:
“Protecting those who come to You is Your responsibility.”
This is the central promise of the Rama and Krishna avataras.

4. There is no spirituality without purity of mind
The Dasa says directly:
“mana śuci māḍi”
More than outer rituals, the heart must be pure.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In this song the Dasa tells a great secret.
What should we ask of the Lord?
Not the world…
but to be the servant of the Lord of the world.
Because once we become the Lord's servant,
our whole life becomes His responsibility.”

@@ 7.7 | “rāma mantrava japisō” – Raga Jonpuri |
This whole kirtanam praises the glory of Rama's name.
Its central message:
“Rama's name is enough.
It is the great mantra that uplifts a person.”

Pallavi
rāma mantrava japisō hē manujā
O man! Chant the Rama mantra.
The Dasa calls out to people directly.

ā mantra ī mantra necci nī keḍa bēḍā
Do not get confused wandering after this mantra and that mantra.
Simple remembrance of the Name matters more than spiritual confusion.

Charanam 1
sōmaśēkhara tanna satige hēḷida mantra
This is the mantra Shiva taught to his wife Parvati.
The Puranas tell of Lord Shiva speaking of the glory of Rama's name.

kula hīnanādaru kūgi japisuva mantra
A mantra that can be chanted aloud even by one of low birth.
For Rama's name, caste, learning and qualification are no barrier.

sale bīdiyoḷu nuḍiva mantra
A mantra that can be said even in the street.
Not a mantra needing secret initiation; it belongs to everyone.

halavu pāpaṅgaḷa hata geḍisuva mantra
A mantra that destroys many sins.

Charanam 2
sulabhadindali svarga sūre gombuva mantra
A mantra that easily gives the highest state.
“svarga” can also be taken as a state of bliss.

snāna maunaṅgaḷige sādhanavāda mantra
A mantra that is the support of practices like holy bathing and silence.
The life of every spiritual practice is remembering the Name.

jñānigaḷu manadi dhyānipa mantra
The mantra the wise meditate on in their minds.
It is no ordinary mantra; it is the object of meditation of the wise.

hīna guṇaṅgaḷa hiṅgisuva mantra
A mantra that removes base qualities.
Rama's name gives purity of mind.

Charanam 3
ēnambē vibhīṣaṇage paṭṭakaṭṭida mantra
The mantra that won Vibhishana his coronation.
Surrender to Rama lifts a person up.

sakala vēdaṅgaḷige sāravenipa mantra
The mantra called the essence of all the Vedas.
In the two syllables “Rā-ma” lies the essence of the Veda.

mukuti mārgake idē mūla mantra
This is the root mantra for the path of liberation.

bhakuti rasake omme baṭṭe tōruva mantra
A mantra that at once lets one taste the nectar of devotion.
Rama's name melts the heart.

sukhanidhi purandara viṭhalana mahā mantra
This is the great mantra of Purandara Vittala, the treasure of bliss.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Purandara Dasa says:
Rama's name belongs to everyone.
It
destroys sin,
gives purity of mind,
grows devotion,
and leads to liberation itself.
Even the essence of the Veda is in Rama's name.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The simplicity of Rama's name
This mantra needs
no great qualification,
no secret initiation,
no restriction of time or place.
It can be said even in the street.

2. The Name – a path of equality
“kula hīnanādaru”
The Dasa gives a great social message:
“No one is unfit for the Lord's name.”

3. The essence of the Veda
“sakala vēdaṅgaḷige sāra”
Rama's name contains
dharma,
devotion,
surrender,
and compassion.

4. Remembering the Name – the power to change the heart
“A mantra that removes base qualities.”
True nama japa
reduces anger,
dissolves ego,
softens the mind.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Rama's name is not just two syllables.
It is courage to a frightened mind…
peace to a troubled mind…
purity to one fallen into sin…
liberation to the devotee.
That is why the Dasa calls —
‘rāma mantrava japisō hē manujā!’”

@@ 7.8 | “vēṅkaṭācala nilayam” – Raga Sindhu Bhairavi |
This wonderful kirtanam describes, briefly and beautifully, the divine auspicious form, compassion and protection of devotees of Lord Venkateswara, who dwells at Tirumala.
The song is like an experience of darshan.

Pallavi
vēṅkaṭācala nilayam vaikuṇṭha puravāsam
O You who dwell on the Venkata hill!
O Supreme, whose abode is Vaikuntha!
Tirumala is Vaikuntha on earth.
The Lord of Vaikuntha comes and stands on the hill for the sake of people.

paṅkaja nētram
O You whose compassionate eyes are like lotuses!
The Lord's eyes are the form of compassion that embraces the devotee.

parama pavitram
O most holy Supreme!
Even thinking of Him purifies the mind.

śaṅkha cakradhara cinmaya rūpam
O divine form of pure consciousness, bearing conch and discus!
Conch — protection.
Discus — destruction of adharma.
Cinmaya rupa — the form of pure consciousness.

Charanam
ambujōdbhava vinutam
O You who are praised by Brahma, born from the lotus!
The Supreme, worthy of worship even by Brahma.

agaṇita guṇanāmam
O You who have countless auspicious qualities and names!
The Lord's qualities cannot be counted.

tumburu nārada gānavilōlam
O You who delight in the songs of Narada and Tumburu!
The Lord rejoices in music, nama sankirtanam and devotion.

makara kuṇḍaladhara madana gōpālam
O Gopala, wearing fish-shaped earrings, who enchants even Manmatha!
The Lord's beauty is the blissful power that draws the devotee.

bhakta pōṣaka śrī purandara viṭhalam
O You who protect and nurture devotees!
O Purandara Vittala!
The Lord's greatest quality is protecting His devotees.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Purandara Dasa
has darshan of the Supreme who dwells at Tirumala.
He experiences Him as
the Lord of Vaikuntha,
the one with compassionate eyes,
the bearer of conch and discus,
the one praised by the gods,
the lover of music,
the God who protects devotees.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Tirumala – Vaikuntha on earth
“vaikuṇṭha puravāsam”
The Dasa says:
“The same Narayana who is in Vaikuntha is at Tirumala.”
That is why crores of people are drawn to that hill.

2. The Lord's accessibility (saulabhya)
The Lord of Vaikuntha,
for the sake of people,
comes to the hill
and gives darshan.
This is the Lord's compassion.

3. His love of nama sankirtanam
“tumburu nārada gāna vilōlam”
The Lord
delights not in pomp,
but in sincere devotional songs.

4. Nurturer of devotees
To His devotees the Lord gives
not only material things,
but courage of mind,
spiritual growth,
and compassion.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“The Perumal we see at Tirumala is not a statue.
He is the Supreme the Vedas seek…
the lover of music of whom Narada sings…
the Compassionate One standing on the hill to protect devotees.
That is why the Dasa sings in rapture —
‘vēṅkaṭācala nilayam… vaikuṇṭha puravāsam!’”

@@ 7.9 | “nīnē anātha bandhu” – Raga Kapi |
This whole kirtanam is a deep feeling of surrender.
People, relations and powers can help only up to a limit.
But:
“In the end, only the Lord protects.”
The Dasa makes us feel this through several Puranic events.

Pallavi
nīnē anātha bandhu karuṇā sindhu
You are the true kinsman of the helpless!
O ocean of compassion!
“anātha bandhu” — the support of those who have no one.

Charanam 1
madagajavella kūḍidarēnu adara samayakē odagalilla
Even with all the mighty elephants together,
they could not help in the hour of need.
This points to the liberation of Gajendra.
When the crocodile seized him,
strength was of no use,
the herd could not help.

manmadanayya madhusūdanā endarē mudadinda bandu odagida śrī kṛṣṇa
The moment he called “Madhusudana!”,
Sri Krishna came at once, gladly, and saved him.
The Lord waits for a sincere call.

Charanam 2
patigaḷu aivaru iddarēnu satiya samayakē odagalilla
Though she had five husbands,
they could not help her in her hour of shame.
This refers to the disrobing of Draupadi.

gati nīnē śrī kṛṣṇā endarē ativēgati bandu odagida śrī kṛṣṇa
The moment she called “Krishna! You are my refuge!”,
Sri Krishna came with great speed and saved her.
The power of complete surrender.

Charanam 3
śilaya rakṣisi kulakē tandē
You saved her who had become a stone and brought her back to her family.
This is the uplifting of Ahalya.
She was restored by the darshan of Rama.

baliya golidu satpadavanittu
You subdued Mahabali and gave him a high state.
To Mahabali the Lord gave compassion along with correction.

nambida bhakutara salahuva dēvā
O God who protects the devotees who trust in You!
This is the central line of the song.

celuva purandara viṭhala śrī kṛṣṇa
O beautiful Purandara Vittala! O Sri Krishna!

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Purandara Dasa tells one truth through several Puranic events:
all human help goes only so far.
In the end, the Lord is the one who protects.
When there is true surrender, the Lord blesses at once.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The glory of surrender
What Gajendra, Draupadi and Ahalya have in common:
they called the Lord when they were in the state of “I cannot”.
It was at that moment that grace overflowed.

2. The limits of worldly support
Strength,
family,
position,
knowledge —
all help only up to a point.
The Dasa says:
“nīnē anātha bandhu.”

3. The speed of the Lord's compassion
“ativēgati bandu”
The Lord may seem to delay.
But when there is true surrender, grace comes at once.

4. Compassion that does not abandon even the sinner
Through examples like Ahalya and Mahabali,
he gives the faith that
“the Lord can lift up even one who has erred.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In the world, someone or other may leave us.
But the Lord alone is ‘anātha bandhu’.
He came when Gajendra called…
He came when Draupadi called…
Today, if we too call sincerely, would He not come?”

@@ 7.10 | “murahara nagadhara” – Raga Arabhi |
The central feeling of this kirtanam:
“My final refuge, my final goal, my final support — it is You.”
Throughout the song the Dasa remembers many lilas and qualities of Lord Krishna and surrenders to Him.

Pallavi
murahara nagadhara nīnē gati
O slayer of Mura! O bearer of the mountain (Govardhana)! You are my refuge.
“murahara” — He who conquered the demon Mura.
“nagadhara” — He who held up the mountain.
The Lord is
the destroyer of adharma,
the protector of devotees.

dharaṇī lakṣmīkānta nīnē gati
O Lord of Bhudevi and Mahalakshmi! You are my refuge.

Charanam 1
śakaṭa mardana śaraṇāgata vatsala
O slayer of Shakatasura! O You who lovingly protect those who surrender!
The child Krishna's lila and the protection of the surrendered, in one line.

makara kuṇḍaladhara nīnē gati
O beautiful one wearing fish-shaped earrings! You are my refuge.

Charanam 2
akaḷaṅka carita ādi nārāyaṇa
O primal Narayana, whose story is spotless!
All the Lord's lilas are holy.

rukmiṇi pati kṛṣṇa nīnē gati
O Krishna, Lord of Rukmini! You are my refuge.

Charanam 3
mana manegaḷa pokku kena hālu beṇṇaya pramita metta hari nīnē gati
O Hari, who went from house to house stealing and eating milk and butter! You are my refuge.
This is the sweetness of the childhood lila.
The Supreme is
the highest in Vedanta,
the butter thief in Gokula.

Charanam 4
anudina bhaktara biḍada kāyva kanna mahima kṛṣṇa nīnē gati
O Krishna of wondrous glory, who protects devotees every day without abandoning them! You are my refuge.
The Lord never abandons His devotee.

Charanam 5
pannaga śayana suparṇa gamana
O You who recline on Adishesha! O You who ride Garuda!
The Lord's supreme Vaishnava form.

pūrṇa carita hari nīnē gati
O Hari, full of every auspicious quality! You are my refuge.

Last line
hōnna hoḷayali purandara viṭhala cenna lakṣmīkāntā nīnē gati
O Purandara Vittala, shining like gold! O beautiful Lord of Lakshmi! You are my refuge.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Purandara Dasa
remembers many lilas of Krishna,
and experiences Him as
protector of devotees,
the child,
the Supreme,
the Compassionate One,
the Lord of Lakshmi.
In the end
he surrenders, saying:
“I have no other refuge; You are my refuge.”

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. “nīnē gati” — the peak of devotion
The life of this song is:
“You alone are my support.”
In the world there may be many supports.
But the final support of the soul is the Lord.

2. Childhood lila and supremacy
In one song come
the slaying of Shakatasura,
the Govardhana story,
the butter-stealing lila,
reclining on Adishesha.
This is the sweetness of the Krishna avatara:
“simplicity and the Supreme, together.”

3. Loving protector of the surrendered
“śaraṇāgata vatsala”
The Lord's greatest quality:
never abandoning those who trust Him.

4. The Lord – a God who comes into daily life
Krishna, stealing butter from house to house,
is not a God far away,
but the Lord who comes into the home and plays.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“The same hand that lifted Govardhana…
stole butter in Yashoda's house.
The same Supreme who reclines on Adishesha…
runs every day to protect His devotees.
That is why the Dasa says again and again —
‘nīnē gati… nīnē gati…’”

@@ 7.11 | “mōsa hōdenallō” – Raga Shubhapantuvarali |
This kirtanam is full of deep detachment and regret.
Here the Dasa speaks with anguish, remembering the time wasted in worldly life.
Its central message:
“A life lived without thinking of the Lord is a life cheated.”

Pallavi
mōsa hōdenallō nānu mōsa hōdenallō
Alas! I have been cheated!
I have wasted my life!
This is not regret for worldly failure.
It is regret for having lost the experience of the Lord.

Charanam 1
śēṣa śayananigē sēvakanāgadē
Without becoming a servant of Vishnu who reclines on Adishesha,

vāsudēvanigē dāsanāgadē
without becoming a servant of Vasudeva —
the true identity of human life is being the Lord's servant.

Charanam 2
mallige mulle maruga davana tandu
Bringing flowers like jasmine and mullai, maruga and davana,

pullābjākṣana pūjaya māḍadē
without worshipping the Lord with lotus eyes —
Worship is not flowers alone; it is also offering the mind.

Charanam 3
yauvanadalli śrī haripada nambade
Not trusting in Hari's holy feet in my youth,

mūvattu varuṣa mōhābdhiyali biddu
I lay fallen for thirty years in the ocean of delusion.
“mōhābdhi” — the ocean of samsara, full of desire, ego and worldly bondage.

Charanam 4
satyabhāme sarasana koṇḍāḍadē
Without delighting in the lilas of Krishna with Satyabhama —
A life without experience of the Lord's lilas is empty.

mṛtyubhaṭara kai kaṭṭige guriyāgi
I am about to fall into the hands of the soldiers of death!
The awakening that time is passing and death is coming near.

Charanam 5
lajje biṭṭu nā gajje kaṭṭi koṇḍu
Without casting off shame and tying ankle-bells on my feet,

sajjanarondige sarvadā kuṇiyade
