without always dancing and singing with the good —
Regret for not joining the devotees in the joy of nama sankirtanam.

Last charanam
suravara-taruvāgi susthira-vādande
I did not gain the lasting fruit, like the wish-fulfilling tree of the gods!
Regret that life has not given a lasting spiritual fruit.

purandara viṭhalana caraṇava piḍiyade
because I did not hold on to the feet of Purandara Vittala!
This is the whole reason for the song:
“A life that did not hold on to the Lord was wasted.”

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Purandara Dasa
grieves, remembering a life spent in worldly delusion.
He laments that he did not think of the Lord in his youth,
and that he lost worship, devotion and satsang.
In the end he says:
“A life that does not hold the Lord's holy feet is the real deception.”

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Spiritual awakening
This song is a “wake-up call”.
The Dasa asks:
“What are we spending our life on?”

2. The importance of youth
“yauvanadalli…”
Youth is
the time when there is strength,
the time when the mind is enthusiastic.
The seed of devotion must be sown in that very time.

3. The need for satsang
“sajjanarondige kuṇiyade”
A life without
bhajan,
nama sankirtanam,
and satsang
with devotees dries up.

4. Worldly success ≠ success in life
Even with money, position and fame,
if there is no relationship with the Lord, the mind becomes empty.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“When the Dasa says ‘I have been cheated’, it is not for losing money.
It is for losing the Lord.
He warns us that even if one gains the whole world,
a life without Hari's name is empty.”

@@ 7.12 | “narasiṁha pāda” – Raga Behag |
This kirtanam is a wonderful experience of the Narasimha avatara, in which fierceness and compassion come together.
Its central message:
“If we remember Narasimha's holy feet with devotion,
even mountains of sin will crumble.”

Pallavi
narasiṁha pāda bhajanaya māḍō
Sing the bhajan of Narasimha's holy feet.
“Devotion to the feet” — the sign of surrender.

narasiṁha pāda bhajanaya māḍalu
If you praise Narasimha's holy feet with devotion…

durita parvatava khaṇḍisuva kuliśadantē
it works like the thunderbolt that shatters mountains of sin.
“kuliśa” = Indra's thunderbolt (vajra).
The Lord's name,
though it seems gentle,
has the great power to shatter sins.

Charanam 1
daraḷana morakēḷi tavakatidali bandu
Hearing the cry of Prahlada, He came in haste,
Instant grace at the devotee's cry.

duruḷana karuḷa tan koraḷalli dariṇida
and tore open the belly of the wicked Hiranyakashipu with His claws.
The destruction of adharma.

Charanam 2
surarella naḍugalu
All the gods trembled.
The fierceness of the Narasimha avatara.

siridēvi morayiḍa
Even Mahalakshmi prayed in fear.
The supreme might of the avatara.

varakambadalli banda vaikuṇṭha pati namma
Our Lord of Vaikuntha, who came forth from the pillar!
He came to make true Prahlada's words: “He is everywhere.”

Charanam 3
hari viriñcādigaḷu karavetti mugiyalu
When Brahma and the other gods bowed with folded hands,

parama śāntanāda purandara viṭhala
He became calm, the supremely peaceful Purandara Vittala.
He who took on fierceness for His devotee becomes compassion before His devotee.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Purandara Dasa
sings the glory of the Narasimha avatara.
To protect His devotee, the Lord came forth from a pillar itself.
He destroyed adharma.
The Dasa says that remembering His holy feet destroys sins.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Protection of the devotee
The centre of the Narasimha avatara:
“protecting Prahlada.”
The Lord
did not look at the time,
did not look at the place,
did not look at the form.
He came at once to protect His devotee.

2. “He is in the pillar too”
Prahlada said:
“He is everywhere.”
To make it true,
the Lord came forth from the pillar itself.
This is the principle of the Indweller.

3. Fierceness – only for the devotee's sake
Narasimha's anger is
not against the devotee,
but against adharma.
To the devotee
He is the form of compassion.

4. Devotion to Narasimha's feet
The Dasa says directly:
“The bhajan of Narasimha's feet is enough.”
Those holy feet
remove fear,
shatter sin,
give courage.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Prahlada said, ‘He is here too.’
At once the Lord broke the pillar and came out.
To protect the devotee's faith, the Supreme roared as a lion.
That is why the Dasa says —
‘narasiṁha pāda bhajanaya māḍō!’”

@@ 7.13 | “bārō brahmādi vandyā” – Raga Madhmad Sarang |
This whole kirtanam is a song full of sweet devotion (madhurya bhakti), lovingly inviting Krishna home.
It is like a mother's call, and like a devotee's rapturous plea.
Its central feeling:
“Krishna! Come dancing and singing to my very home!”

Pallavi
bārō brahmādi vandyā
O You who are worshipped by Brahma and the other gods! Come!

bārō vasudēva nandana
O son of Vasudeva! Come!
He calls the Supreme very intimately.

bārō, kṛṣṇa bārō
Come, Krishna! Come!
The simple call of devotion.

Charanam 1
dhigi dhigi nī kuṇidāḍuta bārō dīna rakṣakanē
Come dancing “dhigi dhigi”, O protector of the poor!
Here the Dasa experiences
the dance of the child Krishna,
His childlike joy.

jagadīśa nī kuṇidāḍuta bārō cenna kēśavanē
O Lord of the world! O beautiful Keshava! Come dancing!
Even the Lord of the world comes to the devotee as a child.

Charanam 2
gollara manege pōgalu bēḍa, gōvinda kēḷō
Don't go to the cowherds' houses, Govinda — listen!
This is playful devotion — a loving, pampering complaint.

ninage, hālu beṇṇe mosarikkuvē uṇṇa bārō
I will give You
milk,
butter,
curd —
everything; come and eat right here!
Like Yashoda's feeling, the devotee invites the Lord into the home.

Charanam 3
doḍḍa doḍḍa muttina hāra hāki nōḍuvenō
I will adorn You with big, big pearl necklaces and gaze at You!
The devotee's desire — to adorn the Lord and enjoy Him.

nīnu doḍḍa purada dvāraka vāsa purandara viṭhala
O Purandara Vittala, who dwells in the great city of Dwaraka!
O Lord of Dwaraka, come to my home too!

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Purandara Dasa
lovingly invites Krishna.
He
asks Him to come dancing,
promises Him milk and butter,
longs to adorn Him.
It is filled with the sweet devotion that invites the Supreme into the home.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Sweet devotion (madhurya bhakti)
In this song there is
no fear,
no distance,
no difficult Vedanta.
Only an intimate love.

2. Inviting the Lord into the home
The peak of devotion:
“The Lord who is in the temple must be in my home too.”
This happens through
nama sankirtanam,
pooja,
and satsang.

3. The accessibility of the Supreme
“brahmādi vandyā” — the Supreme whom the gods worship.
Yet
the devotee calls that same Lord: “Come, eat!”
This is the sweetness of Krishna bhakti.

4. The Lord – the form of joy
“dhigi dhigi nī kuṇidāḍuta bārō”
Krishna is the form of
laughter,
dance,
music,
and joy.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“What is the peak of devotion?
Not keeping the Supreme at a distance out of fear…
but the closeness that calls, ‘Krishna, come to my home.’
That is why the Dasa calls like a child —
‘bārō kṛṣṇa bārō!’”

@@ 7.14 | “gōvinda ninna nāmavē” – Raga Janasammodini |
This kirtanam is very short, but the spiritual depth in it is very great.
Its central message:
“Govinda's name itself is sweetness.
Realising the glory of that Name is true bliss.”

Pallavi
gōvinda ninna nāmave candā
O Govinda! Your name itself is beautiful! Sweet!
“candā” — beauty, sweetness, a joy that captivates the mind.
The Dasa
experiences the sweetness of the Name
even more than the form.

gōvinda ninna nāmave candā
O Govinda! How beautiful Your name is!
The repetition — rapture in experiencing the Name.

Charanam 1
aṇurēṇu tṛṇa kāṣṭa paripūrṇa gōvinda
O Govinda, who fills the atom, the speck of dust, the blade of grass and the piece of wood!
The Lord is
not only in a great temple,
but in every atom of the universe.

nirmalātmakanāgi iruvude ānanda
To be pure in heart is true bliss.
The Lord's name purifies the mind.

Charanam 2
sṛṣṭi sthiti laya kāraṇa gōvinda
O Govinda, the cause of creation, preservation and dissolution!
He is
the creator,
the sustainer,
and in the end the one who draws everything into Himself.

ī pari mahimeya tiḷiyuvude ānanda
To realise this glory is bliss.
The bliss of devotion is
not outer pleasure,
but realising the truth of the Lord.

Last charanam
parama puruṣa śrī purandara viṭhalana
Sri Purandara Vittala, the Supreme Person,

hiṅgada dāsara salahuvude ānanda
protecting His servants, whom He never abandons — that itself is bliss.
The Lord
will not let go of His devotees,
and will guide them.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Purandara Dasa
praises the sweetness of Govinda's name.
He says the Lord fills everything.
He says purity of mind and knowledge of the Lord are true bliss.
He concludes that protecting devotees is the Lord's very nature.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Experiencing the Name
“gōvinda ninna nāmavē candā”
For a true devotee,
music is sweet,
the form is sweet,
but the Name is the sweetest.

2. All-pervasiveness
“aṇurēṇu tṛṇa kāṣṭa”
This is the highest Vedanta:
the Lord is in the atom too.
The Upanishadic thought:
“aṇōraṇīyān mahatō mahīyān” — smaller than the smallest, greater than the greatest.

3. Purity of mind is bliss
Today people have
more comforts,
less peace.
The Dasa says:
“To be pure in heart is bliss.”

4. The Lord's love for His devotees
“hiṅgada dāsara”
The Lord
will not leave the devotee halfway,
but will guide him all through life.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“There may be many sweet things in the world.
But all of them change with time.
The sweetness the Dasa found is different —
in the single name ‘Govinda’ he found complete bliss.
That is why he says in rapture —
‘gōvinda ninna nāmavē candā!’”

@@ 8.1 | “smara vāraṁ vāram” – Raga Jog |
In this wonderful kirtanam Sadasiva Brahmendra calls directly to the mind:
“O mind! Remember Nanda's son again and again!”
This song beautifully joins together
Krishna's lilas,
remembrance of the Name,
the principle of Parabrahman,
and the bliss of devotion.

Pallavi
smara vāraṁ vāraṁ cētaḥ
O mind! Remember, again and again!
“smara” — to remember, to meditate, to hold in the heart.

smara nandakumāraṁ vāram
Remember Krishna, the son of Nanda, again and again.
The mind wanders among worldly things.
He turns that same mind towards the remembrance of Krishna.

Charanam 1
ghōṣa kuṭīra payō ghṛta cōram
O thief who stole milk and ghee from the cowherds' huts!
The sweetness of the childhood lila.
The Supreme is
the One sought in the Vedas,
the butter thief of Gokula.

gōkula bṛndāvana sañcāram
O You who roamed Gokula and Brindavan!
Krishna is
not a God far away,
but the Lord who lives mingling with His devotees.

Charanam 2
vēṇu ravāmṛta pāna kaṭhōram
O You who enchant everyone with the nectar of the music of Your flute!
Krishna's flute-song is
a divine call that draws the soul from within.

viśva sthiti laya hētu vihāram
O Supreme, the cause of this world's
creation,
preservation,
and dissolution!
In one line:
the child Krishna,
and Parabrahman.

Charanam 3
paramahaṁsa hṛt-pañjara kīram
O parrot who lives in the cage of the hearts of the Paramahamsas!
A very beautiful image.
Paramahamsas = the realised sages,
hṛt pañjaram = the cage of the heart,
kīram = parrot.
That is:
the Lord who lives sweetly in the hearts of the wise.

paṭutara dhēnuka baka saṁhāram
O hero who destroyed Dhenukasura and Bakasura!
The Lord is
sweet to devotees,
stern to adharma.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Sadasiva Brahmendra
tells the mind to settle in the remembrance of Krishna.
He joins the sweet lilas of the child Krishna
with the truth that He is the Parabrahman, the cause of the world.
He experiences the form of bliss who lives in the hearts of the wise.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. “smara” — the central practice of devotion
The key word in this song is
“smara” — remember.
In Kali yuga
the mind is always thinking of something.
The saint says:
“Turn that thinking towards Krishna.”

2. Lila and Parabrahman
In one song:
the butter thief,
and the Supreme who runs the world.
That is the sweetness of the Krishna principle.

3. The flute-song – the call to the soul
Krishna's flute is
not only music,
but a call that draws the soul to the Lord.

4. “hṛt pañjara kīram” – a wonderful image
The Lord is
not only in the temple;
He lives sweetly in the heart of the wise.
This is a deep spiritual experience.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“The mind is always thinking of something or other.
Sadasiva Brahmendra calls us to turn that very thinking — ‘Remember Nanda's son.’
Because that remembrance
begins as lila, and ends in the experience of Parabrahman.”

@@ 8.2 | “khēlati mama hṛdayē” – Raga Atana |
This kirtanam is the expression of a lofty spiritual experience.
Here Sadasiva Brahmendra experiences in rapture:
“Sri Rama is playing in my heart!”
In this song
devotion,
Vedanta,
the Rama principle,
and the experience of inner bliss
all come together.

Pallavi
khēlati mama hṛdayē śrī rāmaḥ
Sri Rama is playing in my heart!
“khēlati” — plays, moves about joyfully.
This is not mere remembrance;
it is the experience of inner realisation.

khēlati mama hṛdayē
He dwells joyfully in my heart.
The Lord is not someone to be searched for outside;
He is to be experienced within.

Charanam 1
mōha mahārṇava tāraṇakārī
O You who carry us across the great ocean of delusion!
Rama, who brings to shore the soul drowning in the sea of samsara and maya.

rāga dvēṣa mukhāsuramārī
The slayer of demons such as raga (excessive desire) and dvesha (hatred).
The real Ravana is
not outside,
but the lust and anger within the heart.

Charanam 2
śānti vidēhasutā sahacārī
Rama, together with Sita, who is peace itself.
“vidēhasutā” = Sita.
Sita is the form of the power of
peace,
compassion,
and purity.

daharāyōdhyā nagara vihārī
He who moves about in the city of Ayodhya that is the small space of the heart.
The Upanishadic idea of the “dahara akasha”:
the heart itself is the shrine of God.
That is:
the true Ayodhya is our heart.

Charanam 3
paramahaṁsa sāmrājyōddhārī
He who upholds the spiritual kingdom of the Paramahamsas.
Rama as the inner experience of the wise.

satya jñānānanda śarīrī
Whose form is truth, knowledge and bliss.
This is a direct Vedantic line:
satya, jñāna, ānanda.
The nature of the Supreme:
sat — eternal,
cit — knowledge,
ānanda — supreme bliss.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Sadasiva Brahmendra
experiences that Rama lives in his heart.
He describes Rama as
the one who carries us across the ocean of delusion,
the destroyer of desire and hatred,
the form of compassion together with Sita,
the Supreme who lives in the Ayodhya of the heart,
the very form of sat-cit-ananda.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The inner experience of Rama
In this song Rama is
not only in Ayodhya;
He lives in the devotee's heart.

2. The real demons
“rāga-dvēṣa”
Our
desire,
anger,
hatred,
and ego
are the Ravanas within.
Rama's name destroys them.

3. The heart = Ayodhya
“dahara ayōdhyā”
A wonderful Vedanta:
the temple is outside too;
but the true temple is within.

4. Devotion + Advaita
Sadasiva Brahmendra is
an Advaitic sage,
but also a devotee of the highest order.
So in him
the lila-filled devotion to Rama
and the Vedanta of Parabrahman flow together.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“That Rama lived in Ayodhya is history.
That Rama plays in my heart is experience.
It is in that experience that Sadasiva Brahmendra sings in rapture —
‘khēlati mama hṛdayē śrī rāmaḥ!’”

@@ 8.3 | “jaya tuṅga taraṅgē gaṅgē” – Raga Kuntalavarali |
This kirtanam sings the glory of Mother Ganga, the river Ganges.
But it is not just praise of a river.
Here Ganga is described as the symbol of
purity,
liberation,
compassion,
and the flow of wisdom.

Pallavi
jaya tuṅga taraṅgē gaṅgē
O Ganga of high-rising waves! Victory to you!
“tuṅga taraṅgē” — waves that rise high.
They are
not mere waves of water,
but waves of compassion.

jaya tuṅga taraṅgē
O divine Ganga, flowing with lofty waves! Victory to you!

Charanam 1
kamalabhavāṇḍa karaṇḍa pavitrē
O you who sanctified even the water-pot (kamandalu) of Brahma!
Ganga
arose from Vishnu's foot,
rested in Brahma's kamandalu,
and came down to earth.

bahu vidha bandhacchēda lavitrē
O sickle that cuts many kinds of bondage!
The bondage of samsara,
the bondage of sin,
the bondage of ego —
she has the power to remove these.

Charanam 2
dūrīkṛta jana pāpa samūhē
O you who drive away the heaps of people's sins!
Ganga
cleanses not only the body,
but purifies the mind as well.

pūrita kacchapa guccha grāhē
O you who are full of tortoises, crocodiles and other creatures!
A river full of natural abundance and compassion for living beings.

Charanam 3
paramahaṁsa guru bhaṇita caritrē
O you whose glory is sung by the Paramahamsa gurus!
A holiness praised even by the realised sages.

brahma viṣṇu śaṅkara nuti pātrē
O you who are praised by all three — Brahma, Vishnu and Shiva!
Ganga is
a holy power worshipped even by the gods.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Sadasiva Brahmendra
sings the divine glory of Ganga.
He describes her as
the destroyer of sin,
the destroyer of the bonds of samsara,
a holy power,
a divine flow praised by the Trimurti.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Ganga – not just a river
In India's spiritual tradition Ganga is
not only water,
but a flow of divine compassion.

2. “bandhacchēda lavitrē”
Ganga
must not only cleanse the body,
but also dissolve the bonds of the mind.
The true bath in the Ganga is
a change of heart.

3. Ganga and wisdom
A river keeps flowing.
In the same way,
wisdom must not stop,
and compassion must keep flowing.

4. A holiness praised by all three gods
Ganga is connected with
Vishnu's foot,
Shiva's matted locks,
Brahma's kamandalu.
So
she is a divine symbol that unites all the traditions.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Ganga does not flow only from the Himalayas.
For thousands of years she has been flowing within the soul of this Bharata.
That is why Sadasiva Brahmendra sings in rapture —
‘jaya tuṅga taraṅgē gaṅgē!’”

@@ 8.4 | “bhajarē yadunātham” – Raga Pilu |
This kirtanam is a deep confluence of devotion and Vedanta.
Here Sadasiva Brahmendra calls to the mind itself and teaches:
“O mind! Worship Krishna, the Lord of the Yadus!”
In this song come together
the child Krishna,
the devotion of the gopis,
the lila of a human appearance,
and the principle of Parabrahman.

Pallavi
bhajarē yadunāthaṁ mānasa
O mind! Worship Krishna, the Lord of the Yadu clan.
“bhaja” is not mere singing:
it is remembering,
meditating,
loving,
and surrendering.

bhajarē yadunātham
Worship the Lord of the Yadus again and again.

Charanam 1
gōpa vadhū parirambhaṇa lōlam
Krishna, who delights in the embrace of the gopis.
This is not worldly;
it is the devotion of the union of the individual soul with the Supreme.
The gopis are
the form of complete surrender.

gōpa kiśōrakam-adbhuta līlam
Krishna, who as a young cowherd performs wondrous lilas.
The sweetness of the childhood lila.

Charanam 2
kapaṭāṅgīkṛta mānuṣa vēṣam
The Supreme who has put on a human guise.
“kapaṭa” — like a costume in a play.
That is,
Parabrahman is acting as a human being.

kapaṭa nāṭya kṛta kutsita vēṣam
He who wears a humble guise in this play of the world.
The Lord is
not only on the royal throne;
He also comes as a simple cowherd.

Charanam 3
paramahaṁsa hṛt tatva svarūpam
The very truth that shines in the hearts of the Paramahamsas.
He is the Parabrahman experienced by the wise.

praṇava payōdhara praṇava
The support of the supreme principle, the Pranava “Om”.
The essence of the Vedas is “Om”.
Krishna is the very meaning of that Pranava.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Sadasiva Brahmendra
turns the mind to devotion to Krishna.
He joins the sweet lilas of Gopala Krishna
with the truth that He is the principle of Parabrahman.
He experiences the Supreme playing as a human.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. “mānasa” — inner devotion
Devotion is
not just outer ritual,
but a change in the direction of the mind.

2. The devotion of the gopis
“parirambhaṇa lōlam”
This is
not ordinary love,
but complete self-offering.
The gopis are in the state of
“We are Yours.”

3. The Lord – in human guise
“kapaṭāṅgīkṛta mānuṣa vēṣam”
This is a wonderful Vedanta:
the Supreme descends into human life and plays.

4. Krishna = the form of the Pranava
At the end the saint moves from lila to Vedanta.
The butter thief,
the cowherd,
is at the same time
the meaning of the Pranava “Om”.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“That Krishna who plays with the gopis…
shines in the hearts of the wise as the very form of the Pranava.
That is why Sadasiva Brahmendra calls to the mind itself —
‘O mind, worship the Lord of the Yadus!’”

@@ 8.5 | “brūhi mukundēti” – Raga Kurinji |
This whole kirtanam is filled with the joy of remembering the Name.
Here Sadasiva Brahmendra does not call the mind — he calls the tongue directly:
“O tongue! Say ‘Mukunda’!”
This song is a confluence of
the glory of the Name,
devotion to Krishna,
Rama's name,
and the relish of the Vishnu Sahasranama.

Pallavi
brūhi mukundēti rasanē
O tongue! Say “Mukunda”!
“rasanē” = O tongue / O speech.
“Mukunda” —
the giver of liberation.

brūhi mukundēti rasanē
He says it again:
“O tongue! Keep on saying Mukunda.”

Charanam 1
kēśava mādhava gōvindēti
Say “Keshava! Madhava! Govinda!”
Each name is a meditation.

kṛṣṇānanda sadānandēti
Say “Krishna! Form of bliss! Eternal bliss!”
The Lord's name is
not only peace of mind,
but an experience of bliss.

Charanam 2
rādhā ramaṇa harē rāmēti
Say “Beloved of Radha! Hare! Rama!”
Krishna and Rama are the one Supreme.

rājīvākṣa ghanaśyāmēti
Say “O lotus-eyed one! O You of the colour of the rain-cloud!”
Through the Name comes meditation on the form.

Charanam 3
garuḍa gamana nandaka hastēti
Say “O You who ride Garuda! O You who hold the sword Nandaka!”
The Lord's heroic form.

khaṇḍita daśakandhara mastēti
Say “O You who destroyed ten-headed Ravana!”
A remembrance of the Rama avatara.

Charanam 4
akrūra priya cakradharēti
Say “Beloved of Akrura! Bearer of the discus!”
His closeness to His devotees.

haṁsa nirañjana kaṁsa harēti
Say “O form of the Paramahamsa! O spotless one! O slayer of Kamsa!”
Lila and Parabrahman come together again.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Sadasiva Brahmendra
engages the tongue in the Lord's name.
Saying many divine names, he experiences the joy of remembering the Name.
He shows that Krishna, Rama and Mukunda are all the one Supreme.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. A way to discipline the tongue
Controlling the mind is difficult.
But
the tongue can be engaged in the Name.
That is why
nama japa
and nama sankirtanam
are the simplest practice.

2. Name and form together
When we say “Govinda”,
the lila comes to mind,
the form appears in the heart,
devotion grows.

3. Remembering the Name = the path of bliss
The saint does not speak of difficult yoga.
What he says is:
“Keep on saying it.”
That itself is the path to
purity of mind,
devotion,
and liberation.

4. All names belong to the one Supreme
Keshava,
Madhava,
Rama,
Krishna,
Mukunda…
The names are many;
but the Supreme is one.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“The mind keeps running.
But if we give the tongue a job, the mind too begins to change.
That is why Sadasiva Brahmendra calls the tongue itself and says —
‘rasanē! mukundēti brūhi!’”

@@ 8.6 | “pibarē rāmarasam” – Raga Ahir Bhairavi |
This kirtanam is the peak experience of the nectar of the Name.
Here Sadasiva Brahmendra gives a wonderful invitation to the tongue:
“Drink the nectar of Rama's name!”
This is not just a song —
it is an experience of spiritual nectar.

Pallavi
pibarē rāmarasaṁ rasanē
O tongue! Drink the nectar of Rama's name!
“pibarē” = drink.
He does not only say “say it”.
He says: “Taste Rama's name like nectar.”

pibarē rāmarasam
Drink the nectar of Rama's name again and again.
Remembering the Name is
not a duty,
but an experience of bliss.

Charanam 1
dūrīkṛta pātaka saṁsargam
Rama's name removes even contact with sin.
Rama's name
not only washes away sin,
but also weakens the tendency to sin.

pūrita nānāvidha phalavargam
It grants many kinds of fruit:
peace of mind,
devotion,
wisdom,
liberation.

Charanam 2
janana maraṇa bhaya śōka vidūram
It removes birth, death, fear and sorrow.
Rama's name
gives courage to the mind,
and lessens the fear of samsara.

sakala śāstra nigamāgama sāram
It is the essence of all
the shastras,
the Vedas,
and the Agamas.
The summary of the Vedas — remembering the Name.

Charanam 3
paripālita sarasija garbhāṇḍam
The Supreme who protects the universe created by Brahma, born of the lotus.

parama pavitrīkṛta pāṣaṇḍam
A power that purifies even those of other paths and those who have gone astray.
Rama's name rejects no one.

Charanam 4
śuddha paramahaṁsāśrama gītam
The Name sung by the pure Paramahamsas.
(Variation: “paramahaṁsāśrita” — the Name in which the Paramahamsas take refuge.)
Remembering the Name is necessary even for the wise.

śuka śaunaka kauśika mukha pītam
The nectar drunk by sages such as Shuka, Shaunaka and Vishvamitra (Kaushika).
Rama's name is
not ordinary japa,
but the supreme nectar the sages experienced.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Sadasiva Brahmendra
asks us to experience Rama's name as nectar.
He describes it as
the destroyer of sin,
the remover of fear,
the essence of the Vedas,
the supreme nectar praised by the wise.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The Name = nectar (rasa)
“rāmarasam”
Devotion is
not dry philosophy,
but an experience of rasa.

2. Rama's name – a simple practice
Even one who cannot study all the Vedas
can say “Rama”.
That is why
the Name itself is the Veda of Kali yuga.

3. The Name that removes fear
“janana maraṇa bhaya śōka vidūram”
The deepest fears of a person:
death,
separation,
impermanence.
Rama's name
gives inner courage.

4. The wise too chanted the Name
Some think:
“Nama japa is for beginners.”
But the saint says
sages like
Shuka,
Shaunaka,
and Kaushika
experienced this very nectar.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Just as we cannot live without drinking water,
the soul dries up without the nectar of the Name.
That is why Sadasiva Brahmendra pleads with the tongue —
‘rasanē! Drink the nectar of Rama!’”

@@ 8.7 | “mānasa sañcararē” – Raga Sama |
This kirtanam is deeply meditative.
Here Sadasiva Brahmendra calls directly to the mind:
“O mind! Move about in Parabrahman!”
In this song
Krishna's beauty,
the music of His flute,
devotion,
and meditation on Parabrahman
all blend together.

Pallavi
mānasa sañcara rē brahmaṇi
O mind! Move about in Parabrahman.
“sañcara” = roam, abide, direct the mind.
The mind
must stop wandering among worldly things
and settle in Brahman.

mānasa sañcara rē
O mind! Wander joyfully in that divine contemplation.

Charanam 1
mada śikhi piñchālaṅkṛta cikurē
In Krishna, whose hair is adorned with a peacock feather.
The sweet form of the child Krishna.

mahanīya kapōla vijita mukurē
Whose lovely cheeks outshine even a mirror.
The Lord's beauty is
not only outer beauty,
but a joy that draws the mind.

Charanam 2
śrī ramaṇī kuca durga vihārē
O Supreme, who plays in the fortress of Mahalakshmi's heart!
“śrī ramaṇī” = Lakshmi.
The Lord is
the form of compassion, united with Lakshmi's grace.

sēvaka jana mandira mandārē
O You who shine as the mandara flower in the homes of Your devotees!
The devotee's home
becomes a temple,
and the Lord dwells there.

Charanam 3
paramahaṁsa mukha candra cakōrē
He who is like the chakora bird that delights in the moon-like faces of the Paramahamsas.
A wonderful image.
Just as the chakora bird delights in moonlight,
the Lord delights in the bliss on the faces of the wise.

paripūrita muraḷī rava dhārē
O Supreme, overflowing with the music of the flute!
Krishna's flute is
divine music that draws the soul.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Sadasiva Brahmendra
turns the mind to meditation on Parabrahman.
He describes Krishna's divine form, the music of His flute, His bond with Lakshmi, and His closeness to devotees.
He experiences Krishna as the blissful experience of the Paramahamsas.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Changing the direction of the mind
The centre of this song:
“Where is the mind roaming?”
The mind that wandered in the world
must settle in Brahman.

2. Meditation through beauty
Sadasiva Brahmendra
first speaks of the beauty of Krishna's form,
then leads us to the state of Parabrahman.
This is
the path from devotion to the form to the formless truth.

3. The devotee's home is a temple
“sēvaka jana mandira mandārē”
The Lord is
not only in a great temple,
but in the devotee's heart and home.

4. The flute-song – the call to the soul
The flute is
not only music,
but the voice of the Supreme calling the soul from within.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“The mind is always wandering somewhere.
Sadasiva Brahmendra gently turns it —
‘O mind, move about in Brahman.’
And who is that Brahman?
That very Krishna with the peacock feather, playing the flute!”

@@ 8.8 | “sarvaṁ brahmamayam” – Raga Madhuvanti |
This kirtanam is entirely the peak experience of Advaita Vedanta.
Here Sadasiva Brahmendra speaks from the state of a supreme sage:
“Everything is full of Brahman!”
This is
not mere philosophical argument,
but the expression of experienced wisdom.

Pallavi
sarvaṁ brahmamayaṁ rē rē
Ah! Everything is full of Brahman!
“sarvam” = everything,
“brahmamayam” = filled with the Supreme.

sarvaṁ brahmamayam
Nothing can be seen as separate; in everything is the same Brahman.
The Upanishadic thought:
“sarvaṁ khalvidaṁ brahma.”

Charanam 1
kiṁ vacanīyaṁ kimavacanīyam
What is to be spoken? What is to be left unspoken?
If everything is Brahman,
speech too is Brahman,
silence too is Brahman.

kiṁ racanīyaṁ kimaracanīyam
What is to be made? What is to be left unmade?
In the state where there is no second,
the very difference between action and non-action dissolves.

Charanam 2
kiṁ paṭhanīyaṁ kimapaṭhanīyam
What is to be studied? What is to be left unstudied?

kiṁ bhajanīyaṁ kimabhajanīyam
What is to be worshipped? What is to be left unworshipped?
If everything is He,
what is there to leave, and what to attain?

Charanam 3
kiṁ bōddhavyaṁ kimabōddhavyam
What must be known? What need not be known?

kiṁ bhōktavyaṁ kimabhōktavyam
What must be enjoyed? What must not be enjoyed?
Once the state of wisdom comes,
the division of
“this is the world”,
“this is God”
dissolves.

Last charanam
sarvatra sadā haṁsa dhyānaṁ kartavyam
Everywhere, always, one should meditate on the Paramahamsa principle.
“haṁsa” is
the symbol of the principle of the Supreme Self.
“haṁ-sa” — the contemplation of “sō'ham” (I am That).

bhō mukti nidānam
That itself is the cause of liberation.
Liberation is
not going to another world,
but the state of seeing Brahman in everything.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Sadasiva Brahmendra
expresses the Advaitic experience that “everything is Brahman”.
All pairs of opposites — in action, knowledge, devotion and experience — dissolve.
He says that meditation that sees the Supreme everywhere is the path to liberation.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The summary of Advaita
This one line is an entire Upanishad:
“sarvaṁ brahmamayam.”

2. The dissolving of duality
We divide things:
this is good,
this is bad,
this is spiritual,
this is worldly.
What the sage sees is
the same Supreme in everything.

3. Wisdom = a change of vision
The world does not change.
The vision changes.

4. Hamsa meditation
The swan (hamsa)
is said to have the power to separate milk from water.
In the same way the sage
leaves what is impermanent
and knows the eternal.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“The devotee sees the Lord in the temple.
The sage sees Him in everything.
In the rapture of that experience Sadasiva Brahmendra declares —
‘sarvaṁ brahmamayaṁ rē rē!’”

@@ 9.1 | “āḍum cidambaramō” – Raga Behag |
In this wonderful Tamil kirtanam, Gopalakrishna Bharati sings in rapture of the darshan of the Ananda Tandava (dance of bliss) of Nataraja at Chidambaram.
In this song
the secret of Chidambaram (Chidambara Rahasyam),
the dance of bliss,
devotion to Shiva,
and the glory of the Name
are all blended.

Pallavi
āḍum cidambaramō ayyan kūttāḍum cidambaramō
Is this the Chidambaram where He dances?
The Chidambaram where our Lord performs His dance?
“āḍum” — the dance of bliss.
“kūttu” — the cosmic dance.
This is not just a dance:
it is the symbol of the five divine acts —
creation,
preservation,
dissolution,
concealment,
and grace.

Anupallavi
āḍum cidambaram anbar kaḷikkavē
The Chidambaram where He dances so that His devotees may rejoice.
The Lord's dance is
not out of anger,
but to give joy to His devotees.

nāḍum cidambaram namaccivāyavenru
The Chidambaram that is sought with the cry “Namah Shivaya”.
Chidambaram is
not only a temple,
but the very life of the mantra “Namah Shivaya”.

Charanam
yārum ariyāmal ambala vāṇanār
The Lord of the Hall (Ambalavanar), whom no one can fully know.
The “secret of Chidambaram” —
a divine experience that the intellect cannot fully grasp.

sīraḍiyār pārkka sēvaḍi tūkki ninru
He stands with His holy foot raised, so that His devotees may see.
Nataraja's raised foot is
the symbol of liberation.

bālakṛṣṇan pōṭrum panimadi saḍaiyinār
He with the cool crescent moon in His matted locks, praised by Balakrishna.
Here “Balakrishna” is the composer's signature (mudra).
“panimadi” = the cool crescent moon.
Shiva, with Ganga and the moon in His locks.

tāḷa mattaḷam pōḍa tāttattai yenavē
To the beat of cymbals and drum, He dances to the syllables “tāttattai”.
This is a direct vision of the dance of the Lord of the Hall (Sabhanayaka).

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Gopalakrishna Bharati
describes the dance of bliss of Nataraja of Chidambaram.
He also joins with it the glory of the name “Namah Shivaya”.
He experiences Shiva as the compassionate one who dances for His devotees.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Chidambaram – the outer hall and the inner hall
Chidambaram is
not only a town;
“cit + ambaram” —
the space of consciousness.
That is,
when the heart is pure, Nataraja dances right there.

2. The dance of bliss
Shiva's dance is
not only destruction,
but the movement of the universe.
The world itself is a cosmic dance.

3. “Namah Shivaya” – the glory of the five-syllable mantra
Throughout this song,
Name and form come together.

4. The raised holy foot
“sēvaḍi tūkki ninru”
Nataraja's raised foot is the symbol of
surrender,
the destruction of ego,
and liberation.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“It is not only Shiva who dances in Chidambaram.
When the ego in our mind dissolves, bliss begins to dance in the heart.
That is why Gopalakrishna Bharati asks in rapture —
‘āḍum cidambaramō!’”

@@ 9.2 | “eppō varuvārō” – Raga Senjurutti |
This kirtanam is an expression of very deep devotion of longing.
Here Gopalakrishna Bharati sings the inner voice of a devotee:
“When will He come?
When will my sorrow end?”
It is a song that melts
the longing for the Lord's darshan,
the thirst of the soul,
and inner devotion to Shiva.

Pallavi
eppō varuvārō endan kalitīra
When will He come, so that my sorrow may end?
“kali” = suffering, anguish of mind, the weariness of samsara.
This is not ordinary waiting:
it is a thirst for darshan.

ceppiyadillai cidambaranādan
The Lord of Chidambaram has not told me when He will come!
The devotee's sweet complaint.

Charanam 1
naṟparuvam vandu nādanait tēḍum
The right time of life has come; now I seek the Lord.
The true purpose of human life:
the search for God.

kaṟpanaigaḷ muṭra kāṭci tandāḷa
He must give me darshan, so that all my imaginings come to an end.
When the Lord is realised,
the wandering of the mind settles.

Charanam 2
aṟpasuga vāzhvil ānandam koṇḍēn
I spent my time rejoicing in the petty pleasures of worldly life.
The devotee's regret:
“Time was wasted on impermanent things.”

poṟpadattaik kāṇēn ponnambalavāṇan
I have not seen the golden feet of the Lord of the Golden Hall.
“ponnambalavāṇan” = Nataraja.
Even with worldly pleasures,
the emptiness of having no experience of God.

Charanam 3
bālakṛṣṇan pōṭrip paṇindiḍum īsan
The Lord whom Balakrishna praises and bows to.
“Balakrishna” — the composer's signature.

mēlē kādal koṇḍēn veḷippaḍak kāṇēn
I have great love for Him; but I cannot see Him openly.
This is
the peak of longing in devotion —
the state of “He is in my heart, but I want His direct darshan.”

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Gopalakrishna Bharati
expresses the mind of a devotee longing for the darshan of Nataraja of Chidambaram.
He realises that worldly pleasures are impermanent.
In the end he says that the Lord's darshan alone is true bliss.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The sign of devotion – longing
In true devotion
knowledge alone is not enough;
there must be longing.
Throughout this song:
“a thirst for darshan.”

2. The emptiness of worldly pleasure
“aṟpasuga vāzhvil ānandam koṇḍēn”
A person
may have gained many things,
yet the heart may still be empty.
Why?
Because there is no experience of God.

3. Darshan = peace of mind
“kaṟpanaigaḷ muṭra”
The mind
keeps running with
thoughts,
worries,
and desires.
The experience of the Lord
gives the mind fulfilment.

4. Inner devotion to Shiva
This is not a song of sorrow.
It is
the melting of the devotee's heart,
the call of a soul that must unite with Shiva.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In the world we seek many people.
But one day the weary mind begins to seek God.
It is in that thirst of the soul that these lines are born —
‘eppō varuvārō… endan kalitīra…’”

@@ 10.1 | “tava dāsōham” – Raga Punnagavarali |
This kirtanam is entirely the essence of the devotion of surrender.
Here Tyagaraja melts before Rama and says:
“I am Your servant!”
This is not just a word — it is the state of offering one's whole life.

Pallavi
tava dāsōhaṁ tava dāsōham
I am Your servant! I am Your servant!
“tava” = Your,
“dāsōham” = I am a servant.
This is
the complete surrender of the ego.

dāśarathē
O Rama! Son of Dasharatha!
Rama is
the form of dharma,
the form of compassion.

Charanam 1
vara mṛdu bhāṣa virahita dōṣa naravara vēṣa
O sweet-spoken one! O faultless one! O You who appeared in human form!
Rama is
gentle in speech,
pure in character,
and in appearance, like a man.

dāśarathē
O Rama!

Charanam 2
sarasija nētra parama pavitra
O most holy one with lotus eyes!
“Lotus eyes” —
the sign of compassion.

surapati mitra dāśarathē
O friend of Indra!
Rama is
a helper even to the gods.

Charanam 3
ninnu kōritirā nirupama śūra
I desire You alone, O matchless hero!
The devotee's state of mind:
nothing else is needed.

nannēlukōrā dāśarathē
Accept me, O Rama!
Complete surrender.

Charanam 4
manavini vinumā marava samayamā
Hear my plea; is this a time to forget me?
The devotee's
eager waiting.

inakula dhanamā dāśarathē
O treasure of the solar dynasty!
Rama is
the protector,
the treasure of dharma.

Charanam 5
ghana sama nīla muni janapāla
O You who are dark as the rain-cloud! O protector of the sages!
Rama's beauty + compassion.

kanaka dukūla dāśarathē
O You who wear golden silk!
The divine, kingly form.

Charanam 6
dhara nīvaṇṭi daivamu lēdaṇṭi
I have said there is no God like You in this world!
A declaration of complete devotion.

śaraṇanu koṇṭi dāśarathē
I have taken refuge in You!
This is the peak of devotion.

Last charanam
āgama vinuta rāga virahita
O You who are praised by the Vedas! O You who are free from attachment!
Rama is
the essence of the Vedas,
free from likes and dislikes.

tyāgarājanuta dāśarathē
O Rama, praised by Tyagaraja!
Tyagaraja humbles himself
and exalts Rama above himself.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Tyagaraja
expresses the state of complete surrender.
He experiences Rama as
the form of compassion,
the form of dharma,
the Supreme God.
He says that “I am Your servant” is the peak of devotion.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. “dāsōham” – the destruction of ego
The first step of devotion:
the thought “I am the doer” fades.

2. Rama – the Supreme in human form
Rama is
not a God far away,
but the embodiment of dharma who lived just as we do.

3. Surrender = complete freedom
“I have taken refuge in You.”
This is
putting down the burden of the mind,
complete trust.

4. The nature of Tyagaraja
Tyagaraja is
not only a musician,
but a sage of surrender.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“However high a person rises, as long as the ego of ‘I’ remains, peace does not come.
The moment that ‘I’ dissolves and says ‘I am Your servant’ is the beginning of spirituality.
That is what Tyagaraja turns into music —
‘tava dāsōhaṁ, dāśarathē!’”

@@ 10.2 | “śrī rāma śrī rāma” – Raga Sahana |
This kirtanam is the melting prayer of a devotee.
Here Tyagaraja asks Rama with longing and with a sense of right:
“Rama! Why have You still not shown compassion?”
In this song
the devotee's anguish,
the right he feels before God,
surrender,
and faith
all come together.

Pallavi
śrī rāma śrī rāma śrī manōharamā
O Sri Rama! O beautiful one who captivates hearts!
“manōhara” —
the one who steals the mind.

ēlarā nīdaya intaina rādayā
Why has Your compassion not come even after so long?
The devotee's melting question.

Charanam 1
cāladā sadaya svāmi tāḷadayā
O compassionate Lord! Still more delay?
The longing of “How long must I wait?”

ippuḍē lēdaṭa ikhanu brōtuvaṭa
Will You protect me at least now, or not?
This is not anger;
it is the cry of devotion that feels it has a right.

Charanam 2
eppuḍō kaṭakaṭa ika dayāḷuvaṭa
When will You show compassion?
The devotee's mind
experiences both faith
and longing
at the same time.

inka ī marmamā idi nīku dharmamā
What is this secret? Is it Your dharma to behave like this?
Tyagaraja's
intimacy in speaking directly to God.

Charanam 3
paṅkaja vadanamā bāguga jūḍumā
O lotus-faced one! Look at me kindly, just once.
The longing for the Lord's gracious glance.

ē janma pāpamō evvari śāpamō
The sin of which birth is this? Whose curse is this?
The devotee looks within himself for the cause of life's sorrow.

Charanam 4
ē nāṭi kōpamō nēriya nā pāpamō
Anger from which day? Or is it my sin?
The devotee's frame of mind: “What wrong have I done?”

ennāḷḷi dīnata idi nīku yōgyatā
My misery for so many days — is this worthy of You?
The devotee
places his sorrow before God with a sense of right.

Charanam 5
paliki poṅgavaṭa parama śāntuḍavaṭa
They say You are the very form of supreme peace.

bhakta kāntuḍaṭa padma nētruḍaṭa
They say You are the beloved of devotees, the lotus-eyed one.
The devotee:
“If so, show compassion to me!”

Charanam 6
sarvamu nīvaṭa satya rūpuḍaṭa
You are everything; You are the very form of Truth.
Devotion slowly rises to the state of Vedanta.

rāga virahita tyāgarājanuta
O Rama, praised by Tyagaraja, who is free from attachment!
In the end
the devotee dissolves again in surrender.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Tyagaraja
longs for Rama's compassion.
He shares his sorrow with a sense of right.
He searches for the cause of life's suffering.
In the end he realises that Rama Himself is the form of Truth.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. A sense of right in devotion
A true devotee
does not speak only in fear;
he speaks with a sense of belonging.

2. Honesty in prayer
Tyagaraja
does not hide his anguish.
He says directly:
“I am suffering.”

3. Sorrow → contemplation
In this song,
sorrow is the beginning,
and at the end comes philosophical realisation.

4. Devotion = relationship
Rama is
not a God far away,
but an intimate God one can talk to.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“The highest state of devotion is this —
to take even the anguish of the mind to God Himself.
That is why Tyagaraja weeps, asks, and in the end surrenders —
‘śrī rāmā… ēlarā nīdaya…’”

@@ 10.3 | “pāhi kalyāṇa rāma” – Raga Kharaharapriya |
This whole kirtanam expresses Rama's glory and the devotee's complete dependence on Him.
Here Tyagaraja experiences the state of
“Without Rama I have nothing.”
This song is a wonderful kirtanam that blends
devotion,
surrender,
detachment,
and Vedanta.

Pallavi
pāhi kalyāṇa rām
O auspicious Rama! Protect me!
“kalyāṇa rāma” —
the giver of auspiciousness,
the one who purifies life.

pāvana guṇa rām
O Rama, form of holy qualities!
Rama is the embodiment of
dharma,
compassion,
and peace.

Charanam 1
nā jīvādhāramu
You are the support of my life.

nā śubhākāramu
You are the auspicious form of my life.

nā nōmu phalamu
You are the fruit of my vows.

nāmēnu balamu
You are the strength of my body.
The devotee
links every part of his life with Rama.

Charanam 2
nā vaṁśa dhanamu
You are the wealth of my family.

nādaidō tanamu
You are my true possession.
Worldly wealth is impermanent;
devotion to Rama alone is lasting.

nā cittānandamu
You are the joy of my mind.

nā sukha kandamu
You are the root cause of my happiness.

Charanam 3
nādu santōṣamu
You are my happiness.

nā muddu vēṣamu
You are the beauty that captivates me.

nā manōharamu
You are the Lord who steals my heart.

nādu śṛṅgāramu
You are my spiritual adornment.
The devotee's whole heart is filled with Rama.

Charanam 4
nā pāli bhāgyamu
You are my great good fortune.

nādu vairāgyamu
You are also the cause of my detachment.
True detachment is
not hatred of the world,
but fulfilment in the experience of God.

nādu jīvanamu
You are my very life.

nādu yauvanamu
You are my enthusiasm and vitality.

Charanam 5
āgama sāramu
You are the essence of all the Agamas.

asura dūramu
You remove demonic qualities.

mullōkādhāramu
You are the support of the three worlds.

mutyāla hāramu
You are beautiful as a necklace of pearls.

Charanam 6
dēvādi daivamu
You are the God of the gods.

durjanā bhāvamu
You destroy the arrogance of the wicked.

paramaina brahmamu
You are the Supreme Brahman.

pāpēbha siṁhamu
You are like a lion to the elephant of sin.
The heroic power that destroys sins.

Charanam 7
idi nirvikalpamu
This is truth beyond doubt.

īśvara janmamu
This is the glory of the Lord's incarnation.

idi sarvōnnatamu
This is the highest truth.

idi māyātītamu
This is the state beyond maya.

sāgara guptamu
A secret deep as the ocean.

tyāgarājāptamu
The supreme truth that Tyagaraja experienced.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Tyagaraja
experiences Rama as everything in his life.
He says Rama is
his life,
his joy,
his wealth,
his detachment,
the Supreme Brahman.
In the end he says that devotion to Rama is the way beyond maya.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. The state of “everything is Rama”
Throughout this song
each line begins with “my…”.
But in the end:
“everything is Rama.”

2. True wealth
“nā vaṁśa dhanamu”
For Tyagaraja
wealth is not money,
but devotion to Rama.

3. Devotion → detachment
Detachment does not come by hating the world.
When the bliss of God increases, worldly desire decreases.

4. Rama = the Supreme Brahman
In the end Tyagaraja
rises from Rama the avatara
to Rama the Parabrahman.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“A devotee praying to God is the beginning.
But one day he realises — ‘God is my life.’
It is in that state of complete devotion that Tyagaraja melts —
‘nā jīvādhāramu… kalyāṇa rāmā!’”

@@ 10.4 | “marugēlarā” – Raga Jayantashri |
This kirtanam is the expression of a very deep spiritual experience.
Here Tyagaraja asks Rama:
“Why are You hiding, Rama?”
But the depth of this song is that
in the end he realises:
“He is not hidden; He is in everything.”

Pallavi
marugēlarā ō rāghavā
O hero of the Raghus! Why are You hiding?
“marugēlarā” = why do You hide?
This is
the devotee's longing,
the search for the experience of God.

Anupallavi
marugēla carācara rūpa parātpara
O Supreme, who are everything that moves and does not move! Why do You hide?
“carācara rūpa” —
living beings,
the world,
the universe —
all are His form.

sūrya sudhākara lōcana
O You whose eyes are the sun and the moon!
A Vedantic image:
the sun = wisdom,
the moon = compassion.

Charanam
anni nīvanucu antaraṅgamuna
Searching within my heart, knowing “You are everything”,
Tyagaraja
does not search outside;
he searches within.

dinnagā vedaki telisi koṇṭinayyā
I searched deeply and came to know.
The experience of God comes
not through book knowledge,
but through inner seeking.

ninnegāni madinenna jāla norula
Apart from You, whom can I think of?
The peak of devotion:
the mind settling on One alone.

tannu brōvavayya tyāgarājanuta
O Rama, praised by Tyagaraja! Protect me!
In the end
wisdom
and devotion
become one in surrender.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Tyagaraja
searches for Rama.
Then he realises that He is in everything.
He says that the experience of God comes through inner seeking of the soul.
In the end he settles in surrender to Rama.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Is God hidden?
We ask:
“Where is God?”
Tyagaraja's answer:
“He does not hide; it is our mind that is covered.”

2. Outer search → inner search
The turning point of this song:
“antaraṅgamuna” — within.
Inner seeking is itself
meditation,
devotion,
and wisdom.

3. The all-pervading Rama
“carācara rūpa”
Rama is
not only in Ayodhya;
He is in everything.

4. Devotion and Vedanta becoming one
This kirtanam
begins with the longing of devotion,
and ends in the Advaitic experience.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“People search the whole world for where God is hiding.
But one day the mind turns within.
Then it becomes clear —
‘It is not He who was hidden… it was our ignorance that hid Him.’
It is in that experience that Tyagaraja calls —
‘marugēlarā ō rāghavā!’”

@@ 10.5 | “kamalāpta kula” – Raga Brindavana Saranga |
This kirtanam is a prayer of surrender that melts with longing for compassion.
Here Tyagaraja pleads with Rama very gently:
“O Rama, ocean of compassion! Protect me!”
In this song
Rama's glory,
the devotee's fear,
the longing for compassion,
and surrender
are all beautifully expressed.

Pallavi
kamalāpta kula kalaśābdhi candra
O moon that rose from the ocean of the solar dynasty!
“kamalāpta kula” = the solar dynasty (the sun is the friend of the lotus).
“kalaśābdhi candra” = the moon that brightens that dynasty.
This points to Rama's
glorious lineage,
gentleness,
and divine beauty.

kāvavayya nanu karuṇā samudra
O ocean of compassion! Protect me!
The devotee
places his trust not in his own strength,
but in God's compassion.

Charanam 1
kamalākalatra kausalyā suputra
O Lord of Lakshmi! O noble son of Kausalya!
Rama is the avatara in whom
the divine
and human dharma
are blended together.

kamanīya gātra kāmāri mitra
O You of beautiful form! O friend of Shiva!
“kāmāri” = Shiva (the enemy of Kama).
This
recalls the oneness of Hari and Hara.

Charanam 2
munu dāsula brōcinadella cāla vini
I have heard of all Your glories in protecting the devotees of old.
The devotee
gains faith by hearing the Puranas.

nī caraṇāśrituḍai-tinayya
So I have taken refuge at Your holy feet.
“caraṇam” means
protection,
peace,
complete handing over.

Charanam 3
kani karambuna nākabhayam-iyyavayyā
With Your compassionate hand, grant me freedom from fear!
“abhayam” —
fearlessness,
spiritual protection.

vanaja lōcana
O lotus-eyed one!
A glance full of compassion.

śrī tyāgarāja vinuta
O Rama, praised by Tyagaraja!

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Tyagaraja
praises Rama as the ocean of compassion.
Because Rama protected the devotees of old, he too surrenders.
In the end he asks for “abhayam”.
He says devotion to Rama is itself protection.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Trust in compassion
The foundation of devotion:
not “I am worthy”,
but “He is compassionate.”

2. Why are the Puranas important?
“munu dāsula brōcina”
The stories of devotees of old
give faith,
and grow devotion.

3. Abhayam – a person's deepest need
Inside everyone there is
fear,
insecurity,
impermanence.
What the devotee asks for is
“abhayam.”

4. The oneness of Hari and Hara
“kāmāri mitra”
This line shows the traditional spiritual view that
Shiva and Vishnu are not different.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In the world a person seeks protection from many people.
But after realising that everything is impermanent, in the end he falls at God's feet.
This song is that cry of surrender —
‘karuṇā samudrā… kāvavayya nanu…’”

@@ 10.6 | “jō jō jō rāma” – Raga Ritigowla |
This kirtanam is a lullaby.
But it is no ordinary lullaby —
Tyagaraja experiences the Rama dwelling in his heart as a child, and sings Him a lullaby.
This song holds
vatsalya bhakti (parental devotion),
Rama's divine glory,
and the wonderful experience that
Parabrahman and the child Rama are one.

Pallavi
jō jō jō rāma ānandaghana
Sleep, sleep, Rama! O You who are full of bliss!
“ānandaghana” —
a mass of bliss,
the form of complete bliss.

jō jō daśaratha bāla rāma
O child Rama of Dasharatha! Sleep!
Here the devotee
sees Rama not as the Supreme,
but as his own child.

jō jō pūja lōla rāma
O Rama who loves worship! Sleep!
The compassionate one who accepts the love of His devotees.

Charanam 1
jō jō raghukula tilaka rāma
O Rama, jewel of the Raghu clan! Sleep!
Rama is
the glory of His lineage,
the light of dharma.

jō jō kuṭila tarālaka rāma
O Rama, beautiful with curly locks! Sleep!
A mother's devotion that delights in the beauty of the child Rama.

Charanam 2
jō jō nirguṇa rūpa rāma
O Rama, the attributeless Supreme! Sleep!
Nirguna Brahman —
Parabrahman beyond all qualities.

jō jō suguṇa kalpa rāma
O Rama, wish-fulfilling tree of all auspicious qualities! Sleep!
Advaita and devotion become one:
nirguna (without attributes)
and saguna (with attributes)
are both the same Rama.

Charanam 3
jō jō ravi śaśi nayana rāma
O Rama, whose eyes are the sun and the moon! Sleep!
Rama in His cosmic form.

jō jō phaṇivara śayana rāma
O You who recline on Adishesha! Sleep!
A remembrance of Mahavishnu sleeping on Adishesha.

Charanam 4
jō jō mṛdutara bhāṣa rāma
O gentle-spoken Rama! Sleep!
Rama's peaceful nature.

jō jō mañjuḷa vēṣa rāma
O Rama of lovely appearance! Sleep!
The devotee
delights in every aspect of Rama.

Last charanam
jō jō tyāgarājārcita rāma
O Rama worshipped by Tyagaraja! Sleep!

jō jō bhakta samāja rāma
O Rama who shines amid the gathering of devotees! Sleep!
Rama is
not only a personal God,
but the life of the community of devotees.

Brief meaning
In this kirtanam Tyagaraja
experiences Rama as a child and sings Him a lullaby.
At the same time he says that Rama is the attributeless Parabrahman.
He brings together devotion, parental love, and Vedanta.

Significance to share in an upanyasam
1. Vatsalya bhakti
Here the devotee
does not fear God;
he embraces Him as a child.

2. Nirguna and saguna
The greatest philosophy of this song:
“nirguṇa rūpa rāmā”,
“suguṇa kalpa rāmā”.
That is,
the formless Supreme
and Rama with form
are not different.

3. Lullaby = meditation
Devotees
are not really putting God to sleep;
they are calming the wandering of their own minds.

4. Rasa in devotion
Tyagaraja's devotion is
not only teaching,
but experience,
rasa,
and love.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“The Vedas search for the Supreme.
But what does the devotee do?
He places that very Supreme on his lap as a child and sings a lullaby.
That is the wonder of devotion.
That is why Tyagaraja sings in rapture —
‘jō jō jō rāmā… ānandaghanā…’”

@@ 11.18 | “rāghavā rāmā rāghavā rāmā” – Divyanama Sankirtanam |
Type – Divyanama Sankirtanam / Namavali

The Divyanama
rāghavā rāmā rāghavā rāmā rāghavā rāmā rāghavā ॥
gōvindā harē gōvindā harē gōvindā harē gōvindā ॥
gōpālā harē gōpālā harē gōpālā harē gōpālā ॥
śaṅkarā śiva śaṅkarā śiva śaṅkarā śiva śaṅkarā ॥
ṣaṇmukhā śiva ṣaṇmukhā śiva ṣaṇmukhā bāla ṣaṇmukhā ॥
sadgurō jaya sadgurō jaya sadgurō jaya sadgurō ॥
viṭhala hari viṭhala hari viṭhala hari viṭhala ॥

Detailed meaning of each Name

1. “rāghavā rāmā”
Word meanings
rāghavā – born in the Raghu dynasty
rāmā – the giver of joy
Detailed meaning
Sri Rama, who incarnated as the jewel of the Raghu dynasty and established dharma in the world.
Spiritual meaning
The name “Rama” gives peace of mind and steadiness in dharma.

2. “gōvindā harē”
Word meanings
gōvindā – the Lord who protects all beings
harē – the remover of sins
Detailed meaning
O Govinda, who removes the sorrows and sins of His devotees!
Spiritual meaning
The name “Govinda” is the name of surrender.

3. “gōpālā harē”
Word meanings
gōpālā – the protector of the cowherds and the cows
Detailed meaning
Krishna, who lived among simple devotees and protected them.
Spiritual meaning
The Lord is not only for the great; He is very close to simple hearts.

4. “śaṅkarā śiva śaṅkarā”
Word meanings
śaṅkarā – the one who does good
śiva – the auspicious one
Detailed meaning
Lord Shiva, who gives auspiciousness and spiritual good to the world.
Spiritual meaning
The name of Shiva gives peace and detachment to the mind.

5. “ṣaṇmukhā bāla ṣaṇmukhā”
Word meanings
ṣaṇmukhā – Murugan, who has six faces
bāla ṣaṇmukhā – Murugan in the form of a child
Detailed meaning
Bala Murugan, full of wisdom and valour.
Spiritual meaning
Murugan is the God of wisdom; the power that conquers ego.

6. “sadgurō jaya”
Word meanings
sadguru – the true Guru
jaya – victory, glory
Detailed meaning
Victory to the Sadguru, who leads souls on the path of wisdom.
Spiritual meaning
In nama sankirtanam, the Guru and God cannot be separated.

7. “viṭhala hari”
Word meanings
viṭhala – Panduranga Vittala
hari – the remover of sins
Detailed meaning
The compassionate Vittala, who stands at Pandharpur for the sake of His devotees.
Spiritual meaning
The name of Vittala is the sign of devotion, simplicity and nama sankirtanam.

Overall essence of the whole Divyanama
This Divyanama joins in one flow of sankirtanam the names of many forms of God:
Rama,
Krishna,
Shiva,
Murugan,
the Sadguru,
Vittala.

Key spiritual highlight
The highest idea this song conveys:
“Whichever name we say,
what we reach is the one Supreme.”
This is the heart of the Divyanama tradition.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“There is a wonderful spiritual unity in this Divyanama.
Rama, Govinda, Shiva, Shanmukha, Vittala —
though the names are many, the divine bliss experienced is one.
That is why in nama sankirtanam there is no quarrel; there is only confluence.
Whichever name a devotee says with love,
that very name takes him to the Supreme.”

@@ 11.19 | “śrī rāmā śrī rāmā” – Raga Kedaram |
Type – Divyanama Sankirtanam

The Divyanama

Pallavi
śrī rāmā śrī rāmā sūrya kulāmbudhi sōma maṅgaḷa dhāmā ērē ॥

Word meanings
śrī rāmā – auspicious Rama
sūrya kula – the solar dynasty
ambudhi sōma – shining like the moon rising from the ocean
maṅgaḷa dhāmā – the abode of auspiciousness
ērē – come / bless us

Detailed meaning
O auspicious Sri Rama, who shines like the moon in the solar dynasty! Come and bless us.

Spiritual meaning
Rama's name gives peace and auspiciousness to the devotee's mind.

Charanam 1
sukhadhāmā sukhadhāmā pūrṇa manōratha kāma mēgha śyāmā ērē ॥

Word meanings
sukhadhāmā – the abode of bliss
pūrṇa manōratha kāma – the one who fulfils good wishes
mēgha śyāmā – the one with a body dark as a rain-cloud, like a blue gem

Detailed meaning
O form of bliss, dark as the rain-cloud, who fulfils the good wishes of devotees! Come.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's darshan gives fulfilment and joy to the mind.

Charanam 2
śāntāyana śāntāyana kamala daḷāyata nayana ahipati śayana ērē ॥

Word meanings
śāntāyana – the form of peace
kamala daḷāyata nayana – eyes like long lotus petals
ahipati śayana – the one who reclines on Adishesha

Detailed meaning
O Narayana, who reclines on Adishesha and blesses peacefully with lotus-like eyes! Come.

Spiritual meaning
Meditation on the Lord's peaceful form gives peace of mind.

Charanam 3
dattātrēya dattātrēya dūrīkṛta bhava tāpatraya adbhuta gātra ērē ॥

Word meanings
dattātrēya – Lord Dattatreya
bhava tāpatraya – the three kinds of suffering of worldly life
dūrīkṛta – the one who removes
adbhuta gātra – the one with a wondrous divine form

Detailed meaning
O Dattatreya of wondrous form, who removes the sufferings of samsara! Bless us.

Spiritual meaning
Dattatreya is regarded as the symbol of the Guru principle.

Charanam 4
gōvinda gōvinda sujana manōratha svānanda śrīmukha canda ērē ॥

Word meanings
gōvinda – the Lord who protects all beings
sujana manōratha – the one who fulfils the wishes of the good
svānanda – natural bliss
śrīmukha canda – a face like the moon

Detailed meaning
O moon-faced Govinda, who creates bliss in the hearts of the good! Come.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's name is like moonlight that cools the mind.

Final nama sankirtanam
ērē ērē ērē rāmā mēghaśyāmā maṅgaḷa dhāmā ॥
ērē ērē ērē kṛṣṇā pāṇḍuraṅgā paṇḍharīnāthā ॥
hari viṭhala ērē guru viṭhala ērē ॥
guru viṭhala ērē prabhu viṭhala ērē ॥

Word meanings and meaning
pāṇḍuraṅgā – Vittala, who stands for His devotees
paṇḍharīnāthā – the Lord of Pandharpur
guru viṭhala – Vittala in the form of the Guru
prabhu viṭhala – the Lord of the world

Detailed meaning
O Supreme, who blesses as Rama, as Krishna, as Panduranga and as the Guru! Come to us.

Overall essence of the whole Divyanama
This song joins in one flow of bliss
devotion to Rama,
devotion to Krishna,
the Dattatreya principle,
the sankirtanam of Vittala's name,
and devotion to the Guru.

Key spiritual highlight
The central feeling of this Divyanama:
“The Lord may come in many forms;
but the devotee's call is one —
‘ērē’ — ‘Come!’”
This is the beautiful longing of devotion.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Throughout this Divyanama a devotee's call rings out — ‘ērē!’
That is, ‘Come to me!’
Though we call the Lord in many forms —
as Rama, as Krishna, as Vittala, as the Guru —
the longing in the devotee's heart is one.
That longing is the very life of nama sankirtanam.”

@@ 11.20 | “rāmabhadra rārā” – Raga Anandabhairavi |
Type – Divyanama Sankirtanam

The Divyanama

Pallavi
rāmabhadra rārā śrī rāmacandra rārā tāmarasa lōcana sītā samēta rārā ॥

Word meanings
rāmabhadra – auspicious Rama
rārā – come
tāmarasa lōcana – the one with lotus-like eyes
sītā samēta – together with Sita

Detailed meaning
O Sri Ramachandra, who blesses with lotus-like eyes, together with Sita! Come.

Spiritual meaning
Here the devotee calls the Lord not as a distant God, but as a loving relation welcomed into the home.

Charanam 1
muddu muddugā rārā nava mōhanāṅga rārā ॥

Word meanings
muddu muddugā – very sweetly
nava mōhanāṅga – the one who captivates with ever-new beauty

Detailed meaning
O Rama, who captivates the mind with ever-new divine beauty! Come sweetly.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's beauty never grows old with time; the more we experience it, the newer it becomes.

Charanam 2
addampu cēkkiḷḷavāḍa ambujākṣa rārā ॥

Word meanings
cekkiḷḷavāḍa – the one with rosy cheeks
ambujākṣa – the one with lotus eyes

Detailed meaning
O beautiful Rama, with rosy cheeks and lotus eyes! Come.

Spiritual meaning
On the path of devotion, experiencing the Lord's beauty is very important.

Charanam 3
paṭṭarāṇi prēma nā paṭṭukomma rārā ॥

Word meanings
paṭṭarāṇi – Sita, the crowned queen
prēma – love
paṭṭukomma – embrace me

Detailed meaning
Come and embrace me, with the loving compassion of Sita.

Spiritual meaning
The experience of the Lord is the embrace of love.

Charanam 4
gaṭṭiga kausalya muddu baṭṭi vēga rārā ॥

Word meanings
kausalya muddu baṭṭi – Kausalya's darling son
vēga rārā – come quickly

Detailed meaning
O beloved son of Kausalya! Come to us quickly.

Spiritual meaning
The devotee feels Rama as a member of his own family.

Charanam 5
mujjagamulagun ādi mūla brahma rārā ॥

Word meanings
mujjagamulu – the three worlds
ādi mūla brahmam – the Supreme

Detailed meaning
O Supreme, the support of the three worlds! Come.

Spiritual meaning
Here the child Rama Himself is raised to the status of Parabrahman.

Charanam 6
gajjala cappuḷḷu ghallu ghallu managa rārā ॥

Word meanings
gajjala cappu – the sound of anklets
ghallu ghallu – the jingling sound

Detailed meaning
Come walking beautifully, with Your anklets jingling.

Spiritual meaning
Here devotion blends with music, dance and beauty.

Charanam 7
sāmagāna lōla nā cakkanayya rārā ॥

Word meanings
sāmagāna lōla – the one who loves the chanting of the Sama Veda
cakkanayya – the handsome Lord

Detailed meaning
O handsome Lord who loves the chanting of the Vedas! Come.

Spiritual meaning
Worship through sound (nadopasana) and devotion to the Lord are one.

Charanam 8
rāmadāsu nēlina bhadrācalēśa rārā ॥

Word meanings
rāmadāsu – Bhadrachala Ramadas
nēlina – protected / blessed
bhadrācalēśa – the Lord of Bhadrachalam

Detailed meaning
O Rama of Bhadrachalam, who protected Ramadas with Your grace! Come.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord never abandons a true devotee.

Overall essence of the whole Divyanama
This song lovingly calls Rama as
the form of beauty,
the compassionate one who blesses together with Sita,
Kausalya's darling son,
Parabrahman,
the protector of devotees.

Key spiritual highlight
The central feeling of this song:
“Even calling the Lord is an experience of bliss.”
The call “rārā” fills the whole song with the longing of devotion.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In this song the devotee does not call Rama merely as a God;
he calls Him as a beloved guest welcomed into the home.
The one word ‘rārā’ — ‘come’ — is the life of the whole song.
As child Rama, as Sita Rama, as Parabrahman, as the protector of devotees —
this Divyanama lets us experience Rama in many moods.
So when we sing this song,
the devotee's heart itself becomes Ayodhya.”

@@ 11.21 | “bēga bārō” – Raga Mand |
Type – Divyanama Sankirtanam

The Divyanama

Pallavi
bēga bārō bēga bārō nīla mēgha varṇā
bēga bārō bēga bārō vēlāpurada cenna ॥

Word meanings
bēga bārō – come quickly
nīla mēgha varṇā – the one with the colour of a dark rain-cloud
vēlāpurada cenna – the beautiful Lord who blesses at Velapura (Belur)

Detailed meaning
O Lord, shining beautifully with the colour of the dark rain-cloud! Come quickly to bless us.

Spiritual meaning
The devotee's longing to see the Lord in his heart is expressed with great intensity.

Charanam 1
indira ramaṇa gōvinda bēga bārō
nandana kanda mukunda bēga bārō ॥

Word meanings
indirā ramaṇa – the Lord of Lakshmi
gōvinda – the Lord who protects all beings
nandana kanda – the son of Nandagopa
mukunda – the giver of liberation

Detailed meaning
O Lord of Lakshmi, darling son of Nandagopa, Mukunda who gives liberation! Come.

Spiritual meaning
Krishna is experienced both as a child and as the principle of the Supreme.

Charanam 2
dhīra udhāra gambhīra bēga bārō
hāra alaṅkāra raghuvīra bēga bārō ॥

Word meanings
dhīra – the courageous one
udhāra – the generous, compassionate one
gambhīra – the majestic one
raghuvīra – Rama, the hero of the Raghu dynasty

Detailed meaning
O hero of the Raghus, full of courage, compassion and majesty! Come adorned, and bless us.

Spiritual meaning
Lord Rama is the majestic form of dharma.

Charanam 3
raṅga utuṅga narasiṅgha bēga bārō
gaṅgeya paḍada pāṇḍuraṅga bēga bārō ॥

Word meanings
raṅga – the Lord of Srirangam
utuṅga – the exalted one
narasiṅgha – the Narasimha avatara
pāṇḍuraṅga – Vittala

Detailed meaning
O Lord who blesses as the exalted Narasimha and as Panduranga! Come.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord appears in many incarnations to protect His devotee.

Charanam 4
hayyā vijaya sahāya bēga bārō
uragādri vāsa hayavadana bēga bārō ॥

Word meanings
vijaya sahāya – the one who stands by us for victory
uragādri vāsa – the one who dwells on the hill
hayavadana – Hayagriva

Detailed meaning
O Lord who, as Hayagriva, the form of wisdom, gives victory to devotees! Come.

Spiritual meaning
Hayagriva is the deity who is the foundation of wisdom.

The spiritual beauty of the words “bēga bārō”
The whole life of this song is
“bēga bārō” — “Come quickly!”
This is no ordinary call.
It is the expression of
the devotee's longing,
the thirst for the Lord's darshan,
the melting of the heart,
and the joy of nama sankirtanam.

Overall essence of the whole Divyanama
In this song many forms of Vishnu —
Krishna,
Rama,
Narasimha,
Panduranga,
Hayagriva —
are joined in one flow of devotion.

Key spiritual highlight
The central truth this song conveys:
“The Lord may be in many forms;
but the devotee's call is one —
‘Come!’”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In this ‘bēga bārō’ Divyanama the whole longing of the devotee's heart rings out.
He calls the Lord as Rama, as Krishna, as Narasimha, as Panduranga.
But in every line there is only one feeling —
‘Without You I have no peace; come quickly!’
This is the secret of supreme bliss in Divyanama Sankirtanam.”

@@ 11.22 | “dēva bandā namma” – Divyanama Sankirtanam |
Type – Divyanama Sankirtanam (Purandara Dasa)

The Divyanama

Pallavi
dēva bandā namma raṅga bandānō
dēvara dēva śikhāmaṇi bandānō ॥

Word meanings
dēva bandā – God has come
namma raṅga – our Ranganatha
dēvara dēva – the God of the gods
śikhāmaṇi – the one who shines as the crest-jewel

Detailed meaning
Our Ranganatha has come! The supreme God, the chief of the gods, has come to bless us!

Spiritual meaning
Here the devotee announces the Lord's arrival with joyful excitement.

Charanam 1
uraga śayana bandā garuḍa gamana bandā
narahari rūpa śrī nārāyaṇa bandā ॥

Word meanings
uraga śayana – the one who reclines on Adishesha
garuḍa gamana – the one who comes riding Garuda
narahari rūpa – the Narasimha avatara
śrī nārāyaṇa – Narayana together with Mahalakshmi

Detailed meaning
Narayana, who reclines on Adishesha, who comes on Garuda, who took the form of Narasimha — He has come.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord is both the peaceful form and the heroic protector of devotees.

Charanam 2
daśaratha bāla bandā daśamukha kāla bandā
daśavidha rūpa bandā sītā samēta bandā ॥

Word meanings
daśaratha bāla – Rama, the son of Dasharatha
daśamukha kāla – the one who destroyed Ravana
daśavidha rūpa – the one who took the ten incarnations
sītā samēta – together with Sita

Detailed meaning
Sita Rama, who incarnated as Dasharatha's son and destroyed Ravana — the Supreme of the ten avataras — has come.

Spiritual meaning
The Rama avatara is the symbol of the protection of dharma.

Charanam 3
nigama gōcara bandā nitya tṛptanu bandā
nagumukha purandara viṭhala bandānō ॥

Word meanings
nigama gōcara – the one known through the Vedas
nitya tṛptanu – the one who is always in complete bliss
nagumukha – the one with a smiling face
purandara viṭhala – Vittala, praised by Purandara Dasa

Detailed meaning
Purandara Vittala, praised by the Vedas, blessing joyfully with a smiling face, has come.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord does not make the devotee afraid; He is the compassionate one who comes near with a smile.

The devotional feeling of the word “bandā”
The centre of this song is
“bandā” — “He has come!”
This is not just an announcement of arrival.
It is the expression of
the devotee's great joy,
the rapture of seeing God,
the excitement of nama sankirtanam,
a flood of experience of the Lord.

Overall essence of the whole Divyanama
This song experiences the Lord in many forms:
Ranganatha,
Narayana,
Narasimha,
Rama,
the Lord of the ten avataras,
Vittala.

Key spiritual highlight
The main truth this song conveys:
“The Lord is not far away.
When the devotee calls, He ‘has come’.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In this Divyanama the devotee is not calling the Lord;
he is joyfully announcing that He has come.
When we sing ‘dēva bandā’ — ‘The Lord has come!’ —
the devotee's heart overflows with rapture.
So this song is not just a kirtanam;
it is a burst of the joy of the Lord's darshan.”

@@ 11.23 | “yēhi gōpāla” – Divyanama Sankirtanam |
Type – Divyanama Sankirtanam

The Divyanama

Pallavi
yēhi gōpāla śrī vanamālā rādhā lōla āyāhi ॥

Word meanings
yēhi / āyāhi – come
gōpāla – Krishna, protector of the cowherds and the cows
vanamālā – the one who wears a garland of forest flowers
rādhā lōla – the one who loves Radha

Detailed meaning
O Gopala, wearing the forest garland, the beloved of Radha! Come and bless us.

Spiritual meaning
Here the devotee calls Krishna as the very form of love.

Refrain
harē nanda kumāra āyāhi kṛṣṇa navanīta cōra āyāhi ॥

Word meanings
nanda kumāra – the son of Nandagopa
navanīta cōra – the butter thief

Detailed meaning
O child Krishna, darling son of Nandagopa, the butter thief! Come.

Spiritual meaning
“Butter thief” points to Krishna who steals the hearts of His devotees.

Charanam 1
mēgha śyāmaḷa mōhana rūpa muraḷīdhara āyāhi ॥

Word meanings
mēgha śyāmaḷa – dark as a rain-cloud
mōhana rūpa – the form that captivates the mind
muraḷīdhara – the one who holds the flute

Detailed meaning
O Krishna, dark as the rain-cloud, who enchants the world playing the flute! Come.

Spiritual meaning
Krishna's music is the divine sound that draws the soul to the Supreme.

Charanam 2
kaṁsa dhvaṁsana kāḷiya mardana kanakāmbaradhara āyāhi ॥

Word meanings
kaṁsa dhvaṁsana – the one who destroyed Kamsa
kāḷiya mardana – the one who subdued the serpent Kaliya
kanakāmbaradhara – the one who wears golden silk

Detailed meaning
O heroic Krishna, who destroyed Kamsa and subdued Kaliya! Come.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord destroys the devotee's ego and demonic tendencies.

Charanam 3
paṅkaja caraṇa paṅkajanābha paṅkaja nayana āyāhi ॥

Word meanings
paṅkaja caraṇa – feet like lotuses
paṅkajanābha – the one from whose navel the lotus arose
paṅkaja nayana – the one with lotus eyes

Detailed meaning
O Pankajanabha, with lotus-like feet and eyes! Come.

Spiritual meaning
The lotus image points to the Lord's purity, beauty and compassion.

Charanam 4
(kṛṣṇa) prēmikā bhāvuka pūjita caraṇa brahmādi vandya āyāhi ॥

Word meanings
prēmikā bhāvuka – devotees full of love
pūjita caraṇa – the feet that are worshipped
brahmādi vandya – the one worshipped by Brahma and the other gods

Detailed meaning
O Krishna, worshipped by loving devotees and by Brahma and the gods! Come.

Spiritual meaning
The highest qualification for reaching the Lord is not knowledge, but love.

The spiritual beauty of the word “āyāhi”
The whole life of this song is
“āyāhi” — “Come!”
It is the expression of
the devotee's longing for God,
the thirst for Krishna's darshan,
the rapture of nama sankirtanam,
the call of love.

Overall essence of the whole Divyanama
This song experiences Krishna in many moods:
the child Krishna,
the flute-player,
the beloved of Radha,
the destroyer of demons,
the Supreme Self.

Key spiritual highlight
The truth this Divyanama conveys:
“If we call Krishna with love,
He will come and stand in the devotee's heart.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Throughout this ‘yēhi gōpāla’ Divyanama the devotee's loving call rings out.
He calls Krishna ‘butter thief’, ‘flute-player’, ‘subduer of Kaliya’.
But in every line the feeling is one —
‘Krishna! Come and dwell in my heart!’
This is the sweet secret of devotion to Krishna.”

@@ 11.24 | “ambē gaurī maiyā” – Raga Mand |
Type – Devi Divyanama Sankirtanam

The Divyanama

Pallavi
ambē gaurī maiyā jaya maṅgaḷa mūrti maiyā ॥

Word meanings
ambē – O Mother
gaurī – Goddess Parvati
maiyā – Mother
jaya – victory be
maṅgaḷa mūrti – she whose form is auspicious

Detailed meaning
O Mother Gauri, form of auspiciousness! Victory to you.

Spiritual meaning
The Devi is experienced as a Mother full of compassion and auspiciousness.

Charanam 1
tumko niśidina gāvata harē brahma śivatī ॥

Word meanings
niśidina – night and day
gāvata – they sing
brahma śiva – Brahma and Shiva

Detailed meaning
Brahma and Lord Shiva sing your praise night and day.

Spiritual meaning
Without the power of the Devi (Shakti), no divine action takes place.

Charanam 2
māṅga sindūra virājita ṭīkā ṭīkā mṛdu mada kō ॥

Word meanings
sindūra – vermilion (kumkum)
virājita – brightly adorned
ṭīkā – the mark on the forehead

Detailed meaning
O Devi, shining with a lovely forehead adorned with a kumkum mark!

Spiritual meaning
Kumkum is the symbol of Shakti, auspiciousness and divine feminine power.

Charanam 3
kānana kuṇḍala śōbhita nāsāgrē mōti ॥

Word meanings
kuṇḍala – earrings
nāsāgrē mōti – a pearl ornament on the tip of the nose

Detailed meaning
O Mother, wearing beautiful earrings and a pearl nose-ornament!

Spiritual meaning
Here the Devi's divine beauty is described through the eyes of devotion.

Charanam 4
kōṭika candra divākara rājata rājata sama jyōti ॥

Word meanings
kōṭika candra – crores of moons
divākara – the sun
sama jyōti – equal radiance

Detailed meaning
O you whose divine radiance is like the light of crores of suns and moons!

Spiritual meaning
The Devi is Parashakti — the root cause of all light.

Charanam 5
tāvata pīta jaṭānē nārī nārī hari hārī ॥

Word meanings
jaṭā – matted locks
hari hārī – the consort of Hara (Shiva)

Detailed meaning
O Devi, who shines as the power of Lord Shiva, the companion of the One with matted locks!

Spiritual meaning
Shiva and Shakti are a principle that cannot be separated.

Charanam 6
ujjala tējō unnayanā candra vadananīkō ॥

Word meanings
ujjala tējas – bright radiance
candra vadana – a face like the moon

Detailed meaning
O compassionate Mother, shining with a moon-like face!

Spiritual meaning
The Devi's face gives peace and coolness to the devotee's mind.

Overall essence of the whole Divyanama
This song praises Gauri Devi as
the form of auspiciousness,
the Mother of the world,
Shiva's Shakti,
the great radiance,
the queen of beauty,
the compassionate Mother.

Key spiritual highlight
The central feeling of this Divyanama:
“The Devi is not a frightening power;
she is the Mother-power who gives love and auspiciousness.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This ‘ambē gaurī maiyā’ Divyanama does not show the Devi only as Parashakti;
it lets us experience her as a loving Mother.
She is the auspicious one adorned with kumkum,
the moon-faced one, Shiva's Shakti, the Mother of the world.
So when we sing this song,
the devotee is not calling a deity —
he is calling his own Mother.”

@@ 11.25 | Nāma Mahimai Ślōkams – Verses on the glory of the Name |
Type – Verses explaining the glory of the Name
Main idea – the greatness of the sankirtanam of the Lord's name in Kali yuga

1. visṛjya lajjāṁ yōdhītē mannāmāni nirantaram
kulakōṭi samāyuktō labhatē māmakaṁ padam ॥

Word meanings
visṛjya lajjām – casting off shyness
mat nāmāni – My names
nirantaram – without a break
kulakōṭi samāyuktaḥ – together with crores of generations
māmakaṁ padam – My supreme abode

Detailed meaning
He who chants My names without a break, free of all shyness, reaches My supreme abode together with many generations of his family.

Spiritual meaning
Nama sankirtanam is not only a personal benefit; it uplifts the whole family line.

2. viṣṇōr gānaṁ ca nṛtyaṁ ca naṭanaṁ ca viśēṣataḥ
brahman brāhmaṇa jātīnāṁ kartavyaṁ nitya karmavat ॥

Word meanings
viṣṇōr gānam – singing the name of Vishnu
nṛtyam / naṭanam – joyful dance
nitya karmavat – like a daily duty

Detailed meaning
Singing Vishnu's name, dancing in joy and doing kirtan with devotion should be observed like a daily duty.

Spiritual meaning
Devotion is not only quiet meditation; it is also joyful expression.

3. kālakṣēpō na kartavyaḥ kṣīṇamāyuḥ kṣaṇē kṣaṇē
yamasya karuṇā nāsti kartavyaṁ hari kīrtanam ॥

Word meanings
kālakṣēpa – wasting time
kṣīṇamāyuḥ – life is getting shorter
yamasya karuṇā nāsti – Yama, the lord of death, will not wait
hari kīrtanam – the sankirtanam of Hari's name

Detailed meaning
Every moment life is growing shorter. Death does not delay. So, without wasting time, we must sing Hari's name.

Spiritual meaning
Nama sankirtanam is the urgent spiritual practice of life.

4. nāhaṁ vasāmi vaikuṇṭhē na yōgihṛdayē ravau
madbhaktā yatra gāyanti tatra tiṣṭhāmi nārada ॥

Word meanings
nāhaṁ vasāmi vaikuṇṭhē – I do not dwell only in Vaikuntha
madbhaktā yatra gāyanti – wherever My devotees sing
tatra tiṣṭhāmi – there I stand

Detailed meaning
“O Narada! I am not only in Vaikuntha. Wherever My devotees sing My name, there I am.”

Spiritual meaning
