The place where nama sankirtanam happens is itself Vaikuntha.

5. harēr nāmaiva nāmaiva nāmaiva mama jīvanam
kalau nāstyēva nāstyēva nāstyēva gatiranyathā ॥

Word meanings
harēr nāmaiva – Hari's name alone
mama jīvanam – is my life
kalau – in Kali yuga
gatir anyathā nāsti – there is no other way

Detailed meaning
In Kali yuga, Hari's name alone is life; there is no other path to liberation.

Spiritual meaning
The dharma of Kali yuga is nama sankirtanam.

6. bhaktāssaṅgamya ca anyōnyaṁ dīpaṁ madhyē nidhāya ca
jyōtirmayaṁ māṁ dhyāyantaḥ kīrtayantaśca madguṇān ॥
pradakṣiṇaṁ prakurvantaḥ bhū pradakṣiṇajaṁ phalam
labhantē nātra sandēhaḥ vinatā nanda vardhana ॥

Word meanings
bhaktāssaṅgamya – devotees gathering together
dīpaṁ madhyē nidhāya – placing a lamp in the middle
madguṇān kīrtayantaḥ – singing My qualities
bhū pradakṣiṇajaṁ phalam – the merit of going round the whole earth

Detailed meaning
If devotees gather together before a lamp, sing the Lord's name and go round in pradakshina, they will gain the merit of circling the whole earth.

Spiritual meaning
Group nama sankirtanam has a wonderful power.

7. nāma saṅkīrtanaṁ yasya sarva pāpa praṇāśanam
praṇāmō duḥkha śamanaḥ taṁ namāmi hariṁ param ॥

Word meanings
nāma saṅkīrtanam – the kirtan of the Lord's name
sarva pāpa praṇāśanam – destroys all sins
praṇāmaḥ – bowing down
duḥkha śamanaḥ – removes sorrow

Detailed meaning
I bow to that Supreme Hari, whose nama sankirtanam destroys all sins, and bowing to whom removes all sorrows.

Spiritual meaning
The Name and the prostration — both are simple ways to liberation.

Overall essence of all the Nama Mahimai verses
These verses say:
nama sankirtanam is the highest practice of Kali yuga;
the Lord is present wherever His name is sung;
the Name destroys sins;
the Name removes sorrow;
the Name uplifts the whole family line;
group bhajan gives very great merit.

Key spiritual highlight
The central truth of these verses:
“In Kali yuga, the simplest, highest and surest path to reach the Lord is nama sankirtanam.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“These verses on the glory of the Name show us the simplest spiritual path in Kali yuga.
No great yagas are needed, no hard austerities, no rare yogic practices.
‘Hari's name’ is enough.
The Lord Himself says — ‘Where My devotees sing My name, there I am.’
So nama sankirtanam is not just music;
it is the spiritual practice that brings the Lord Himself to us.”

@@ 11.26 | “dhanya dhanya hō pradakṣiṇā” – Divyanama Sankirtanam |
Type – Guru bhakti abhang / nama sankirtanam bhajan song

The Divyanama

Pallavi
dhanya dhanya hō pradakṣiṇā sadgurū rāyācī
– mājhē śrī gurū rāyācī ॥

Word meanings
dhanya dhanya – blessed, holy
pradakṣiṇā – going round (circumambulation)
sadgurū rāyācī – of the Sadguru, the king among gurus
mājhē – my

Detailed meaning
Going round my Sadguru is most holy and blessed.

Spiritual meaning
Worshipping the Guru equals all forms of worship of God.

Charanam 1
jhālī tvarā suravarā vimāna utarāyācī ॥

Word meanings
suravarā – the gods
vimāna – divine aerial chariot
utarāyācī – came down

Detailed meaning
The gods hurry down in their divine chariots to see this devotion to the Guru.

Spiritual meaning
Devotion to the Guru is a lofty practice praised even by the gods.

Charanam 2
padōpadī apāra jhālyā puṇyācyā rāśī
sarvahī tīrthē ghaḍalī āmhā ādikarūni kāśī ॥

Word meanings
padōpadī – at every step
puṇyācyā rāśī – heaps of merit
sarvahī tīrthē – all the holy places
kāśī – the holy city of Kashi

Detailed meaning
At every step of going round the Guru, boundless merit is gained; the fruit of all pilgrimages, beginning with Kashi, is attained.

Spiritual meaning
Devotion to the Guru is higher than all pilgrimages.

Charanam 3
mṛdaṅga ṭāḷa ḍhōla bhakta bhāvārthē gātī
nāma saṅkīrtanē brahma (nitya) ānandē nācatī ॥

Word meanings
mṛdaṅga ṭāḷa ḍhōla – mridangam, cymbals and drum
nāma saṅkīrtanē – in nama sankirtanam
brahma ānandē – in supreme bliss
nācatī – they dance

Detailed meaning
With the mridangam and cymbals sounding, the devotees sing and dance in supreme bliss in nama sankirtanam.

Spiritual meaning
True devotion expresses itself as joy.

Charanam 4
kōṭī brahma hatyā haritī karitā daṇḍavata
lōṭāṅgaṇa ghālitā mōkṣa lōḷē pāyāta ॥

Word meanings
kōṭī brahma hatyā – crores of sins
daṇḍavata – full prostration
mōkṣa – liberation

Detailed meaning
If we make a full prostration to the Guru, crores of sins are removed; through complete surrender, liberation is gained.

Spiritual meaning
True prostration is letting go of the ego.

Charanam 5
guru bhajanācā mahimā na kaḷē āgamā nigamānsī
anubhava tē jāṇatī jē guru padiñcē rahivāsī ॥

Word meanings
āgamā nigamānsī – even to the Agamas and Vedas
mahimā na kaḷē – the glory cannot be fully known
anubhava – experience

Detailed meaning
The glory of devotion to the Guru cannot be fully described even by the Vedas; only those who have experienced it can know it.

Spiritual meaning
The experience of the Guru is beyond book knowledge.

Charanam 6
pradakṣiṇā karūni dēha bhāvē vāhilā
śrī raṅgātmaja viṭhala puḍhē ubhā rāhilā ॥

Word meanings
dēha bhāvē vāhilā – offered up even the sense of the body
viṭhala – Panduranga
ubhā rāhilā – stood right there

Detailed meaning
To the devotee who went round the Guru in complete surrender, Vittala gives darshan in person.

Spiritual meaning
Through the Guru one gains the darshan of the Lord.

Overall essence of the whole abhang
This abhang says:
going round the Guru is the greatest merit;
nama sankirtanam gives supreme bliss;
devotion to the Guru removes sins;
the Guru's grace is the way to the darshan of the Lord.

Key spiritual highlight
The central truth of this abhang:
“One who has reached the Guru has reached all the holy places.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This abhang beautifully brings out the supreme glory of devotion to the Guru.
It says going round the Guru is higher than a pilgrimage to Kashi.
It says the gods themselves come down to see the devotees singing and dancing in nama sankirtanam.
In the end, by the Guru's grace, Vittala gives darshan in person.
This is the secret of the path of devotion —
the Guru is the bridge that takes us to the Lord.”

@@ 11.27 | “nāmāvaḷi” – Divyanama Sankirtanam |
Type – Divyanama namavali / sankirtanam chant

The Namavali

Line 1
nārada prahlāda aura bhakta bṛnda gāyā ॥

Word meanings
nārada – the sage Narada
prahlāda – Prahlada
bhakta bṛnda – the gathering of devotees
gāyā – sang

Detailed meaning
Narada, Prahlada and the other devotees sang the Lord's name together.

Spiritual meaning
Nama sankirtanam is the path walked by sages and great devotees.

Line 2
jaya gōvinda jaya gōpāla mādhava yadurāyā ॥

Word meanings
jaya – victory be
gōvinda – the Lord who protects all beings
gōpāla – Krishna of the cowherds
mādhava – the Lord of Lakshmi
yadurāyā – the king of the Yadu clan

Detailed meaning
O Govinda! O Gopala! O Madhava! O king of the Yadus! Victory to You.

Spiritual meaning
In many different names, the one Supreme is experienced.

Line 3
mādhava yadurāyā madhura nāma dhēyā ॥

Word meanings
madhura nāma – the sweet name
dhēyā – to be meditated upon / chanted

Detailed meaning
Madhava's sweet name is to be meditated upon always.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's name is nectar to the soul.

Line 4
bhakta bṛnda gāyā paramānanda pāyā ॥

Word meanings
bhakta bṛnda – the gathering of devotees
paramānanda – supreme bliss
pāyā – attained

Detailed meaning
The devotees sang nama sankirtanam together and attained supreme bliss.

Spiritual meaning
Group nama sankirtanam gives the experience of bliss.

Line 5
bhakta bṛnda kī jay kṛṣṇa nāma kī jay ॥

Word meanings
kī jay – victory to
kṛṣṇa nāma – Krishna's name

Detailed meaning
Victory to the devotees! Victory to Krishna's name!

Spiritual meaning
The devotee and the Lord's name are both holy.

Line 6
gōvinda gōpāla jay jay jay gōvinda gōpāla jay ॥

Word meanings
jay jay jay – the cry of victory, again and again

Detailed meaning
O Govinda! O Gopala! Victory to You, again and again.

Spiritual meaning
The peak of the namavali — a flood of the bliss of the Lord's name.

Overall essence of the whole Namavali
This namavali says:
nama sankirtanam is the path walked by great devotees like Narada and Prahlada;
Krishna's name is sweet;
when devotees gather and sing, supreme bliss is born;
the Name itself is the great grace of Kali yuga.

Key spiritual highlight
The central truth of this namavali:
“Where the Lord's name is sung, supreme bliss is born by itself.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This namavali is very simple; but within it lies a great secret of devotion.
We sing the very same name that Narada and Prahlada sang.
When we do group sankirtanam, crying ‘Govinda! Gopala!’, the mind naturally becomes joyful.
So this namavali is not just a chant;
it is the nectar of the Name that takes devotees to the state of supreme bliss.”

@@ 11.28 | “pāhi pāhi gajānana” – Divyanama Sankirtanam |
Type – Vinayaka Divyanama / namavali

The Divyanama

Line 1
pāhi pāhi gajānana pārvati nandana gajānana ॥

Word meanings
pāhi pāhi – protect us, bless us
gajānana – the elephant-faced one
pārvati nandana – the son of Goddess Parvati

Detailed meaning
O son of Parvati, who blesses with an elephant's face! Protect us.

Spiritual meaning
Vinayaka is regarded as the first among all divine blessings.

Line 2
mūṣika vāhana gajānana mōdaka hasta gajānana ॥

Word meanings
mūṣika vāhana – the one whose vehicle is a mouse
mōdaka hasta – the one holding a modaka in His hand

Detailed meaning
O Vinayaka, whose vehicle is the mouse, holding the modaka offering in His hand!

Spiritual meaning
The mouse stands for the restless mind; Vinayaka is the one who masters it.

Line 3
cāmara karṇa gajānana vilambita sūtra gajānana ॥

Word meanings
cāmara karṇa – the one with large ears like fans
vilambita sūtra – the one wearing a long sacred thread

Detailed meaning
O Gajanana, with large fan-like ears and a long sacred thread!

Spiritual meaning
The large ears stand for listening carefully to the prayers of devotees.

Line 4
vāmana rūpa gajānana mahēśvara putra gajānana ॥

Word meanings
vāmana rūpa – a small, lovely form
mahēśvara putra – the son of Lord Shiva

Detailed meaning
O son of Maheshvara, who blesses in a lovely divine form!

Spiritual meaning
Vinayaka is a God who shines in both simplicity and greatness.

Line 5
vighna vināyaka gajānana pāda namastē gajānana ॥

Word meanings
vighna – obstacles
vināyaka – the leader who removes obstacles
pāda namastē – salutations to Your feet

Detailed meaning
O Vinayaka, who removes all obstacles! I bow to Your holy feet.

Spiritual meaning
Surrender to Vinayaka makes the journey of life easy.

Shloka
gajānanaṁ bhūta gaṇādi sēvitaṁ
kapittha jambū phala sāra bhakṣitam ॥

Word meanings
bhūta gaṇādi sēvitam – served by Shiva's ganas
kapittha jambū phala – the one who loves wood-apple, jamun and such fruits
sāra bhakṣitam – the one who enjoys their essence

Detailed meaning
I bow to Gajanana, served by Shiva's ganas, who enjoys the nectar-essence of fruits.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord happily accepts even a simple, loving offering.

Shloka – continued
umāsutaṁ śōka vināśa kāraṇaṁ
namāmi vighnēśvara pāda paṅkajam ॥

Word meanings
umā sutam – the son of Goddess Uma (Parvati)
śōka vināśa kāraṇam – the one who removes sorrows
vighnēśvara – the Lord of obstacles
pāda paṅkajam – the lotus-like holy feet

Detailed meaning
I bow to the lotus feet of Vinayaka, the son of Uma, who removes sorrows and obstacles.

Spiritual meaning
Worshipping Vinayaka removes even the obstacles of the mind in life.

Overall essence of the whole Divyanama
This song praises Vinayaka as
the son of Parvati,
the remover of obstacles,
the compassionate God,
the protector of devotees,
the remover of sorrow,
the one who clears all hindrances.

Key spiritual highlight
The central truth of this Divyanama:
“When Vinayaka's grace comes, the obstacles on life's path move away.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This ‘pāhi pāhi gajānana’ Divyanama shows Vinayaka's divine accessibility very sweetly.
The simplicity of riding a mouse, the love of giving modakas, the large ears that listen to devotees' prayers — all these reveal His compassion.
So Vinayaka is not only the remover of obstacles;
He is the first companion on the devotee's journey of life.”

@@ 11.29 | “kṛṣṇa rāma gōvinda” – Raga Punnagavarali |
Type – Divyanama Sankirtanam / namavali

The Divyanama

Pallavi
kṛṣṇa rāma gōvinda harē rāma kṛṣṇa gōvinda ॥

Word meanings
kṛṣṇa – the Supreme Self, dark as a rain-cloud
rāma – the form of bliss
gōvinda – the Lord who protects all beings
harē – the remover of sins

Detailed meaning
O Krishna! O Rama! O Govinda! Remove our sorrows and sins and bless us.

Spiritual meaning
The chanting of the Lord's name is the life of this song.

Line 2
kṛṣṇa kēśava gōvinda harē rāma kṛṣṇa gōvinda ॥

Word meanings
kēśava – the slayer of the demon Keshi / the one with beautiful hair

Detailed meaning
O Krishna! O Keshava! O Govinda! Protect us.

Spiritual meaning
Each name of the Lord carries its own divine power.

Line 3
kṛṣṇānanda mukunda murārē vāmana mādhava gōvinda ॥

Word meanings
kṛṣṇānanda – the one who gives the bliss of Krishna
mukunda – the giver of liberation
murāri – the slayer of the demon Mura
vāmana – the Vamana avatara
mādhava – the Lord of Lakshmi

Detailed meaning
O Murari, who gives bliss and liberation! O Govinda, who blesses as Vamana and as Madhava!

Spiritual meaning
The Lord blesses in many incarnations according to the devotee's need.

Line 4
śrīdhara kēśava rāghava viṣṇō lakṣmīnāyaka narasiṁha ॥

Word meanings
śrīdhara – the one who holds Lakshmi
rāghava – Rama, born in the Raghu dynasty
viṣṇō – the all-pervading one
lakṣmīnāyaka – the Lord of Mahalakshmi
narasiṁha – the man-lion avatara

Detailed meaning
O Sridhara who holds Lakshmi! O Raghava! O Vishnu who fills everything! O Narasimha!

Spiritual meaning
All the avataras are expressions of the one Supreme.

Line 5
nanda nandana gōvinda harē rāma kṛṣṇa gōvinda ॥

Word meanings
nanda nandana – the son of Nandagopa

Detailed meaning
O Krishna, darling son of Nandagopa! Protect us.

Spiritual meaning
The Supreme is experienced also in the form of a child.

Line 6
navanīta cōra gōvinda harē rāma kṛṣṇa gōvinda ॥

Word meanings
navanīta cōra – the butter thief

Detailed meaning
O child Krishna, the butter thief! Steal our hearts too.

Spiritual meaning
Krishna captures the minds of His devotees with love.

Line 7
bhaktavatsala gōvinda harē rāma kṛṣṇa gōvinda ॥

Word meanings
bhaktavatsala – the one with great love for devotees

Detailed meaning
O Govinda, who protects devotees with compassion!

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's greatest quality — compassion for His devotees.

Line 8
bhāgavatapriya gōvinda harē rāma kṛṣṇa gōvinda ॥

Word meanings
bhāgavatapriya – the one who loves His devotees

Detailed meaning
O Govinda, who loves the company of His devotees!

Spiritual meaning
What is dearest to the Lord is the community of devotees.

Line 9
vēṇuvilōla gōvinda harē rāma kṛṣṇa gōvinda ॥

Word meanings
vēṇuvilōla – the one absorbed in the music of the flute

Detailed meaning
O Govinda, who enchants the world playing the flute!

Spiritual meaning
Krishna's flute-song draws the soul to the Supreme.

Line 10
vijaya gōpāla gōvinda harē rāma kṛṣṇa gōvinda ॥

Word meanings
vijaya gōpāla – Gopala who gives victory

Detailed meaning
O Gopala, the support of all victories! Protect us.

Spiritual meaning
True victory is the Lord's grace.

Final namavali
kṛṣṇa rāma gōvinda nārāyaṇa
rāma kṛṣṇa gōvinda nārāyaṇa

Overall essence of the whole Divyanama
This song praises Krishna in many names:
Govinda,
Keshava,
Mukunda,
Murari,
Narasimha,
Vamana,
Nandalala,
Bhaktavatsala.

Key spiritual highlight
The central truth of this Divyanama:
“Chanting the Lord's name again and again itself gives bliss and liberation.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“The nectar of the Name flows all through this ‘kṛṣṇa rāma gōvinda’ Divyanama.
Each name is a memory of an avatara, an experience of compassion.
‘navanīta cōra’, ‘bhaktavatsala’, ‘vēṇuvilōla’ — all these reveal the Lord's accessibility.
So this song is not just a namavali;
it is a supreme mantra that immerses the devotee in the bliss of the Name.”

@@ 11.30 | Gōpikā Gītam – Srimad Bhagavatam |
Source – Srimad Bhagavatam (Canto 10, Chapter 31)
Context – the song of supreme devotion of the gopis, pining in separation from Krishna during the Rasa lila
Mood – supreme detachment, supreme love, the longing of the individual soul for the Supreme

Dhyana shloka
tanmanaskāḥ tadālāpāḥ tadviceṣṭāḥ tadātmikāḥ ।
tadguṇānēva gāyantyō nātmāgārāṇi sasmaruḥ ॥

Meaning
The gopis' minds were completely absorbed in Krishna.
They spoke only of Him; they thought only of Him; they sang only of His qualities.
They forgot their homes, their bodies and the world.

Spiritual meaning
This is “ananya bhakti” — the state in which nothing but the Lord is in the mind.

punaḥ pulinam āgatya kālindyāḥ kṛṣṇa bhāvanāḥ ।
samavētā jaguḥ kṛṣṇaṁ tadāgamana kāṅkṣitāḥ ॥

Meaning
Gathering again on the bank of the Yamuna, the gopis began to sing of Krishna, longing for Him to return.

Spiritual meaning
Even the pain of separation becomes remembrance of the Lord.

Shloka 1
jayati tē'dhikaṁ janmanā vrajaḥ
śrayata indirā śaśvadatra hi ।
dayita dṛśyatāṁ dikṣu tāvakāḥ
tvayi dhṛtāsavaḥ tvāṁ vicinvatē ॥

Simple meaning
Krishna! Because You were born there, Vraja has become blessed.
Mahalakshmi herself dwells here for ever.
We, who live only by thinking of You, wander in every direction searching for You.
Show Yourself to us just once!

Spiritual meaning
The place where the Lord dwells becomes holy ground.

Shloka 2
śaradudāśayē sādhujāta sat-
sarasijōdara śrī muṣā dṛśā ।
suratanātha tē'śulka dāsikāḥ
varada nighnatō nēha kiṁ vadhaḥ ॥

Simple meaning
O Krishna, who looks at us with eyes that put to shame even the beauty of the lotus!
We are maidservants who live only for You.
Your glance is killing us while we are still alive!

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's darshan gives both bliss and longing at once.

Shloka 3
viṣajalāpyayād vyāla rākṣasād
varṣa mārutād vaidyutānalāt ।
vṛṣa mayātmajād viśvatō bhayād
ṛṣabha tē vayaṁ rakṣitā muhuḥ ॥

Simple meaning
From poisoned water, the serpent, the demons, the storm, lightning and fire — from every danger You have saved us again and again.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord protects us from outer dangers and from inner fears.

Shloka 4
na khalu gōpikā nandanō bhavān
akhila dēhināṁ antarātmadṛk ।
vikhanasā'rthitō viśvaguptayē
sakha udēyivān sātvatāṁ kulē ॥

Simple meaning
You are not merely the son of a gopi.
You are the Supreme Self within the hearts of all beings.
You incarnated to protect the world.

Spiritual meaning
Krishna is the Supreme who appeared in human form.

Shloka 5
viracitābhayaṁ vṛṣṇi dhurya tē
caraṇamīyuṣāṁ saṁsṛtēr bhayāt ।
karasarōruhaṁ kānta kāmadaṁ
śirasi dhēhi naḥ śrīkaragraham ॥

Simple meaning
To those who, fearing the cycle of birth, have reached Your feet, You give freedom from fear.
Place Your lotus hands on our heads and bless us.

Spiritual meaning
Surrender to the Lord — freedom from the fear of samsara.

Shloka 6
vraja janārtihan vīra yōṣitāṁ
nijajanasmaya dhvaṁsana smita ।
bhaja sakhē bhavat kiṅkarīḥ sma nō
jalaruhānanaṁ cāru darśaya ॥

Simple meaning
O Krishna, who removes the sorrows of the people of Vraja!
O You who captured our hearts with Your sweet smile!
We are Your maidservants; show us Your lotus face.

Spiritual meaning
The darshan of the Lord's face is the very life of the devotee.

Shloka 7
praṇatadēhināṁ pāpakarśanaṁ
tṛṇacarānugaṁ śrīnikētanam ।
phaṇiphaṇārpitaṁ tē padāmbujaṁ
kṛṇu kucēṣu naḥ kṛndhi hṛcchayam ॥

Simple meaning
Your holy feet destroy sins.
Place those feet, which danced on Kaliya's hoods, upon our hearts and remove the ache of our longing.

Spiritual meaning
Remembrance of the Lord calms the agitation of the mind.

Shloka 8
madhurayā girā valgu vākyayā
budha manōjñayā puṣkarēkṣaṇa ।
vidhikarīrimāḥ vīra muhyatīḥ
adharasīdhunā''pyāyayasva naḥ ॥

Simple meaning
O lotus-eyed Krishna!
Your sweet words enchant us.
Revive us with the nectar of Your lips.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's word is itself nectar to the soul.

Shloka 9
tava kathāmṛtaṁ taptajīvanaṁ
kavibhirīḍitaṁ kalmaṣāpaham ।
śravaṇa maṅgalaṁ śrīmadātataṁ
bhuvi gṛṇanti tē bhūridā janāḥ ॥

Simple meaning
Your stories are nectar to suffering souls.
They remove sins; they bring auspiciousness to those who hear them.
Those who spread Your stories are the greatest givers in the world.

Spiritual meaning
Spreading the stories of the Lord is a lofty service.

Overall essence (Shlokas 1–9)
In these opening shlokas of the Gopika Gitam,
the pain of separation from Krishna,
the longing to experience the Lord,
supreme surrender,
the bliss of His name, form and stories,
and the soul's search for the Supreme
are expressed very deeply.

Shloka 10
prahasitaṁ priya prēma vīkṣaṇaṁ
viharaṇaṁ ca tē dhyāna maṅgalam ।
rahasi saṁvidō yā hṛdi spṛśaḥ
kuhaka nō manaḥ kṣōbhayanti hi ॥

Simple meaning
Krishna!
Your sweet smile, Your loving glance, Your lilas — all these are auspicious for our meditation.
The sweet words You spoke to us in private now stir our hearts.

Spiritual meaning
Memories of experiencing the Lord live for ever in the devotee's mind.

Shloka 11
calasi yadvrajāc cārayan paśūn
nalinasundaraṁ nātha tē padam ।
śilatṛṇāṅkuraiḥ sīdatīti naḥ
kalilatāṁ manaḥ kānta gacchati ॥

Simple meaning
Krishna!
When You go to the forests to graze the cows, our minds wilt, fearing that Your tender feet will be hurt by stones and blades of grass.

Spiritual meaning
True devotion — thinking of the Lord's “pain” rather than one's own.

Shloka 12
dinaparikṣayē nīla kuntalaiḥ
vanaruhānanaṁ bibhradāvṛtam ।
ghanarajasvalaṁ darśayan muhuḥ
manasi naḥ smaraṁ vīra yacchasi ॥

Simple meaning
In the evening, when You return from grazing the cows, Your beauty — with a dust-covered face and curling locks — awakens loving memories in our minds.

Spiritual meaning
Even the Lord's simple lilas are an experience of supreme bliss to the devotee.

Shloka 13
praṇata kāmadaṁ padmajārcitaṁ
dharaṇi maṇḍanaṁ dhyēyamāpadi ।
caraṇapaṅkajaṁ śantamaṁ ca tē
ramaṇa naḥ stanēṣvarpayādhihan ॥

Simple meaning
Your lotus feet, worshipped by Brahma, the ornament of the earth, are to be meditated upon in every danger.
Place those feet in our hearts.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's feet — the refuge that gives protection and supreme peace.

Shloka 14
surata vardhanaṁ śōkanāśanaṁ
svarita vēṇunā suṣṭhu cumbitam ।
itara rāga vismāraṇaṁ nṛṇāṁ
vitara vīra nastē'dharāmṛtam ॥

Simple meaning
Your lips, made nectar-sweet by Your flute, increase love, remove sorrow, and make people forget worldly desires.
Grant us that nectar of Your lips.

Spiritual meaning
The experience of the Lord makes us forget worldly attachments.

Shloka 15
aṭati yadbhavān ahni kānanaṁ
truṭiyugāyatē tvāmapaśyatām ।
kuṭilakuntalaṁ śrīmukhaṁ ca tē
jaḍa udīkṣatāṁ pakṣmakṛd dṛśām ॥

Simple meaning
When You go to the forest by day, every moment without seeing You feels like an age.
Our eyes long to see Your face with its curling locks; even the blinking of our eyelids is an obstacle.

Spiritual meaning
Time without the Lord is a long darkness for the devotee.

Shloka 16
patisutānvaya bhrātṛ bāndhavān
ativilaṅghya tē'ntyacyutāgatāḥ ।
gatividastava udgīta mōhitāḥ
kitava yōṣitaḥ kastyajēn niśi ॥

Simple meaning
Leaving husbands, children, relatives — everything — enchanted by the music of Your flute, we came to You.
Is it You who now leaves us?

Spiritual meaning
Love for the Lord goes beyond all worldly ties.

Shloka 17
rahasi saṁvidaṁ hṛcchayōdayaṁ
prahasitānanaṁ prēma vīkṣaṇam ।
bṛhaduraḥ śriyō vīkṣya dhāma tē
muhuratispṛhā muhyatē manaḥ ॥

Simple meaning
Whenever we remember the words You spoke in private, Your smile, Your loving glance and Your broad chest, our minds are enchanted again and again.

Spiritual meaning
Remembrance of the Lord takes complete possession of the devotee.

Shloka 18
vraja vanaukasāṁ vyaktiraṅga tē
vṛjina hantryalaṁ viśva maṅgalam ।
tyaja manāk ca nastvat spṛhātmanāṁ
svajana hṛdrujāṁ yanniṣūdanam ॥

Simple meaning
Krishna!
You incarnated to remove the sorrows of the people of Vraja; You are the very form of the world's auspiciousness.
Do not leave us, who live longing only for You.
Remove the ache of our hearts.

Spiritual meaning
The state of having no support other than the Lord — supreme surrender.

Overall essence (Shlokas 10–18)
In these shlokas the gopis melt, remembering
Krishna's smile,
His flute-song,
His holy feet,
the beauty of His face,
the pain of separation,
and supreme surrender.

Namavali
rāma rāghavā sītā rāma rāghavā
kṛṣṇa kēśavā rādhā gōpī mādhavā

Shloka 19
yattē sujāta caraṇāmburuhaṁ stanēṣu
bhītāḥ śanaiḥ priya dadhīmahi karkaśēṣu ।
tēnāṭavīm aṭasi tadvyathatē na kiṁ svit
kūrpādibhir bhramati dhīr bhavadāyuṣāṁ naḥ ॥

Word meanings
yat tē – that of Yours
sujāta caraṇāmburuham – very tender, lotus-like feet
stanēṣu – on our breasts
bhītāḥ śanaiḥ – fearfully, gently
priya – O beloved
dadhīmahi – we placed
karkaśēṣu – thinking they would be hard
tēna – with those feet
aṭavīm aṭasi – You walk in the forest
tad vyathatē na kiṁ svit – do they not hurt?
kūrpādibhiḥ – by stones, thorns and the like
bhramati – is troubled
dhīḥ – the mind
bhavadāyuṣāṁ naḥ – of us, whose life is You

Simple and complete meaning
Beloved Krishna!
Your holy feet are like a very tender lotus.
Even when we placed those feet on our breasts, we placed them gently, afraid that our breasts were too hard and would hurt Your feet.
With such tender feet You now walk in the forest.
Thinking that the stones, thorns and sharp grass there might wound Your feet,
our minds, whose very life is You, sink into deep distress.

Spiritual meaning
This is the peak of devotion.
Here the gopis
do not think of their own sorrow;
they think only of the “pain” in Krishna's feet.
The Lord's well-being has become their very life.
This is
“not devotion for one's own sake, but devotion for the Lord's sake.”

The deep Bhagavata principle
In this shloka there are three great secrets:
1. The Lord's tenderness
Krishna's feet are softer than the lotus.
2. Supreme love
The gopis think even their own bodies are “hard” —
so tenderly do they love the Lord.
3. Ananya bhakti
The state of “we live for Him”.

Key spiritual highlight
The central truth of the Gopika Gitam:
“The pain of separation from the Lord is the peak of supreme devotion.”
And:
“The remembrance of the Lord is the devotee's very life.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“The Gopika Gitam is not an ordinary love song.
It is the tears of supreme devotion, the soul longing for the Supreme.
Just as the gopis search for Krishna, every soul is searching for God.
The gopis did not think of Krishna as a mere human beloved; Krishna was their very life.
So a single moment without Him became an age for them.
This one last shloka is enough to know the devotion of the gopis —
even when placing Krishna's feet on their breasts, they fear ‘our breasts are hard; will they hurt Him?’
This is not ordinary love;
it is the supreme love of ‘the Lord's well-being is our life’.
That is why the acharyas say
the devotion of the gopis is the highest peak of devotion.”

@@ 11.31 | “tvamēva mama śaraṇam” – Raga Simhendramadhyamam |
Type – Sharanagati kirtan (song of surrender)

Pallavi
tvamēva mama śaraṇaṁ tvāṁ vinā anyaṁ na jānē ॥

Word meanings
tvamēva – You alone
mama – my
śaraṇam – refuge
tvāṁ vinā – apart from You
anyaṁ na jānē – I know no other

Simple and complete meaning
You alone are my true refuge.
Apart from You I know no one else; nor will I accept anyone else.

Spiritual meaning
This is “ananya sharanagati” —
accepting the Lord as one's only support.

Line 2
nānyaṁ smarāmi na ca āśrayāmi
na cintayāmi na bhajāmi ॥

Word meanings
na anyaṁ smarāmi – I think of no one else
na āśrayāmi – I take refuge in no one else
na cintayāmi – I hold nothing else in my mind
na bhajāmi – I worship no one else

Simple and complete meaning
I do not think of anyone else;
I do not seek refuge with anyone else;
I do not contemplate anything else;
I do not worship anyone else.
You are my everything.

Spiritual meaning
The peak of devotion — the whole mind settled in the Lord.

Charanam 1
na hi mē puṇyaṁ na hi naipuṇyaṁ
na hi kāruṇyaṁ na hi dākṣiṇyam ॥

Word meanings
na hi mē puṇyam – I have no merit
naipuṇyam – no skill
kāruṇyam – no quality of compassion
dākṣiṇyam – no noble courtesy

Simple and complete meaning
I have no great merit;
no special skill;
no lofty qualities such as compassion.

Spiritual meaning
The first step of surrender — the giving up of ego.

Line 2
na hi mē satyaṁ na hi sāhityaṁ
na hi kṛta kṛtyaṁ na hi tava dautyam ॥

Word meanings
satyam – firmness in truth
sāhityam – learning / scholarship
kṛta kṛtyam – the state of having done what should be done
tava dautyam – I have not served You

Simple and complete meaning
I have no true firmness in truth;
no great learning;
I have not fully done the duties I ought to do;
nor have I done the service due to You.

Spiritual meaning
Without the Lord's grace, none of our practices is complete.

Charanam 2
na hi mē jñānaṁ na hi tē dhyānaṁ
na ca suvidhānaṁ na hi na hi maunam ॥

Word meanings
jñānam – knowledge of the Self
dhyānam – the practice of meditation
suvidhānam – proper spiritual observance
maunam – silence / the state of austerity

Simple and complete meaning
I have no true knowledge;
no deep meditation;
no proper spiritual observance;
no austerity of silence.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's grace matters more than the strength of our practices.

Line 2
dharaṇī dhara mādhava madhu sūdana
tāṭaṅki vēṅkaṭa dāsa śaraṇya ॥

Word meanings
dharaṇī dhara – the one who upholds the earth
mādhava – the Lord of Mahalakshmi
madhu sūdana – the slayer of the demon Madhu
śaraṇya – the giver of refuge

Simple and complete meaning
O upholder of the earth! O Madhava! O Madhusudana!
O compassionate one who gives refuge!
Protect me.

Spiritual meaning
In the end the devotee trusts not in his own practices,
but in the Lord's compassion.

Overall essence of the whole kirtan
This whole kirtan is filled with the feeling of supreme surrender:
“I am nothing,”
“You are everything,”
“I have no strength of practice,”
“Your grace is my support.”

Key spiritual highlight
The central truth of this kirtan:
“The Lord's grace is the true protection.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This ‘tvamēva mama śaraṇam’ kirtan is surrender in its purest form.
Here the devotee puts forward nothing — not merit, not knowledge, not austerity, not achievement.
He only says, ‘I have nothing; You alone are my refuge.’
That is the peak of true devotion.”

Shlokas that come at the end of the Gopika Gitam
(Srimad Bhagavatam: Canto 10, Chapter 32)

Shloka 1
iti gōpyaḥ pragāyantyaḥ pralapantyaśca citradhā ।
ruruduḥ susvaraṁ rājan kṛṣṇa darśana lālasāḥ ॥

Word meanings
iti – thus
gōpyaḥ – the gopis
pragāyantyaḥ – singing
pralapantyaḥ – lamenting in many ways
citradhā – in various ways
ruruduḥ – wept
susvaram – in sweet voices
rājan – O King (Shuka speaking to Parikshit)
kṛṣṇa darśana lālasāḥ – filled with longing to see Krishna

Simple and complete meaning
O King!
Thus the gopis sang of Krishna in many ways, lamented for Him, and wept in sweet voices out of longing to see Him.

Spiritual meaning
Separation from the Lord itself pours out as nama sankirtanam.

Shloka 2
tāsāmāvirabhūc chauriḥ smayamāna mukhāmbujaḥ ।
pītāmbaradharaḥ sragvī sākṣān manmatha manmathaḥ ॥

Word meanings
tāsām – before them
āvirabhūt – appeared
śauriḥ – Krishna
smayamāna mukhāmbujaḥ – with a lotus face blossoming in a smile
pītāmbaradharaḥ – wearing yellow silk
sragvī – wearing garlands
sākṣāt manmatha manmathaḥ – so beautiful that He enchants even Manmatha, the god of love

Simple and complete meaning
At that moment Krishna appeared before them, His face blossoming with a smile, wearing yellow silk and garlands — beautiful enough to enchant even the god of love.

Spiritual meaning
At the peak of supreme longing, the Lord's darshan comes.

Shloka 3
taṁ vilōkyāgataṁ prēṣṭhaṁ prītyutphulla dṛśō'balāḥ ।
uttasthur yugapat sarvāstanvaḥ prāṇamivāgatam ॥

Word meanings
taṁ vilōkya – seeing Him
āgataṁ prēṣṭham – that their beloved had come
prīti utphulla dṛśaḥ – with eyes blossoming in joy
abalāḥ – the gopis
uttasthuḥ – rose up
yugapat sarvāḥ – all at the same moment
tanvaḥ prāṇam iva āgatam – like a body when life returns to it

Simple and complete meaning
Seeing that their beloved Krishna had come, the gopis, with eyes blossoming in joy, all rose up at the same moment —
like a body to which its departed life has returned.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord is the devotee's very life.

Shloka 4
kṛṣṇasya mukha lāvaṇya sudhāṁ nayana bhājanaiḥ ।
āpīyāpīya nātṛpyan santastaccaraṇaṁ yathā ॥

Word meanings
kṛṣṇasya mukha lāvaṇya sudhām – the nectar of the beauty of Krishna's face
nayana bhājanaiḥ – with the vessels of their eyes
āpīya āpīya – drinking again and again
na atṛpyan – were not satisfied
santaḥ – the sages
tat caraṇaṁ yathā – just as with the Lord's holy feet

Simple and complete meaning
With their eyes the gopis drank again and again the nectar of the beauty of Krishna's face.
Yet they were never satisfied — just as the wise are never satiated in experiencing the Lord's holy feet.

Spiritual meaning
The experience of the Lord is boundless bliss; it never feels like “enough”.

Overall essence of all the shlokas
This section shows
the supreme pain of separation of the gopis,
the great bliss of Krishna's darshan,
the experience of the Lord's beauty,
and the state of devotion in which the Lord is one's life.

Key spiritual highlight
The central truth of these shlokas:
“Supreme longing finally blossoms into the darshan of the Lord.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“When the gopis called Krishna, it was not just a song — it was the cry of life itself.
At the peak of that supreme longing, Krishna appears.
This is the secret the Bhagavatam tells us:
‘Where there is true longing, the Lord Himself comes.’”

@@ 11.32 | “tava caraṇāmbujamēva” – Divyanama Sankirtanam |
Type – Sharanagati kirtan (song of surrender)

Pallavi
tava caraṇāmbujamēva gatirmē
muraḷīdhara gōpāla ॥ tava ॥

Word meanings
tava – Your
caraṇa ambujam – lotus-like feet
ēva – alone
gatiḥ mē – my refuge / my final shelter
muraḷīdhara – the one who holds the flute
gōpāla – Gopala Krishna

Simple and complete meaning
O Gopala, who holds the flute!
Your lotus-like feet alone are my one refuge and final goal.

Spiritual meaning
Complete surrender at the Lord's holy feet.

Charanam 1
śyāmaḷa sundara pītāmbaradhara
kōmaḷa bimbādhara gōpāla ॥ tava ॥

Word meanings
śyāmaḷa sundara – the beautiful dark one
pītāmbaradhara – the one who wears yellow silk
kōmaḷa – tender
bimbādhara – the one with lips red as the bimba fruit

Simple and complete meaning
O beautiful dark one!
O You who wear yellow silk!
O Gopala with tender red lips!
Your holy feet are my refuge.

Spiritual meaning
Meditating on the Lord's divine form melts the mind in devotion.

Charanam 2
karuṇā sindhō kalaya kaṭākṣaṁ
śaraṇāgatapriya gōpāla ॥ tava ॥

Word meanings
karuṇā sindhō – O ocean of compassion
kalaya kaṭākṣam – grant Your gracious glance
śaraṇāgatapriya – the one who loves those who surrender

Simple and complete meaning
O Gopala, ocean of compassion!
O You who love those who surrender to You!
Cast Your glance of grace upon me.

Spiritual meaning
The first grace the Lord gives to one who surrenders — His gracious glance.

Charanam 3
sādhana rahitaṁ śaraṇāgataṁ – pari
pālaya māṁ dīnaṁ gōpāla ॥ tava ॥

Word meanings
sādhana rahitam – one without the strength of spiritual practice
śaraṇāgatam – one who has surrendered to You
pālaya mām – protect me
dīnam – humble / helpless

Simple and complete meaning
I have no great practices or strength of austerity.
But I have surrendered completely to You.
Protect me, helpless as I am, O Gopala!

Spiritual meaning
Surrender is higher than the strength of practices.

Charanam 4
bṛndāvana sañcāra mukunda
prēmika pālaka gōpāla ॥ tava ॥

Word meanings
bṛndāvana sañcāra – the one who roamed in Brindavan
mukunda – the giver of liberation
prēmika pālaka – the protector of loving devotees

Simple and complete meaning
O Mukunda, who played His lilas in Brindavan!
O Gopala, who protects His loving devotees!
Your holy feet are my only refuge.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord never abandons His devotees.

Overall essence of the whole kirtan
This whole kirtan is filled with the feelings of
meditation on the Lord's beauty,
prayer for compassion,
surrender without the strength of practice,
and “the Lord alone is my refuge”.

Key spiritual highlight
The central truth of this kirtan:
“Even without the strength of practice, if there is true surrender, the Lord will protect.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“In this ‘tava caraṇāmbujamēva’ kirtan, the devotee does not speak of his strength; he admits his weakness.
He says, ‘I have no practices; but I will not leave Your holy feet.’
That is surrender in its purest form.”

@@ 11.33 | “aṅganām aṅganāmantarē” – Raga Shankarabharanam |
Theme – description of the Rasa Krida (Rasa lila)

Pallavi
aṅganām aṅganāmantarē mādhavaḥ
mādhavaṁ mādhavaṁ ca antarēṇāṅganā ।
itthamākalpitē maṇḍalē madhyagaḥ
sañjagau vēṇunā dēvakī nandanaḥ ॥

Word meanings
aṅganām aṅganām antarē – between every two gopis
mādhavaḥ – Krishna
mādhavaṁ mādhavaṁ ca antarē aṅganā – and between every two Krishnas, a gopi
ittham kalpitē maṇḍalē – in the circle of the Rasa formed in this way
madhyagaḥ – standing in the middle
sañjagau vēṇunā – played the flute
dēvakī nandanaḥ – Krishna, the son of Devaki

Simple and complete meaning
In the Rasa circle, Krishna wondrously appeared as a Krishna between every two gopis, and a gopi between every two Krishnas; and in the centre of that circle, Krishna, the son of Devaki, sweetly played his flute.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord has a personal relationship with each and every devotee.

Charanam 1
kēkikē gātṛtānēka paṅkēruhā
līna haṁsāvaḷī hṛdyatā hṛdyatā ।
kaṁsa vaṁśāṭavī dāha dāvānalaḥ
sañjagau vēṇunā dēvakī nandanaḥ ॥

Simple and complete meaning
With a sweetness surpassing the dance of peacocks and the beauty of swans gliding on lotus ponds, Krishna — the hero who was like a forest fire that burnt Kamsa's clan — played His flute.

Spiritual meaning
In Krishna, at the same time,
beauty,
gentleness,
and valour
all shine together.

Charanam 2
kvāpi vīṇābhi rārāviṇā kampitaḥ
kvāpi vīṇābhi rākiṅkiṇī nartitaḥ ।
kvāpi vīṇābhi rāmāntaraṁ gāpitaḥ
sañjagau vēṇunā dēvakī nandanaḥ ॥

Simple and complete meaning
In some places the music of the veena sounded;
in some places there was dancing with the tinkle of anklet-bells;
in some places the kirtan of Rama's name rang out;
in the midst of all this Krishna played His flute.

Spiritual meaning
All music finally reaches the Lord.

Charanam 3
cāru candrāvaḷī lōcanaiś cumbitō
gōpa gōpa vṛnda gōpālikā vallabhaḥ ।
vallavī bṛnda bṛndārakō kāmukaḥ
sañjagau vēṇunā dēvakī nandanaḥ ॥

Simple and complete meaning
Surrounded by the loving glances of gopis such as Chandravali, beloved of the cowherds and the gopis, captivating the hearts of all the gopis — Krishna played His flute.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord is the centre of the devotees' hearts.

Charanam 4
mauḷi mālā milan matta bhṛṅgī latā
bhīta bhīta priyā vibhramāliṅgita ।
srasta gōpī kucābhōga sammēḷitaṁ
sañjagau vēṇunā dēvakī nandanaḥ ॥

Simple and complete meaning
Bees, intoxicated by His flower garlands, hovered around;
the gopis, overflowing with love, embraced Krishna;
in the midst of that rapturous Rasa lila, Krishna played His flute.

Spiritual meaning
The Rasa Krida is the symbol of the blissful union of the soul and the Supreme Soul.

Charanam 5
cāru cāmīkarābhā sabhāmāvibhuḥ
vaijayantī latā vāsitōraḥ sthala ।
nanda bṛndāvanē vāsitā madhyagaḥ
sañjagau vēṇunā dēvakī nandanaḥ ॥

Simple and complete meaning
With a divine body shining like gold, His chest adorned with the Vaijayanti garland, seated in the midst of Nanda's Brindavan, Krishna played His flute.

Spiritual meaning
Brindavan — the divine state of the Lord's bliss.

Charanam 6
bālikā tālikā tāla līlālaya
saṅga sandarśita bhrūlatā vibhramaḥ ।
gōpikā gīta dattāvadhānaḥ svayaṁ
sañjagau vēṇunā dēvakī nandanaḥ ॥

Simple and complete meaning
The gopis sang, keeping time with their hands;
even the movement of their eyebrows joined the rhythm;
giving full attention to the song they sang, Krishna played His flute.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord delights in the devotee's kirtan.

Charanam 7
pārijātaṁ samuddhṛtya rādhāvarō
rōpayāmāsa bhāmā gṛhasyāṅgaṇē ।
śīta śītē vaṭē yāmunīyē taṭē
sañjagau vēṇunā dēvakī nandanaḥ ॥

Simple and complete meaning
Krishna, the beloved of Radha, brought the Parijata flower-tree and blessed (planting it in Satyabhama's courtyard);
seated in the cool shade of the banyan on the bank of the Yamuna, He played His flute.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord performs His lilas only to give bliss to His devotees.

Overall essence of the whole kirtan
This kirtan very beautifully describes
the Rasa lila,
the bliss of the flute-song,
the devotion of the gopis,
the Lord's lila of infinite forms,
the union of the soul with the Supreme.

Key spiritual highlight
The central truth of this kirtan:
“The Lord has a unique, blissful relationship with each devotee.”

The deep philosophy of the Rasa Krida kirtan
Though this “aṅganām aṅganāmantarē” kirtan openly describes the Rasa lila, its inner meaning is very lofty.

What is the Rasa lila?
The Bhagavatam explains that the Rasa lila is
not an ordinary dance,
not worldly love,
but the blissful union of the Supreme Soul and the individual soul.

“A Krishna for each gopi” — its secret
The very first line says:
“aṅganām aṅganāmantarē mādhavaḥ”.
That is,
the Lord is a personal experience for each devotee;
to the devotee, the Lord is experienced as if He belonged to him alone;
yet He is with everyone at the same time.
This is the Vedantic principle of
“the Supreme Soul of infinite forms”.

The philosophy of the flute-song
The line that comes again and again in this kirtan is
“sañjagau vēṇunā dēvakī nandanaḥ”.
The flute is
an empty bamboo;
it has no sound of its own;
only when Krishna blows does music come.
Its philosophy:
“When the ego is emptied, the Lord expresses His music through us.”

Who are the gopis?
The acharyas say:
the gopis are not ordinary women;
they are fully ripened souls;
souls who have no support other than the Lord.

The Rasa mandala — its spiritual meaning
The circular “Rasa mandala”
has no beginning and no end;
it points to the infinite nature of Parabrahman.
Krishna being at its centre shows that
“the Supreme Soul is the centre of all living beings.”

The main devotional mood of this kirtan
This whole kirtan is filled with
madhurya bhakti (sweet devotion),
the devotion of bliss,
the rasa of the flute-song,
and the experience of the Lord's beauty.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“The Rasa lila is not a worldly love story.
It is the image of the state of supreme bliss in which the soul reaches and experiences the Lord.
Just as Krishna appeared separately to each gopi, the Lord reveals Himself as personal grace in the heart of each devotee.
That is why the Rasa Krida is praised as the supreme secret lila of the Bhagavatam.”

@@ 11.34 | “gōpī gōpāla lāla” – Raga Purvikalyani |
Theme – description of the bliss of the Rasa lila (Surdas)

Pallavi
gōpī gōpāla lāla rāsa maṇḍala māhī
tāttā thēyi tā sudhaṅga, niratata kahi bāhī ॥

Word meanings
gōpī gōpāla lāla – Gopala Krishna, darling of the gopis
rāsa maṇḍala māhī – in the middle of the Rasa circle
tāttā thēyi tā – dance syllables / rhythm sounds
niratata – was dancing
bāhī – moving His arms

Simple and complete meaning
Gopala Krishna, the darling of the gopis, stood in the middle of the Rasa circle and danced in bliss, moving His arms to the dance syllables.

Spiritual meaning
The Rasa lila — the outpouring of the Lord's bliss.

Charanam 1
druma druma druma druma mṛdaṅga chana nana nana rūpa raṅga
dhṛga tā dhṛga tāla taṅga ughaṭata rasanāyī ॥

Simple and complete meaning
The mridangam thundered;
the Rasa lila unfolded with sweet music and rhythm;
everyone was immersed in that rasa of bliss.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's name, music and dance — all these are means that lift up devotion.

Charanam 2
bīca lāla bīca bāla prati prati ati dyuti rasāla
avigata gati ati udāra nirakhi dṛga sarāhī ॥

Simple and complete meaning
On every side, Krishna's beauty shone;
seeing His wondrous dance movements and His great grace, everyone gazed in wonder and delight.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's lilas are beyond the reach of the human intellect.

Charanam 3
śrī rādhā mukha śarada canda pōñchata jala śrama ananda
śrī vraja canda laṭaka laṭaka karata mukuṭa chāhī ॥

Simple and complete meaning
On Radha's face, shining like the autumn moon, the sweat of bliss glistened;
the crown of Krishna, the moon of Vraja, sparkled as it swayed.

Spiritual meaning
The bliss of devotion fills both body and mind with rapture.

Charanam 4
tattata tata sughara gāta sa ri ga ma pa da nī ga ṭhāṭha
aura padahi pralāda dāmpa dampati ati sādahī ॥

Simple and complete meaning
With the notes “sa ri ga ma pa da ni” ringing out, beautiful singing and dancing took place;
the devotees enjoyed the lila of that divine couple with great bliss.

Spiritual meaning
Music is a path to the experience of the Lord.

Charanam 5
kāvata rasa bharē ananda tāna tāna sura abhaṅga
umaṅgata chabi ati ananda rījhata hari rādhahī ॥

Simple and complete meaning
In the Rasa lila full of bliss, divine music sounded;
seeing that great beauty, Hari and Radha rejoiced.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord too rejoices in the love of His devotees.

Charanam 6
chāyē dēvana vimāna dēkhata sura śakra bhāna
dēvāṅgana nidhāna rījhi prāṇa vārahī ॥

Simple and complete meaning
The gods came in their aerial chariots and watched this Rasa lila;
the celestial maidens too were enchanted and filled with bliss.

Spiritual meaning
The Rasa lila — a divine experience rare even for the gods.

Charanam 7
cakita thakita yamunā nīra khaga mṛga jagamaga śarīra
bali bali jāya sūradāsa rāsa sukha tihārahī ॥

Simple and complete meaning
Even the waters of the Yamuna fell still in wonder;
birds and animals were thrilled;
“I offer myself to this bliss of the Rasa!” sings Surdas in rapture.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's bliss touches everything, the sentient and the insentient.

Overall essence of the whole kirtan
This kirtan vividly describes
the divine beauty of the Rasa lila,
music, dance and bliss,
the supreme bliss of Radha and Krishna,
and the experience of the Lord's rasa.

Key spiritual highlight
The central truth of this kirtan:
“The Lord's bliss can enchant all the worlds.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“This kirtan says that it was not only people who were thrilled seeing the Rasa lila — the gods, the Yamuna, the birds and the animals were all in rapture.
Why?
Because where the Lord reveals Himself, bliss naturally overflows.”

@@ 11.35 | “jhūlata rādhā” – Raga Darbari Kanada |
Theme – the blissful lila of Radha and Krishna at the Holi festival (Meerabai)

Pallavi
jhūlata rādhā saṅga giridhara
jhūlata rādhā saṅga ॥

Word meanings
jhūlata – swings / plays joyfully
rādhā – Radha
saṅga – with
giridhara – Krishna, who lifted Mount Govardhana

Simple and complete meaning
Radha swings joyfully together with Giridhara Krishna.

Spiritual meaning
The state of bliss in which the devotee and the Lord become one.

Charanam 1
abira gulāla kī dhūma macāyī
bhar picakārī raṅga giridhara ॥

Word meanings
abira, gulāla – coloured powders used at the Holi festival
dhūma macāyī – joy spread everywhere
picakārī – a squirt for coloured water
raṅga – colour / joy

Simple and complete meaning
Coloured powders like abir and gulal spread everywhere;
Giridhara sprayed coloured water and delighted everyone in the play of Holi.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's love fills life with colour.

Charanam 2
lāla bhayī bṛndāvana jamunā
kēsara cūbata raṅga giridhara ॥

Word meanings
lāla bhayī – became red
bṛndāvana – Brindavan
jamunā – the river Yamuna
kēsara – saffron / saffron colour

Simple and complete meaning
With the colours of Holi, Brindavan and the Yamuna glowed red;
Giridhara drenched everyone in the colour of joy.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's lila fills even nature with rapture.

Charanam 3
nācata tāḷa adhāra suravara
dhimi dhimi bājē mṛdaṅga giridhara ॥

Word meanings
nācata – danced
tāḷa – rhythm / beat
suravara – the gods
dhimi dhimi – the sound of the mridangam
bājē – sounded

Simple and complete meaning
Even the gods danced to the rhythm;
with the mridangam sounding “dhimi dhimi”, Giridhara's lila became still more joyful.

Spiritual meaning
The kirtan of the Lord gives joy even to the gods.

Charanam 4
mīrā kahē prabhu giridhara nāgara
mōhana muraḷī bhujaṅga giridhara ॥

Word meanings
mīrā kahē – says Meerabai
giridhara nāgara – O Giridhara, my Lord
mōhana – the one who captivates the mind
muraḷī – the flute
bhujaṅga – music that winds and flows, enchanting like a serpent's coils

Simple and complete meaning
“O Giridhara! The enchanting music of Your flute captivates everyone,” sings Meerabai in rapture.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's name and flute-song draw the devotee completely.

Overall essence of the whole kirtan
This kirtan sweetly describes
the Holi lila of Radha and Krishna,
the bliss of Brindavan,
the festival of music, dance and colour,
and the great joy of devotion.

Key spiritual highlight
The central truth of this kirtan:
“In the Lord's presence, life becomes a colourful bliss.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Just as colours spread outside during Holi, in the experience of the Lord the colours of grace spread over the heart.
The lila of Radha and Krishna is not mere play; it is a divine experience revealing the state of supreme bliss of devotion.”

@@ 11.36 | “kōlāṭṭam” – Raga Sindhu Bhairavi |
Theme – a kolattam song full of devotion to Krishna (Tamil)

Pallavi
nandakumāranai nāḍiḍuvāy nenjē
nambiyē ponnaḍi tēḍiḍuvāy nittam ॥

Word meanings
nandakumāran – Krishna, the son of Nandagopa
nāḍiḍuvāy – seek
nenjē – O my heart
nambiyē – with complete faith
ponnaḍi – golden holy feet
tēḍiḍuvāy – strive to reach
nittam – always

Simple and complete meaning
O my heart! Seek Krishna, the son of Nanda.
With complete faith, always strive to reach His golden holy feet.

Spiritual meaning
The mind must always be settled at the Lord's holy feet.

Charanam 1
bṛndāvanantanil tāṇḍavamāḍiyē
andi pagal pugazh pāḍiḍuvāy ॥

Word meanings
bṛndāvanantanil – in Brindavan
tāṇḍavamāḍiyē – dancing in bliss
andi pagal – night and day
pugazh pāḍiḍuvāy – sing His glory

Simple and complete meaning
Thinking of Krishna dancing joyfully in Brindavan, sing His glory night and day.

Spiritual meaning
Nama sankirtanam must be the lifelong practice of our life.

Charanam 2
pullum magizhndiḍum pūttiḍumē koḍi
kallum karaindiḍum kāṭṭinilē cinna
pullāṅguzhal iniyōsaiyināl jaga-
mellām kuḷirndiḍa seybavanai ॥

Word meanings
pullum magizhndiḍum – even the grass rejoices
koḍi pūttiḍumē – the creepers burst into flower
kallum karaindiḍum – even stones melt
pullāṅguzhal iniyōsai – the sweet sound of Krishna's flute
jagamellām kuḷirndiḍa – so that the whole world is soothed

Simple and complete meaning
By the sweet sound of Krishna's flute,
even the grass rejoices,
the creepers burst into flower,
even stones melt,
and the whole world receives coolness and bliss.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's name and flute-song have the power to transform even the inanimate world.

Charanam 3
mēganiram koṇḍa mēniyanai mayil
tōgai muḍikoṇḍa mādavanai nanda-
gōkula bālanai kumbiḍuvāy nittam
āgama vēdyanai acyutanai ॥

Word meanings
mēganiram koṇḍa mēni – a divine body the colour of a dark cloud
mayil tōgai muḍi – the one who wears a peacock feather as a crown
mādavan – the Lord of Lakshmi
gōkula bālan – the child who grew up in Gokula
āgama vēdyan – the Supreme made known by the Vedas and Agamas
acyutan – the one who never falls / never wavers

Simple and complete meaning
Always bow to Madhava, the child of Gokula, with a divine body the colour of a dark cloud, wearing a peacock feather.
He is Achyuta, praised by the Vedas and Agamas.

Spiritual meaning
The child Krishna Himself is Parabrahman.

Charanam 4
nīraja nētranai nirmalanai jagat
kāraṇanai paripūraṇanai sudda-
kāruṇyamē uruvānavanai guru
māruta mandira nāyaganai ॥

Word meanings
nīraja nētran – the one with lotus-like eyes
nirmalan – the spotless one
jagat kāraṇan – the cause of the world
paripūraṇan – the one complete in every quality
sudda kāruṇyamē uruvānavan – the form of pure compassion
guru māruta mandira nāyagan – the Lord of Guruvayur (the abode of Guru and Vayu)

Simple and complete meaning
Bow to Guruvayurappan — lotus-eyed, spotless, the cause of the world, complete in every way, compassion itself in form.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord is the complete form of compassion.

Overall essence of the whole kirtan
This kirtan very simply and sweetly brings out
devotion to Krishna,
the glory of the flute-song,
nama sankirtanam,
the Lord's beauty,
compassion and bliss.

Key spiritual highlight
The central truth of this kirtan:
“The remembrance and name of Krishna soothe the whole world.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“The song says that at the sound of Krishna's flute the grass rejoices and even stones melt.
Its meaning is that the experience of the Lord never fails to soften the heart.
That is why devotees say, ‘Krishna's name is the nectar of the world.’”

@@ 11.37 | “kummi” – Divyanama Sankirtanam |
Theme – Krishna's little lilas and the rapture of devotion (Tamil kummi song)

Pallavi
dēvādi dēvar pugazh bālanaḍi ēlēlō
dēvargaḷaik-kāttiḍum gōpālanaḍi ēlēlō

Word meanings
dēvādi dēvar – the One above all the gods
bālan – the child Krishna
gōpālan – Krishna, protector of the cowherds

Simple meaning
That child Krishna, praised by all the gods, shines as Gopala who protects the cowherds.

Spiritual meaning
Even in the form of a child, the Lord is the Supreme Self.

Charanam 1
tannan taniyāga vandu azhaikkirāṇḍi ēlēlō
cinnañjiru līlaigaḷuk-kizhukkirāṇḍi ēlēlō

Simple meaning
Krishna comes all alone and calls His devotees;
through small, little games He draws everyone to Himself.

Spiritual meaning
The way the Lord approaches the devotee is very subtle.

Charanam 2
kaṇ simiṭṭi arugil nammaik-kūvurāṇḍi ēlēlō
kai kāṭṭi kalakka nammai ēvurāṇḍi ēlēlō

Simple meaning
Krishna winks and calls us near;
He beckons with His hand and stirs our minds.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's compassion draws us to Him.

Charanam 3
madi vadanan mandahāsam mayakkudaḍi ēlēlō
māyanivanai maruviḍavē bayakkudaḍi ēlēlō

Simple meaning
The sweet smile of the moon-faced Krishna enchants the mind;
yet approaching that wondrous Krishna also brings a sense of awe.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord gives both attraction and reverence at the same time.

Charanam 4
pinnalgaḷaic-cērttu muḍi pōḍurāṇḍi ēlēlō
piragennamō pārppadaip-pōl tēḍurāṇḍi ēlēlō

Simple meaning
Krishna gathers and ties up His locks;
then He pretends to be searching for something.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord's lilas playfully draw the human mind.

Charanam 5
pārkkum kaḷḷap-pārvaiyadaip-pāraḍiyō ēlēlō
yārkkum idu eḷidenru nī kūraḍiyō ēlēlō

Simple meaning
Look at that mischievous glance of Krishna — so captivating;
He cannot be easily understood.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord is simple and deep at the same time.

Charanam 6
māmi idai kaṇḍāl namakku irukkudaḍi ēlēlō
manamennamō ivaniḍamē rusikkudaḍi ēlēlō

Simple meaning
When we look at Krishna, the mind is drawn to Him alone.

Spiritual meaning
A natural attraction to the Lord arises.

Charanam 7
anbargaḷaik-kai koḍuttuk-kāppavaṇḍi ēlēlō
inbamuḍan visvan paṇi ērpavaṇḍi ēlēlō

Simple meaning
Krishna takes His devotees by the hand and protects them;
He gladly accepts their service.

Spiritual meaning
The Lord never abandons the devotee.

Essence of the whole kirtan
This “kummi” song shows, in a form that blends humour and devotion,
Krishna's childhood lilas,
the gopis' attraction,
the sweetness of devotion,
and the Lord's mischievous smile and compassion.

Key spiritual truth
“The Lord is at the same time a child, a friend, an attraction, and compassion — everything.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Krishna's lilas make us smile and draw us in.
But the truth behind that smile is the supreme compassion with which the Lord draws us to Himself.”

@@ 11.39 | “śiva śiva” – Raga Saurashtram |
Theme – a meditative hymn to Lord Shiva (Narayana Tirtha)

Pallavi
śiva śiva bhava bhava śaraṇaṁ mama bhavatu sadā tava smaraṇam

Word meanings
śiva śiva – remembering Shiva again and again
bhava bhava – the one who removes bhava (samsara)
śaraṇaṁ mama – my refuge
bhavatu sadā – may there be always
tava smaraṇam – remembrance of You

Simple and complete meaning
“O Shiva! You are my refuge. May the remembrance of You always stay in my mind. You are the one who removes my sins.”

Spiritual meaning
Remembering Shiva is the way to be freed from the bondage of samsara.

Charanam 1
gaṅgādhara candracūḍa jagan maṅgaḷa sarvalōka nīḍa

Word meanings
gaṅgādhara – the one who bears Ganga on His head
candracūḍa – the one who wears the moon
jagan maṅgaḷa – the auspiciousness of the world
sarvalōka nīḍa – the support (nest) of all the worlds

Simple meaning
Lord Shiva, who bears Ganga on His head and wears the moon, gives auspiciousness to all the worlds.

Spiritual meaning
Shiva is the sustaining power of the world.

Charanam 2
kailāśācala vāsa śivakara purahara darahāsa

Simple meaning
Shiva, who dwells on Mount Kailasa, blesses with a divine smile; He destroyed the three cities (Tripura).

Spiritual meaning
Shiva is the form of compassion that destroys the ego.

Charanam 3
bhasmōd-dhūḷita dēha śambhō parama puruṣa vṛṣavāha

Simple meaning
Shiva, the Supreme Person whose body is smeared with sacred ash, rides on Nandi the bull.

Spiritual meaning
The ash reminds us of the impermanence of life.

Charanam 4
pañcānana phaṇi bhūṣa śiva parama puruṣa muni vēṣa

Simple meaning
Shiva with five faces, adorned with serpents, in the form of the supreme sage.

Spiritual meaning
Shiva is the form in which knowledge and meditation unite.

Charanam 5
ānanda naṭana vinōda saccidānanda vidaḷita khēda

Simple meaning
Shiva, who delights in the dance of bliss, removes sorrows as the form of sat-cit-ananda.

Spiritual meaning
His dance = the movement of the universe.

Charanam 6
nava vyākaraṇa svabhāva śiva nārāyaṇa tīrtha dēva

Simple meaning
Shiva, who embodies all the grammars (all knowledge), the God praised by Narayana Tirtha.

Spiritual meaning
Shiva, learning and wisdom are one.

Essence of the whole kirtan
This kirtan explains together
meditation on Lord Shiva,
His form of compassion,
His dwelling on Kailasa,
His lila of the cosmic dance,
and liberation from samsara.

Key spiritual truth
“Remembrance of Shiva is the supreme way to cross samsara.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Lord Shiva is not a destroyer; He destroys the ego and gives bliss.
Remembering Him purifies the mind and leads it to supreme bliss.”

@@ 11.40 | “dhārō jagannātha” – Raga Nadanamakriya |
Theme – a hymn of the ten avataras (the Lord is the one Supreme in all His incarnations)

Pallavi
dhārō jagannātha dhārō harē
dhārō parabrahma dhārō harē

Word meanings
dhārō – the one who upholds / gives support
jagannātha – the Lord of the world
harē – the remover of sorrow
parabrahma – the highest, supreme truth

Simple and complete meaning
“O Jagannatha! O Hari! O Parabrahman! You alone uphold everything.”

Spiritual meaning
The Lord is the sustaining power of the universe.

The sequence of the ten avataras
1. The Fish avatara
matsyāvatāra dhārō harē
He who saved the world from the flood.

2. The Tortoise avatara
matsya kūrmāvatāra dhārō harē
He who became the support of Mount Mandara.

3. The Boar avatara
varāhāvatāra dhārō harē
The boar form that rescued the earth.

4. The Narasimha avatara
narasiṁhāvatāra dhārō harē
Half man, half lion — to protect His devotee.

5. The Vamana avatara
vāmanāvatāra dhārō harē
He who, in the form of a small mendicant, measured the universe.

6. The Parashurama (Bhrigurama) avatara
bhṛgurāmāvatāra dhārō harē
The warrior avatara who destroyed adharma.

7. The Rama avatara
rāmāvatāra dhārō harē
The embodiment of dharma.

8. The Balarama avatara
balarāmāvatāra dhārō harē
The representative of strength.

9. The Buddha avatara
buddhāvatāra dhārō harē
He who taught compassion and peace.

10. The Kalki avatara
śrīmad kalkyāvatāra dhārō harē
The establisher of dharma who will come in the future.

Concluding charanam
daśa vidha rūpa dhārō harē
bahu-dayāla vāla dhārō harē

Simple and complete meaning
The Lord incarnates in many forms and protects all beings with compassion.

Spiritual meaning
The one Supreme Self establishes dharma in many forms.

(The song continues with the Govinda namavali and the concluding shlokas — “āñjanēya matipāṭalānanam…”, “yatra yatra raghunātha kīrtanam…”, “manōjavam mārutatulya vēgam…”, “kāyēna vācā…”, “svasti prajābhyaḥ…” — which are given in full in the Lyrics tab.)

Essence of the whole kirtan
This song explains
the oneness of the ten avataras,
the many forms of the Supreme Self,
the dharma of protecting the world,
compassion and the protection of creation.

Key spiritual truth
“The one Supreme Self comes as many avataras and protects dharma.”

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“We should not see the ten avataras separately.
All of them are expressions of one supreme compassion.
The Lord always comes to establish dharma — this is the essence of this kirtan.”

@@ 11.41 | Dīpa Samārōpaṇam – the concluding lamp worship |
Theme – the concluding lamp salutation / final auspicious hymn

1. The opening auspicious hymn
kṛṣṇa rāma mukunda mādhava vāsudēva jagadgurō
Meaning:
O Krishna! O Rama! O Mukunda! O Madhava! O Vasudeva!
O Teacher of the world — salutations to You.

matsya kacchapa nārasiṁha varāha rāghava pāhimām
Meaning:
O Lord who is the Fish, the Tortoise, Narasimha, the Boar and Rama — protect me.

dēva dānava nāradādi munīndra vandya dayānidhē
Meaning:
O ocean of compassion, worshipped by the gods, the demons and great sages like Narada.

dēvakīsuta dēhi mē tava pādabhaktim añjalām
Meaning:
O son of Devaki! Grant me devotion at Your lotus feet.

2. Hymn to Rama's name
rāma rāma namōstutē jaya rāmacandra namōstutē
Meaning:
O Rama! Salutations to You. O victorious Ramachandra, salutations to You.

rāmabhadra namōstutē jaya rāghavārya namōstutē
Meaning:
O auspicious Rama, salutations to You. O noble Raghava, salutations to You.

3. Hymn to the Lord
dēva dēva namōstutē jaya dēvarāja namōstutē
Meaning:
O God of the gods, salutations to You. O Lord even of Indra, the king of the gods, salutations to You.

vāsudēva namōstutē jaya varadarāja namōstutē
Meaning:
O Vasudeva, salutations to You. O king who grants boons (Varadaraja), salutations to You.

Overall essence (for the upanyasam)
This Dipa Samaropanam says:
1. All are the one God —
Rama, Krishna, Vishnu are the one Supreme Self.
2. The oneness of the avataras —
from the Fish to Rama, all are His forms.
3. Final surrender —
“Devotion to Your feet is everything to me.”

A closing line for the upanyasam
“The lamp may go out, but the lamp of devotion never goes out — it shines for ever at the Lord's feet.”

The 3rd Ashtapadi – description of springtime (Vasanta Ashtapadi)
This is a famous section from Jayadeva's Gita Govinda.

Opening shloka
vasantē vāsantī kusuma sukumāraiḥ avayavaiḥ
bhramantīṁ kāntārē bahuvihita kṛṣṇānusaraṇām
Meaning:
In springtime Radha, with limbs as tender as spring flowers, wanders through the forest,
searching for Krishna everywhere.

amandaṁ kandarpajvara janita cintākulatayā
valad bādhāṁ rādhāṁ sarasamidam ūcē sahacarī
Meaning:
To Radha, troubled by the pain of separation caused by Kamadeva,
her companion speaks words of comfort.

Verse 1
lalita lavaṅga latā pariśīlana kōmaḷa malaya samīrē
madhukara nikara karambita kōkila kūjita kuñja kuṭīrē
Meaning:
A forest where creepers like clove sway in the gentle breeze,
and bees and cuckoos fill the bowers with music.

viharati haririha sarasa vasantē
nṛtyati yuvati janēna samaṁ
Meaning:
In springtime Krishna plays and dances with the young gopis.

sakhi virahi janasya durantē
Meaning:
But for devotees in separation, it is hard to bear.

Verse 2
unmada madana manōratha pathika vadhūjana janita vilāpē
aḷikula saṅkula kusumasamūha nirākula vakula kalāpē
Meaning:
Women pine in the pain of love;
the forest, full of flowers and bees, stays peaceful.

Verse 3
mṛgamada saurabha rabhasa vaśaṁvada navadaḷa māla tamālē
yuvajana hṛdaya vidāraṇa manasija nakharuci kiṁśukajālē
Meaning:
A fragrant breeze blows through the forest;
like the arrow of Kamadeva, it pierces the heart.

Verse 4
madana mahīpati kanakadaṇḍaruci kēsara kusuma vikāsē
miḷita śilīmukha pāṭala ṭala kṛtasmara tūṇa vilāsē
Meaning:
Kamadeva, like a king, is adorned with flowers;
he prepares his arrows and plays.

Verse 5
vigaḷita lajjita jagadavalōkana taruṇa karuṇa kṛtahāsē
virahi nikṛntana kuntamukhākṛti kētaki danturitāśē
Meaning:
Krishna smiles with compassion;
but the devotees in separation suffer.

Verse 6
mādhavikā parimaḷa militē nava mālikayāti sugandhau
munimanasāmapi mōhanakāriṇi taruṇā kāraṇa bandhau
Meaning:
The fragrance of jasmine spreads in the breeze;
it captivates even the minds of sages.

Verse 7
sphuradati mukta latā parirambhaṇa mukuḷita puḷakita cūtē
bṛndāvana vipinē parisara parigata yamunā jalapūtē
Meaning:
In Brindavan, Krishna plays with nature;
the Yamuna flows peacefully.

Final shloka
śrī jayadēva bhaṇitamidam udayati hari caraṇa smṛti sāram
Meaning:
What Jayadeva has spoken — this song awakens the remembrance of Hari.

Overall essence
This Ashtapadi says:
springtime = an outburst of emotion;
Radha's pain of separation;
Krishna's lilas;
devotion + love + viraha (separation).

Spiritual truth
“Even viraha is a form of devotion — it makes us think of the Lord ever more deeply.”

@@ 11.42 | Maṅgaḷam – the final benediction |
Theme – the concluding auspicious hymn (final benediction)

1. The general prayer for auspiciousness
dēhi maṅgaḷaṁ vai dēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
May all be well;
grant me auspiciousness.

śrī raghurāma dēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
O Rama, born in the Raghu dynasty, grant me auspiciousness.

2. The Lord in many forms
sītā manōhara dēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
O Rama, who captivated Sita, grant auspiciousness.

rādhā manōhara dēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
O Krishna, who delighted Radha, grant auspiciousness.

girijā manōhara dēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
O Shiva, who delighted Parvati, grant auspiciousness.

vallī manōhara dēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
O Murugan, who won Valli's heart, grant auspiciousness.

sēnā manōhara dēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
O commander of the army of the gods, grant auspiciousness.

vallabhā manōhara dēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
O beloved Supreme, grant auspiciousness.

3. Grace to devotees
karirāja varada dēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
O You who blessed Gajendra the elephant king — auspiciousness.

akrūra varada dēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
O Krishna who blessed Akrura — auspiciousness.

prahlāda varada dēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
O Narasimha who protected Prahlada — auspiciousness.

āñjanēya varada dēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
O Rama who blessed Hanuman — auspiciousness.

4. The form that does good to the world
dinakara kulabāla dīnajanānukūla
Meaning:
The Lord who protects the solar dynasty and blesses the poor.

anupama guṇaśīla dēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
O Lord of matchless qualities — auspiciousness.

5. The lila of Gokula
gōkula paripāla gōpavēṣa suśīla
Meaning:
Krishna, who protects Gokula, in the form of a cowherd.

gōpījana vilōla dēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
O Krishna, who captivated the hearts of the gopis — auspiciousness.

6. The final supreme goal
vēṅkaṭagirivāsa paṅkajākṣa ramēśa
Meaning:
O Lakshmi Narayana, who dwells on the Venkata hill at Tirupati.

saṅkaṭa bhavanāśa dēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
O Lord who destroys all sorrows — auspiciousness.

The last line
dēhi maṅgaḷaṁ vaidēhi maṅgaḷam
Meaning:
May all be auspicious — O Rama, together with Sita (Vaidehi), bless us.

Overall essence (to conclude the upanyasam)
This Mangalam says:
1. All are the one God —
Rama, Krishna, Shiva, Murugan — all are the one Supreme.
2. Compassion for devotees —
Gajendra, Prahlada, Akrura, Hanuman.
3. The final goal of life —
mangalam = the Lord's compassion + peace of mind + liberation.

Concluding thought
“Mangalam is not an ending — it is the full blossoming of devotion.”

@@ 11.43 | “ratna jaḍita” – Raga Kapi |
Type – Prarthana abhang (prayer)

1. The jewel-studded throne
ratna jaḍita siṁhāsana, varī śōbhē raghu nandana
Meaning:
On a throne adorned with jewels, Rama of the Raghu dynasty is seated in splendour.

2. Sita at His side
vāma bhāgī sītā bāyī, jagajjananī mājī āyī
Meaning:
On His left is Sita, seated with Rama as the Mother of the world.

3. The family of service
paścāt bhāgī lakṣumaṇa, puḍhē añjanī nandana
Meaning:
Behind is Lakshmana, in front is Anjaneya — both stand in service.

bharata śatrughna dō bhāyī, cāmara ḍāḷitī thōhī bāhī
Meaning:
Bharata and Shatrughna, on either side, wave the chamara fans — they are serving.

4. The line of devotees
naḷa nīla jāmbavanta, sugrīvāṅgada vibhīṣaṇa bhakta
Meaning:
Nala, Nila, Jambavan, Sugriva, Angada, Vibhishana — all are devotees.

5. The final surrender
dēha buddhi nēṇō kāhī, dāsa aṅkita rāmā pāyī
Meaning:
My body and my intellect are not mine — everything is offered at Rama's feet.

Overall essence (to conclude the upanyasam)
This abhang says:
1. The divine royal court —
Rama on the throne, the king of dharma.
2. The unity of the family —
Sita, Lakshmana, Bharata, Shatrughna — the whole family of Rama.
3. The service of devotees —
from Anjaneya to Vibhishana — all is devotion.
4. The final state —
“There is no ‘I’ — everything belongs to Rama.”

A closing line for the upanyasam
“The darshan of Rama is not a royal court — it is the perfect peace of devotion.”

@@ 11.44 | “dēvā hōyīna” – Divyanama Sankirtanam |
Type – Prarthana abhang (prayer) of Sant Tukaram

1. God and the beggar
dēvā hōyīna bhikārī, paṇḍharīcā vārakarī
Meaning:
O God! Even if You come as a beggar,
I will always remain a varkari (devotee-pilgrim) of Pandharpur.

2. My dharma – the Name
hāci mājhā nēma dharma, mukhī viṭhalācē nāma
Meaning:
My only rule and dharma — Vittala's name always on my lips.

3. True worship
hēci mājhī upāsanā, lāgēn santāñcyā caraṇa
Meaning:
My true worship — to cling to the feet of the saints.

4. The essence of Tukaram
ahō tukā mhaṇē dēvā, hēci mājhī bhōḷī sēvā
Meaning:
Tukaram says — O God, my service is very simple,
but it is filled with true love.

Overall essence (for the upanyasam)
The centre of this abhang:
1. The Lord's simplicity —
even God may appear as a beggar.
2. The state of devotion —
nama japa itself is dharma.
3. True worship —
service to devotees is itself worship.
4. Surrender —
“My service is very simple — but complete.”

The last line
“Devotion is not something grand — it is the truth found in simplicity.”

@@ 11.45 | “hē dayāḷuvā” – Raga Mand |
Type – Prarthana abhang (prayer)

1. hē dayāḷuvā rāmā hē kṛpāḷuvā
Word by word:
hē = O (a call)
dayāḷu = the compassionate one
rāmā = Rama
kṛpāḷu = the merciful one
Full meaning:
O compassionate Rama! O merciful Lord!

2. hē dayāḷuvā sakyā svāmi rāghavā
Word by word:
sakyā = friend / one who is close
svāmi = Lord
rāghavā = Rama of the Raghu dynasty
Full meaning:
O Raghava, my close friend, show compassion.

3. prathama kāmalā rāmā lāvilī savē
Word by word:
prathama = at first
kāmalā = love / longing
lāvilī = You accepted
savē = as if
Full meaning:
At first it seemed You had lovingly accepted me.

4. maga upēkṣitā rāmā yōgyatā navē
Word by word:
maga = and then
upēkṣitā = neglected
yōgyatā = worthiness
navē = not
Full meaning:
Now it seems You have neglected me, as though I were unworthy.

5. sakala jāṇasī maja antarasthiti
Word by word:
sakala = everything
jāṇasī = You know
maja = my
antarasthiti = inner state
Full meaning:
You fully know the state of my heart.

6. maga tujhā prati rāmā kāya vinanti
Word by word:
maga = then
tujhā prati = to You
kāya = what
vinanti = plea
Full meaning:
That being so, what shall I plead before You?

7. dāsa tumacā rāmā vāṭa pāhatō
Word by word:
dāsa = servant
tumacā = Your
vāṭa pāhatō = am waiting, watching the path
Full meaning:
Your servant waits, watching the path for You.

8. bōlatā nayē rāmā kaṇṭha dāṭatō
Word by word:
bōlatā = speaking
nayē = cannot
kaṇṭha = throat
dāṭatō = is choked
Full meaning:
I cannot speak; my voice chokes.

9. viṭhala viṭhala rāmā viṭhala viṭhala
Word by word:
viṭhala = the name of the Lord
rāmā = Rama
the repetition = remembrance
Full meaning:
Vittala! Rama! Your name is my life.

Overall essence
The content of this abhang:
1. The devotee's state —
the feeling of “I am unworthy”.
2. The Lord's knowledge —
He knows everything.
3. Wordless devotion —
even the state of being unable to speak is devotion.
4. Final surrender —
“Vittala's name is my life.”

The last line
“The devotion that cannot be spoken is the language closest to God.”

@@ 12.1 | Gaurī Kalyāṇam – Rādhā Kalyāṇam – Raga Kurinji |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word by word meaning

Opening verse
gaurī kalyāṇa vaibhavamē / rādhā kalyāṇa vaibhavamē
Just like the splendour of Gauri's wedding, the splendour of Radha's wedding is divine.

Description of the decorations
citrambu mēḍalō koluvakūḍāla
In a beautiful hall, the gods sat as in a royal court.
mutyāla tōraṇālu kaṭṭiri vāru
Festoons of strings of pearls were hung.

Decoration of the palace / hall
yennagalēni ratnāla kōṭalu dīrci
A palace was built of countless jewels.
pañca ratnāla gōpura kaṭṭu dīrci
A tower was raised of the five gems.

Pillars and lamps
rāṇiñcu nīlāla kambāla naḍuma
Pillars adorned with sapphires.
māṇikya dīpālu ettiri vāru
Ruby lamps were lit.

Arrangements for the wedding
koṭṇambu peṭṭamani yatnambu jēsi
They spent great wealth and made splendid arrangements.
koṭṇambu kaicāla paripūrṇa muganu
All the arrangements were completed in full.

The wedding scene
śrī rādhā samētulaku śōbhanamu nēḍu
Today the wedding of Radha and Krishna is most auspicious.
prēmatō bandhuvulan piluvanambuduru
With love they invited all their relatives.

Summary meaning
This song describes the divine scene of the wedding of Radha and Krishna.
In it:
the divine hall is decorated;
a palace of jewels rises;
festoons of pearls hang;
divine lamps shine;
devotees join in with joy;
the Radha–Krishna wedding takes place.

Spiritual significance
This is not just a description of a wedding.
It shows:
1. Spiritual decoration = inner purity
Jewels and pearls = the worth of devotion.
2. The hall = the heart
The place where God is seated = a pure mind.
3. The Radha–Krishna wedding =
the union of the individual soul and the Supreme Soul.

Takeaways for the upanyasam
What to emphasise in an upanyasam:
1. The outer form vs the inner meaning
Outside: the wedding decorations.
Within: the union of the soul and the Supreme.
2. Jewels = the qualities of devotion
Pearl = purity,
ruby = love,
sapphire = steady devotion.
3. The main message
“This wedding must take place within us.”

A closing line for the upanyasam
“Though this splendour of Radha Kalyanam takes place outside,
in truth it is the divine union that takes place in our hearts.”
Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 12.2 | “śrī kṛṣṇa jaya jaya” – Raga Kurinji |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word by word meaning

Opening lines – praise of the Lord
śrī kṛṣṇa jaya jaya rādhā manōhara
O Sri Krishna! Victory, victory! O beautiful one who enchants Radha!
kāruṇya sāgara karuṇā nidhē jaya jaya
O ocean of compassion! O treasure-house of mercy! Victory, victory!

Description of the wedding decorations
śrī kṛṣṇuni peṇḍlikini śṛṅgāra vatulella
For Sri Krishna's wedding, all the beautiful women
baṅgāru taṭṭalō pasupulan teccēru
brought auspicious offerings (pasupu – turmeric) on golden plates.

The divine form of Gopala
kāyajuni kannaṭṭi kanakanuḍā gōpāla
O golden Gopala, victorious over Kama!
māyalella telisērā mādhava pādamulīrā
Bow to the feet of Madhava, who knows all the illusions of maya.

The arrival of the gods
indrādi dēvatulu etiriñci gōvula
Indra and the other gods came and bowed down.
mandala tripuka vaccina madhusūdana pādamulīrā
Bow to the feet of Madhusudana, who pervades all three worlds.

Summary meaning
This song is
the scene of the gods celebrating the wedding of Radha and Krishna,
a song praising the Lord's compassion, beauty, and victory over maya.
It shows three main things:
1. The Lord's compassion —
Krishna is the ocean of compassion.
2. Victory over maya —
Madhava, who has conquered maya.
3. The divine wedding —
a wedding which the gods themselves come to join.

Spiritual significance
1. The peak of devotion
“jaya jaya” is not mere praise;
it is joyful surrender.
2. The vanishing of maya
“māyalella telisērā”
When devotion comes, maya disappears.
3. The union of the soul and the Supreme
Surrender at Krishna's feet =
the state of liberation of the soul.

Takeaways for the upanyasam
The main points to say in an upanyasam:
1. The wedding takes place outside,
but within, it is the union of the soul and the Supreme.
2. The arrival of the gods —
where devotion happens, the gods come of their own accord.
3. The main message —
“The only way to conquer maya is surrender to Krishna.”

A closing line
“This ‘śrī kṛṣṇa jaya jaya’ kirtan is not merely praise of the wedding of Radha and Krishna;
it is the awakening of the divine union that takes place in our minds.”
Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 12.3 | “allō nērē ḍillō” (Muthu Kutthal – 1) |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word by word meaning

1. The opening call
allō nērē ḍillō rādhā kalyāṇambu celuvanta māyā
The wedding of Radha and Krishna appears like a divine, splendour-filled play of maya.

2. An invitation to see the Lord
suranātha varyuni jūtāmu rārē
Come, let us see the foremost Lord of the gods.
suguṇāla vāluni jūtāmu rārē
Come, let us see the abode of all good qualities.

3. Divine beauty
surucira gātruni jūtāmu rārē
Come, let us see the one with the beautiful form.
śubha nārasiṁhuni jūtāmu rārē
Come, let us see the auspicious Narasimha.

4. The Lord's abode
garuḍādri vāsuni kandāmu rārē
Come, let us see the one who dwells on Garudadri.
gāmbhīrya vēṣuni kandāmu rārē
Come, let us see the one of majestic form.

5. The giver of boons
karivīra varaduni kandāmu rārē
Come, let us see the Lord who grants boons at Karivira.
kāmita phaladuni kandāmu rārē
Come, let us see the Lord who grants the fruits one desires.

6. The embodiment of compassion
karuṇālavāluni kandāmu rārē
Come, let us see the abode of compassion.
kāñcana dēhuni kandāmu rārē
Come, let us see the one with the golden form.

7. The rapture of devotion
aṅgitōtsavamunaku amarina vāḍē
He is the one seated at every festival.
hāhā vīḍenta mōhakuḍaina vāḍē
Ah, how enchanting this Lord is!

8. Service to the Lord
sēvē śrī dēva dēvēśam
We serve the God of gods.
sēvitam brahmādi ādi dēvēśam
Brahma and the others worship Him.

9. The Lord of the Venkata hill
vēṅkaṭātrayamuna velasina vāḍē
He is the Lord who shines on the Venkata hill.
vihaya prahlāduni vinutikai nāḍē
He is the one praised by Prahlada.

10. Grace to Rukmini
vidarbha dēśamunaku sanu tenci
Going to the land of Vidarbha,
vēga bhakta rukmiṇini pālincināvu
You swiftly protected Your devotee Rukmini.

Summary meaning
This song is a song of divine invitation.
In it:
the call “come, let us see the Lord”;
praise of His many forms;
a description of His compassion, majesty and beauty;
a reminder of the Rukmini–Krishna lila.

Spiritual significance
