1. “jūtāmu rārē” – a spiritual invitation:
the devotional feeling that invites us to experience God.
2. One God in many forms:
Narasimha, Venkatesha, Krishna — the one Paramatma.
3. Devotion = experience:
this is not a song merely to listen to;
it is the experience of “seeing” God.

Takeaways for the upanyasam
1. Devotion is an invitation —
“Come, see the Lord.”
2. God appears in many forms,
but He is the one Truth.
3. The main message —
“The only eye that sees God is the eye of devotion.”

A closing line
“This ‘allō nērē ḍillō’ is not just a song;
it is a spiritual invitation to see God through the eyes of devotion.”
Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 12.4 | “suvvi bāla kṛṣṇa” (Muthu Kutthal – 2) |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word by word meaning

1. The “suvvi” chant
suvvi bāla kṛṣṇa / suvvi rādhā kṛṣṇa
Auspicious greetings are sung to the child Krishna and to Radha.
“suvvi” = an auspicious sound, like a lullaby.

2. Praise of Ranganatha
vimalabhōgi śayana raṅga vēda vēdāntaraṅga
Ranga, who reclines on the pure serpent;
He who is the inner essence of the Vedas and Vedanta.
amita pāvanāntaraṅga akhila raṅga
Ranga, of boundless purity, who fills the whole world.

3. Kasturi Ranga
vastuvaina māṇikya vaiḍūrya mēḍalōna
He who is seated in a mansion of rubies and cat's-eye gems —
kastūri raṅga
Kasturi Ranga, the fragrant divine form.

4. The power of devotion
bhāgavatula pāda jalamu paini callu koṇṭē cālu
It is enough to sprinkle on oneself the water that has washed the feet of devotees;
it removes all sins.
(Then why set out on a journey to the Bhagirathi?)

5. Devotion vs pilgrimage
bhāgavatamu jihvanunci paṭhana jēyu cuṇṭē cālu
It is enough to recite the Bhagavatam with one's tongue throughout life;
pilgrimages are not needed.
(Then why seek to drink nectar?)

6. The Lord's qualities
vasudēva varakumāra dānavēndra madavidāra
The dear son of Vasudeva,
who destroys the pride of the asura kings;
viśva pūjita
the one worshipped by the whole world.

7. The state of the devotees
rukmiṇī mukhāravinda sañcarīka dēvabṛnda
The one who, like a bee, hovers around Rukmini's lotus face; the gods gather around Him;
vandya padayuga
His two feet are worshipped by all — kings and hosts of sages.

8. A lila of compassion
kariyu gācinaṭṭi mā ghanuḍa raṅga
Our great Ranga, who saved the elephant (Gajendra) when he was caught in danger by the crocodile.

9. The lila of Bala Krishna
uṭṭi mīda pāla kaḍava andu konna mudduraṅga
The darling child Krishna who reaches the milk pot hung on the rope sling — the butter thief;
nanda nandanuḍā gōvinda raṅga
Govinda, the son of Nanda.

10. The spirit of compassion
atithi vacci ākalanna anna minta iḍina cālu
If a guest comes and says he is hungry, it is enough to give him a little food.
(Then why long to perform many sacrifices?)

Summary meaning
This song reveals three main levels:
1. The lila of Bala Krishna —
the love, play and compassion of the child Krishna.
2. The principle of Ranganatha —
the Paramatma who is the essence of the Vedas.
3. The greatness of devotion —
devotion is higher than everything else.

Spiritual significance
1. Devotion ≠ pilgrimage —
true devotion alone is enough.
2. God = compassion —
by compassion He protects everything.
3. The child form = love —
Krishna = the embodiment of pure love.

Takeaways for the upanyasam
1. “suvvi” = a lullaby of joy —
devotion is like a mother's love.
2. The place of devotees —
the water of the devotees' feet is itself holy.
3. The main message —
“If there is devotion, everything is attained.”

A closing line
“This ‘suvvi bāla kṛṣṇa’ kirtan,
through the Lord's child-lila,
reveals the pure love of devotion.”
Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 12.5 | “pāhimām śrī kṛṣṇa” – Raga Saurashtram (Muthu Kutthal – 3) |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word by word meaning
pāhimām śrī kṛṣṇa rādhā vilōlā
Protect me, O Sri Krishna, who sports with Radha.
sakala jagadādhāra sanmukha dhīrā
O valiant one who upholds the whole world; O embodiment of good qualities.
rādhikā priya bandhō jaya dayā sindhō
O dear companion of Radha; O ocean of compassion — victory to You.
mṛṭa dṛṭa dhanur bhaṅga muni manōraṅga
He who broke Kamsa's bow; He who delights the minds of the sages.
kāruṇya guṇa dhāma kalyāṇa śyāma
O treasure-house of compassion; O auspicious dark-hued Krishna.
sakala sadguṇa pūrṇa sajala ghana varṇa
He who is full of all good qualities; He who is dark like a rain-filled cloud.
madana mōhana gātrā maṅgaḷa caritrā
The beautiful one who enchants the mind; He whose story is auspicious.
vanacarāvṛta saumya vara sārva bhauma
The gentle king who plays in the forests; the emperor of the worlds.
bhakta hṛdya nivāsa bṛndāvanēśā
He who dwells in the hearts of devotees; the Lord of Brindavan.

Simple (and complete) meaning
This song is entirely a hymn of surrender (sharanagati) to Lord Sri Krishna. With full faith the devotee prays, “Protect me.” At the same time, Krishna's compassion, beauty, valour and His being the support of the world are praised. In the end it is made clear that the devotee's heart itself is Brindavan, and that Krishna abides there.

Spiritual meaning
“pāhimām” is the beginning of surrender.
Krishna is the ocean of compassion; He is not the one who punishes, but the one who protects.
The devotee's heart is His true abode.
This is not outer worship; it is a state of inner self-offering.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“pāhimām” is the first step of devotion.
That moment of complete self-offering to God is the beginning of the spiritual journey.
Krishna is not one to be searched for outside.
He already abides in the devotee's heart.

@@ 12.6 | “suvvi candraśēkharulaku” – Raga Madhyamavati (Muthu Samarpanam) |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Meaning, verse by verse
Verse 1 – suvvi candraśēkharulaku …
Auspicious greetings (suvvi) to Chandrasekhara (Shiva), who rides the Nandi, who bears the Ganga, who is with Gauri (Parvati), and who dwells on Kailasa.
Verse 2 – suvvi lakṣmi narasiṁhuniki …
Auspicious greetings to Lakshmi Narasimha, who rides Garuda, who is the support of all, and who dwells in Vaikuntha.
Verse 3 – tanacina manasuna gaṇapatini …
Keeping Ganapati in our minds and realising the meaning of the Pranava (Om), we worship Him with devotion.
Verse 4 – nīḍugan āḍava dhūḍulu …
The devotees standing around express their devotion through songs, music and conversation.
Verse 5 – maṅgaḷamagu maṅgaḷamanucu …
The women join in joyfully in an auspicious mood and are immersed in music and devotion.
Verse 6 – kalikiki cilukala kolukiki …
Radha's beauty is compared with the grace of Lakshmi, and this divine beauty is described.
Verse 7 – talaru bōṇula vāri śiramuna …
A description of the ritual of adorning the deities with sandal paste, oil and flowers.
Verse 8 – paṭuva kāṭuka loppa beṭṭiri …
The drishti (warding off the evil eye) and the flower decorations.
Verse 9 – aṣṭāṅga vajrāla tālilu …
The festival scene, with divine ornaments — pearl garlands, diamonds and other adornments.
Verse 10 – mēlaina mutyāla hārālu …
The beauty of Radha and the deities, and the service of devotees waving the chamaram (fan).
Verse 11 – paṭṭābhirāma svarūpulu …
The Lord seated in an auspicious form as the Paramatma.
Verse 12 – akṣatala gōpāla kṛtiyaina suvvi …
Whoever sings this, at that very moment the Lord grants His grace, and the glance of Lakshmi's grace is received.

Simple (and complete) meaning
This whole suvvi song is the scene of a divine, auspicious festival. It describes a joyful hall in which all the deities — Shiva, Vishnu, Lakshmi, Radha and Ganapati — take part. The devotees delight the Lord with music, flowers, decoration and service. In the end it is made clear that those who join in this nama sankirtanam receive the Lord's grace immediately.

Spiritual meaning
The feeling that all the deities are one.
Devotion = service + music + surrender.
The Lord is always ready to grant His grace.
Taking part in nama sankirtanam is itself the path to liberation.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“suvvi” is not just a song; it is the sound of devotion melted in bliss.
God is not outside; He is right within this auspicious scene.

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 12.8 | Maṅgaḷa Snānam |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings
1. Krishna's divine form (vasudēva tanayuḍu kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa …)
Krishna, the son of Vasudeva,
shines in childhood with great sweetness and a gentle smile.
By His grace He protects all the directions.
He shines with a face as beautiful as the moon.

2. Remembering the Putana lila (pūtanu nēsanu vinumā …)
Remember that Krishna who destroyed Putana.
He is the one who removes the sorrows of the Kali age.
Just to see Him is bliss.

3. Radha's devotion (vṛṣabhānu tanayunu kōri …)
Radha, the daughter of Vrishabhanu,
thought of Krishna every day, and depended on Him alone — in mind, in deed and in love.
That devotion immersed her in the experience of bliss.

4. Krishna's compassion (ati vitaraṇa kala vāḍē …)
He is of boundless compassion.
He is the Paramatma who protects all beings.
He is the one God of surrender for His devotees.
He destroys the asuras and establishes dharma.
Krishna, the brother of Balarama, is auspicious.

Simple (and complete) meaning
This Mangala Snanam song praises Krishna's divine bath, His adornment and His lilas.
Three main feelings come through here:
Krishna's beauty and compassion;
His divine power, which destroys demonic forces such as Putana;
Radha's complete devotion and surrender.

Spiritual meaning
Krishna = the embodiment of compassion.
Radha = the symbol of complete devotion.
The lilas = the expression of divine power.
“Mangala Snanam” = the purification of the soul.
This song tells us:
the true bath is not of the body; it is the purification of the mind.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Mangala Snanam” is not an outer ceremony.
It is the state in which our mind is completely immersed in the thought of Krishna.
Only in that state do bliss and liberation arise.

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 12.9 | Māṅgalya Dhāraṇam |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings
1. The auspicious wedding scene (ānandam ānanda māyanē …)
Krishna, bliss itself in form, shines today as the bridegroom.
In the same way, Radha shines as the bride.
The fruit of all my vows (nōmu) has been fulfilled completely today.

2. The arrival of the guests and the rituals (viyyāla vārella vacciri vāru …)
All the relatives and elders have arrived for the wedding.
The mangala snanam (auspicious bath) was performed, and all the arrangements took place joyfully.
Silver, decoration, celebration — everything was arranged splendidly.

3. Worship of Krishna (janalu kṛṣṇuni pūja jēsiri …)
All the people worshipped Krishna,
and seated Him together with Radha on the same seat.

4. Mangalya dharanam (ratnāla boṭṭu kṛṣṇuḍu …)
Then Krishna tied around Radha's neck
the auspicious mangalasutra made of gems.
This is the peak of the divine wedding.

Simple (and complete) meaning
This song describes the most important moment of the Radha–Krishna wedding — the mangalya dharanam (the tying of the sacred thread).
There are three main aspects in it:
the fulfilment of devotion — the fruit of the vows attained;
the divine wedding ceremony — the state in which devotion and lila become one;
mangalya dharanam — the symbol of the union of the individual soul and the Supreme Soul.

Spiritual meaning
Radha = devotion.
Krishna = the Paramatma.
Mangalyam = everlasting union.
The wedding = the union of jivatma and Paramatma.
This event is not an outer wedding;
it is an inner, spiritual union.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
“Mangalya dharanam” is not a ritual.
It is the moment when devotion reaches its peak
and becomes one with the Paramatma forever.
In that union alone is true bliss born.

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 13.5 | “nānoru viḷaiyāṭṭu” – Raga Navarasa Kanada |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings
1. The humble state of the soul
nānoru viḷaiyāṭṭu bommaiyā jagannāyakiyē umaiyē undanukku
Am I a plaything, a doll, to you? O Mother of the world, O Uma!
nānilattil pala piravi eḍuttu tiṇḍāḍiyadu pōdādā
Is it not enough that I have taken many births in this world and suffered?

2. A plea of surrender
aruḷamudaip-paruga ammā ammā venru alaruvadaik-kēṭpadānandamā
Does it give you joy to hear my voice crying out “Amma, Amma” to drink the nectar of your grace?
oru pugalinri un tiruvaḍi aḍaindēnē tiruvuḷḷam iraṅgādā
With no other refuge, I have surrendered at your feet. Will your heart still not melt with compassion?

Simple (and complete) meaning
This song expresses a devotee's deep feeling of surrender (sharanagati).
The soul thinks of itself as a small doll, says it has suffered through many births in this world, and at last surrenders completely to the Goddess.

Spiritual meaning
The soul = not a plaything of God, but a soul standing and pleading for her compassion.
The cycle of births = a round of suffering.
Surrender = the only way to liberation.
The cry “Amma” = the peak of devotion.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
This song says:
after all knowledge and all experience are over,
a person finally realises one single truth —
“Without your compassion, I am nothing.”
That realisation is the beginning of true devotion.

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 14.1 | “kuḻandaiyāga mīṇḍum” |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings
1. Remembering Krishna's child-lila
kuḻandaiyāga mīṇḍum kaṇṇan pirakka māṭṭānā
Will Krishna (Kannan) not be born again as a child?
pullāṅkuḻal ūdi nammai mayakka māṭṭānā
Will He not play the flute again and draw us into devotion?

2. The Gopala lila
māḍu kanṟu ōṭṭi cenṟu mēyka māṭṭānā
Will that Gopala lila of grazing the cows and calves not come again?

3. The wonder of Bala Krishna

maṇṇai uṇḍu ulagattaiyē kāṭṭa māṭṭānā

4. The divine teaching

Will He not teach us the Bhagavad Gita again?

Hearing it, would we not also become good people?

Simple (and complete) meaning
This is a song of longing, sung with devotion while remembering Krishna's child-lilas and His teachings. It expresses the deep wish that Krishna should come again and guide us.

Spiritual meaning
Krishna lila = the divine play that draws us into devotion.
The flute = the divine call that captivates the mind.
The Bhagavad Gita = the guide for life.
The purpose of devotion = to transform us.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
This song says:
Krishna is not one to be searched for outside;
His lilas and His teachings transform us from within.

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 14.4 | “kausalyāsuta” – Raga Nadanamakriya (Sri Bharathi Teertha Swami) |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings

Spiritual meaning

Surrender = the path to liberation.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
This song says:
Rama is not merely an avatara;
He is the living form of dharma, compassion and the path of surrender.
If we think of Him,
ego is destroyed, the mind becomes pure, and the soul is uplifted.

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 15.1 | “nārāyaṇa tē namō” – Raga Behag (Annamacharya) |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings
1. Salutation to Narayana
nārāyaṇa tē namō namō
O Narayana, salutations to You again and again.
nārada sannuta namō namō
O You who are praised by Narada, salutations to You.

2. The Lord who destroys evil
murahara bhavahara mukunda mādhava
O destroyer of the asura Mura; O Mukunda who ends the cycle of births; O Madhava.
garuḍa gamana paṅkaja nābhā
O Pankajanabha (lotus-navelled one), who rides on Garuda.

3. The form of the Paramatma
parama puruṣa bhava bandha vimōcana
O Supreme Person, who frees us from the bondage of birth and death.
naramṛga śarīra namō namō
O You who took the man-lion form (Narasimha), salutations to You.

4. The form of peace and compassion
jaladhi śayana ravicandra vilōcana
O You who recline on the ocean; O You whose eyes are the sun and the moon.
jalaruha bhavanuta caraṇayugā
O You whose two feet are worshipped by Brahma (the lotus-born) and the gods.

5. The protector of Govardhana
bali bandhana gōvardhana vallabha
O You who bound Emperor Bali; O You who protected (with) the Govardhana hill.
nalinōdara tē namō namō
O You with the lotus navel, salutations to You.

6. The primordial God
ādi dēva sakalāgama pūjita
O primordial God, worshipped by all the Vedas (agamas).
yādava kula mōhana rūpā
O You whose beauty enchants the Yadava clan.

7. The Lord of Venkata
vēdōddhara śrī vēṅkaṭanāyaka
O Lord of Venkata, the essence of the Vedas (who rescued the Vedas).
nāda priya tē namō namō
O Lord who loves music (nada), salutations to You.

Simple (and complete) meaning
This Annamacharya kirtan worships Narayana completely. He is the Paramatma who appears in all the avataras; He destroys evil; He protects His devotees; and as Venkateshvara He showers grace on His devotees.

Spiritual meaning
Narayana = the Paramatma.
The avataras = the protection of dharma.
Surrender = the path to liberation.
Venkateshvara = the peak of compassion.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
This song says:
though Narayana appears in many forms, His one purpose is —
to protect His devotees by His compassion, and to free them from the cycle of birth and death.

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 15.2 | “indariki abhayambu” – Raga Harikambhoji (Annamacharya) |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings
1. The hand of refuge (abhaya hasta)
indariki abhayambu liccu cēyi
The hand that grants abhaya (fearless protection) to all.
kanduvagu manci baṅgāru cēyi
The most beautiful, golden divine hand.

2. The protector of the Vedas
velalēni vēdamulu vedaki teccina cēyi
The hand (of the Lord) that searched for and brought back the endless Vedas.
ciluku gubbali kinda cērcu cēyi
The hand that gathers even little birds safely beneath it.

3. The hand that upholds the universe
kalikiyagu bhūkānta kaugalincina cēyi
The mighty hand that holds up and protects the Earth.
valanaina konagōḷḷa vāḍi cēyi
The powerful hand that subdues the asuras and protects.

4. The hand that establishes dharma
purasatula mānamulu polla cēsina cēyi
The hand that destroys injustice and establishes dharma.
turagambu barapeḍi doḍḍa cēyi
The divine hand that acts swiftly and moves everything.

5. The hand that grants liberation
tiru vēṅkaṭācala dīśuḍai mōkṣambu
The Lord who, from Tiruvenkatam, grants moksha;
teruvu prāṇulakella telipeḍi cēyi
the hand that shows the path of liberation to all living beings.

Simple (and complete) meaning
In this song Annamacharya praises the holy hand of Narayana. That hand gives protection to all, protects the Vedas, upholds the world, destroys the asuras, and finally grants liberation.

Spiritual meaning
The divine hand = the symbol of protection.
The abhaya hasta = a life free from fear.
Venkateshvara = the peak of compassion.
Moksha = the final goal.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
This song says:
even a single “movement of the hand” of the Lord
has the power to grant protection, dharma and liberation.
If we surrender to Him,
fear goes away and life becomes divine.

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 15.3 | “muddugārē yaśōdā” – Raga Kurinji (Annamacharya) |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings
1. Krishna, Yashoda's pearl
muddugārē yaśōdā muṅgiṭa mutyamu vīḍu
Krishna, like a rare pearl shining in Yashoda's lap (in her courtyard).
diddarāni mahimala dēvakī sutuḍu
The son of Devaki, of immeasurable glory.

2. The radiance of Gopala
anta ninta golletala aracēti māṇikyamu
The ruby (held in the palm) of all the gopis — their king.
pantamāḍē kamsuni pāli vajramu
A power like the vajra (diamond) that destroys Kamsa.

3. The beauty of the universe
kāntula mūḍu lōkāla garuḍa pacca pūsa
The Garuda-emerald that makes all three worlds shine.
centala mālōnunna cinni kṛṣṇuḍu
The little Krishna who shines (as if) in a garland of flowers.

4. The gems of His lila
rati kēḷi rukmiṇiki raṅgumōvi pagaḍamu
The beautiful gem (coral) that gives joy to Rukmini.
miti gōvardhanapu gōmēdhikamu

5. The radiance of the divine weapons
satamai śaṅkha cakrāla sandula vaiḍūryamu
The vaidurya (cat's-eye) shining in the light of the conch and discus.
gatiyai mammu gācē kamalākṣuḍu
The lotus-eyed one who protects us, like a ray of light (our refuge).

6. The subduer of Kaliya
kāḷiṅguni talalapai gappina puṣyarāgamu
The pushyaraga (topaz) gem shining on Kaliya's heads.
yēlēṭi śrī vēṅkaṭādri indra nīlamu
He who is like the sapphire (indranila) shining on Venkatadri.

7. The supreme Narayana
pāla jalanidhilōna bāyani divya ratnamu
The divine gem shining in the Ocean of Milk.
bālunīvale tirige padmanābhuḍu
Padmanabha, who plays about like a child.

Simple (and complete) meaning
This kirtan describes little Krishna as many divine gems. He is at once a child, a protector, the embodiment of beauty, the form of power, and the Paramatma.

Spiritual meaning
Krishna = the Paramatma in the form of a child.
The gems = symbols of divine qualities.
Yashoda's love = the purity of devotion.
The lila = the way that removes the delusion of the world.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
This song says:
Krishna is not one to be searched for outside;
He is the supreme Truth in which love, beauty, power and compassion all appear together in one form — as a child.

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 15.4 | “brahmamokaṭē” – Raga Bouli (Annamacharya) |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings
1. The sound of bliss (tandanānā)
This “tandanānā” is the joyful rhythm of devotion — an expression of a heart filled with bliss.

2. The supreme Truth
brahmamokaṭē para brahmamokaṭē
Brahman is one — the supreme Truth is one.

3. The one Lord in everyone
kanduvagu hīnādhikamulindu lēvu
In truth there is no distinction of high and low.
andariki śrī harē antarātmā
What dwells within everyone is Hari alone — the inner Self (antaratma).

4. Oneness in all beings
indulō jantukulamintā-nokaṭē
All living beings are one.

5. The equality of life's stations
niṇḍāra rājū nidrincu nidrayunokaṭē / aṇḍanē baṇṭu nidranadiyunokaṭē
The sleep of a king and the sleep of a servant are the same.

6. No social distinction
meṇḍaina brāhmaṇuḍu meṭṭu bhūmiyokaṭē / caṇḍāluḍuṇḍēṭi sari bhūmiyokaṭē
The earth on which a brahmin walks and the earth on which a chandala lives are the same;
there is no high or low on the basis of birth.

7. Nature is one
kaḍagi-yēnugu mīda kāyu-yeṇḍokaṭē / pudami śunakamu mīda polayu-yeṇḍokaṭē
The sun that shines on an elephant and the sun that shines on a dog are the same;
there is no difference in the sunlight.

8. The equality of devotion
kaḍu puṇyulanu pāpa karmulanu sarigāva
The power that dissolves both merit and sin equally —
the name of Venkateshvara alone brings everything into balance.

9. The final Truth
jaḍiyu śrī vēṅkaṭēśvaru nāmamokaṭē
The name of Sri Venkateshvara alone is the one Truth;
that name is everything.

Simple (and complete) meaning
This kirtan states the highest Advaitic truth that Annamacharya teaches:
“High and low, caste and difference, merit and sin — all are finally one; and that One is Venkateshvara.”

Spiritual meaning
Everything is the one Brahman.
There is no caste distinction.
All living beings are equal.
The final Truth = the divine Name.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
This song says:
though differences appear outside,
the inner truth is one —
it is “Brahman”, or “the name of Venkateshvara”.
To realise this is the peak of devotion.

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 15.5 | “jō acyutānanda” – Lullaby style (Annamacharya) |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings
1. A lullaby calling the Lord
jō acyutānanda jō jō mukundā
O Achyuta, O Mukunda — sleep, sleep.
rāvē paramānanda rāma gōvindā
Come, O Rama, O Govinda, the form of supreme bliss.

2. Krishna's child-lila
nanduniṇṭanu cēri nayamu mīraṅgā
You have come to Nanda's house and dwell there sweetly.
candravadanalu nīku sēva cēyaṅgā
The moon-faced gopis serve You.
andamuga vāriṇḍḷa āḍucuṇḍaṅgā
You play in their houses.
mandalaku doṅga mā muddu raṅgā
O our darling Ranga, the thief who steals the hearts of all.

3. Calling the child Krishna
aṅgajuni ganna māyanna iṭu rārā
O magical Kanna, victorious over Manmatha, come here.
baṅgāru ginnelō pālu pōsērā
They are feeding You milk in a golden bowl.
doṅga nīvani satulu poṅgucunnārā
The women laugh, calling You a thief.
muṅgiṭa nāḍarā mōhanākārā
Will You come out in front and play, O beautiful Krishna?

4. Annamacharya's lullaby
aṅgugā tāḷḷapākannayya cāla
Annamacharya of Tallapaka sings sweetly.
śṛṅgāra racanagā ceppē nī jōla
This song took shape as a beautiful lullaby for You.
saṅgatiga sakala sampadala nīvēḷa
All wealth is Your grace alone.
maṅgaḷamu tirupaṭḷa madana gōpāla
O auspicious Gopala, who shines at Tirupati.

Simple (and complete) meaning
This song is an expression of devotion in which Krishna is thought of as a child and a lullaby is sung to Him. He is a child at play, and at the same time the Paramatma. Bala Krishna, raised in the love of the gopis, shows the sweetness that is the peak of devotion.

Spiritual meaning
Krishna = the Paramatma in the form of a child.
The lullaby = the sweetness of devotion.
The gopis = pure devotion.
The lila = divine bliss.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
This song says:
God is not one who is far away;
He is the form of love who plays with us like a child.
Even singing Him a lullaby is a spiritual experience.

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 16.1 | “nāmadēva kīrtana” – Abhang (on Sant Namadev) |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings
1. The blissful dance of devotion
nāmadēva kīrtana karī prēmabharē nācē pāṇḍuraṅgā
As Namadev sings kirtan with devotion, Panduranga dances in bliss.
janābāyī mhaṇē dēvā bōlū(m) abhaṅgā
Janabai says — this is the Lord's abhang (song of devotion).

2. A kirtan full of devotion
abhaṅga bōlatā kīrtanī raṅga ōḍhavaḷā
As the abhang is sung in the kirtan, the colour of devotion spreads.
prēmācīyā chandē(m) dēvā nācū(m) lāgaḷā
In the moonlight of love, the Lord begins to dance.

3. The Lord's bliss
nācata ḍōlata dēvācā kaḷala pītāmbara
The Lord, wearing His yellow silk (pitambara), dances and sways.
sāvadha hōī dēvā, aisē bōlē kabīra
As Kabir says, the Lord is ever watchful.

4. The union of the devotees
yēkaca ghartī jhālī candra bhāgē vāḷū vāṇḍī
On the banks of the Chandrabhaga, Namadev and Jnanadev are together;
mājhā nāmadēva mājhā gnānadēva gōpāla layyā(m) vāṇḍī
they are united as one in devotion.

5. The path of liberation
nāmayācī janī lōḷē santāncē pāyīm
Namadev's feet protect the lives of the devotees;
janma maraṇa pāsūni mukta karīkē viṭhōbāyī
Vithoba (Panduranga) frees us from the cycle of birth and death.

Simple (and complete) meaning
This abhang shows Namadev's love for Panduranga (Vithoba) and the bliss of devotional kirtan. It says that when kirtan is sung, the Lord Himself dances in bliss of His own accord.

Spiritual meaning
Abhang = the expression of pure devotion.
Kirtan = the power that awakens the Lord.
Panduranga = the form of compassion.
Devotion = direct communion with the Lord.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
This song says:
true kirtan is not just music;
it is the power that, through the fire of devotion, makes the Lord dance.
When the devotee sings,
the Lord Himself dances in bliss.

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 16.2 | “hari mukhē mhaṇā” – Abhang (Sant Jnanadev) |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings
1. The glory of the Lord's Name
hari mukhē mhaṇā hari mukhē mhaṇā
Always say “Hari” with your mouth.
puṇyācā gaṇanā kōṇa karī
Who can count the merit of that chanting of the Name?

2. The glory of Rama nama
jay jay rām
The name of Rama gives victory.

3. The Lord opens the door
dēvāciyā dvārī ubhā kṣaṇabharī
Even if one stands at the Lord's door for a single moment,
tēṇē muktī cārī sādhi yēlyā
in that moment all four kinds of liberation are attained.

4. The power of the Name
viṭhala viṭhala … pāṇḍuraṅgā
The name of Panduranga is everything.

5. The way to cross samsara
asōni saṁsārī jivhē vēgu karī
Even while living in samsara, engage your tongue swiftly in the Name.
vēda śāstra ubhārī bāhya sadā
(In it) the essence of the Vedas and Shastras is revealed.

6. Jnanadev's truth
gnānadēva mhaṇē
Jnanadev says:
vyāsāciyā khuṇē dvārakēcā rāṇā pāṇḍavā gharī
Following Vyasa's indication, the Lord who reigns in Dwaraka dwells with the Pandavas.

Simple (and complete) meaning
This abhang says:
the Lord's Name is the direct path to liberation. Even the essence of the Vedas and Shastras is “nama smaranam” (remembrance of the Name). If one chants the name of Panduranga, the bondage of samsara goes away.

Spiritual meaning
Nama smaranam = the direct path to liberation.
Merit = immeasurable in the Name.
Panduranga = the embodiment of compassion.
Devotion = the essence of the Vedas.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
This song says:
we need not travel far in search of liberation;
if we say the Name even for a moment, the Lord opens His door.

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 16.3 | “hari mhaṇā” – Abhang |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings
1. One Lord, many names
hari mhaṇā tumhī gōvindā mhaṇā tumhī
You alone are Hari; You alone are Govinda.
gōpāla mhaṇā tumhī nārāyaṇā
You alone are Gopala; You alone are Narayana.
The one Lord appears in many forms.

2. The true form of Rama
rāma mhaṇā rājā rāma mhaṇā sītā
Rama is the king, and the supreme dharma united with Sita.
rāma mhaṇā ātmā rāma mhaṇā
Rama is the Atma that dwells within us.
(The names that follow — Kodanda Rama, Kalyana Rama, Ayodhya Rama, Atmabhi Rama, Pattabhi Rama, Ananda Rama — are all chanted as the one Rama.)

3. Krishna's form of lila
śyāma mhaṇā ghana śyāma mhaṇā mēgha
He who is beautiful like a dark rain-cloud.
śyāma mhaṇā rādhē śyāma mhaṇā
Krishna, the very life of Radha.

4. Gopala's lila with His devotees
kuñjavihārā gōvindā
Govinda, who plays in the groves.
gōrasacōrā gōvindā
Govinda, the thief of milk and butter.
gōpikā hṛdaya nandakiśōrā
The child Krishna, Nanda's son, who lives in the hearts of the gopis.
pītāmbaradhara gōvindā
Govinda, who wears the yellow silk (pitambara).

5. The culmination of chanting the Name
gōvinda gōpāla hari nārāyaṇā
The Lord's names that grant victory.

Simple (and complete) meaning
This abhang says:
though the Lord has many names, in truth He is the one Paramatma. As Rama, as Krishna, as Govinda and as Narayana, He is the one who dwells within us.

Spiritual meaning
The oneness of God (Ekam Sat).
Many names = one Truth.
The Atma = Rama.
Devotion = nama smaranam.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
This song says:
though the names are many, the truth is only one.
That One is the Lord who dwells in our heart.

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 16.4 | “dāsācī sampatti” – Abhang (Sant Ramdas tradition) |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings
1. The devotee's wealth
dāsācī sampatti, rāma sītāpati
The true wealth of a devotee — Rama, the Lord of Sita.
jīvācā saṅgāti, rāma ēka
The soul's only companion is Rama alone.

2. Rama is every relation
rāma ēka mātā, rāma ēka pitā
Rama alone is mother; Rama alone is father.
rāma ēka bhrātā, sahōdara
Rama alone is brother.

3. Not worldly wealth
sahōdara vidyā, vaibhava kāñcana
Learning, riches and gold are all impermanent.
sarvahā svajana, rāma ēka
The one true kinsman is Rama.

4. The guide of life
rāma ēka svāmī, rāmahā kaivārī
Rama alone is our master and protector.
lābhayā saṁsārī, rāma ēka
In samsara, the only real gain is Rama.

5. The final Truth
rāma ēka gnāna, rāma ēka dhyāna
Rama alone is knowledge; Rama alone is meditation.
rāma samādhāna, rāmadāsī
Complete peace lies in Rama alone — so says Ramdas.

Simple (and complete) meaning
This abhang says:
the relations, wealth, knowledge and security we seek in the world are all impermanent. But Rama alone is our true wealth, our relation, our wisdom and our liberation.

Spiritual meaning
Rama = everything.
Worldly relations = impermanent.
Devotion = single-pointed focus.
Liberation = faith in Rama.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
This song says:
do not search for anything outside;
everything is contained in Rama.
If we hold on to Him,
life attains fullness.

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 16.5 | “jyācē vaṁśī” – Shloka + Abhang (Sant Ramdas tradition) |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings

The shloka
sasya śyāmaḷa sundaram tava vapuhu cittē sadā rājatām
May Your beautiful dark form, green-dark like young crops, shine in my mind forever.
tvan nāmāvaḷī kīrtanam raghupatē jihvāñcalē vardhatām
O Raghupati, may the chanting of Your names grow ever on my tongue.
svāmin mē sukha jīvanam bhavatu tē pādābjayōhō santatam
O Lord, may my life rest happily at Your lotus feet always.
chāyāyām vasataha tṛṇīkṛta surā vāsasya dāsasya ca
For Your servant who dwells in their shade, even the world where the gods live is as worthless as a blade of grass.
The essence:
“The Lord's feet are life; the Name is breath.”

The abhang
1. Birth into devotion
jyācē vaṁśī kula dharma rāma sēvā
The family whose family dharma is the service of Rama — that is the highest lineage.
tyācē vaṁśī maja janma dēgā dēvā
O Lord, grant me birth in such a family.

2. The tongue that speaks the Name
jyācī vāṇī raṅgalī rāma nāmī
The tongue becomes sweet in the name of Rama;
tyācī majasi saṁvāda ghaḍō svāmī
(O Lord, may I have the company and conversation of such people.)

3. A prayer to the Lord
vāram vāram vinantī parī sāvī
Again and again I pray to the Lord.
kṛpā sindhū satvara siddhi nyāvī
O ocean of compassion, bless me quickly.

4. The wish for Ramdas's company
mhaṇē uddhava cidghana mahārāja / rāmadāsācā saṅga ghaḍō maja
May I have the company (satsang) of Ramdas.

Simple (and complete) meaning
This song says:
the name of Rama is the breath of life.
A family that lives in that Name is the truly holy lineage.

Spiritual meaning
The Name = the power of life.
Rama seva = the highest dharma.
The company of devotees = the path to liberation.
Compassion = the Lord's grace.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
This song says:
“Even my birth should be for the service of Rama;
my tongue should be immersed in the name of Rama.”

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 17.1 | “sunō sunō sādhujī” – Sant Kabirdas |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Opening jai-ghosh
siyāpati rāmacandra kī jai … tapōvana gnānānanda kī jai
Victory to Ramachandra, Lord of Sita; to Krishnachandra, Lord of Radha; to Chandrachuda, Lord of Gauri; to Hanuman, son of the Wind; and to Gnanananda of Tapovanam.

Word meanings

1. sunō sunō sādhujī rājā rāma kahō jī
sunō sunō = listen, listen carefully
sādhujī = O holy people (sadhus)
rājā rāma = Rama the king
kahō jī = say / understand
Meaning:
“O sadhus, listen carefully — I am speaking of Rama.”

2. bhāva bhakti kā bhūkhā sōī, jōga jugata nahī(n) pāvē
bhāva bhakti = devotion full of feeling
kā bhūkhā = one who hungers for
sōī = He alone (the Lord)
jōga jugata = yogic methods / philosophical techniques
nahī(n) pāvē = cannot attain
Meaning:
“The Lord is not reached through yogic techniques; He longs only for hungry, heartfelt devotion.”

3. bhakta bhūla gayē rāma dīvānē, nija pada vākō dēvē
bhakta = devotees
bhūla gayē = have forgotten (themselves)
rāma dīvānē = those madly in love with Rama
nija pada = His own state
vākō dēvē = He gives to them
Meaning:
“True devotees forget themselves; the Lord gives them His own state.”

4. The examples of Dhruva, Gajendra, Vidura and Sudama
Dhruva
vyādha kaun ācār kiyā thā = did no great ritual
dhruva kī umara tōḍī = a small child
Meaning:
“Though Dhruva was a small boy, he attained the Lord through devotion.”

Gajendra
kahā(n) gajēndra paḍhā thā vidyā = had no learning
Meaning:
“Though Gajendra had no learning, he was saved through devotion.”

Vidura
jāt vidura kī kaḍavī = regarded as being of low birth
Meaning:
“Vidura attained the Lord through his great devotion; caste does not matter.”

Sudama
bhārī dhan sē rāma milata nahī(n) = the Lord is not attained by great wealth
Meaning:
“Though Sudama was poor, he attained the Lord through devotion.”
(Ugrasena and Kubja, named in the same verse, are further examples of the same truth.)

5. kahata kabīrā suna bhāī sādhu, rāma milata hai bhakti
kahata kabīrā = Kabirdas says
suna bhāī sādhu = listen, O sadhus
rāma milata hai = Rama is attained
bhakti = through devotion alone
Meaning:
“Kabir says — Rama is attained through devotion alone.”

6. jantar mantar laṭpaṭ sē nahī(n), rāmabhajan sē mukti
jantar mantar = charms, mantras and rituals
laṭpaṭ sē nahī(n) = not by confused methods
rāmabhajan = singing and chanting the name of Rama
mukti = liberation
Meaning:
“Not rituals — only the bhajan of Rama's name gives liberation.”

Simple (and complete) meaning
In this bhajan Kabirdas says:
to attain the Lord, no learning, caste, wealth or yogic ritual is needed.
If there is true devotion alone, the Lord comes of His own accord.

Spiritual meaning
Devotion = the direct way to reach the Lord.
Ritual = not the real support.
Humility = what the Lord loves most.
The Name = the door to liberation.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
The key truth of this bhajan:
“The Lord is not far away; if we call Him with the hunger of devotion, He comes at once.”

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 17.2 | “guru kṛpā añjana pāyō” – Sant Kabirdas |
(The full lyrics are in the Lyrics tab.)

Word meanings

1. The Guru's grace – the opening of the eyes
guru kṛpā añjana pāyō mērē bhāī
guru kṛpā = the Guru's compassion
añjana = the collyrium (kajal) applied to the eyes
pāyō = I received
mērē bhāī = O my brother
Meaning:
“By the Guru's grace I received the collyrium for my eyes.”

2. The true vision
rāma binā kacu dēkhata nāhī
rāma binā = other than Rama
kacu = anything
dēkhata nāhī = I do not see
Meaning:
“I see nothing at all except Rama.”

3. Everything is the Lord
andara rāma bāhara rāma
andara = within
bāhara = outside
rāma = the Lord
Meaning:
“Rama is within, and Rama is outside.”

jahā(n) dēkhē vahā(n) pūraṇahī kāma
jahā(n) dēkhē = wherever I look
vahā(n) = there
pūraṇahī = only fullness
Meaning:
“Wherever I look, there is only the complete, perfect Lord.”

4. Rama in waking and in dream
jāgata rāma sōvata rāma
jāgata = in the waking state
sōvata = in sleep
Meaning:
“Whether awake or asleep, it is Rama.”

sapanō(n) mē dēkhata rājā hī rāma
sapanō(n) mē = in dreams
rājā hī rāma = Rama Himself appears as the King
Meaning:
“Even in dreams, Rama alone appears.”

5. Wisdom of experience
kahata kabīrā anubhava nīkā
kahata kabīrā = Kabir says
anubhava = experience
nīkā = sweet, beautiful
Meaning:
“Kabir says — this is my (sweet) experience.”

jahā(n) dēkhē vahā(n) rāma sarikhā
jahā(n) dēkhē = wherever I look
rāma sarikhā = like Rama
Meaning:
“Wherever I look, I see only Rama.”

Simple (and complete) meaning
Kabirdas says:
once the eyes are opened by the Guru's grace, there is nothing else in the world — Rama alone is everywhere. In waking, in dream and in sleep, the one Lord pervades all.

Spiritual meaning
The Guru = the one who opens the eye of truth.
The Name = makes us see the Lord in everything.
The universe = the expression of the Lord.
The wisdom of experience = the final Truth.

A beautiful closing for an upanyasam
This bhajan says:
“When the Guru's grace comes, the world fades away and only the Lord is seen.”

Hari nama sankirtanam jayati!

@@ 17.3 | “śrī rāmacandra kṛpālu” – Raga Yaman Kalyan (Sant Tulsidas) |

Word meanings

1. The opening call to devotion

haraṇa bhavabhaya dāruṇam

“Worship Rama, who destroys the terrible fear of birth and death.”

2. The beautiful divine form

kañjamukha karakañja pada kañjāruṇam

kandarpa agaṇita amita chavi

Spiritual meaning

The Name = purifies the mind.
Devotion = destroys fear.
